# 1761 Pochayiv Menaion — Vol. 7: March (Pochayiv Lavra, 1761) <a id="1761-pochayiv-menaion-vol-7-march-pochayiv-lavra-1761"></a>

*FRONT MATTER: TITLE PAGE:*
*[Typographical Decorative Border]*

**BOOK OF THE MENAION**  
**MONTH OF MARCH**

To the glory of the Holy, Consubstantial, Life-Creating, and Undivided Trinity: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit; under the reign of the Great King Augustus the Third;[^1]  
By the blessing of His Excellency, the Lord Sylvester Lubieniecki-Rudnytsky,[^2] Exarch of the Metropolia of Kyiv, Halych, and all Rus', Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh;  
By the resolve of the Most Reverend Lord Father Hieromonk Hypatius Bilynsky,[^3] Protoarchimandrite of the Order of Saint Basil the Great;  
Through the diligence and at the expense of the monks of the same Order, in the Holy Miraculous Lavra of Pochaiv, issued in print.  
In the year from the creation of the world according to the Greek chronographers: 7269; and from the Nativity of Christ: 1761.[^4]

*(Vacat — Fol. 1v: Unnumbered blank verso)*

# MONTH OF MARCH

*March 1: Holy Venerable-Martyr Eudokia:*

*The Month of March, having 31 days.*  
*On the 1st day: the Holy Venerable-Martyr Eudokia.*

*If it be the Fast, we sing Alleluia. But if not: the Troparion.*[^5]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4:*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Dal yesi znameniye):*

Having first fasted, and having withered the wantonness of the flesh by self-control, by suffering thou didst cast down a second time the crafts of the enemy, and didst obtain the victory over him, O Eudokia, blessed of God. Therefore Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, crowned thee for both these contests.

With the furrows of thy judgments, as a fertile and God-inspired soil, Thou didst sow the divine seed, and yielding a hundredfold harvest of martyrdom, Thou didst truly blossom forth; and Thou hast gathered them into spiritual granaries by the power of the Spirit Who transformed thee, and Who by His grace transformed thee with a better transformation, O all-praised Eudokia.

Thou didst raise the dead by thy life-creating illumination, O glorious Eudokia, having first put to death the bodily passions by the pains of self-control; and now thou dwellest in the heavens with the martyrs, having run well the course of suffering by the help of the Spirit, interceding for all who hymn thee in faith.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*

Rejoice, ray radiant as the sun, throne of the unwaning Sun, who didst dawn the incomprehensible Sun! Rejoice, mind glistening with divine splendors, lightning flash that enlightens the ends of the earth: true golden gleam, all-undefiled and all-immaculate one, who hast shone forth an unwaning light upon the faithful.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben:*

Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee in return for the good things Thou didst for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion.

*Troparion, Tone 8:*

Having bound thy soul to the love of Christ with uprightness of mind, and having surpassed corruptible, pleasant, and fleeting things by forgetting them as a disciple of the Word, thou didst first put the passions to death through fasting, and second didst put the enemy to shame through suffering. Therefore Christ hath deemed thee worthy of a double crown, O glorious Eudokia; O venerable passion-bearer, pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Come, ye peoples, let us sing a song to Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparia:*

Deign, O Word, to sweeten the affliction of my soul through the supplications of Thy passion-bearer Eudokia,[^6] and grant me to sing of her.

Who will not proclaim the abyss of Thy compassion? For Thou didst cause to be saved a stranger who was formerly in the grip of grievous sins.

As one who offered sweet wine instead of evil, she received the teaching of venerable Germanus, which brought forth the fruit of piety.

Having Christ as a spark in thy heart, O all-pure one, through endurance thou didst truly consume deceit and bodily passions like brushwood.

*Theotokion:*

Truly thou hast been shown to be a God-like dwelling, O pure Virgin, being more spacious and more honorable; and now the angelic choir rejoices in thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish me in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Troparion:*

Having obtained Christ as a bulwark and an invincible rampart within her breast, Thy venerable one was not shaken by the assaults of the enemy, but utterly destroyed him.

...this honorable body; and those who embrace its fruits are made partakers of sweet fragrance, and seeing it they marvel that in truth a dead body pours forth healings of all diseases.[^7] Therefore come ye all now with zeal, together with me the humble one, and having cleansed our defilement, let us draw forth the grace of miracles!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

The helper of the enemy, who of old ensnared thee, when trampled under thy feet groaned greatly because of the rain of thy God-inspired words, crying: Woe is me, I am vanquished, O all-praised Eudokia, by the lips that unceasingly offer supplication unto God!

Having put to death the desires of thy flesh through much self-control, thou didst grant boldness unto thy mothers in their resurrection unto God, and unto Him here by thine undoubting prayer; for the prayer of those who fear Him slays [the enemy].

Bearing the wounds of Christ upon thy neck, as a God-befitting adornment, thou didst draw Him by the desire of incorruption, therefore He found thee an undefiled bride, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Gideon of old prefigured thy divine birthgiving, O pure one, clearly bearing the fleece full of water which he wrung out from the wool; for God the Word took up His abode in thee, O all-pure one. Blessed is the fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Him Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, praise ye God as the Lord, all ye works, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Having attained with thy mind unto the true Lover, thou didst readily endure bodily wounds and the bitter pain of fire, O all-praised martyr, blessing Christ unto all ages.

With the staff of the Cross thou didst shatter the tempest of afflictions, and didst save the rudder of the soul into the harbors of the Kingdom of God, O glorious one, blessing Christ unto ages.

Thou didst strip off the disfigurement of the passions and didst clothe thy soul with the fair garment of dispassion. Therefore Christ, having betrothed thee as a bride, took thee into the heavenly chambers.

*Theotokion:*

...through thee, O Theotokos, our foremother was loosed from condemnation, and lo, now women suffer for Christ; and female nature rejoices, as the first-martyr and virgin of virgins cries aloud.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

God the Word, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake was ineffably enfleshed of the Holy Virgin, with one mind we the faithful magnify in hymns.

*Troparia:*

The sight of thy face, O pure one, having shone forth like the sun with a spiritual radiance, showed forth a marvelous vision unto the unbelievers who beheld it; for having loved Christ as heaven, thou hast worthily been glorified by Him, O all-honored one.

O divine martyr, having brightly fed the lamp of thy soul with the oil of thy blood, rejoicing thou hast now entered into the divine bridal chamber of Christ with the wise virgins; with them remember all who praise thee in faith.

As a sweet-scented rose from the thorns of deceit, O God-wise one, thou didst blossom forth in beauty, and hast perfumed the souls of the faithful, O Eudokia; and thou didst steadfastly repel the deceitful stench of the unbelievers. Therefore we bless thee.

O pure martyr, by thy prayers unto God and the Redeemer, save from all afflictions those who celebrate thy prayerful memory with love, and wash away the soul's defilement from me who gladly hymn thee.

*Theotokion:*

O all-holy Virgin, pray to thy Son, God the Lover of mankind, on their behalf; for we have acquired thee as our hope, and through thy protection we who praise thee in faith are delivered from afflictions, and are saved from transgressions and diseases.

*[Decorative Headpiece Band]*

*March 2: Holy Hieromartyr Theodotus of Cyrenia:*

*In the same month, on the 2nd day: the Holy Hieromartyr Theodotus, Bishop of Cyrenia.*  
*Alleluia, or the Troparion.*[^8]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4:*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant among the Martyrs" (Yako dobla):*

A most sacred priest, the foundation of the Church, an unshakable pillar hast thou been shown to be, O all-glorious one; a most true wonderworker, a glorious servant of God, and a radiant luminary of the consecrated [priests] Having desired the beauty of Christ, and consumed by His vine: through suffering thou wast united unto Him, O God-wise and all-glorious one.

Having been cleansed first by Christ's Baptism, and with thy tears, and after this by the blood of martyrdom, thou wast a temple, a threefold place of sanctification.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst blossom forth as the joy of Anna, censing the King, O Virgin; and the women who rejoiced at thy birthgiving were loosed from the curse through thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Preletno / Skoro predvari):*[^9]

Illumined with divine light, thou didst forsake the darkness of deceit; for thou wast enriched with holy divine repose, and filled with divine grace, by a brief word thou didst raise the dead. Therefore, having gloriously finished the contest of martyrdom, thou wast divinely adorned with a crown and didst put the deceiver to shame. O Eudokia, equal-to-the-angels, pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As the only one among women who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we the generations of mankind bless thee; for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou didst feed with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy birthgiving, and with one voice we cry unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who worship thy most holy birthgiving in faith.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb, the Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy compassion. O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, Who alone art the Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*

With the river of thy divine blood, O glorious Eudokia, thou didst dry up the abyss of godlessness.

She who of old ensnared the foolish with the nets of fornication, now catches the faithful for the Kingdom of God.

The corruptible wealth gathered by thee in injustice, having dispersed it righteously, thou didst inherit the wealth of incorruption.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast shone forth light, O Lord, unto those in the night and darkness of transgressions, taking flesh from the pure Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God beside Thee.

*Troparia:*

The unwaning Light having truly shone upon the glorious one, delivered her from the gloom of passions, and having guided her to the path of dispassion, showed her forth as good unto all.

Having prayed to Christ God the King, as she who bore a son, by invoking His honored name, O most glorious one, thou didst raise up the dead child of the king.

With manly purpose thou didst glorify Christ at the tribunal, confessing God incarnate; and beheaded in the neck by the sword, thou dost dance in choir with the martyrs.

*Theotokion:*

As having thy protection as our hope, O faithful, and surrounded by the tribulations of bitter afflictions, we earnestly flee unto thee, O Lady Virgin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Wallowing in the abyss of sins, I call upon the fathomless abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

The holy zeal of the faith hath seized thee, O martyr Eudokia: thou didst trample down the pride of the infidels, and didst win the victory of suffering.

Having mingled the streams of martyrdom with drops of blood, thou didst accomplish one cleansing from both.

Formerly filled with the stench of much impurity, she is now filled with sweet fragrance, and joins in the choir of virgins.

*Theotokion:*

O pure Theotokos, who art joy itself, deem him who faithfully cries "Rejoice" unto thee with the angel, worthy of thy joy; and heal my sorrowful soul.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*

Having contested well in thy contest, and after death sanctifying us through the outpouring of miracles, O praiseworthy one; as we flee in faith into thy divine temple, celebrating we pray thee, O venerable martyr Eudokia: Deliver us from spiritual infirmities, that we may draw forth the grace of miracles.

*Ikos:*

The church of Grotenka,[^10] which planted the garden of immortality in my midst, [presents] this honorable body thou wast consecrated, and having planted the tree of life in the midst, [thou didst serve] the Giver of life, O hieromartyr Theodotus.

Stretched upon the tree with ox sinews, and cruelly handled by the guards, and shut up in prison, and thy feet grievously pierced with nails, and laid upon fiery coals, thou wast shown to be unarmed, glorifying Him Who strengthened thee unto ages, O hieromartyr Theodotus.

Through the sinews of thy contests thou didst cast down the enemy and overthrow his power, and having fought most radiantly, thou dwellest now crowned in the highest Kingdom, deemed worthy of forgiveness; and praying that they who celebrate in faith may be saved, [O adornment] of martyrs, Theodotus.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*

Deliver my humble soul from condemnation and from grievous transgressions, O all-holy Bride of God, and from deadly afflictions by thy prayers, that I may obtain justification before the King on the day of trial, which the assemblies of the saints have attained; show me forth cleansed by weeping and the pouring forth of tears before the end.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben:*

When the Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she was amazed and cried aloud: What is this vision, O desired Son? Is this what the ungrateful and lawless assembly hath rendered unto Thee, who enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable descent, O Master!

*Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "Thou hast fittingly been a divine gift, O blessed one."*[^11] *Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Israel of old, having traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, by the crosswise-stretched hands of Moses overcame the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*

Direct thy movements unto Him, O martyr, whose venerable commandments thou didst keep: thou wast a hierarch, and didst most manifestly shine forth with the blood of martyrdom.

Having lived divinely and piously, and having put the movements of the flesh to death by ascetic struggles, thou didst receive life, O adornment of hierarchs and completion of martyrs.

The Holy Spirit dwelt in thy most wise heart from thy swaddling clothes, O venerable one, showing thee forth as upright, compassionate, gentle, and a divine hierarch and a divine hierarch, O martyr Theodotus.

*Theotokion:*

Human nature, which was formerly deceitfully enslaved by error, O pure one, having given birth to Christ, obtained freedom, glorifying thee with high praise.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge, and my foundation!

*Troparia:*

By the sighs of thy tears and the streams of thy blood, thou didst extinguish the fierce coals of deceit, O martyr and hierarch Theodotus.

Thy pure life showed forth divine sacred actions, and made thee a true witness of Christ's passions.

Standing before the tribunal, and wounded by cruel blows, thou didst not deny the name of Christ, O martyr Theodotus.

*Theotokion:*

Heal my mind, O all-pure one, which hath grown weak from the assaults of the deceitful serpent and is enslaved to sin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised upon the Cross" (Voznesyisya):*

Having made the robe of thy hierarchal office more radiant by steeping it in thy blood, thou didst take up thine abode in the heavens, O Theodotus, seeing with a pure mind Him Whom thou didst desire. Therefore we glorify thy most sacred memory, and cry aloud: Remember us, as thou standest with boldness before God!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Who can tell the multitude of my evil thoughts, and the surge of unlawful imaginations, O all-immaculate one? Or who can recount the assaults of my bodiless enemies and their malice? But by thy prayer, grant me deliverance from them all!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee, Who wast begotten of the unoriginate Father, and in the last times born of her in the flesh, upon the Cross, O Christ, she cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How is it that Thou, Who art glorified as God by the angels, dost now will to be crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thy long-suffering!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, the Church stood in her place, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Thy body covered with blood from wounds made thy mind firmer and in truth made thee a stronger martyr, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Inflamed with eternal love, thou didst endure the most cruel pains from wounds, as though another were suffering as though another were suffering, thou didst remain calm, O greatest among martyrs, Theodotus.

Thou didst endure the torments that approached thy body, O God-wise father; for with the honor of thy mind thou didst behold the abiding rewards, which lightened thy pains.

*Theotokion:*

O most radiant chamber of the Good One, all-holy Theotokos: show us forth as temples of the Holy Spirit, who in thy holy temple offer praise unto the Lord.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, O my Lord, hast come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from the darkness of ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparia:*

Lifted up upon the tree, O wise Theodotus, thou didst endure most firmly the tearings of thy flesh, greatly causing us to marvel.

Thou didst endure the bodily scrapings of iron claws,[^12] O Theodotus, having the Master Who strengthened thee and established thee in faith.

Having dyed thy sacred vesture with the drenchings of thy blood, O blessed Theodotus, thou didst show it forth more radiant.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst conceive God Who became man of thee, O benefactress; therefore, generations of generations, we unceasingly call thee blessed.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of thanksgiving, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast cast into prison as an evildoer, O Theodotus, though thou wast a keeper of the divine commandments of the Master, O glorious one, Who hath greatly made radiant thy divine feast.

Thou didst stand before the tribunal of unjust judges, and didst condemn men who acted lawlessly against God, preferring unrighteousness, O God-wise hierarch, rather than the truth.

Thou didst shatter the waves of cruel torments like a rock, O all-blessed one, having ascended by the ascent of thy heart unto Christ God, the cornerstone.

*Theotokion:*

For Christ our God, having taken up His abode in thy womb, casts out the sin dwelling in mankind, O all-pure one, and in His love for mankind makes them His own.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Thou didst refute the false belief of the sea,[^13] and by the faith of the true confession thou didst wound the deceit of idolatrous godlessness; and having become a divine whole-burnt offering, by the working of wonders thou didst bedew the ends [of the earth], O father, hierarch Theodotus: pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

...mercy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

From the furnace, the Abrahamic youths in Persia, burned by love of piety rather than by flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Inflamed with the divine love of the Spirit, O father, laid upon a bed of fiery coals,[^14] thou didst remain unconsumed, crying: Blessed art Thou, my God and Lord!

Thou didst remain as though another were suffering; for having gone entirely with thy mind unto God, O blessed one, thou didst disregard lacerations and burnings by the immaterial nod of Christ.

By the wisdom of thy words, O blessed one, in the midst of the tribunal thou didst manifestly refute those who were full of unreason, who were diseased with impiety, and who preferred demonic idols rather than the Creator.

*Theotokion:*

Together with the choir on high, all we cry "Rejoice!" unto thee, O Mother of God: animate heaven, and most glorious chamber, and fiery throne upon which Christ rested in the flesh.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Daniel, having stretched out his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, having girded themselves with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Resisting the enemy with thy shins, O martyr Theodotus, for being pierced through the feet with nails, O glorious one, thou didst walk upon the convenient path to the heavenly Kingdom, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Proclaiming God Who came upon the earth, and Who by His divine passions destroyed the all-destructive passion, thou didst endure divine suffering with thy blessed flesh, crying aloud, O Theodotus: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

With drops of blood and with the stream of sacred teachings, thou didst water the hearts of the faithful by grace, O all-wise one, and didst make divine understandings sprout forth unto the Cultivator of good things, unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst renew all human nature, O all-pure one, having given birth to the heavenly rain Who became man; and thou hast made new us who had grown old, who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The cornerstone not-cut-by-hand was cut from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin even Christ, Who joined together the separated natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparia:*

The divine reliquary of the most sacred shepherd's relics pours forth sovereign healings, and stills passions, and truly looses cruel diseases by the divine grace of the Savior.

As a hieromartyr having authority to bind and to loose, loose the bonds of mine evils, and bedew my soul which is burned by the fire of sin, praying unto the All-Bountiful.

Today the Church faithfully celebrates thy most sacred memory, O God-bearer, and thy departure unto the Lover of mankind; and rejoicing she weaves for thee diadems of divine songs.

Having acquired thee as a radiant day-star, O God-proclaimer, with Thy luminous rays of divine sufferings and Thy miracles, we are illumined by pure splendors, O Theodotus.

*Theotokion:*

Having given birth unto the Light for us, O Virgin, Who shone forth ineffably from the Father before the morning star, thou didst destroy the darkness of foolishness, and didst enlighten those sleeping in the shadow of sin.

*March 3: Holy Martyrs Eutropius and his companions Cleonicus and Basiliscus:*

*In the same month, on the 3rd day: the Holy Martyrs Eutropius, and his companions Cleonicus and Basiliscus.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4:*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Vsekhvalnii muchenitsy):*

Appearing as three in number, O martyrs, ye contended against the harsh judges; and having endured with faith every kind of most cruel suffering, ye attained the Kingdom on high. Therefore pray ye that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

Let us praise in spiritual songs Eutropius, firm Cleonicus, and Basiliscus; for they burned up the matter of godlessness by the grace of piety, and now they enlighten the ends [of the earth] with the divine splendors of fire, like most radiant luminaries, having darkened all deceit.

Having your heads cut off, ye crushed the head of the enemy with your fair feet, O valiant sufferers: undeceived stars, animate sacrifices, [treasures] of the heavenly temple treasures of the heavenly temple: glorious Eutropius, Basiliscus, and Cleonicus, ask peace for all!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Rejoice, marvelous report to hear, O pure one; rejoice, holy tree planted by God in paradise; rejoice, destruction of the deceitful demons; rejoice, two-edged weapon that didst cut off the head of the enemy through thy marvelous birthgiving, O all-immaculate one: recall us who have become estranged.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding her Lamb upon the Cross, having neither form nor beauty, the immaculate ewe-lamb and Lady weeping cried aloud: Woe is me! Whither hath Thy beauty departed, O sweetest One? Where is Thy majesty? Where is the grace that shone forth from Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?

*Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "I sing the glory of the three greatest prize-bearing martyrs through the honor of their sufferings."*[^15]  
*By Kyros Joseph. Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God; for being immortal, O all-powerful One, it shattered the adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites.

*Troparia:*

Illumined with the transcendent splendors of the thrice-radiant dawn, O all-praised martyrs: enlighten with the light of your suffering those who now graciously call you blessed.

With words like arrows, O all-wise ones, ye wounded the hearts of the most lawless; therefore ye patiently endured wounds and sores, confessing the holy name of God.

Proclaiming before the tribunals of the tormentors Him Who willed to suffer for our sake with sacred words, O ever-memorable martyrs, ye firmly endured the torments inflicted upon you.

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown to be more spacious than the heavens, having enfleshed Him Whom nothing can contain: pray unto Him therefore to deliver us from all distress, and to save us from eternal torment.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry aloud unto Thee: Holy is the animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Beholding divine things with pure applications of the mind, with manly disposition ye hastened to the torments, O blessed ones, overcoming the bodiless enemy together with his body therefore ye are glorified.

Having endured the severing of your limbs for Christ, O wise ones, by the divine outpourings of your blood ye reverently sanctified the earth; and ye stopped the blood offered unto demons upon pagan altars,[^16] O martyrs.

Ye were placed upon the height of suffering like radiant stars, truly illuminating the universe with unwaning beams, and dissolving the darkness of deceit, O holy ones; therefore ye are faithfully called blessed.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst receive Christ in thy womb, Who emptied the belly of Hades by His all-effective counsel: pray unto Him, O thou who art full of divine joy, to deliver from the bondage of sin all who ever faithfully call thee blessed.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Grob tvoi Spase):*

The trinity of martyrs confessed their honorable contest before many peoples, and having suffered they were numbered with the choirs of the incorporeal; today let us praise them in faith, celebrating their sacred and world-saving memory.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O hope of Christians, all-pure Virgin, unceasingly pray with the powers on high unto God Whom thou didst bear beyond mind and word, to grant remission of all our sins and correction of life unto those who ever glorify thee with faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The undefiled ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hung dead upon the Tree, weeping spoke, maternally crying aloud: How shall I endure Thine ineffable descent, O my Son, and Thy voluntary passions, O most good God?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Having perceived with prophetic eyes thee as the mountain overshadowed by divine grace, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparia:*

The three stood in the arena, preaching the one thrice-radiant God, and firmly enduring the inflammation of wounds; they appeared as fiery pillars of the Church, encouraging the pious in Christ.

Having set your feet upon the rock of faith, ye were not shaken by the crafts of many temptations, but appeared immovable, tripping up the impious, O martyrs, and by grace completely confounding them.

Like honorable stones rolling upon the earth, O all-wise ones, ye truly destroyed the wicked building of polytheism, and built yourselves into temples of God and were taken up into the heavenly temple.

*Theotokion:*

Thy womb was shown to be a God-bearing chamber, wherein Christ the King of all rested; thou hast made the pure temples, O Mother, and radiant dwellings of the pious into [abodes] of the unapproachable Godhead.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou Who hast enlightened by the brightness of Thy coming, O Christ, and illumined the ends of the world by Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of those who orthodoxly hymn Thee.

*Troparia:*

Keeping the divine law, O victorious martyrs, with manly mind ye stood against the lawless; and being wounded and having your bodies torn, ye received eternal victory.

Let us praise Basiliscus, and with faith let us honor Cleonicus and wise Eutropius, who suffered with faith for the all-holy Trinity, and shone forth more than the sun.

Working wonders unto the glory of our God, ye drew those who beheld them to the faith of the Savior, O much-suffering martyrs: fair flowers of paradise, confirmations of the Church.

*Theotokion:*

With the brightness of Him Who shone forth beyond mind from thee, O all-undefiled one, illumine our thoughts, and diminish the darkness of soul-corrupting imaginations, that we may hymn thee, O Lady.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

The final abyss hath surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and the restoration.

*Troparia:*

Earth and heaven rejoice, men dance in choir, celebrating the annual feast of those who valiantly endured blessed sufferings for Christ with firmness of mind.

Thou didst honor the most good God by thy good conduct, O Eutropius, and didst conquer the wiles of the enemy; therefore all men honor thee, hoping to receive through thee the remission of transgressions.

Pouring forth fountains of healings, O wise ones, ye drove away the burning heat of diseases and sorrows, sending forth good health unto all who ever piously flee unto your protection.

*Theotokion:*

O all-undefiled one, who by thy bud didst wither the malice of the enemy that sprouted forth of old through transgression: pluck out from my soul the soul-corrupting and thorny thoughts that have sprouted forth.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The faithful discern thee, O Theotokos, as a spiritual furnace; for as the supremely exalted One saved the three youths, He wholly renewed the world in thy womb, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparia:*

Accounting the severing of limbs, hangings, scrapings, and a violent death as divine delight, the martyrs rejoiced, fervently singing unto God: Praised and supremely glorified is the God of our fathers!

After many torments of the body, ye firmly endured the cross in the likeness of the Redeemer, O glorious Eutropius and great Cleonicus; and ye obtained a blessed end, hymning the praised and supremely glorified God.

Commanding you beforehand to look unto immortal rewards, Christ appeared in the prison; seeing Him, ye were filled with boldness, crying aloud, O glorious martyrs: Praised and supremely glorified is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Deliver us, O all-holy Lady, from the invasion of heathens, from the encounter with the evil one, from every distress, and from eternal condemnation, who cry aloud in faith: Praised and supremely glorified is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

From the furnace the Israelite youths, in the beauty of piety as in a crucible, shone purer than gold, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, hymn and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Having your mind directed unto the one Master, ye were stretched out and severely wounded, O all-praised ones, and being tormented ye cried aloud: Hymn the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

Lifted up upon the cross, like the Master of old, ye were deemed worthy of great glory, O sufferers, crying aloud and saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, hymn and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

Strengthened by divine power, O all-glorious sufferers, ye mightily conquered the arrays of the lawless, singing and saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, hymn and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Triadikon:*

As a unity in essence I hymn Thee, all-holy Godhead, and indivisibly dividing Thee by three hypostases, I cry aloud rejoicing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, hymn and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown to be the temple and the gate of Emmanuel, through which He alone entered and went out, as He alone knows, O pure one, saving those who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, hymn and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The unconsumed burning bush showed forth the type of thy pure birthgiving; and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations that rages against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparia:*

That ye might gain another life, ye accounted all things of this present life as refuse, as the divine Paul taught; therefore, having suffered well, ye walk in the heavens, O renowned crown-bearers.

The divine assembly of holy martyrs appeared as a triangular pillar, casting down the walls of wicked godlessness by grace; and now they dwell in the city of the living God, in the tabernacles of the firstborn.

Having received the crown of incorruption from Christ, O holy ones, and dipped your purple robe in your martyr's blood, ye stand before the King of the ages, illumined with bright outpourings of light and all-rich distributions [of gifts].

Having already drawn near unto God, and celebrating with the incorporeal choirs, ask that heavenly splendor may be obtained for those on earth who ever call you blessed, and who celebrate your memory, O martyrs.

*Theotokion:*

The angelic ranks are struck with awe at Him Who shone forth from thy womb: pray unto Him for our sake, O Virgin Mother, that He may deliver from terrible torment and outer darkness all who hymn thee.

*March 4: Venerable Father Gerasimus of the Jordan:*

*In the same month, on the 4th day: our Venerable Father Gerasimus of the Jordan.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 8:*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (O preslavnago chudese):*

O God-wise father Gerasimus, united unto God through much self-control, prayer, and supplication, thou didst remain unmoved by the crafts of the adversary, and wast shown to be a servant of the Almighty; therefore we honor thee, and celebrating thy divine memory with joy in faith, we praise thee.

Venerable father Gerasimus, ever abiding in faith in the deserts, caves, and mountains, thou didst seek God; and thou didst find Him as thou didst desire, guided in thy soul with irreversible purpose always by good entrances, and didst bring multitudes of monks saved unto God.

Having submitted to the nods of the all-seeing God, Thou didst receive grace from Him to work wonders, and to drive away spirits of wickedness, and to tame wild beasts by the purity of thy mind.[^17] Therefore we honor thee, and celebrate thy divine memory as is meet, with faith, O God-blessed one.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*

We pray thee, O all-pure Lady, our protectress, we who are troubled with sorrows: despise not thy servants that they perish utterly, but hasten to deliver us from present wrath and sorrow, O thou who art full of divine joy, all-holy and pure one; for thou art our wall and unconquerable help.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben:*

Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting the passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud weeping: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How is it that Thou endurest wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and didst deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "I praise Gerasimus, as weaving a crown."*[^18]  
*Tone 8.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone hath been gloriously glorified.

*Troparia:*

Christ gave unto thee, O Gerasimus, who didst contend well, the glory of immortality and the divine crown of incorruption.

Having the Giver of light Who listened to thy supplications, thou didst diminish the darkness of demons, O father Gerasimus.

By the streams of thy tears, O father, like a well-watered tree, thou didst bring forth fruits of immortality, O God-wise Gerasimus.

*Theotokion:*

O all-pure Theotokos, who without pain didst bear Christ beyond mind and word: still the sicknesses of my heart.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

Following Him Who endured voluntary crucifixion, O venerable one, thou didst crucify thyself entirely unto the world and passions.

Strengthened by the all-effective nod [of God], O wise Gerasimus, thou wast able to trample down all the nets of the strong one.

With humble mind and humble spirit, O wise one, thou wast strengthened, and didst utterly subdue soul-corrupting passions.

Like a great sun, with the beams of thy wonders thou dost enlighten the hearts of all who faithfully praise thee.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear, O all-undefiled one, the Son Who shares the throne and essence with the Father, Who showed forth the paths of life unto all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared Today" (Yavilsya yesi dnes):*

Having overcome the wiles of the demons by strong grace and divine working, O venerable one, thou didst become a god by adoption;[^19] therefore we faithfully honor thee, O Gerasimus.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Stretching forth thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, cover those who hope in thee and cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercies unto all, O Christ!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn in thy maternal womb, thou didst cry aloud piteously: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my unwaning Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparia:*

Thy heart was a temple of the Spirit, enlightened, O father; therefore it pours forth gifts, healing diseases by divine grace.

Submitting to the divine law, O father, from youth thou didst follow the Lord, embracing the life of the bodiless angels while yet in the body.

Thy steps, O venerable one, were guided unto the saving path by the guidance of the Holy Spirit Who dwelt within Thee, O venerable one.

*Theotokion:*

Beseech Jesus, the Lover of mankind, Whom thou didst ineffably bear in thy womb, O all-undefiled one, to save all who hymn thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee: Save us, O Lord, for Thou art our God, and we know no other beside Thee.

*Troparion:*

Having received the rays of the Holy Spirit with a cleansed mind, Thou wast a light unto monks shining beyond the constraint of nature, thy life showed thee forth as an earthly angel in the flesh.

*Troparion:*

Filled with the living waters of the Spirit, thou didst pour forth rivers of wonders, O father Gerasimus.

*Theotokion:*

As a shower the Word incarnate came down upon thee, O Birthgiver of God, unto the enlightenment of our souls.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*

The infirmity of thy flesh, being strengthened by the power of Him Who shone forth unto us pure from the Virgin, subdued all the power of the bodiless enemies, O father, venerable one.

Having muzzled the jaws of the spiritual lions by grace, thou didst subdue the assaults of physical ones, O venerable one; therefore the lion was obedient unto thee, serving thee dutifully as its shepherd.

Having acquired an angelic life and a humble mind in Christ, O father, thou wast meek; and now thou hast settled in the land of the meek, being filled with divine joy.

*Theotokion:*

The radiant brightness of the Father settled in thee, O pure one, and being born in the flesh, He enlightened the world, and delivered it from the darkness of polytheism; therefore we sing thy praise.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Voznesyisya):*

Having been inflamed with love for the highest, thou didst prefer the harshness of the wilderness of Jordan above all the sweets of the world; therefore the wild beast was obedient unto thee even unto death, O father, dutifully serving thee, and piteously died upon thy tomb,[^20] God having thus glorified thee: beseeching Him, remember us also, O father Gerasimus.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparia:*

Light hath shone forth unto thee, O father, and its yoke-fellow, gladness, who in heavenly abodes art ever vigilant and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Having watered thy God-bearing heart with showers of tears, thou didst cause the fruits of virtues to sprout forth, whereby thou hast received heavenly sweetness unto the ages with all the venerable ones.

Bearing praise upon thy lips, in unceasing imitation of the angels, with vigilant mind thou didst cry aloud, O Gerasimus: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*

The fire of the Godhead in no wise consumed thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God; for the bush on the mountain of old prefigured thee, all-pure one, being burned and in no wise consumed.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The God-speaking youths in the furnace, trampling the flame with the fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Illumined, thou didst stand before the Master in ascetic radiances, O wonderworker Gerasimus, and wast numbered among the ranks of the bodiless hosts.

Thou didst utterly reproach the seat of the evil one who leads men to dwell in pleasures, O beauty of ascetics, God-wise Gerasimus.

Thou didst clothe thyself in the garment of incorruption by putting off the passions, O God-wise father Gerasimus, and didst worthily attain the divine bridal chamber.

*Theotokion:*

Having found thee as a sweet-smelling lily in the valleys of life, O all-pure one, the transcendent One breathed sweet fragrance into the world through thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, being made man from the virginal womb; therefore, O faithful, we magnify the all-pure Birthgiver of God.

*Troparia:*

Following in the steps of all who walked well upon the earth, thou didst have love, faith, hope, temperance, wisdom, and unfailing prayer, ineffable self-control, O father Gerasimus, delighting in pure ascents.

O wonder, how the wild beast obeyed thee as if endowed with reason, zealously serving thy command, O Gerasimus, while God glorified thine equal-to-the-angels life by signs, O venerable one, and by countless marvels and wonders!

Thou wast shown to be the confirmation of monks and the instruction of hesychasts, O God-bearer; for thou didst walk the narrowest path of abstinence, expanding thy soul yet more by divine hopes; therefore thou hast found a spacious dwelling in the tents of the elect.

Thou hast flourished in the courts of our God, O father, like a high-grown palm tree,[^21] and hast been exalted like a cedar by divine elevations; therefore we all faithfully celebrate thy memory, wherein pray that we may be saved from all temptations.

*Theotokion:*

Bearing Emmanuel in thy hands as an infant, O pure one full of God's grace, thou didst cry aloud: O my sweetest Child, how shall I give the breast unto Thee Who feedest all things? How shall I wrap Thee in swaddling bands Who clothest the sea with gloom?

*March 5: Holy Martyr Conon of Isauria:*

*In the same month, on the 5th day: Holy Martyr Conon of Isauria.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 8:*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Marvelous Wonder" (O preslavnago chudese):*

O undecieving and divine dawn, light-bearing radiance of the commandments of God, and most excellent passion-bearer of the martyrs, ever-memorable one, thou didst drive away the mist of darkness, as a light-bearing star, O blessed one. O good offering, and undefiled sacrifice! Therefore unceasingly pray unto Christ to save our souls.

Preacher of truth, shown to be a sword-bearing champion against impiety, by thy suffering thou didst sever the heads of the enemies, O all-wise one; and as thou didst proclaim the divine commandment, thou didst speak better things unto the people ineffably. O Conon, companion of the martyrs, pray unto the Redeemer to deliver from passions thy servants who sing thy praise.

O good change by the right hand of the Most High, which the Lord of the fathers wrought in thee, O all-glorious passion-bearer! From an unbelieving root, thou wast shown to be a fair-flowering fruit, O all-blessed and all-praised Conon; and having Christ as Thine excellent commander, thou didst mightily conquer the legions of demons.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*

Rejoice, vehicle of the divine Light, candlestick of the Star, and tabernacle of sanctification! Light hath shone forth unto us from thy most pure womb, enlightening the ends of the world, and sanctifying them by its grace; rejoice, beginning of salvation, all-pure one; rejoice, fearful hearing and speech unto those who hope in thee.

*Stavrotheotokion, in the same Podoben:*

Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting the passions, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried aloud weeping: Woe is me, sweet Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity, and didst deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?

*Canon, the acrostic whereof is: "I sing Conon the crown-bearing martyr."*[^22]  
*Tone 6.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the deep on foot as if on dry land, Israel, seeing the persecutor Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*

Illumine my thought, O martyr, with the light that dwells in thee, supernaturally, to praise in divine hymnody Christ's martyr and steadfast contender.

Instructed by divine, angelic discipline, O blessed one, as an angel on earth thou didst truly excel in the flesh, bridling the lusts and adorning thyself.

Observing the divine laws, O venerable one, thou didst openly abhor Hellenic lawlessness; and thou didst bring many unto God by Thy teachings, convincing them through sacred wonders.

*Theotokion:*

Adorned with the beauty of virtues, O all-undefiled one, thou didst bear Him Who is fairer than all the children of men, Who wisely adorns us with His own beauties.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*

Having adorned thy soul with divine virtues, O venerable martyr, thou didst truly make it a dwelling-place of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit.

Those who of old fell into the depth of delusion wretchedly, are guided to revealed height by thy lofty teachings far-shiningly, O martyr Conon, singing thy praise.

Having become a temple of God, by divine invocations thou didst cast down the idols, O all-blessed one, standing in the temple of the carved image, guiding those who had strayed unto divine understanding.

*Theotokion:*

One of the Holy Trinity is born of thee, O all-pure one, beyond mind and word, renewing earthly things that were enslaved to passions by the foul counsel of the enemy.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Premudrosti):*

Armed with the whole armor of Christ, thou wast shown to be the total destroyer of demons, and wast an unbroken peace-offering, possessing the unassailable pillar of faith, and thou liftest us up to the height of thy wonders; therefore thou hast worthily received crowns of victory from God, O much-suffering martyr Conon. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Fallen into manifold temptations from enemies visible and invisible, and overtaken by the tempest of mine unnumbered transgressions, as unto a fervent protection and my shelter, O pure one, I flee unto the haven of thy goodness. Therefore, O all-pure one, earnestly pray unto Him Who was incarnate of thee without seed for all thy servants who unceasingly beseech thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, praying unto Him ever to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worthily sing thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest through loving-kindness of mercy, O long-suffering Lord. O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner becoming God, crying aloud with a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst cast down the temples of the godless, O holy one, and didst deliver their gods unto utter destruction, O martyr, working wonders and performing most marvelous signs.

As thou didst walk holily and venerably, the Word of God subjected the race of demons unto thee, and glorified thee with many manifestations of wonders.

When thou wast seven years of age, the All-good God gave thee great Michael as an instructor,[^23] clearly teaching thee the greatest mysteries, O most great Conon.

*Theotokion:*

Holy Birthgiver of God, who didst bear the all-holy Word incarnate, grant illumination unto my soul, guiding my mind that is tossed about by trackless ways.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine the souls of those who rise early unto Thee with love, I pray Thee, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the gloom of sin.

*Troparia:*

With the stream of thy sacred blood having divinely dyed for thyself a martyr's robe, O valiant-minded martyr, thou livest rejoicing in the heavenly mansions and splendors.

Having stretched thee out, O blessed one, the malicious ruler scraped thy flesh, while thou didst have a mind wholly stretched out unto God, O ever-memorable one he tore with stripes the one who was calling upon the great name of the Almighty.

Being quickened by grace, thou didst endure the drops of wounds, adorning thyself in martyr's fashion, conquering godlessness, O Conon, and being deemed worthy of immortality.

*Theotokion:*

Being delivered through thee, O all-pure one, from every ancestral curse, we have received a blessing; therefore we bless and magnify thee, and with love we honor and call thee blessed.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven I cry aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*Troparia:*

Having shone forth like the sun in light-beaming virtues and most glorious wonders, and being adorned in all things by the patience of thy sufferings, thou dost enlighten the ends of the earth, O renowned martyr, who praise thee.

Shown to be most fair by the beauties of thy wounds, thou dost work wonders pre-eminently; and having died, again after burial thou dost drive away diseases and expel the spirits of delusion, O blessed one.

Heal the passions of our souls, O wise one, and drive away the infirmities of the flesh: an excellent physician art thou given unto us who honor thee from God, O glorious Conon.

*Theotokion:*

The Lord looked upon thee, and through thee wrought visitation unto those who had departed from Him, O all-pure one; therefore, as thou didst say, the generations of the saved call thee blessed.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Having lived in purity on earth like an angel, therefore thou wast deemed worthy to be a companion of angels, and didst lead thy parents to the knowledge of Christ;[^24] and having confessed the one God in Trinity, thou didst suffer even unto blood, O martyr Conon: pray unceasingly unto Him for all of us.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The angel made the furnace to drop dew for the venerable youths, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

An unwaning light shone forth unto thee, who didst hate the darkness of passions, O venerable one; and gladness received thee who didst finish the good fight with zeal: therefore remember us as thou standest before Christ.

Thy heart was meek, and thy mind humble, O venerable one, whereby thou didst destroy all the haughtiness of the enemy, being exalted by divine virtues.

...and by terrible wonders, O Conon, foundation of the martyrs.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast terrible unto all the demons, commanding them to submit to thy sacred orders, O God-wise one; for God glorified thee, who didst glorify Him by good works upon the earth, O Conon.[^25]

*Theotokion:*

Having created all things by His will, He truly willed to dwell in thy most undefiled womb, O Birthgiver of God; for God alone is glorified in the counsels of the saints, being great and terrible.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee do we supreme-exalt unto all ages.

*Troparia:*

Firmly didst thou resist the self-conceited one who thought to compel thee to offer honor unto those abominable foreign gods, O sufferer; therefore, having suffered lawfully, thou hast been glorified.

Those wounded by demonic assaults are healed by thy divine teachings, and receive deliverance from sorrows, singing of thy grace, O God-wise one.

Draw health from all diseases, ye who approach the shrine of the venerable one in faith; for it manifestly pours forth by the grace of the Spirit, and dries up the torrents of passions.

*Theotokion:*

Offer supplication unto God the Creator, to save our souls, O full of God's grace, and to deliver us from the domination of passions and the temptation of malicious men.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was manifested unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast seen as a ship sailing dry across the worldly tempest, O God-bearer, saving a cargo of good things unto the fair havens of rest, of the kingdom of God, and of splendor.

Having clothed thyself with strength as with armor, O all-blessed one, thou didst strip the enemy's ranks, and didst subjugate them beneath thy fair feet; and having become a sincere servant of the Master, thou didst deliver those enslaved by the enemy.

Make remembrance of us who praise thee with love, standing as a crown-bearer before Christ standing with the countless hosts on high: that by thy prayers we may find mercy, and relief from grievous evils, O all-praised Conon.

*Theotokion:*

With thy light illumine me, O Virgin, who am ever held fast by the night of despondency, bowed down unto the earth, and committing every sin through corruption of the mind, that being saved through thee, I may sing thy praise, O much-hymned one.

*March 6: The Forty-two Holy Martyrs of Amorium:*

*In the same month, on the 6th day: The 42 Holy Martyrs of Amorium.*[^26]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 4:*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Being Called from on High" (Zvannyi svyshe):*

Having appeared in the latter times, O victorious martyrs, unwaning stars in the venerable firmament of the Church: by the brightness of your suffering ye enlightened the whole world beneath the sun, and destroyed the darkness of delusion, O all-praised ones; and now ye have passed over unto eternal radiance. Therefore in faith we celebrate your light-bearing and sacred suffering, enriched by your good intercession.

By Christ's testimony, O martyrs, ye were bound with grievous bonds, and shut up in prison for a long time, O divine keepers of the lamp of faith; therefore the ill-named beast, raging, put you to death with the sword, since ye would not obey his wicked commands. And now, forty and two, ye have joyfully inherited the kingdom on high, O most glorious ones.

With Constantine let us loudly praise Basil and Callistus, Aetius and Theophilus,[^27] and the rest of the divine choir of passion-bearers; for they chose to die for the Life of all, being joyfully slaughtered; and now they rest in the city of the living God, and pray that we may find remission of transgressions in the day of judgment, and complete deliverance.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Deliver from every assault of the enemy those who piously bow down before thy childbearing O all-undefiled Birthgiver of God, being the protectress of our whole race! For we all have now acquired thee as help, refuge, and confirmation, and intercessor before the good Christ and Lord: pray unto Him, we beseech thee, to grant peace unto the world, and remission of sins unto those who flee beneath thy shelter.

*Or Stavrotheotokion:*

Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding hung upon the Tree Thy Son and God, Who hung the earth upon the waters without restraint, and created all creation; for I shall arise and be glorified, and shall break the realm of Hades with power, and shall destroy its strength, and shall deliver those bound from its malice, as I am tender-hearted, and shall lead them unto My Father, as the Lover of mankind.

*If it happen on a Saturday or on Sunday, an idiomelon is also sung. Glory..., Tone 2, by Methodius:*[^28]

The Church today feasts mystically, clothed with a new robe as with purple and fine linen, dyed with the blood of the new passion-bearers; for having nurtured them in piety, she hath offered them unto Thee, O Christ, as an undefiled, acceptable, and well-pleasing sacrifice. Therefore, Thou Who didst show them forth as conquerors over the lawless, and didst crown and glorify them: send down upon us also through their prayers Thy great mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Glory..., Tone 6, by the Soukiote:*[^29]

A God-crowned troop of newly-manifested martyrs, an undefiled slaughter offered zealously for Christ, an elect host of forty-two: come, ye lovers of the martyrs, having spiritually accomplished their all-sacred memory, let us cry aloud unto them: Crush the savagery of the godless Agarenes,[^30] and deliver piously-minded people from bitter circumstance by your prayers.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors and crushed the feeble insolence of the demons: through their prayers save our souls.

*At Matins, the Canon, a work of Ignatius.*[^31] *Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having passed through the water as if dry land, and escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!

*Troparia:*

Fallen into the depth of sins, and fleeing unto the sea of Thy compassions, guide me unto the haven of salvation by the supplications of the passion-bearers, O Christ!

Shining with the beauty of virtues, wearing a purple robe made of martyr's blood, holding the Cross as a scepter, thou reignest with the Lord, O Theodore.[^32]

In hymns let us, O faithful, praise Theodore, Callistus, and Bassoes, and Theophilus with them, together with valiant Constantine, and the rest of the martyrs.

*Theotokion:*

The deceiver and enemy poured soul-corrupting venom into the ears of Eve; but having born Christ, O Mother of God, thou hast healed his hurt.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Lord, Creator of the vault of heaven, and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Illumined with the dawn of piety, O truly glorious one, and adorned with the beauty of divine deeds, thou didst destroy the gloomy impiety of barbarian godlessness, O blessed one.

Through the rivers of blood that flowed from the most pure bodies of Thy passion-bearers, do Thou, O tender-hearted Lord, grant unto them Thy kingdom and the stream of delight.

Having blossomed like unerring stars, pillars of the holy Church, upon the firmament: the most valiant ones have illumined the whole universe with the beams of their sufferings.

*Theotokion:*

Being incarnate in thy womb as God and man for the sake of the better, He Who alone is the Lover of mankind saves man from the gates of death, O most pure Mother of God, only all-hymned one.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Voznesyisya):*

Having contended on earth for Christ's sake, ye appeared as pious crown-bearers, and received the heavens to dwell in joy; for having cast down every craft of the enemy by the pains and blood of your wounds, ye ever grant remission of sins from on high unto those who praise you.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Premudrosti):*

Grievously were ye bound by the enemies, and shut up in prison for many seasons, being preserved by faith ye remained unhurt; and when severed by the sword from your bodies, O holy ones, ye were manifestly bound with divine longing. Therefore, like luminaries ye shone forth in the world, enlightening all by the grace of the Spirit, O blessed passion-bearers: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who feast with love with love your holy memory.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As the only Virgin among women, who without seed hast borne God in the flesh, all generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead made its abode in thee, and as an infant thou dost nurse the Creator and Lord. Therefore we, the race of angels and of men, worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who bow down in faith before thy most holy birth.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly lamenting, cried: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest through loving-kindness of mercy, O long-suffering Lord. O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn Thy divine passions with faith.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who hast not left the bosom of the Father, and hast visited our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Adorned with the beauties of thy contests, thou didst clothe thyself in a mantle dyed red with the blood of thy martyrdom, and didst adorn thyself with the diadem of victory. Therefore with the martyrs before the face of the Lord thou dost dance in company, O all-blessed Theodore:

In prison thy feet were bound with iron, O invincible Callistus; thou didst pray unhindered to tread the unbeguiling path of martyrdom, and being loosed from the body at once by the sword, thou wast bound by love of the Creator.

A divine host, a God-gathered regiment, from many places and cities a pair of sufferers simply, with a four-numbered decad[^33] now shone forth, having suffered lawfully, and being beheaded for Christ the Head of all.

*Theotokion:*

Thou art the praise of the faithful, O unwedded one, thou art the protectress, thou art also the refuge of Christians, a rampart and a haven; for unto thy Son thou bearest supplications, O all-blameless one, and dost save from perils those who with faith and love glorify thee as the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light unwaning, and the alien darkness of the wretched one hath covered me? But convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*

Foreseeing the crowns and the abiding glory, O Theodore, with bold spirit thou didst cry aloud unto those suffering with thee: In no way be affrighted of death, through which after a little while ye shall receive unspoken rejoicing and life.

Wounded by love of thy Master, O blessed one, and consumed by fire with longing for Him, thou didst drink the cup of martyrdom, which is in no way defiled by second defilements,[^34] O ever-memorable sufferer Callistus.

With patient-vigilant spirit, thou wast first to volunteer for suffering, a foundation of courage wast thou unto those suffering with thee, O Theodore; with whom thou didst inherit eternal resting places, and ever-abiding sweetness.

*Theotokion:*

Grant remission of transgressions through thy prayers unto thy servants, delivering them from assaults, perils, and afflictions, and from the oppression of blasphemous heretics,[^35] O Virgin Birthgiver of God all-blameless.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*

The streams of thy blood poured forth like a river dried up the turbid torrents of delusion, and watered the hearts of all the faithful, O praiseworthy Theodore.

Raising up Thy martyrs from the deep passions, Thou didst establish them upon the rock of endurance, O Lover of mankind, turning away their steps from enemies and crooked paths.

Fairly rushing forth like a river out of Eden, ye martyrs gladden the city of God with your blood, having piously cleansed the defilement of impiety.

*Theotokion:*

As an animate ark thou didst contain the Lawgiver, and as a holy temple thou didst receive the Holy One Who became man, for the benefaction of men, O pure Birthgiver of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Vyshnikh ishcha):*

The newly-manifested stars of the faith, who suffered eagerly for Christ, we all worthily crown with praiseworthy crowns, as they pray unto Christ for us; for they are pillars and ramparts of the Christian realm.

*Ikos:*

Abhorring and abominating from the soul the godless faith of the Agarenes and the delusion of the cruel demon, O sufferers, and bearing whole in your hearts the divine seal of Christ, ye gave not your backs to the enemies that hated Him; but rather all of you eagerly for His sake were led as lambs to the slaughter, after your end having found immortal glory like the sun: as pillars and ramparts of the Christian realm.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The youths who came from Judea in Babylon of old trod down the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparia:*

Having suffered lawfully, thou didst receive crowns of righteousness from the only Master, and now thou delightest in divine beauty, O Theodore, crying aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Led voluntarily like a lamb to the slaughter, to be a sacrifice of martyrdom, thou wast laid upon the banquet on high, singing as thou wast slaughtered: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Being inflamed in heart with the fire of Thy love, O Word, the martyr Callistus consumed the briars of blasphemy, singing unto Thee: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Most foolish Ishmael,[^36] thinking to persuade the God-wise to deny Christ, was put to shame; for when about to die, they piously cried aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*

Having taken flesh from a virginal womb, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore knowing thy Mother to be the Birthgiver of God, thankfully we cry aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Sevenfold did the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindle the furnace for the God-fearing; but seeing them saved by a better power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Redeemer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

A choir of sufferers, an assembly shining with the beauties of martyrdom, the air was sanctified at thy return,[^37] O martyr, and the angelic hosts assembled, the earth was sanctified where thy body lies, O patient-vigilant, thrice-blessed Theodore.

Having now become an inheritance of the Lord, and being led of thine own will to the slaughter, thou didst not accept to be bound with cords, O Callistus, when thy holy head was severed; therefore as a crown-bearer having ascended, thou dancest with the heavenly hosts unto all ages.

...unto all ages.

Let Callistus be called blessed, and Theodore praised, together with Constantine the marvelous Theophilus, Bassoes the most valiant, and the rest of the choir of martyrs, dancing with angelic choirs and crying unceasingly: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

Cast into the river, the glorious martyrs,[^38] after the torment of the sword, were led unto the calm haven of relief in Christ; having dried up the waters of delusion by streams of blood, they taught us to sing: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

O all-pure Parent of God, cleanse the wounds and sinful sores of my soul, washing them with the fountains that flowed from the side of thy Offspring, and purifying them with the streams proceeding therefrom; for unto thee I cry, and unto thee I flee, and thee I call upon, who art full of God's grace.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Heaven was astonished at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God was manifested unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparia:*

Having emulated the courage of ancient passion-bearers, thou didst truly obtain their calling and joy, O Theodore; but remember us who piously celebrate thy holy memory, as having boldness before the Master that we may be saved.

Having been baptized in your own blood into Him Who was baptized in the streams of the Jordan, and having piously emulated His death, He united in the river the severed heads to the bodies of the martyrs cast in,[^39] and guided them unto a stormless haven.

O divine four-numbered decad of martyrs with the honorable pair, dwelling now in joy in the heavens, we pray you: intercede for us who celebrate upon earth your all-holy and light-bearing memory, that we may be delivered from every affliction.

*Theotokion:*

Without seed, O Virgin, thou didst conceive the Word of God Who created all things, without the desires of the flesh; and without corruption thou didst give birth, without maternal pangs; therefore confessing thee as the Birthgiver of God in tongue and heart, we magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give Ear, Ye Women" (Zheny uslyshite):*

...What satiety shall there be for lovers of the martyrs of the divine memory of the martyrs, as Basil the Great said aforetime?[^40] But now it lies before us to praise the forty and two divine martyrs, who patiently suffered for Christ the Savior.

*Theotokion:*

Having given birth to Hypostatic Wisdom, the supersubstantial Word, and the Physician of souls and bodies: heal the grievous and temporal sores and wounds of my soul, and calm the pains of my heart, O Virgin.

*March 7: The Holy Hieromartyrs Who Were Bishops in Cherson: Basil, Ephraim, Capito, Eugenius, Aetherius, and the rest.*[^41]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Dal yesi znameniye):*

Invincible martyrs, and all-praised hierarchs, and universal luminaries, immovable pillars of the divine Church, and foundation of dogmas, guides of the pious, and destroyers of delusion were ye, O heavenly-minded fathers, illuminators of our souls, companions of the angels, champions of the Trinity!

Let glorious Ephraim and God-wise Basil, great Capito and divine Agathodorus, with Elpidius, Aetherius, and glorious Eugenius be called blessed with divine words; for having lived venerably, and suffered sacredly, they appeared as citizens of the heavenly kingdom.

Having put to death the carnal mind through ascetical struggles, glorious Basil raised up a dead man by divine invocation;[^42] and Capito again, most sacred shepherd, standing in the midst of the flame rejoicing, was seen unburned;[^43] through their prayers, O Lover of mankind, grant us propitiation and Thy great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:*

Wash away the defilement of my passionate heart, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God, and cleanse all its wounds and festerings that come from sin, O pure one, and of my mind calm the instability of my mind, that I, thy wretched and unprofitable servant, may magnify thy power and great protection.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*

Beholding Christ the Lover of mankind crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one cried aloud weeping: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst unto them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O Tender-hearted One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*At Matins, the Canon, whose acrostic is: "I honor the sevenfold choir of pastors."[^44] A work of Joseph.[^45] Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, ancient Israel of old, by Moses' hands stretched out in the figure of the Cross, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparia:*

Bedewed with divine outpourings of light, O martyrs, deliver from the gloom of passions those who ever piously praise this your light-bearing and radiant feast.

The Word of God, having taken flesh, showed you forth as all-blessed shepherds, O venerable ones, to proclaim His Godhead unto those who had gone astray and were perishing in ignorance.

Being filled with the life-giving waters of the Spirit, ye gave drink unto those who had wasted away through the fierce burning heat of godlessness, and guided them unto the sweet water of salvation.

*Theotokion:*

The condemnation of our forebears was loosed beyond mind by thy childbearing, O all-pure one, and the first ones received entrance into paradise, praising thee with a loud voice.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!

*Troparia:*

Having kindled thy mind with divine fire, O God-wise Basil, as an excellent shepherd thou didst burn up the matter of godlessness.

By the light of Christ, O glorious one, thou didst draw unto the illumination of holy baptism those who were in the darkness of grievous evils.

Thy paths were directed straight unto God, O Basil; by grace they turned all men away from the deceitful way unto the knowledge of God.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst bear the mighty Lord Who delivers men from the tyranny of the enemy, O all-pure, all-hymned Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "The Beauty of Virginity" (Krasote devstva):*

With the myrrh with the myrrh of anointing having been sanctified, ye shepherds appeared unto a God-wise people, and having offered yourselves as pure lambs, ye were brought unto the Chief Shepherd the Word, Who was sacrificed like a sheep, O all-praised martyrs and universal luminaries; therefore we all celebrate your divine memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

As an uncultivated vine, O Virgin, thou hast budded forth the fairest cluster, pouring out for us the wine of salvation, that gladdens the souls and bodies of all; therefore, blessing thee ever as the cause of good things, with the angel we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee dead hanging upon the Cross, maternally weeping said: What did the lawless and ungrateful council of the Hebrews render unto Thee, having enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I praise Thy divine condescension.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Let the choir of most sacred martyrs be hymned, illumined by the grace of priesthood and martyrdom, and singing rejoicing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Ephraim and Agathodorus, Basil and Capito, Aetherius, Eugenius, and divine Elpidius were shown forth by the Holy Spirit to be God-bearing pilots of the Church.
