Having been instructed in divine things, O divine fathers, ye piously and truly shepherded the people of God in holiness and righteousness, and received a blessed end through martyrdom.

Those dead through unbelief obtained eternal life by the resurrection of the dead youth,[^46] believing through thee in Christ our God, O most sacred martyr Basil.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst quicken deadened men, O Virgin, having borne Hypostatic Life beyond all thought, Christ our God, Who became man through loving-kindness of mercy.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come as a Light into the world, a holy Light, turning from gloomy ignorance those who praise Thee in faith.

*Troparia:*

Ye drank the cup of honorable martyrdom, O God-bearing fathers, faithfully calling upon the name of the Lord.

The only One Who breathes resurrection into the dead, through thy prayer raised up the dead youth, O Basil, making thy preaching awesome.

Preaching the Father and the Son to be of equal honor, O fathers, ye drove away the polytheistic tyranny of idol madness.

*Theotokion:*

Blessed are the people who know thee, O all-blameless one, to be the Mother of the Master of all, and who bless thee with love.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed through mercy from Thy side.

*Troparia:*

With praises let us crown Agathodorus and Eugenius, Basil the great, and wise Elpidius, as hierarchs and noble sacred martyrs.

With lawful disciplines of the Spirit, O all-wise ones, setting forth the saving laws unto the lawless, ye drew them unto the illumination of piety.

Ye appeared from different cities as teachers,[^47] but as shepherds of one people, and heirs of the divine city, wherein dwell the martyrs who have well-pleased God.

*Theotokion:*

The shadows of the Law and the riddles of the prophets prefigured thee, O Virgin, who by the ineffable Word didst have a childbearing that saves the world, O all-blameless one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Living in the Highest" (V vyshnikh zhivyi):*

The light-bearing day of the shepherds who shone as bishops in Cherson hath arrived; we hymn their feast, who suffered for Christ's sheep. O hieromartyrs, pray unto Christ the Chief Shepherd, that He may number us also among the sheep of His right hand, that we may cry aloud unto you: Rejoice, O sacred fathers, who poured out your blood for Christ!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

In the Persian furnace the Abrahamic youths, inflamed rather by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Rejoicing, thou didst enter the furnace, O father Capito, and didst remain unburned, like the three youths,[^48] snatching from the fire to come the unbelievers who came to believe through thy divine wonders.

Neither terrified nor afraid of the command, O sacred father Capito, thou didst clothe thyself in the sacred vestment, carrying glowing coals therein, O blessed one.

Thou didst stop the mouths of senselessness with thy divine words, instilling the understanding of piety in all who had slipped into the depth of idol madness, O God-pleasing sacred minister Capito.

*Theotokion:*

The Word of God Who with His hand fashioned Eve, O Maiden, wishing to rebuild all things, is fashioned from thy womb, receiving a beginning, though co-enthroned with the Father and the Spirit.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Daniel, stretching forth his hands, shut the lions' gaping mouths in the den; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Having put the flesh to death through sufferings, ye obtained immortal life, O venerable fathers, for all those who had served dead gods, who, being saved, ever cry with you: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

With one accord, O faithful, let us hymn Eugenius and Agathodorus, Ephraim and Elpidius, with Aetherius, Basil, and Capito, as overthrowers of the enemy and hierarchs of Christ, crying aloud unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Thou didst gladden the people by thy teachings, O father Ephraim,[^49] and now thou hast passed over unto the unwaning radiance and unto ever-abiding rejoicing, receiving the reward of thy labors and crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having lived on earth like an angel, O all-blessed Basil, thou didst reign over carnal passions by grace; and thou hast passed over unto heavenly kingdoms, standing before Christ the King, and crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

The supersubstantial One born of thee was seen as man, twofold in nature, O all-pure one, in energies and in wills,[^50] like unto those whom He willed to resemble when He appeared; unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The cornerstone, Christ, was cut without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, uniting the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparia:*

Celebrating the sacred memory of the holy hieromartyrs with sacred hymns, come, all ye people, with a loud voice let us honor them who pray for us unto our all-bountiful God.

Ye were seen as precious stones, O divine martyrs, dragged upon the earth by the godless, and crushing the strongholds of delusion by the power of God crushing them: therefore worthily are ye called blessed.

Today the city of Cherson celebrates your memory;[^51] for unto it ye appeared as pillars and divine ramparts and confirmations, shepherds and teachers, and God-blessed intercessors.

Already the choir of fathers is numbered with the angelic choirs: Ephraim with Eugenius, and Capito, Elpidius and Aetherius, glorious Basil, and God-wise Agathodorus.

*Theotokion:*

Holding Christ in her arms, Who holds all things in His hand, the Maiden full of God's grace marveled and cried aloud, saying: How do I know Thee both as my Son and as my incomprehensible Creator?

*March 8: Our Venerable Father and Confessor Theophylact, Bishop of Nicomedia.*[^52]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera, Tone 1:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Vsekhvalnii muchenitsy):*

O father Theophylact, guarded by divine watches thou wast preserved unhurt, and didst appear as an unshakable pillar of the Church, unmoved by temptations of heretical wiles;[^53] and now pray thou that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

O father Theophylact, beholding God, O blessed one, as far as is attainable and visible, enjoying Him through the higher union, and deified through communion: rejoicing, thou didst receive the only Desired and Blessed One, having endured, O all-honorable one, dwelling now with the angels.

O father Theophylact, passing from the earth thou didst ascend unto heaven, and wast deemed worthy to dwell in the heavenly mansions, O blessed one, on account of the exile which thou didst endure for Christ's sake;[^54] Whom do thou now beseech to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

O all-holy one, heal my soul that is grievously diseased with evil passions, who didst bear Christ, the Physician and Savior of all, Who healed every wound of him who was wounded by the devil's malice, and delivered us from death.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding her own Lamb upon the Cross, the unblemished Lady, lacking form or comeliness, weeping cried aloud: Woe is me! Whither hath Thy comeliness set, O sweetest One? Whither Thy magnificence? Whither the shining grace of Thy form, O my most beloved Son?

*The Canon, whose acrostic is: "I will praise divine Theophylact in hymns." A work of Lord Theophanes.[^55] Tone 4.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.

*Troparia:*

Standing before the Master's throne, O God-bearing father Theophylact, preserve those who in faith celebrate thy memory.

The grace of the Spirit worthily anointed thee, O all-blessed one, for the sake of the truth and for the goodness of thy manner of life.

To obtain the promised things, O blessed one, thou didst trample upon all earthly things, and didst follow Christ God.

*Theotokion:*

He Who was well-pleased to be born from thy womb, O Bride of God, is all desire: sweetness is He, and unwaning Light.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparia:*

Submitting thyself unto the laws of thy Master, O glorious hierarch, thou didst take no heed of the decrees of most iniquitous emperors.[^56]

Thou didst venerate the divine and most pure image of Christ honorably, rejecting the impiety of the impious.

Thou didst first endure the martyrdom of conscience ascetically, and now art crowned with a martyr's crown.

*Theotokion:*

That Thou mightest show us, O Master, the path leading unto holiness, Thou didst dwell in the most pure womb.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Premudrosti):*

Having fasted, O venerable wise father, thou wast anointed a hierarch to celebrate the divine mysteries for the safekeeping of souls; and having venerated Christ's icon, thou didst endure exiles, afflictions, and long confinement; therefore, pouring out healings after thy repose, thou dost heal the suffering and enlighten those who hymn thee, O hierarch Theophylact: beseech Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory* Both now... Theotokion:*

Having fallen into multifaceted trials from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my numberless sins, and fleeing unto thy warm protection and covering, O pure one, I take refuge in the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O all-pure one, fervently entreat Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, entreating Him ever to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, cried aloud weeping, and bitterly wailing, shouted: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O long-suffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion, and grant remission of sins unto those who in faith hymn Thy divine passions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I considered Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast not terrified by the fire of temptations, O all-blessed God-inspired one, nor did delicate sweetness weaken thy strength, O Theophylact.

God set a guard before thy mouth and lips, O venerable one; therefore thou didst remain unshaken when the impious stood against thee.

Waiting patiently thou didst endure, having venerated the image of Him Who set thy feet upon a rock and became thy benefactor.

*Theotokion:*

Having been delivered from the debt of sin by thy childbearing, O all-unblemished one, with joy we send up a hymn unto thee, O Bride of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee: Save us, O Lord, for Thou art our God, and we know no other besides Thee.

*Troparia:*

Thy hands lifted up unto the Creator, O venerable God-inspired one, vanquished the wicked.

Holding thy right hand, the Most High, O God-bearing father, guided thee unto heavenly enjoyment.

Having traversed the narrow path, O Theophylact, thou didst attain unto the broad expanse of paradise, O marvelous one.

*Theotokion:*

Those who do not know thee as the Birthgiver of God, O Mother of God, shall not see the Light that was born of thee, O all-pure one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God.

*Troparia:*

Having mortified the senses through temperance, thou didst reign over the passions; and now, O Theophylact, thou dost enjoy the splendors of dispassion.

Thou didst teach all men to venerate the image of the Savior, having shone forth manifestly in deeds and doctrines, O Theophylact.

Christ showed thee forth as a cloud pouring out a shower of life, O father, unto the flock of God, O Theophylact.

*Theotokion:*

Comely beyond all the sons of men is Thy Son, O all-pure one, in the beauty of the Godhead, though He became flesh for our sake.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Vyshnikh ishcha):*

Thou didst appear as a radiant luminary at the ends of the earth, O hierarch Theophylact: having proclaimed the Word consubstantial with the Father and the Spirit, thou didst elucidate the council of the divine fathers, and wast shown forth as a well-pleaser of the Trinity; before Whom standing, pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparia:*

Having finished the divine course and kept thy faith unto the end, thou didst receive the crown as reward for thy afflictions, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God, unto the ages.

Borne upon the chariot of divine virtues, thou didst ascend unto the heights, and dost now stand before God, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

He Who fashioned the hearts of men singly,[^57] O God-bearer, knew the divine nature of thy heart, and seated thee upon the thrones of teaching, O thrice-blessed Theophylact.

*Theotokion:*

Unto thee do I now flee, O Birthgiver of God, and unto thy covering and divine protection, praying to be delivered from grievous transgressions, O blessed, all-pure one, who didst bear God in the flesh.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

Where the dwelling of those who rejoice is, there hast thou now settled, O confessor and champion of the truth; and light hath dawned for thee, as for a righteous man, O all-wise one, who didst suffer the struggles of martyrs; with whom also thou criest aloud: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou didst receive the crown of endurance, O God-accepted one; for Thou didst settle in bitter exile,[^58] awaiting to receive the reward of thy sufferings; and Thou didst not miss thy good hope, crying now unto Christ: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

Radiantly is thy memory celebrated now, O God-bearer, shining with the light of confession; for thou didst honor the holy image of Christ, and of the Birthgiver of God, and of all the saints; with whom thou dost sing: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

O most pure Mother of God, destroy the wounds of my soul and the sinful arrows, having borne God Who is over all from virginal and unwedded loins, O pure Maiden; Whom youths bless, priests hymn, and people supreme exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Truly do we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparia:*

Thou wast vouchsafed great gifts, O father, having suffered peril for Christ with a patient mind, O God-bearing, God-blessed one.

Look down upon us who hymn thee, O blessed one, delivering us from trials and afflictions, and crushing heretical wiles.

Having lived chastely and righteously as a manifest priest, O sufferer, thou wast a luminary, having woven for thyself a purple robe from thy blood.

*Theotokion:*

Like the dawn unto those in darkness and astray, having the Sun of righteousness, Christ borne upon the clouds, O Virgin, thou hast shone forth!

*In the same month, on the ninth day: Holy Forty Martyrs who suffered in the Lake of Sebaste.*[^59]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," the Idiomelon of the day comes first, twice, and the Martyrikon, and three Prosomoia from the Triodion; and for the Holy Martyrs, four Stichera. Stichera Idiomela, Tone 2. A work of John the Monk:*[^60]

Enduring present things valiantly, rejoicing in what is hoped for, the holy martyrs said one to another: It is not garments that we put off, but the old man we lay aside; severe is the winter, but sweet is paradise; painful is the ice, but sweet the reception; let us not turn aside, O soldiers, let us endure a little, that we may be crowned with victory wreaths from Christ God and the Savior of our souls! *(Twice)*

Casting aside all garments, the holy martyrs entered into the lake without trembling, saying one to another: For the sake of the paradise which we lost, let us not spare corruptible garments today; having once been clothed because of the corrupting serpent, let us now strip ourselves for the resurrection of all; let us despise the melting ice, and hate the flesh, that we may be crowned with victory wreaths from Christ God and the Savior of our souls! *(Twice)*

Devouring tortures as enjoyment, running unto the freezing lake as unto warmth, the holy martyrs said: Let us not fear the winter hour, that we may escape the dreadful fire of Gehenna! Let the foot be burned away, that it may dance eternally; let the hand be severed, that it may be lifted up unto the Lord! Let us not spare mortal nature, let us choose death, that we may be crowned with victory wreaths from Christ God and the Savior of our souls!

*Glory, the same Tone, the first Sticheron: "Enduring present things..."*  
*Both now... Theotokion: "The shadow of the Law hath passed away..."*  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day, and of the Saints, three:*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 43).*[^61]

Thus says the Lord: All nations are gathered together, and rulers shall be gathered from among them. Who will declare these things among them? Or who will declare to you things from the beginning? Let them bring forth their witnesses, and let them be justified; and let them hear, and speak the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I too am a witness, says the Lord God, and the Servant Whom I have chosen; that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, and after Me there shall be none. I am God, and besides Me there is no Savior. I have declared, and have saved, I have brought to reproach, and there was no strange god among you. Ye are My witnesses, and I am the Lord God, and even from the beginning I am He; and there is none that can deliver out of My hands; I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus says the Lord God, Who delivers you, the Holy One of Israel.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 3).*[^62]

The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the sight of the senseless they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction, but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality. And having been a little chastised, they shall be greatly rewarded; for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering sacrifice. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign over them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His elect.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapters 5 and 6).*[^63]

The righteous live unto the ages, and in the Lord is their reward, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive the kingdom of majesty, and a crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take to Him His jealousy for complete armor, and make the creation His weapon for the vengeance of His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and true judgment instead of a helmet. He shall take holiness for an invincible shield; He shall sharpen severe wrath for a sword, and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. Then shall the right-aiming thunderbolts go forth, and from the clouds, as from a well-drawn bow, shall they fly to the mark. And hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as out of a stone bow. The water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall inundate them sharply. A spirit of power shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall it blow them away; and lawlessness shall make the whole earth desolate, and evil-doing shall overthrow the thrones of the mighty of the mighty. Hear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye that be judges of the ends of the earth! Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and boast yourselves in the throngs of nations! For sovereignty is given you from the Lord, and power from the Most High.

*And the rest of the Office of the Presanctified Gifts.*  
*If the Presanctified Liturgy is not celebrated, we sing on the Aposticha the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day, twice, and the Martyrikon.*  
*Glory, of the Saints, Tone 2:*

In hymns let us, the faithful, praise the forty martyr passion-bearers, and sweet-voicedly cry aloud unto them, saying: Rejoice, O passion-bearers of Christ: Hesychius, Meliton, Heraclius, Smaragdus, and Domnus, Eunoicus, Valens, and Vivianus, Claudius, and Priscus! Rejoice, Theodulus, Eutychius, and John, Xanthius, Helianus, Sisinius, Cyrion, Angias, Aëtius, and Flavius! Rejoice, Acacius, Ecdicius, Lysimachus, Alexander, Elias, and Gorgonius, Theophilus, Dometian, and divine Gaius, and Gorgonius! Rejoice, Eutychus, and Athanasius, Cyril, and Sacerdon, Nicholas, and Valerius, Philoctemon, Severian, and Chudio![^64] As ye have boldness toward Christ our God, O all-honorable martyrs, fervently beseech Him that they may be saved who in faith celebrate your all-honorable memory!

*Both now...: "Birthgiver of God, thou art the true vine..." After "Now lettest Thou..."*  
*Troparion, Tone 1:*

By the sufferings which the saints endured for Thee, be Thou entreated, O Lord, and heal all our sicknesses, O Lover of mankind, we beseech Thee!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion: "When Gabriel voiced unto thee..."*  
*Litany, and three great prostrations, and the Dismissal.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Saints, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion. And the usual Kathismata. After the first and second Kathisma, Sessional Hymns of the Triodion; we do not say litanies. After the third Kathisma, the priest says the little litany.*  
*Then the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Yavilsya yesi dnes):*

As greatest stars ye ever enlighten the honorable firmament of the Church, and illuminate the faithful, O divine forty martyrs of Christ!

*Glory, the same Tone, Podoben: "Lifted up" (Voznesyisya):*

With most divine purpose having traversed martyrdom, O marvelous passion-bearers, through fire and water ye passed, and ye pass and ye passed over into the broad expanse of salvation, receiving as an inheritance the heavenly kingdom, wherein make divine entreaties for us, O wise forty!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

Those who thankfully hymn thee from the heart and fervently ask thy mercies, O Birthgiver of God and Sovereign Lady, thy servants crying aloud and saying: O all-holy Virgin, forestall and deliver us from enemies invisible and visible, and from every threatening; for thou art our protection!

*If it be Saturday outside of the first week of the Fast:*  
*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: "The honorable firmament," twice. Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Receiving the supplication from us who flee unto thy covering, O all-pure Virgin, cease not to pray unto the Lover of mankind that He save thy servants.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn: "With most divine purpose," twice. Glory... Both now... Theotokion: "Those who thankfully..."*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless" (Sobeznachalnoe):*

Behold the adornment of the holy martyrs, fourfold radiant and chosen by God, the multitude of forty soldiers in truth: tested by frost and fire, O wise ones, ye were shown forth as soldiers of Christ the King of all, Whom also they beseech that we may be saved. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

I weep for myself before the judgment now, pondering my wicked and grievous deeds, and the depth of transgressions that grew up with me from my youth, O pure one, oppressing my mind; but by thy intercession grant me remission, and vouchsafe that I may attain salvation.

*The Gradual Hymns (Anavathmoi), the first Antiphon of Tone 4.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: We went through fire and water, and Thou leddest us out into a place of refreshment.*[^65]  
*Verse: Thou hast proved us with fire, as silver is proved.*[^66]  
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 105 [Luke 21:12–19].*[^67]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron Idiomelon, Tone 2:*

David prophetically cried aloud in the Psalms: "We went through fire and water, and Thou leddest us out into refreshment." But ye, O martyrs of Christ, fulfilling the word by deeds themselves, passed through fire and water, and entered into the kingdom of heaven; therefore pray, ye forty passion-bearers, that great mercy may be granted unto us!

*Two Canons of the Martyrs, to eight. The first The first Canon, whose acrostic is: "I hymn the God-crowned regiment of martyrs." A work of John the Monk.[^68] Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of Egyptian bondage, for He is glorified.

*Troparia:*

I hymn the God-crowned regiment of forty martyrs of Christ with God-inspired songs; celebrate their yearly memory radiantly, for He is glorified!

Having rejected all earthly names, the forty chose the name of Christ, by which they now dwell in the highest.

Those who are in Christ, having hated the flesh and the world, put off the old man with temporal clothing, and clothed themselves in the garment of incorruption.

*Theotokion:*

Who can worthily tell of thy conception surpassing reason? For thou didst bear God in the flesh, O all-pure one, Who appeared as the Savior of us all.

*Another Canon, whose acrostic is: "I hymn the God-crowned regiment of martyrs." A work of Lord Theophanes.[^69] Tone 2, the same Heirmos.*  
*Troparia:*

O forty martyrs, through your prayers make good things bountiful unto us now, who with pure love of heart call upon you!

Enlightened in the heavens by the beams of the threefold Sun, O forty, shelter us who hymn you upon earth, frozen by the winter of temptations!

O indestructible ramparts of piety and unshakable bulwarks of the Church, ye forty who bear the name of Christ, pacify him who is now grievously troubled!

*Theotokion:*

Supernaturally thou didst bear the Creator and Lord Who is over all, Who became flesh for the sake of us men, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unwedded Mother!

*Katavasia: "I will open my mouth..."*  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee!

*Troparia:*

Having despised military service and life, beauty of bodies and wealth, the glorious forty inherited Christ in place of all.

Being unsparingly struck with stones by the tyrant's command, the stones, by the Spirit of God, were turned back upon those who gave the command were turned back; rather upon the tyrant himself who through the serpent's mouth blasphemed the Creator: but his God-fighting mouth was crushed with stones unto torments![^70]

*Theotokion:*

Without seed thou didst conceive God in thy womb, and didst bear Him ineffably incarnate, upon Whom the heavenly hosts dare not gaze, O pure Ever-Virgin!

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

O martial host of martyrs and united choir, crown-bearing martyrs, standing before the Sovereign Lady, ever save those who praise you!

Tempest-tossed by the lake of passions and by the surge of grievous trials, we flee unto you forty, the soldiers of Christ!

Warmed in the bosom of Abraham and adorned with a glorious garment, pray ye that we who are bound by the winter of the cruel enemy may be loosed!

*Theotokion:*

Be thou the confirmation and refuge and protection, O Virgin Bride of God, unto those who in faith flee unto thee and confess thee to be the Mother of God!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Premudrosti):*

Having assembled as soldiers for Christ, O martyrs, having cast down the enemy through suffering, by your deeds ye fulfilled the sayings of the prophet; for through fire and water ye passed valiantly, finding rest, life incorruptible; therefore, adorned with heavenly crowns, ye rejoice with the choirs of the bodiless, O blessed, all-praised passion-bearers: beseech Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having fallen into multifaceted trials from enemies visible and invisible, held fast by the tempest of my numberless sins, and fleeing unto thy warm protection and covering, O pure one, I take refuge in the haven of thy goodness; therefore, O all-pure one, fervently entreat Him Who was incarnate from thee without seed on behalf of all thy servants who unceasingly pray unto thee, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, entreating Him ever to grant remission of sins unto those who worthily hymn thy glory.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

Ye are demented with strange vanity, reaching out for destruction, O most godless ones! said the passion-bearers.

Sharpened swords, torches, fire, and the cross did Christ's persecutors stretch forth against the holy ones did Christ's persecutors stretch forth against the holy ones.

"Dreadful unto us," said the passion-bearers, "is the fire of Gehenna; but present fire, as a fellow servant, we fear not!"

"Let hands be cut off, let the foot be burned," the holy ones cried aloud: "for we shall receive them incorruptible again!"

*Theotokion:*

We pray thee, O all-pure one, who didst conceive God without seed: ever pray for thy servants!

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

Ye have become illumination unto us from God, O martyrs; for ye have clothed yourselves in unapproachable radiance.

O God-chosen assembly, crown-bearing company of martyrs, pray that they who hymn thee may be delivered from tribulations!

Having been vouchsafed to behold the unapproachable light of Christ, O holy ones, vouchsafe that we who are in darkness may shine!

Shining forth, O glorious ones, by the spiritual illumination of Christ, guide us unto the divine dawn!

*Theotokion:*

Hymning thee, O pure one, who didst bear the Incarnate One from thy womb, we glorify thee as the Mother of God.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for besides Thee, we know no other God.

*Troparia:*

By the senseless madness of the persecutors, the passion-bearers, having been condemned to spend the night in the open air in the freezing cold, sang a hymn of thanksgiving unto God.

Rejoicing, O forty martyrs of Christ, having endured the painful ice, standing in the lake, strengthened by the hope of divine crowns.

Laughter is offered by the forty martyrs of Christ unto the serpent that had previously nested in the waters and was choked; for by the ruin-breeding frost he was stripped of his strength.

*Theotokion:*

Unto thee who didst bear Christ the Creator of all, we cry: Rejoice, O pure one! Rejoice, thou who hast shone forth the light upon us! Rejoice, thou who didst contain the uncontainable God!

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

From heretical madness deliver even now the honorable Church of Christ, O forty, in which having been born, ye have passed over into such great dignity and glory.

Ye have appeared unto us, O forty martyrs of Christ, as God-radiant, fiery luminaries, enlightening for the faithful the saving path of piety.

Having passed from earth unto the heavenly tabernacle, O holy martyrs, where standing before Christ the Umpire of the contest, vouchsafe that I may obtain divine joy!

*Theotokion:*

As incomprehensible and hard to contemplate for all, O Sovereign Lady, is the mystery surpassing the mind of thy divine childbirth, O Maiden; for thou didst truly bear the existing God for us.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy lovingkindness: lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*

Joyfully the author of evil snatched away, as Judas the wretched from among the twelve, the child of Eden who fell away from the forty.[^71]

Shameless as he was, he was completely shaken; for as with the robber, and the change with Matthias, so now the torturer was condemned by the calling of him who guarded them.[^72]

Demented and worthy of wailing is he who failed of both lives; for having melted in the fire, he departed unto the unquenchable fire.[^73]

*Theotokion:*

Without knowledge of man, O Virgin, thou didst bear, and thou remainest a virgin forever, showing forth the images of the true divinity of thy Son and God.

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

Vigilant and sober guardians of the human race have ye appeared, mighty ones, and supplicants in prayer, and ye were appointed helpers of the sorrowful.

Shining with unapproachable beauties in the Church of Christ, O all-praised ones, deliver from temptations those who honor you with love!

Having consumed of old the deceit of the raging tyrants, O all-excellent ones, be ye also unto us a speedy help and a wall!

*Theotokion:*

In thee have I placed the hope of my salvation, O Mother Ever-Virgin, and thee have I set forth as the protectress of my life, firm and unshakable.

*Kontakion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Fulfilling the dispensation for our sake" (Ezhe o nas):*

Having left behind all the warfare of the world, ye cleaved unto the Master in the heavens, O forty passion-bearers of the Lord; for having passed through fire and water, O blessed ones, ye worthily received glory from the heavens and a multitude of crowns.[^74]

*Ikos:*

Unto Him Who sits upon the unapproachable throne, Who stretched out the heaven as a skin, and established the earth, and gathered the waters into their gatherings; Who brought forth all things from non-existence into being, and gives breath and life unto all;

...and life; Who receives a hymn from the archangels, and is worshipped by angels, and glorified by all—unto Christ the Almighty, our Maker and God: I, the unworthy one, fall down, offering my supplication, and I ask for the word of grace, that I too may be able piously to hymn the holy ones whom He Himself showed forth as victors, granting them glory from the heavens and a multitude of crowns.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

Unto the golden image worshipped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths paid no heed to the godless decree; but cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^75]

*Troparia:*

Seeing the terrifying crowns of the forty, the guard, having cast aside the love of earthly life, was borne aloft by longing for Thy manifest glory, and sang with the martyrs: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having run unto the soul-corrupting bath, he that loved earthly life was put to death; but the lover of Christ, having become an excellent plunderer of the things seen, as in a bath of incorruption sang with the martyrs: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

When the spiritual fire was kindled in the minds of the forty, the multiform madness of the ungodly was consumed, like wax melting; and unto Thee, O Christ, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

How exceedingly radiant and comely, O Christ, is the strength of Thy power, weaving crowns from opposites for the forty martyrs; for having passed through water and fire, in incorruption they cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Moses foresaw thee on Mount Sinai as the fiery burning bush, O pure one, which unburned bore the unendurable radiance of the ineffable Essence, Which united Itself to the grossness of the flesh, one of the holy Hypostases within it.[^76]

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

Brightly hath your yearly memory arrived, O all-rich ones, enlightening with the far-shining brightness of the Fast; celebrating it in faith, with you we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Loosers of passions, and luminaries shining from afar, purification of the world, destruction of delusion, loud-voiced preachers of truth, expellers of falsehood, ye have appeared unto all, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Spiritual luminaries, shining upon us have ye appeared, O martyrs; save those who are tempest-tossed, and guide those who sail upon the wave of life who are tossed by the storm, singing unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Beautiful unto God and desirable unto the angels is the God-gathered, God-inspired, and all-radiant army of the forty, a divine and terrifying panoply, crying aloud and saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast shown forth as a luminary of the Light, and a radiant cloud, and a place of sanctification, O unwedded one; for thou didst receive the ineffable Word of the Holy of holies, singing unto Whom we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Him Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and changed the flame into dew, praise God as the Lord, O ye works, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages![^77]

*Troparia:*

Having madly commanded every creature against the passion-bearers, the enemy was put to shame by all; for the forty unceasingly sing unto the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.

When the limbs of your bodies were pitilessly crushed for Christ's sake, ye were offered as a divine whole burnt offering unto God; with the angels ye ever keep festival, O martyrs, singing unto Christ unto all ages.

With fortitude of mind, having taken him whom she bore upon her shoulders, the Christ-loving mother brings forth the fruit of piety, a martyr with martyrs, imitating the priestly sacrifice of Abraham.[^78]

"Unto unceasing life, make thy journey in a straight course, O my son!" cried the Christ-loving mother unto her Christ-loving child: "I cannot bear that thou shouldst appear second unto Christ the Umpire of the contest!"[^79]

*Theotokion:*

O pure one, who didst lodge within thy spacious womb the Son Who sits indivisibly in the bosom of the Father: we hymn thee, O Virgin Bride of God, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

Having made yourselves akin unto true life, which is now hidden in Christ, unto a cold and painful end ye gave yourselves up valiantly, O noble ones, asking salvation and remission for us.

Having passed from earth unto heavenly things, O crown-bearing passion-bearers, victorious company of the martyrs of the Church: ask deliverance from tribulations, passions, and afflictions, and salvation for us!

Having broken the bonds of death, the Savior gave the passion-bearers victories over death; for warmed by the frost, the forty sang, asking salvation for all the faithful asking salvation for us.

The assaults of tribulations, the attack of passions, the temptations of demons, the divine company of trophy-bearers manifestly removes through prayer from those who sing unto Christ unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*

Those who were dead and brought down to the dust of death and corruption, thou alone didst raise up, having given birth unto Christ our God, the Author of life, O pure Sovereign Lady, Virgin Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, ineffably incarnate from the Holy Virgin for our sake: with one mind in hymns we the faithful magnify Him.

*Troparia:*

Having stripped yourselves for Christ, and having been struck with stones, ye endured the frost of the air, the ice of the water, and the crushing of your limbs, and were scorched with fire; and ye shine from afar in the swift currents of the river like luminaries, O forty martyrs![^80]

Having acquired the Cross as the rod of divine power, the forty passion-bearers cried aloud unto Christ: O Almighty Master and Victor, let us be crowned by Thy hand, that all of us may unceasingly magnify Thee in hymns!

Though the ice was painful, and exceedingly immeasurable the severity of the frost which ye endured, yet sweet is paradise; for the bosom of the Patriarch Abraham warms you in eternal habitations, O forty martyrs!

Having conquered by sufferings and received crowns from the divine right hand of the Master, pray ye now that peace be granted unto the world, victories unto the Christ-loving King, and salvation unto us, O forty martyrs![^81]

*Theotokion:*

O Bride, Mother, Virgin, the angelic multitudes pray unto thy Son; receive our supplication, O only hope of the wretched, and grant peace unto the world, victories unto the Christ-loving King, and salvation unto us who love thee![^82]

*Another Canon, the same Heirmos:*  
*Troparia:*

Ye were vouchsafed to obtain supernatural radiance, ineffable joy, and glory; deliver now from tribulations, calamities, and enemy cunning those who honor you with love, O soldiers of Christ, forty martyrs!

Having received divine power and strength from heaven, O companions of Christ, all-rich ones, ye became expellers of deception, driving away all unstable idol gloom, and enlightening the world, O forty martyrs!

Adorned with the beauty of honorable martyrdom, and having become partakers of the divine nature: truly rejoice, having been commingled with the clearest and purest Light, O soldiers of Christ, forty martyrs!

Standing now before Christ with pious boldness, and illuminated thence with the light of the Godhead, O glorious ones: fervently pray, O forty martyrs, that they who praise you may be enlightened with the thrice-radiant dawn!

*Theotokion:*

O Virgin, enlighten the gloom that hath multiplied over my soul with the immaterial flashes of thy light, O portal of divine Light; and vouchsafe that he who in faith and love magnifies thee in hymns may be delivered from eternal fire, O pure one!

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Zheny uslyshite):*

Let us worthily hymn the fourfold decade, the victorious regiment, the army of the holy martyrs; for through fire and cold and water they put to shame the regiments of the adversaries, and from Christ the Savior they received crowns of glory.[^83]

*Glory...*

Let the company of the forty martyrs, fourfold in number, of the transcendent Trinity, fourfold in virtues, be hymned with divine songs of the four elements of fire, air, water, and earth, as having suffered for Christ the Master of all.[^84]

*Both now... Theotokion:*

O Mother and Virgin, who hast given birth unto the Deliverer from ruin for all creation,[^85] and as the Maiden who art over all things: O Sovereign Lady, Birthgiver of God, snatch me away from the passions that tyrannize over me, and lead me unto the radiance of emeralds,[^86] and unto the most fair bridal chamber of thy risen Son!

*At the Praises, four Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Raduysya postnikov):*

Come, O brotherly company of martyrs, let us hymn with praises the regiment that was scorched by frost and fire, and that burned up the winter of delusion with kindled zeal: the most valiant army, the most sacred assembly, the indestructible and invincible rampart, the battlements and guardians of the faith, the divine choir of forty martyrs, the intercessors of the Church, who mightily pray unto Christ to send down peace and great mercy upon our souls! *(Twice)*

Rejoice, O steadfast and patient assembly, and victorious host, pillars of piety, soldiers of Christ, strong and invincible weapon-bearers, most mighty in mind and most courageous in soul, truly divine, the holy company beloved of God, the God-chosen host, the forty martyrs: who, equal in suffering and equal in mind, received equal crowns: entreat Christ to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!

Rejoice, O victorious multitude, who fought valiantly in battles, stars passing through fire and cold, and shattering the water's ice; who made earth a heaven and enlightened all; who are now warmed in Abraham's bosom, and keep festival with the angelic hosts, O forty martyrs; flowers breathing forth the sweet fragrance of sacred spiritual distribution: entreat Christ to grant great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory, the same Tone, Idiomelon. A work of John the Monk:*[^87]

Passion-bearers of Christ, ye have made the all-honorable Fast most radiant by the memory of your glorious suffering; for being forty, ye sanctify the Forty Days, having been conformed unto the saving Passion by your suffering for Christ's sake; therefore, having boldness, pray ye that we may attain in peace unto the three-day Resurrection of God and the Savior of our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*

We bless thee, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, and we the faithful glorify thee as is our duty: an unshakable city, an impregnable wall, a firm protectress, and the refuge of our souls.

*At the Aposticha, in the Triodion, the Idiomelon twice, and the Martyrikon.*  
*Glory of the Saints, Tone 2, Idiomelon:*

Having drunk from the chalice of their own blood, by the fire of tortures and the ice of water they gave drink unto the faithful; for the fourfold decade, singing a hymn unto the Savior, had one mind among all, though in many bodies they were offered unto Christ; and the God-espoused mother, taking the Christ-loving youth upon her shoulders, said: "Go, O sufferer, and suffer with the rest!"[^88]

*Both now... Theotokion:*

Impassable portal, mystically sealed, O blessed Virgin Birthgiver of God, receive our supplications, and bring them unto thy Son and God, that through thee He may save our souls!

*Then: "It is good to give thanks unto the Lord..." Trisagion. Troparion. Litany, and three great prostrations. The First Hour with a Kathisma, and the Dismissal.*  
*In its proper time the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours take place according to their order, with Kathismata; and the Beatitudes recited quickly, and the rest.*

At each Hour, three great prostrations.

*On the same day at Vespers, after the usual Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," we set ten verses.*  
*And we sing the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day, twice, and the Martyrikon, and three Prosomoia.*  
*And for the Holy Martyrs, four Stichera.*  
*Stichera, Tone 4:*

The company shining with fortyfold radiance, the entire God-gathered army, shone forth together with the Fast through honorable sufferings, sanctifying and enlightening our souls! *(Twice)*

*Tone 2:*

The lake as paradise, and the cold as heat did the martyrs reckon, O Christ God; the threats of the tyrants did not terrify their thoughts; the valiant ones did not fear the afflictions of torments, having acquired the weapon-bearing Cross; for thereby they conquered the enemy as mighty ones; therefore also they received crowns of grace.

The choir of the forty martyrs, who will not hymn? For they entered into the water of the lake boldly, and contending with the frost, they sang a hymn unto the Lord: "Art Thou angry with us in the rivers, O Lord? Art Thou angry with us in the rivers, O Lover of mankind?[^89] Lighten the weight and the bitterness of the air, for our feet are stained with our own blood; and lead us, O God, into Thine eternal tabernacles, that the bosom of the Patriarch Abraham may warm us!"

*Glory, the same Tone:*

David prophetically cried aloud in the Psalms: "We went through fire and water, and Thou leddest us out into refreshment."[^90] But ye, O martyrs of Christ, fulfilling the word by deeds themselves, passed through fire and water, and entered into the heavenly kingdom; therefore pray, ye forty sacred passion-bearers, that great mercy may be granted unto us!

*Both now... Theotokion: "All my hope..."*  
*If it be Wednesday or Friday, Stavrotheotokion, written on the 14th day of this month (see fol. 35v).*[^91]  
*Entrance with the Gospel.*  
*Prokeimenon of the day, and the readings of the Triodion.*  
*"Let my prayer be set forth..."*  
*We sing the Prokeimenon, Tone 5: Thou, O Lord, shalt keep us and shalt preserve us, from this generation and unto the ages.*[^92]  
*Verse: Save me, O Lord, for the godly man hath ceased.*[^93]  
*Epistle to the Hebrews, pericope 331 [Hebrews 12:1–10].*[^94]  
*Alleluia, Tone 4: Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth, sing ye now unto His name!*[^95]  
*Verse: For Thou, O God, hast proved us; Thou hast tried us with fire, as silver is tried.*[^96]

*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 80 [Matthew 20:1–16].*[^97]  
*And the rest of the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*  
*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous! Praise is meet for the upright.*[^98]

*If the Forty Martyrs fall on any day of the First Week of the Fast, their service is sung on Saturday together with the Holy Great-Martyr Theodore the Recruit (Tyro):*[^99]

*On Friday evening, the Kathisma in order. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: and we sing the Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion twice, and for the Martyrs on 4, and for Theodore on 4; Glory of Theodore; Both now... the first Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance, Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day, and for the Martyrs three; and after "Let my prayer be set forth," three great prostrations, and the rest of the Presanctified. After the prayer behind the ambo, we sing the Canon of St. Theodore over the Kolyvo, as was indicated in the Menaion in order; and the Dismissal.*

*Where the Presanctified Liturgy is not celebrated: In the evening at "Lord, I have cried," for the Holy Martyrs 3, and for Theodore 3; Glory of Theodore; Both now... the first Theotokion of the preceding Tone, without Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and for the Martyrs. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day twice, and the Martyrikon; then the Refrain, and we sing the Sticheron of Theodore: "Of divine gifts," written at "Lord, I have cried"; Glory of the Martyrs, Tone 6: "In hymns let us praise"; after "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion: "All things beyond comprehension"; litany, and three great prostrations. And we sing the Canon of St. Theodore over the Kolyvo, and the Dismissal according to custom.*

*We sing Great Compline. At Compline we sing the Canon of the Departed, and the one of the Menaion in order. The Troparia after the first and after the second Trisagion we say for both Saints; and at the last Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Martyrs.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Martyrs twice; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion: "All things beyond comprehension." After the first Kathisma, both Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. After the second Kathisma, the Sessional Hymn of Theodore; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs, or of Theodore. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, and "Let every breath," and the Gospel of the Martyrs. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Martyrs. Canons of the Martyrs with the Heirmos to 6, and for Theodore both Canons to 8; Katavasia: "I will open my mouth"; Odes 7 and 8 we said beforehand on Friday.*

After Ode 3, the Kontakion, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion; and reading concerning St. Theodore. After Ode 6, the Kontakion of Theodore, and Ikos; and we read the Prologue and Synaxarion. After Ode 9, the Exapostilarion of the Martyrs; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion. At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs 3, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Raduysya postnikov); and for Theodore, Prosomoia 3. Then the Refrain: "We went through fire and water"; and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Martyrs: "Trophy-bearers of Christ"; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory of Theodore; Both now... Theotokion. Litanies, and the Dismissal, and the usual procession into the narthex, and the First Hour. At the Hours, the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory of Theodore; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours; and we say the Kontakia alternating. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Martyrs, Ode 3 on 4; and of Theodore, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, the Troparion of the Martyrs, and of Theodore, and his Kontakion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Theotokos of the temple, or: "O Protection of Christians"; Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Verse of the Martyrs and of Theodore; the Epistle and Gospel first of Saturday, and of Theodore according to pericope, then of the Martyrs.

*If the Forty Martyrs fall on the 4th Sunday of the Fast:*[^100]

*Their service is sung as indicated on the 3rd Sunday of the Fast, except for the veneration of the Cross; only at the Litiya, Glory of the Martyrs, written at "Lord, I have cried," and Both now... of the Triodion. At the Aposticha, Glory of the Martyrs, and Both now... of the Triodion. At "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion twice; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... "Unto Thine all-pure image"; Katavasia of the Triodion.*

*If the Forty Martyrs fall on Monday of the 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, or 6th week of the Fast:*

*On Sunday evening, we sing "Blessed is the man," the first Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion for the day on 4, repeating the first; and for the Martyrs on 6; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... the first Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance, the Great Prokeimenon of the Triodion, and the readings of the Martyrs, three. Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening," and "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion for the day twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... the Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone. After "Our Father," the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now... the Resurrectional Theotokion; litany: "Have mercy on us, O God," and three great prostrations, and the Dismissal. We sing the Midnight Office according to custom with a Kathisma, and the usual Troparia. After "More honorable than the Cherubim," three great prostrations.*

*Day 9*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Martyrs, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion. After the 1st and after the 2nd Kathisma, we say the Sessional Hymns of the Triodion; litanies are not said; and reading in St. Ephrem. After the 3rd Kathisma, the priest says the little litany; and the Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs, both; and reading of the Martyrs. Polyeleos, and the Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, and Gospel of the Martyrs. Then Psalm 50; Sticheron of the Martyrs. Canons of the Martyrs, with the Heirmos, to 8; Katavasia: "I will open my mouth." But where there is a Triodion ode [triōdion]: we sing the Canon of the Martyrs with the Heirmos to 6, and the Triodion ode to 8; Katavasia of the Triodion. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. After Ode 6, Kontakion, and Ikos, and Prologue. Exapostilarion of the Martyrs, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion. At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs on 4; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... its Theotokion. Then the superior or the reader says: "Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light." After this, the litany:*
*Let us complete our morning prayer. Then at the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day, twice; and the Martyrikon; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion. After this: "It is good to give praise," we say once. After "Our Father," the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion. Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God," and three great prostrations, and the First Hour. At the Hours, we say the Troparion of the Martyrs, and after that their Kontakion. And at the end, 3 great prostrations; and after the prostrations we do not say the Trisagion, but we say: "O Christ the true Light,"*[^101] *and the Dismissal. The Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours we sing with Kathismata, at their appropriate time, with reading. At the Sixth Hour, the reading [paremia] of the day is read, according to its order. The prayers of St. Basil the Great after the Hours we say without change, namely: "O Master, God the Father," and "O God and Lord of powers," and "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ."*
*[Note:] When we sing the Polyeleos, we do not strike the bell [kampan] at the Hours. After the Kathisma, the reader says: Glory, Troparion of the Martyrs; Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the Martyrs. After "More honorable than the Cherubim," 3 great prostrations. After the Ninth Hour, we say the Beatitudes quickly without singing. After "Pardon, remit," the Kontakion of the temple; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now, of the temple of the Mother of God. But if there is no temple of the Mother of God: Both now, "Protection of Christians." After this: "Lord, have mercy," 40 times. Glory... Both now Month of March*

*"More honorable than the Cherubim," and 3 great prostrations. Then the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity." And we begin Vespers according to custom: The superior or the reader says: "Come, let us worship," 3 times. And we sing Vespers with prostrations according to the Lenten order. After the usual Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day, twice; and the Martyrikon; and Prosomoia of the Triodion, 3; and of the Martyrs, 4; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... the usual Theotokion according to the Tone.*
*[Note:] But if it be towards Wednesday or Friday, we say the Stavrotheotokion according to the Tone of the Doxastikon. Entrance with the Gospel. "Gladsome Light," Prokeimenon and reading of the day. "Let my prayer be set forth," and 3 great prostrations. Little litanies are not said. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the Martyrs. Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts according to custom. At the Dismissal, the priest commemorates first the celebrated Saints, then the Saint of the incoming day.*
*[Note:] As for the Saint who happened to occur in the daily order, we sing his Canon at Matins according to order; and his Stichera which are written in the evening at "Lord, I have cried," we say at the Praises.*
*[Note:] But if, on account of any great necessity, on the commemoration of great Saints having a Polyeleos, it should not happen that the Presanctified Liturgy is celebrated: We sing the Hours as was said above; after the Ninth Hour and after the prayer: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ," we say the Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me,"*[^102] *Glory: "Praise the Lord, O my soul,"*[^103] *Both now: "Only-begotten Son," and the Beatitudes [In Thy Kingdom] quickly without singing. At the Beatitudes, we say of the Martyrs Ode 3 and Ode 6. Then the Epistle and Gospel of the Martyrs. After this: "Remember us, O Lord." After "Pardon, remit," and after "Our Father," the Kontakion of the temple; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now, of the temple of the Mother of God. But if there is no temple of the Mother of God: "Protection of Christians." Then: "Lord, have mercy," 40 times; and 3 great prostrations. And at that time the paraecclesiarch signals [rings] on two bells [kampans]. And after the 3 great prostrations, we say: "Come, let us worship"; and we sing Vespers with prostrations and the usual Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Prosomoia of the Triodion, 3; and of the Saint whose commemoration has arrived, 3; Glory... Both now... Theotokion. But if it be towards Wednesday or Friday, we say: Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion. Then: "Gladsome Light," Prokeimenon, and reading of the Triodion of the day.*
*At the Aposticha of the day from the Triodion. After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," and after "Our Father," Day 9*

*Troparion: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin, rejoice!" and the rest according to the Lenten order. After "More honorable than the Cherubim," and after the prostrations, we say the Trisagion; "Lord, have mercy," 12 times; "All-Holy Trinity"; then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and 3 prostrations; and "I will bless the Lord at all times";*[^104] *"It is truly meet"; and the Dismissal. At the Dismissal, the priest commemorates the Saint of the incoming day. After the Dismissal, we enter the trapeza, and we eat two cooked dishes with oil, and we drink wine. On Wednesday and Friday, we eat two cooked dishes without oil, and we drink wine.*
*If the Forty Martyrs fall on one of the fasting days [weekdays of Great Lent], we sing their service thus:*
*At the Hours, after "Pardon, remit," and after "Our Father," the Kontakia according to the Typikon. Then: "Lord, have mercy," 40 times; Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim." And after the prostrations, we say the Trisagion; "Lord, have mercy," 12 times; and "All-Holy Trinity." And straightway we begin Vespers, and the usual Kathisma; and little litanies are said after each Kathisma antiphon [stasis]. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: The Idiomelon of the day, twice, and the Martyrikon; and Prosomoia of the Triodion, 3; and of the Martyrs, 4; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now: "The shadow of the Law hath passed away."*[^105] *Entrance; then the Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day, and three readings of the Martyrs. Then we sing: "Let my prayer be set forth," and 3 prostrations; and in order the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*
*Where the Presanctified Liturgy is not celebrated: after "Pardon, remit," and after the great prostrations, the reader says: "Come, let us worship," 3 times; and 3 prostrations. Then the Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul,"*[^106] *and we sing Vespers without prostrations. After the usual Kathisma, Stichera on 6: of the Triodion, Prosomoia, 3; and of the Martyrs, 3; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion. Entrance, and "Gladsome Light," Prokeimenon, and readings of the day, and of the Martyrs. And straightway: "Vouchsafe, O Lord." Then: "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Aposticha of the Triodion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone. After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," after the Trisagion, the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now: "When Gabriel announced unto thee";*[^107] *litany: "Have mercy on us, O God." After this, 3 great prostrations, and the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity"; and "I will bless the Lord"; "It is truly meet"; and the Dismissal. We sing Great Compline without prostrations. After the 1st Trisagion, the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion according to the Tone. After the 2nd Trisagion, the usual Troparia: "Have mercy on us, O Lord." After "It is truly meet," and after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Martyrs. "God is with us" and "God of hosts" we do not sing.*[^108] *After "More honorable than the Cherubim," 3 great prostrations. Of the Saint who occurred, we say [the service]; and the little Dismissal. We sing the Midnight Office according to custom, with a Kathisma.*

*Month of March*

*...with a Kathisma, as was prescribed for Monday of the 2nd week of the Fast. Likewise also Matins, and the entire service of the Martyrs.*
*If the Forty Martyrs fall on the 2nd, 3rd, or 4th Saturday of the Fast:*
*On Friday evening, the Kathisma in order. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon, twice; and from the Oktoechos, the Martyrika, 4; and of the Martyrs, 4; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... the Theotokion, the first of the Tone. Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day, and three readings of the Martyrs. "Let my prayer be set forth," and*
*[Note:] the rest of the Presanctified. But if there is no Presanctified, on Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Martyrs on 6; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion, the first of the Tone. Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and of the Saints. Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord." And at the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion, twice; and the Martyrikon of the Oktoechos according to the Tone; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion. After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," the Troparion of the Martyrs; Glory... Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion of the current Tone; litany, and 3 prostrations. Then the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity"; "Blessed be the name of the Lord"; and Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord";*[^109] *"It is truly meet"; and the Dismissal.*
*[Note:] We sing Great Compline, in which we sing the four-ode canon [tetraōdion] of Saturday. At Matins and at the Hours, we sing the service of the Martyrs alone, as was prescribed above. At the Liturgy: The service of the day, and of the Martyrs.*
*If the Forty Martyrs fall on the 2nd, 4th, or 5th Sunday of the Fast:*
*On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera, and of the Mother of God according to custom. At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of the Anatolian [Eastern], one; and of the Martyrs on 6; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion, the first of the Tone. Entrance, and the readings of the Martyrs. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the temple; and of the Martyrs the Aposticha Stichera; Glory of the Martyrs written after Psalm 50, and Both now... Theotokion. And at the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Stichera alphabetically; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion. At the Blessing of the Loaves, "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," 3 times; and reading in the Epistles of the Apostles. At "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion. After the Kathismata, the Resurrectional Sessional Hymns and their Theotokia; and reading in the Gospel commentary. Polyeleos, and the Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs, both; Glory... the Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs.*

*Day 9*

*Songs of Ascents, and Prokeimenon of the Tone; and the Resurrectional Gospel. Psalm 50; Glory, Sticheron, Tone 8: "Open unto me the doors of repentance,"*[^110] *and the rest. Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Triodion on 4; and of the Martyrs on 6; Katavasia: "I will open my mouth." After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos; Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. After Ode 6, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. After Ode 9, Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Martyrs with the Doxastikon, 4, with their refrains. Then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God";*[^111] *and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Triodion; Glory, the same; Both now: "Thou art most blessed."*[^112] *Great Doxology. Resurrectional Troparion, litanies; and Dismissal. And procession into the narthex; Glory... Both now... the Gospel Sticheron [Eothinon]; and the First Hour. Resurrectional Troparion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the day; and the final Dismissal. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the Martyrs Ode 3 on 4; and the rest of the service of the day and of the Martyrs. Know also that on the 4th and 5th Sundays, the services of the Saints from the Triodion are omitted, and the service of the Martyrs is sung according to the Typikon.*
*If the Forty Martyrs fall on the 3rd Sunday of the Fast, the Sunday of the Veneration of the Precious Cross:*
*On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, Stichera: At "Lord, I have cried," and at the Aposticha, all of the Triodion according to custom. At Great Vespers: after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of the Cross, 4; and of the Martyrs, 3; Glory of the Cross; Both now... Theotokion, the first of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the day; and readings of the Martyrs. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the temple; and the Praises of the Martyrs; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. At the Blessing of the Loaves: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of the Cross, once: "Save, O Lord, Thy people."*[^113] *At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns and their Theotokia; and reading in the Gospel commentary. Polyeleos of the Martyrs, and both Sessional Hymns; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading of the Martyrs. Songs of Ascents of the Tone. Prokeimenon, and Resurrectional Gospel.*

*Month of March*

*...having beheld the Resurrection of Christ. Psalm 50; "Open unto me the doors of repentance."*
*Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Martyrs on 4; and of the Cross on 6; Katavasia of the Cross: "Most divinely did Moses prefigure."*[^114] *After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Martyrs, and Ikos; and Sessional Hymn, twice; Glory... Both now... of the Cross; and reading of the Martyrs. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Cross, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Cross, Prosomoia, 3, taking the first twice, with their refrains; then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God"; and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the day from the Triodion; Glory, the same; Both now: "Thou art most blessed." Great Doxology, with the singing of the Trisagion. After this, Entrance; and we say the Troparion: "Save, O Lord, Thy people," three times; and the veneration of the Precious Cross takes place according to custom, as is set forth in the Triodion. Then litanies, and Dismissal; and procession: Glory... Both now... the Gospel Sticheron; and the catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory of the Cross; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Resurrectional Kontakion. At the Third Hour, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the Martyrs. Likewise also at the other Hours, we say them alternating. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Cross Ode 3 on 4; and of the Martyrs Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Cross, and of the Martyrs. Then Resurrectional Kontakion; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse, first of the Cross, then of the Martyrs.*
*If the Holy Forty Martyrs fall on Wednesday of the 4th Week of the Fast [Mid-Lent / Adoration of the Cross]:*
*We sing their service beforehand on Tuesday of that week; for on Tuesday evening only: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera: Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion, twice; and of the Cross, 4; and of the Martyrs, 4; Glory of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. The other Stichera of the Cross we sing on Wednesday at the Praises. Then Entrance, Prokeimenon, and readings of the day. And "Let my prayer be set forth."*
*If there is no Presanctified Liturgy:*
*On Tuesday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Cross on 6; and of the Holy Forty Martyrs, the feast is rendered at the Hours, as was indicated on Monday of the 2nd week of the Fast.*

*If the Forty Martyrs fall on Thursday of the Great Canon:*
*And their entire service is sung beforehand on Tuesday of that same week.*
*If the Forty Martyrs fall on Saturday of the Akathist:*
*And we sing their service on the 5th Sunday of the Fast.*

*The same month, on the 10th day: The Holy Martyr Codratus and his companions:*[^115]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Nebesnykh chinov):*
With all-praised Codratus, let great Cyprian, the two Dionysiuses, and Anectus be brightly hymned worthily, together with Crescent, and glorious Paul faithfully; as God-crowned passion-bearers of the Lord, and our most fervent intercessors.

Another choir of sacredly victorious and triumphant martyrs hath appeared today, illuminating the assembly of the divine passion-bearers: a steadfast number, a chosen company, over which Codratus is commander, shown forth as a glorious victor, and crowned together with the passion-bearers.

The intricate entanglements of rhetorical wisdom hast thou destroyed like mist by thy cutting word, O all-praised Codratus; having faithfully taught, and patiently suffered for Christ, adorned with both, and crowned together with the passion-bearers.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*In the same Tone, and the same Podoben:*
Held fast by hopeless transgressions, O pure one, having acquired thee as our sole intercessor, O Virgin, we cry aloud in thanksgiving: Purge us from defilements, O Bride of God; for thou art the refuge of the world, and the protection of our race.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*
When the blameless Virgin beheld the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: O my sweet Child, what is this new and most glorious sight? How dost Thou Who holdest all things in Thy hand hang in bitterness, nailed in the flesh unto the Tree?

*Canon, the work of Kyr Joseph.*[^116]
*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*
The captains of old, born of the Virgin, in the abyss of dispassion *Month of March*

...in the abyss of dispassion, drown Thou the tripartite part of my soul, I pray Thee; that unto Thee, as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory.[^117]

*Troparia:*

The sacred and marvelous feast of the sacred martyrs who sacredly suffered hath arrived, light-guided by the illumination of the Spirit of God; having gathered together, O lovers of the feasts, let us duly bless them.

Like a radiant sun, by the light-bearing dawn of virtues, hast thou shone forth upon us, O passion-bearer Codratus, by thy light-beaming rays driving away the darkness of polytheism, O blessed one, and enlightening the assemblies of the faithful.

Proclaiming Jesus the Savior and God as the Rock of life, ye suffered steadfastly; and having received a stone as a fountain of salvation, ye made streams of blood gush forth, O glorious martyrs.[^118]

*Theotokion:*

From the assault of passions, from the demand of sinful thoughts, from the arrows of the enemy, and from every contrary assault, save, O pure Birthgiver of God, the souls of them that hymn thine ineffable childbirth, O all-blameless one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

As the barren church from the nations hath given birth, and the synagogue that was rich in children hath grown weak, unto our marvelous God let us cry aloud: Holy art Thou, O Lord![^119]

*Troparia:*

The steadfast adamants, being cut down with the sword, and patiently compelled by every kind of pain, cried aloud unto our marvelous God: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

By his God-spoken wisdom, Codratus abolished the Hellenic wisdom of contention while suffering; and by his own blood he was divinely adorned in the Spirit.

With Codratus let us, the faithful, piously crown Anectus with hymns, and with Paul, Crescent, divine Cyprian, and Dionysius, who suffered in faith.

*Theotokion:*

Not even a single mind can declare the mystery of thy childbirth which surpasses the mind, O Maiden; for in thy womb, O pure one, thou didst conceive the Word of the First Mind, Who established all things by His Word.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Voznesyisya):*
By the endurance of cruel torments, thou didst slay the proud enemy, O passion-bearer, having endured death by the sword together with the all-wise who suffered with thee, O Codratus; therefore do we celebrate your precious memory, crying aloud: Remember us before the Master, O unconquerable martyrs!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The multitude of mine unclean thoughts, and the tempestuous surging of unbecoming reflections, who can express *Day 10*

...who can express, O all-blameless one? And who can recount the assaults of my bodiless enemies, and their malice? But by thy prayer, O good one, grant me deliverance from them all.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
She who in the latter days gave birth in the flesh unto Him Who was begotten of the unoriginate Father, beholding Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ, cried aloud: Woe is me, O most beloved Jesus! How art Thou Who art glorified as God by the angels now willingly crucified by lawless men, O Son? I hymn Thee, O longsuffering One!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine, hath come with an incorruptible arm, and hath saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^120]

*Troparia:*

By the wisdom of thy words, thou didst put the foolish to shame, and by the instruction of thy God-beholding teachings, O martyr Codratus, thou didst catch divine Anectus; unto thy Master dost thou lead him, a patient martyr.

From the swaddling clothes hast thou been dedicated unto Him Who made all things, having from childhood chosen the understanding of a mature man, and having longed for wisdom, thou becamest entirely a most pure dwelling of Christ, O martyr.

A six-fold candlestick of Thy martyrs, O Lord, having been filled with mystical oil, drove away the night of polytheism, and enlightened them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^121]

*Theotokion:*

The Holy Birthgiver of God, in whom the consubstantial Word of the Father was well pleased to take up His abode as in a fragrant temple, burned not her womb and was not in pain; for she gave birth unto Emmanuel, God and man.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thy light, O Lord, send down upon us, and from the mist of transgressions deliver us, O Good One, granting Thy peace.

*Troparia:*

By the enlightenment of understanding, having driven away the mist of ignorance, unto the Lord dost thou lead an assembly of passion-bearers, O wise Codratus, being crowned together with them.
