Having first ascetic-like conquered the assaults of the passions, secondly didst thou destroy the dominion of the wicked, O martyr Codratus, having suffered most valiantly.

The dry rock is filled with the outpourings of thy precious blood, and yields radiant streams unto the faithful, sanctifying them by divine tokens.

*Theotokion:*

He Who created Eve from the rib, O most pure one, is fashioned from thy pure womb, having willed in His lovingkindness to save Adam *Month of March*

...having willed in His lovingkindness to save Adam, as the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah, praying in the belly of the sea monster, cried aloud: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers![^122]

*Troparia:*

The sixfold company of passion-bearers hath shone forth upon us, like stars upon the honorable firmament of the Church, enlightening the faithful and scattering the darkness of deception.

The abyss of torments did ye cross intrepidly, O martyrs, and ye obtained the calm that ensued, having become a haven of the faithful and most fervent intercessors.

Together with Anectus let us praise glorious Codratus, and with Paul, Dionysius, Crescent, and Cyprian, good-fruit-bearing branches of the vine of Christ.

*Theotokion:*

Thou hast made heavenly the earthly nature of mankind, O all-blameless one, and that which was corrupted hast thou made new; therefore, O Maiden, with unceasing voices we all glorify thee.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

He Who saved Thy youths of Abraham in the fire, and slew the Chaldeans whom justice righteously overtook: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^123]

*Troparia:*

Receiving from on high the dew of endurance in the fire of torments, together with the noble youths the martyrs cried aloud: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

By the shower of your precious blood, O passion-bearers, the rock that was dry of old was filled, pouring forth streams of healings unto them that sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

As a spiritual vessel of myrrh, the reliquary of thy relics, O Codratus, pours forth the myrrh of healings: driving away the foul stench of the passions, and consuming the regiments of demons by divine power.

*Theotokion:*

Blessed is the fruit of thy blessed womb, Whom the heavenly hosts and the assemblies of mankind bless, Who hath delivered us from the ancient curse, O blessed one!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

O Almighty Redeemer of all, having descended, Thou didst bedew the pious ones in the midst of the flame, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless the Lord, hymn the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^124]

*Troparia:*

Enlightened by the brilliance of sufferings, a crown-bearer dost thou stand before the Lord together with them that suffered with thee, O glorious Codratus, singing: All ye works, bless the Lord, sing ye unto the Lord!

*Month of March: Day 10*

Adorned with the light of virtues, at thy demise thou didst inherit martyrly radiance, O all-wise one, singing, O Codratus: All ye works, bless the Lord, praise the Lord!

As a bulwark hath Corinth gained the honorable relics of the saints, and as a free healing dispensary their temple, wherein everyone who flees with faith is delivered from diseases and passions.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered from the primal curse through thee, O pure Mother of God, blessed Sovereign Lady, we bless thee with faith and hymn thee, O all-pure Virgin Bride of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed brought in the curse through the sickness of disobedience; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, hast blossomed forth blessing unto the world through the bud of thy childbearing. Therefore we all magnify thee.[^125]

*Troparia:*
Your martyrly wounds pour forth a fragrance full of grace; streams of dried blood pour forth torrents of healing by the Divine Spirit, and cure soul-corrupting passions; therefore let us worthily call them blessed.

Made comely by the beauty of wounds, thou wast assimilated unto the angels; and mounting the shed blood as upon a chariot, O Codratus, thou didst fly up unto the eternal kingdoms, receiving the rewards of thy sufferings.

Pouring forth divine sweetness from thy mouth, O martyr Codratus, thou didst persuade the assembly of sufferers to suffer with thee: Anectus, and Paul, and Crescent, Cyprian, and divine Dionysius, with whom thou exultest, O worthy of marvel.

All of us honor the day of the feast of your suffering, O passion-bearers; for on this day, having received the crown of incorruption, ye became sons of the light and of the day, exulting around the throne of the King of all.[^126]

*Theotokion:*
Having appeared as the Birthgiver of the Creator of all beyond every mind, O all-immaculate one, thou hast been revealed as higher than the heavens, and queen over all, O pure Virgin Mother of God; therefore we all unceasingly magnify thee.

*In the same month, on the 11th day: Our Father among the Saints Sophronius, Patriarch of Jerusalem.*[^127]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of St. Sophronius, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Yako dobla):*

Being named after chaste wisdom,[^128] called Sophronius by divine foreknowledge, thou wast chaste in deeds, righteous, courageous, and prudent, valiantly crowned with the kindred virtues; and thou didst abide flourishing in both soul and body, like an excellent steward.

From God-blessed lips thou didst thunder forth theological teachings, O all-blessed Sophronius, most clearly theologizing concerning the unoriginate Father, the co-unoriginate Son, and the co-ever-existing Holy Spirit: Trinity in Unity, and Unity in Trinity, one God in identity of essence.

Concerning the flesh, united hypostatically unto the incorporeal and co-unoriginate Word of the Father, O all-wise one, without alteration and without confusion, thou didst wisely teach: acting doubly in both natures, in which He consists and in which He is contemplated, being One, undivided in essence, understood in both the one and the other.[^129]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*
With the showers of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, bedew my mind, thou who didst bear Christ, the Drop that through compassions washes away the immeasurable and countless iniquities of men; dry up the fountain of my passions, and vouchsafe me ever of the torrent of living delight by thy prayers.[^130]

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*
When the Ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she marveled, crying out: What is this sight, O longed-for Son? Did the lawless and disobedient synagogue render this unto Thee, having enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory unto Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon. Whose acrostic is: "I sing the true sacrifice of him who is named after chaste wisdom." By Lord Joseph.*[^131]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Month of March: Day 11*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen and Mother, and I shall appear radiantly celebrating, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.[^132]

*Troparia:*
In chastity didst thou cross the abyss of chaste wisdom, being directed by spiritual winds; and thou didst gather great wealth of gifts of wisdom, O father Sophronius.

As an angel of Christ's Resurrection wast thou, and a divine hierarch of the divine Tomb that hath enriched [us] with a fountain of immortality, of Christ Who raised up the fall of our race.[^133]

By the radiance of thy words and the sharpness of thy mind thou didst enlighten the world, clearly theologizing concerning the essence of the Tri-hypostatic Godhead, the Trinity in three persons, O father Sophronius.

The utterance of thy words and the melodious harp of thy teachings traversed the ends of the earth, teaching the mystery of the dispensation of the Word, O father Sophronius.[^134]

*Theotokion:*
The Son, uncreated by nature and co-ever-existing with the Father, above times and pre-eternal, is fashioned from thee, O Sovereign Lady, and comes under time as man, saving man.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for none is holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^135]

*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the staff of thy words, O venerable one, the Church of Christ repels and drives away the onrushes of the impious and council-gathering heresies.

Having subjected thyself unto the honorable proclamations, O God-bearer, thou wast revealed as a victor by the help of the Spirit, being wisely strengthened, O father Sophronius.

Having put to death all corruptible voluptuousness of the flesh, thou didst quicken thy soul with incorruptible piety, and didst reveal thyself as a divine instrument of the Savior.

Thy theological, fair, and honey-flowing tongue pours forth rivers of the Word, O God-receiver, pouring out divine teachings unto all.

*Theotokion:*
We glorify thee as the Birthgiver of God, having given birth unto God, conforming in designation unto thy childbearing and the callings in both, O all-pure Sovereign Lady.[^136]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Skoro predvari):*
With words didst thou adorn the Church of Christ, having preserved in deeds that which is according to the image of God, O venerable Sophronius; for the chaste wisdom that was in thee shone forth in the world, grace *Month of March*

...grace of teachings shining forth unto all that celebrate thy memory with love, O father.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy divine childbearing, O pure one, thou hast renewed the mortal nature of earthborn men that was corrupted in passions, and hast raised up all from death unto the life of incorruption; therefore, as is due, we all bless thee, O most glorified Virgin, as thou didst foretell.[^137]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee raised upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she cried aloud: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, willing to quicken the dead as the Compassionate One?[^138]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a swift cloud, Jesus the transcendent God came by an undefiled hand, and saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^139]

*Troparia:*
One unoriginate essence in three hypostases, perfect in properties, didst thou wisely proclaim, O father, divided without division, and united without confusion, with one will and Godhead.[^140]

Having a chaste mind and a most prudent understanding, thou didst not cogitate mixture, nor alteration, nor confusion of the two natures unalterably united in one only-begotten Christ, O all-blessed one.

Enlightened by the divine illumination of the Spirit, thou didst destroy the fierce frenzy of Nestorius by the same will and mind, having proclaimed the union of both natures of Christ.[^141]

With the fire of thy teachings, O God-wise one, thou didst consume the coals of them that reject that there are two wills of Christ, different in their respective natures, and two operations together, O venerable father.[^142]

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been adorned beyond all angelic comeliness; for having borne their Creator and Lord, incarnate from thy most pure blood, O Maiden, Who delivers all that glorify Him.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
The impious shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but waking early from the night, we hymn Thee, the Only-begotten, the Radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.[^143]

*Troparia:*
Beholding the honorable Resurrection and the Tomb of Life with unswerving love, thou didst draw forth the secret teachings of the vision of God *Month of March: Day 11*

...secret teachings of the vision of God; and thou didst impart the radiances of the hierarch unto the faithful.

Substantially didst thou love the only Good One, being kindled by the noetic dawn; and thou didst love the fountain of incorruption, stretching forth unto Him in contemplations, O all-wise one.

A living and animate temple of God wast thou, having died unto all things upon earth; having set forth a bountiful table of the Word, O venerable one, and a light of the faith.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond word didst thou give birth unto Christ, understood in two natures, the Only-begotten Son of God, the unoriginate One Who became flesh, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.[^144]

*Troparia:*
Richly was the grace of the All-Holy Spirit poured out in thy lips, O God-wise one; therefore the utterance of thy words is likened unto the flow of a river.[^145]

Like a palm tree in the house of the Lord didst thou blossom forth, delighting the hearts of them that faithfully honor thee with the good fruit of thy words and a pure life, O hierarch.[^146]

By the life-creating weapon of thy words is the heretical council put to death; and the fierce host of the fighters against God is slain and put to death by the swords of thy teachings.

*Theotokion:*
We hymn thy pure childbearing, O Birthgiver of God, whereby we are all saved, being delivered from the snares of death, and grievous sin, and the bonds of Hades.[^147]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Vzbrannoi):*
Roused by divine zeal, and opening the lips of true commandments, having canonical-lawfully handed down the foundations of the Church unto the monastic orders there, O all-wise Sophronius, patriarch among the hierarchs of Jerusalem: thou didst bring all-wise words into the light, wherewith guided we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O radiant boast of the Orthodox!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
He Who saved Thy youths of Abraham in the fire, and slew the Chaldeans whom justice righteously overtook: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^148]

*Troparia:*
By a nod from on high didst thou wisely oppose the heretical tongue-talk; becoming a trophy-bearer, rejoicing thou didst cry aloud: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Being wholly offered unto God, [exercising] most excellent deeds of fasting *Month of March*

...most radiant, O wise one, thou didst plant a garden of virtues with teachings unto God Who sits in the highest heavens.

Obeying the law of Christ, O God-wise one, thou didst piously distribute the wealth of thy art unto the needy, singing: O all-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^149]

*Theotokion:*
Having sanctified thy soul and pre-purified thy body through the overshadowing of the Holy Spirit upon thee, O all-immaculate one, having conceived the Power of the Most High in thy womb, thou didst give birth without seed.[^150]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved the pious youths in the furnace: then indeed prefigured, but now in reality active, it raises up all the universe to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^151]

*Troparia:*
With the myrrh of grace wast thou anointed for the hierarchic sacred ministry, where the salvation of the world was manifested, having served as hierarch, O all-wise father Sophronius, crying: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^152]

Honoring transcendent Wisdom, thou wast crowned with a crown of graces, having received unfading glory and inalienable wealth, the gift of wisdom, O glorious one; and crying: Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

In a hieratically comely manner, O sacred initiate, having clothed thyself in divine righteousness, O all-blessed father, thou didst guide the chosen flock with words and deeds, teaching them: Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^153]

Magnificently and beyond word, the Word co-unoriginate with the Father granted thee word, confounding words and slackening the tongue-babblings of heresies; crying: Praise the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been revealed as a glorious pillar-like temple of the Word Who sanctifies all creation, a holy and fertile mountain, a most radiant mountain, O blessed Sovereign Lady, uniquely sung of; therefore, O Mother of God, we hymn thee and exalt thee above all unto all ages![^154]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut without hands from thee, O Virgin, the unhewn mountain: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures. Therefore rejoicing, O Birthgiver of God, we magnify thee.[^155]

*Troparia:*
The utterances proceeding from thy divine lips, O speaker of God, sweeten the minds of the pious by grace, like a dripping honeycomb, with the comeliness of understandings *Month of March: Day 11*

...thou dwellest within the light that knows no setting, O speaker of God, near the Master and King of all, where the angelic radiances and the armies of the saints exult.

Passing an angelic life upon earth, O father, thou wast vouchsafed angelic blessedness in the heavens, where do thou pray unto Christ that all who hymn thee may be saved.

Like aromatic vials were Thy cheeks, O God-wise one; as an alabaster vessel full of costly spikenard of wisdom didst thou appear, perfuming the honorable Body of Christ with Thy teachings.[^156]

*Theotokion:*
A light cloud did Isaiah behold thee, O Virgin; for the Lord, coming upon thee, cast down all the handmade idols, and manifested His understanding unto them that sing of thee.[^157]

*In the same month, on the 12th day: Our Venerable Father and Confessor Theophanes of Sigriane.*[^158]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (O preslavnago chudese):*

O God-wise father Theophanes, named after the divine theophany of Christ, thou didst follow His life-bearing footsteps; and thou didst forsake the fair things of this life, looking unto Him Whom thou didst desire, in comeliness most preeminent, and being divinely deified by divine nods toward Him, both superior and final.[^159]

O God-wise father Theophanes, abiding in weakness thou didst endure bitter exiles with longsuffering, not sparing thy body for the sake of the honorable icons, O all-praised one, being exiled by the rage of Leo; whereby, breaking the counsel of the lion, thou didst overturn the thoughts and destructive mind of the vainly-wise.[^160]

In truth the Worker of good things richly granted thee rewards for thy sufferings: having given thee power, O all-blessed one, to drive away demons and to heal sicknesses; having vouchsafed thee unspeakable joy where the angelic ranks exult, ever beholding the face of the Almighty.[^161]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*
Rejoice *Month of March*

Rejoice, O chariot of the Divine Light, most radiant star and tabernacle of sanctification; for the Light shone forth upon us from thy most pure loins, enlightening the ends of the world and communing with all by His grace; rejoice, O cause of our salvation, all-pure one; rejoice, O terrible hearing and speaking for them that hope in thee!

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*
What is this visible sight which is seen by mine eyes, O Master? Thou Who holdest all creation art lifted up upon the Tree and tastest death, giving life unto all!—the Birthgiver of God, weeping, spoke, when she beheld God and man, Who ineffably shone forth from her, being lifted up upon the Cross.

*Canon. Whose acrostic is: "Theophanes sings of thee, O Theophanes." By Lord Theophanes.*[^162]
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod steps, ancient Israel of old, by the crosswise hands of Moses, vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^163]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst follow the footsteps of God Who appeared in the flesh, being kindled with piety, O father, receiving from thence a name that is truly kindred, as was prefigured by divine providence.

Delighting in the most longed-for desire of thy Good One, thou didst despise worldly love; and having united thyself unto divine love, thou didst account the beauty of this life as nothing, O all-blessed one.

Rushing forth beast-like, the tyrant Leo persecuted the chosen, unable to endure beholding the icon of Christ honored; with whom he also condemned thee, O Theophanes, unto exile.[^164]

*Theotokion:*
The gate of Thy descent unto us, O Lord, the East, and Sun, and Light—mystically called after Thee—did supernaturally shine forth, having twofold nature as God and man.[^165]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for none is holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^166]

*Troparia:*
Full of the divine light wast thou seen to be, O Theophanes, having united thyself in love unto the Desired One, O blessed one; therefore we honor thy glorious memory.

Having rejected the decrees of the impious lion, thou didst piously uphold the traditions of the Church; therefore Christ glorified thee with wonders.

Quickened by hope and faith, O venerable one, the infirmity of thy flesh *Month of March: Day 12*

...infirmity of thy flesh, being strengthened by soul-fortitude, thou didst dedicate unto God a body formed in common nature.

*Theotokion:*
He Who formed me in the beginning from the dust as man, is fashioned for my sake in thy womb, O all-immaculate one, amending the ancient fall.[^167]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Premudrosti):*
Having become a lover of the love of Jesus, thou didst repel pleasures and worldly confusion; and having lived monastically upon earth like a heavenly angel, thou pourest forth the gifts of wonders, O venerable one. Therefore in thy light-bearing and holy memory we faithfully praise thee, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: O God-bearer Theophanes, pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that honor thy holy memory with love.[^168]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and only one among women who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead took up His abode in thee, and like an infant dost thou nurse the Creator and Lord. Therefore, both angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that bow down with faith before thy most holy childbearing.[^169]

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, and Shepherd, and Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud, weeping, and bitterly lamenting cried out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion which Thou endurest for the mercy of compassion, O longsuffering Lord, Abyss of mercy and inexhaustible Fountain: have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto them that hymn Thy divine passions with faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^170]

*Troparia:*
A heavenly dwelling was prepared for thee as a sufferer, O glorious one; for rejoicing, thou didst pass from earthly tabernacles, crying unto thy Master: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^171]

A crown-bearer wast thou revealed to be, O Theophanes; for, O blessed God-bearer, thou didst not refuse to venerate the honorable likeness of Christ; therefore thou didst also cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Resisting the command of the impious tyrant, the venerable one *Month of March*

...the venerable one paid no heed at all to bodily weakness, crying aloud unto the Creator: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Thou hast been magnified by wonders, O God-bearer; for Christ rewarded thee for thy endurance; therefore thou pourest forth healings unto the needy, as from a most sacred fountain.

*Theotokion:*
Higher than the angels hast thou alone been revealed; for thou didst bear the Word of the Great Council Angel, Who enlightened all that cry with faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^172]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from the gloom of ignorance them that hymn Thee with faith.[^173]

*Troparia:*
With pious word did venerable Theophanes orthodoxly proclaim the victories of the faith by the Holy Spirit.

Richly and God-wisely didst thou distribute thy wealth, according to the command of the Master, following them that live in poverty, O all-praised one.[^174]

Having thy soul according to the image of the Creator, thou didst bow down before the most honorable icon of the Master, kissing it with love.[^175]

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast borne for us under time the timeless Light, O Mother of God, for them that sit in the darkness of this life, and hast enlightened the world.[^176]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.[^177]

*Troparia:*
Direct my life unto virtue, O venerable one, smoothing the path of fasting by thy prayers, O blessed one, along which do thou vouchsafe me, O father, firmly to walk uprightly.

In chaste wisdom didst thou accomplish thy life, O venerable one, with the wisdom that thou hadst, and with courage [thou didst accomplish] righteousness, and thou wast seen adorned with the circle of the virtues.[^178]

Unto the martyrly renown didst thou enter, O Theophanes, since thou didst honor the icons of the Maiden Mother of God and of the martyrs, O father, opposing the persecutors.

*Theotokion:*
The holy Word, having taken up His abode in thy sanctified womb, [revealed] thee to be this, O Birthgiver of God, by an ineffable word; Whom do thou entreat that thy servants may be saved.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Vyshnikh ishcha):*
Having received divine revelation from on high, with haste thou didst depart from the midst of worldly tumult; and having lived in solitude, O venerable one, thou didst receive the operation of wonders and the dignity of prophecy, of wealth[^179] *Month of March: Day 12*

...and a spouse, having been deprived.

*Ikos:*
Preferring nothing upon earth, thou didst joyfully follow Christ Who called thee; and having fervently taken His yoke upon thy shoulders, thou didst find rest in thy soul; which do thou also send down upon me, poor and despondent, who speak and in no wise perform, but am still occupied in worldly matters, and wonder how thou didst escape all things, having been deprived of a companion of life and of wealth.[^180]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the youths of Abraham, fired more by love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^181]

*Troparia:*
Having acquired [the likeness] according to the image and according to the likeness, receiving it through a most radiant life, O father, thou wast adorned on both sides, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Adorned with the crown of confession, O father, by deed didst thou reprove the words of the impious, and didst assure the faithful, crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

With firmest zeal of mind, O father, thou didst patiently endure bodily afflictions, thankfully crying unto thy Master: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
As the Birthgiver of God and Queen of all creation, all we faithful sing of thee, the pure one, and cry aloud unto thee the "Rejoice": Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady![^182]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Stretching forth his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; praise the Lord and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^183]

*Troparia:*
Beholding the firmness of thy opposition, O blessed one, the all-wicked tyrant Leo condemned thee unto most bitter exile; and crying aloud with gladness, thou didst endure this: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^184]

Seeing that the honor of the icon passes over unto the Prototype, thou didst run unto the arena as a most lawful contest-bearer; and having been shown forth as victor, thou didst receive the crown, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^185]

The Lord of all gave Thee a heavenly tabernacle instead of an earthly one; for instead of narrow constraint, thou didst find the expanse of rejoicing in paradisiacal sweetness, crying with the martyrs: Bless *Month of March*

...the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

With the noetic light of the Divine Spirit didst thou shine forth, O blessed Theophanes; therefore thou pourest forth healings now unto them that flee unto thee with faith, and honor thy holy shrine with love, and cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
He Who upholds all creation by divine power and manifestly governs it, is held in thy hands, O all-pure one, like an infant—He Who is co-eternal and co-enthroned with the Father and the Divine Spirit; unto Whom we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
A cornerstone was cut without hands from thee, O Virgin, the unhewn mountain: Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures. Therefore rejoicing, O Birthgiver of God, we magnify thee.[^186]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst disperse all thy wealth unto the poor, O God-bearer; manifestly thou receivest thy righteousness which has shone forth upon thee, enjoying ever-abiding things instead of corruptible things.

Thou healest diseases, O all-blessed one, by the power of the Comforter; manifestly thou drivest away demons by His operation, O most blessed one; therefore, beholding this, we faithful call thee blessed, O God-bearer.

O God-wise one, the light-bearing day of Thy departure summons all the faithful unto communion of sanctification; which we also now celebrate most sacredly, rejoicing.

Granting an ungrudging gift unto me who have fervently woven words of praise for thee, O God-wise one, having shown him forth of like disposition unto thee as of like name, by thy acceptable supplications, O all-blessed one.[^187]

*Theotokion:*
The law of praises is overcome by thy divine glory, O Virgin; but as thou art good and tender-hearted, accept the praise of them that sing of thee, granting cleansing of sins, O Bride of God!

*(Woodcut tailpiece: Ornate baroque vignette with IHS monogram) Month of March: Day 13*
*The Translation of the Relics of our Father among the Saints Nicephorus, Patriarch of Constantinople.*[^188]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Nebesnykh chinov):*
The streams of thy wisdom, drawn by noetic clouds, pour forth unto the world a fountain of Orthodoxy; drawing sweetness from which, we faithful turn away from the outflow of heresies as from abominable waters.

Thy most honorable body, lying in the tomb, did not see corruption at all, but was found whole, O all-blessed Nicephorus; bringing which with diligence, the foster-children of Orthodoxy rejoiced, gloriously blessing thee, O God-wise one.

A temple of God having become through divine deeds, the temple honored by the apostolic calling receives thee, O all-blessed one, sacredly buried after burial, having buried along with thee all the foul-smelling babblings of heresy.[^189]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Tossed about by the tempest of transgressions, I have fled unto the calm haven of thy most pure prayer, O Birthgiver of God; I cry aloud unto thee: Save me, stretching forth thy strong right hand unto thy servant, O all-immaculate one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the immaculate Virgin beheld the Lamb lifted up upon the Cross, weeping she cried aloud: O my sweet Child, what is this new and most marvelous sight? How art Thou Who holdest all things in Thy fist, nailed in the flesh upon the Tree?[^190]

*Canon, Tone 2. By Ignatius.*[^191]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified![^192]

*Troparia:*
Grievously held fast by the tomb of sins, raise me up by Thy immortal beckoning and by Thy compassionate palm; and quicken me, O Merciful One, as Thou art almighty!

Having acquired the rules of the perfections of virtues in a fourfold manner, and having ascended as upon a chariot, O God-bearer, unto the end of the contemplation of God, thou wast taken up from active deed.[^193]

*Month of March*

...thou didst taste of undefiled life, O venerable one; thou didst drink the sober drink from the divine cup; therefore thou wast a receptacle of wisdom.

Driven by a violent hand, O venerable one, from thy throne, and from glory, and from thy flock, by the beasts infuriated by heresy: now thou hast entered into thine enclosure.

*Theotokion:*
By a word didst thou conceive the Father's Word, and beyond word didst thou bring Him forth; and after childbirth, beyond nature and word, O Virgin, thou didst remain even as before childbirth.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant the fear of Thee in the hearts of us who sing of Thee.[^194]

*Troparia:*
Protected with the weapon of the Cross, again writing the divine Incarnation, thou bowest down piously, following the teachings of the fathers.[^195]

Desiring thee as shepherd, who callest thine own by name and drivest away wolves with the staff of faith, thy flock flees unto thee, O wise one.[^196]

As of old Israel carried the body of divine, chaste Joseph, so now the Church of Christ honors the shrine of thy relics.[^197]

*Theotokion:*
Being the temple and sacred dwelling of the Word, O Mother of God, cleansing of transgressions, be thou my protection, O Ever-Virgin!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Bozhestvennyia very):*
With victories thou didst crown the Church, having driven away the delusion of evil doctrine, becoming a sacred trophy-bearer; and having sanctified the ends of the world by the translation of thy holy relics, O venerable father: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.[^198]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-immaculate Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the angels; cleanse me, who beyond all have become dust, defiled by fleshly transgressions, with the divine waters of thy prayers, granting great mercy, O pure one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled Ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross Him Who without travail budded forth from her, lamenting in a motherly manner cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?[^199]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified, O Lover of mankind, Thine unsearchable power.[^200]

*Month of March: Day 13*

...power!

*Troparia:*
An ark of sanctification, from which we draw a fountain of healings, has thy shrine become for them that honor thee.

Thou didst drive away heresies, and didst establish the sheepfold of the flock, having hedged it about with divine teachings.

Duly do we honor the myrrh-receptacle of thy relics, O blessed one, as pouring forth the fragrance of life unto the faithful.

*Theotokion:*
We honor thee as the noetic Paradise, O Virgin, who didst receive the Tree of Life in thy womb, Christ; therefore, honoring thee, we glorify thee.[^201]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, Lord: direct us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God besides Thee.[^202]

*Troparia:*
Beholding it, the leaders of heresy together with them are put to shame with madness, seeing thy shrine, O God-wise one, faithfully honored by the emperor and by all thy flock.[^203]

Thy sacred shrine drives away the evil spirits of demons like a terrible scourge, and receives victory over them from God, of divine glory, O Nicephorus.

A furnace heated by the fire of the Spirit found thee, like gold through faith, shining brightly, reproving the abomination of the heretics.

*Theotokion:*
Adorned, O pure one, by the descent of the All-Holy Spirit, thou dost nurse God at thy breasts, and holdest in thine embrace Him Who upholds all creation in His palm.[^204]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, lead me up![^205]

*Troparia:*
Thou didst endure sufferings for the faith, for the sake of the honorable traditions of the fathers; thou didst bear exiles, through which thou didst acquire most honorable glory.

Firm was thy faith and opposition; it shook the folly of the tyrants; and thou wast a pillar of fire unto the honorable Church, O blessed one.[^206]

As brighter than shining pearls and stones, O wise one, having thy shrine and dust, thy Church is adorned with the crown of Orthodoxy.

*Theotokion:*
The ineffable manner of thy childbirth do the tongues of earthborn and of angels honor, glorifying thee who hast become the Sovereign Lady of all creation.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Lik angel'skii):*
The patriarchal choir honors thy holy memory with praises and songs, O Nicephorus, in the trans *Month of March*

...lation; for it received thy soul in translation, O glorious one; therefore today the honorable Church, magnifying Christ the King, glorifies the only Lover of mankind.[^207]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the golden image was served on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the ungodly command; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^208]

*Troparia:*
By divine power, thy burial robes were preserved incorrupt, and thy bones, by divine beckoning, were not loosed from their jointure, O blessed one, dispersing the foul stench of unclean spirits; and we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The people assemble, and the pious emperor, and the multitude of dignitaries; and having placed thy glorious shrine upon their shoulders in an acceptable manner, like a most holy ark, they bear it with escort and sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^209]

Let the divine church of the Disciples of the Word faithfully receive the converser with apostles, companion of martyrs, rank of patriarchs, and co-sitter with the fathers, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^210]

*Theotokion:*
Beholding of old the unburnt bush burning with fire on Sinai, O all-pure Mother, Moses prefigured thy womb; for having conceived the Divine Fire, thou wast not consumed, but didst give birth unto God and man, the Creator of light.[^211]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as unto the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages![^212]

*Troparia:*
Unto the haven of life from many tempest-tossings hast thou attained, O father, by the wind of the Spirit; and a good cargo hast thou saved with dry foot; and now thou delightest in the calm of that place.

Beholding the tomb, the faithful received it like a new altar, wherein divine whole-burnt offerings are faithfully accomplished, and within, a most sacred corpse: we exalt the Wonder-worker and God above all!

Thy flock rejoices brightly, the radiant choir of monastics celebrates, and the pious purple of faithful emperors, and every assembly of rulers, in the honorable translation of thy relics.

*Theotokion:*
By the Word *Month of March: Day 13*

...having in thy womb conceived without seed the Word Who by word formed all things, O Birthgiver of God, pure Virgin, thou didst give birth beyond word: Whom we exalt above all unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who by eating fell grievously into corruption; Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: we faithful with one accord magnify with hymns.[^213]

*Troparia:*
At thy festivity and at thy afflictions, O father, angelic hosts join in chorus, thankfully glorifying the All-Holy Lord, and entreating peace from on high for us who faithfully honor thy relics.

May the grace of thy relics be an honorable medicine for our infirmities, and thy prayer, O all-praised one, a cleansing unto all, and a path and guide unto God for them that honor thee with faith and love, O blessed one.

Arming the faithful with weapons of victory, protecting them with Thy high hand, strengthen the King against the barbarians; and by the prayers of the venerable one preserve Thy Church, as the firm foundation of the faithful, holding the Orthodox faith.[^214]

*Theotokion:*
Having acquired thee, O all-hymned and pure one, as a strong protection, as hope, wall, and anchor, and firm shelter, and invincible confirmation, and storm-free haven, and only refuge: all we are saved!

*Month of March: Day 14*
*Our Venerable Father Benedict [of Nursia].*[^215]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Egda ot dreva):*
With faith and true love, having renounced the world from infancy, O venerable father, thou didst joyfully follow Christ Who was crucified; and having mortified the flesh with many ascetic struggles, thou didst richly receive the grace of healings to soothe diverse infirmities and drive away evil spirits, being greatly admired.[^216]

*Month of March*

...admired!

Having become the beauty of monastics, thou didst assemble an innumerable multitude to sing unto the Lord, O venerable Benedict, and didst guide all onto paths leading unto Him, for them that well follow thy divine teaching and imitate thy virtuous life, O father; with whom we sacredly honor thee, rejoicing in thy falling asleep.

As of old Elijah, O father, thou didst bring down rain from heaven by divine prayer, and didst make the vessel pour forth oil; and thou didst raise up a dead man, and didst work very many other wonders, unto the glory of the Savior and God of all, O venerable one; therefore we celebrate thy glorious memory with love, O Benedict.[^217]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:*
O Joy of all that sorrow, and Protection of the wronged, and Nourisher of the poor, Comfort of strangers, and Staff of the blind, Visitation of the sick, Shelter and Protectress of the laboring, and Helper of orphans: thou art the Mother of God on high, O all-pure one! Hasten, we pray, that thy servants may be saved!

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*
Beholding the Creator of all, Who endured many offenses and was lifted up upon the Cross, the all-pure one groaned, weeping: O all-hymned Lord, my Son and my God, how, desiring to honor Thy creation, O Master, sufferest Thou dishonor in the flesh? Glory to Thy great compassion, and to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!

*Canon. Whose acrostic is: "A song I bring unto wise Benedict." The work of Joseph.*[^218]
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt; for He is glorified![^219]

*Troparia:*
Unto me who am zealous to praise thy praiseworthy memory, O venerable one, pray that grace may be given me, O Benedict, and deliverance from all transgressions.

In monastic manner, having taken up thy cross from infancy, thou didst follow the Almighty; and having mortified the flesh, thou wast vouchsafed life, O most blessed one.

Having obeyed the law of God, O wonder-worthy one, Thou didst wither the wanton surges of the passions by the struggles of abstinence, and wast enriched with the grace of dispassion.

*Theotokion:*
Enriched with incorruption for thy sake, O all-pure Birthgiver of God, we cry aloud unto thee the "Rejoice" *Month of March: Day 14*

...the "Rejoice", O Rejoicing One, Adornment of the venerable and righteous!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death; and plant the fear of Thee in the hearts of us who sing of Thee.[^220]

*Troparia:*
Thy soul, O God-bearer, having been filled with the living waters of the Divine Spirit, poured forth rivers of wonders, drying up the flow of diseases.

Unto the expanse of paradise hast thou removed, O all-blessed one, having traversed the narrow path; and the snares and disorderly assaults of the demons hast thou constrained.

Watered with the streams of thy tears like a fruitful tree, O Benedict, thou didst divinely bring forth the good fruit of virtues and of wonders by divine grace.

*Theotokion:*
The only Good One, being incarnate, passed through thy womb, O all-immaculate one, and was seen as perfect man: Whom do thou entreat that they who sing of thee may be saved.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The stone being sealed" (Kameni zapechatannu):*
Having lived in a manner pleasing unto God as a monk, thou didst pass thy life virtuously, and didst receive the grace of healings, O Benedict, having wrought terrible wonders; and having gathered a sacred monastery, thou didst lead unto the Lord multitudes of the saved, O most rich one. Glory to God Who enlightened thee! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who glorified thy holy memory![^221]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Thy divine hands, with which thou didst bear the Creator, O most holy Virgin, stretching forth, entreat Him Who was incarnate in His goodness: to deliver us from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who took up His abode in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us by thy childbirth!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
When Thou wast crucified upon the Cross by the lawless, and Thy side was pierced with a lance by the soldiers, O Savior, the all-pure one lamented bitterly, and astounded at Thy great and terrible longsuffering, cried aloud: Glory to Thy love toward mankind! Glory to Thy goodness! Glory to Him Who by Thy death made mankind immortal!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I sing of Thee, for by hearing, O Lord, I heard and was afraid, for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had gone astray; therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Much-Merciful One.[^222]

*Month of March*

...Crucified unto the saving passions, and unto the world, O God-wise father, thou didst please Christ Who willingly stretched forth His hands upon the Cross, O Benedict: Whom do thou entreat that our souls may be saved.

By the struggles of abstinence, O venerable one, thou didst mortify bodily limbs; and the dead didst thou raise up by thy prayer; and unto the paralyzed thou didst grant good running, being faithfully marveled at; and every disease, O father, didst thou heal.[^223]

The dry and waterless souls, O venerable one, thou didst make fruitful by thy life-giving word, with spiritual and divine fruits, O wise one, having been shown forth as God-foreseeing, and the most sacred beauty of monastics.

*Theotokion:*
Like rain upon the fleece, O pure one, into thy honorable womb came down the Word, taking flesh, and manifestly ceased the raining of polytheism, O all-immaculate one, and destroyed the bitter winter.[^224]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, Lord: direct us in the light of Thy commandments, for we know no other God besides Thee.[^225]

*Troparia:*
Entreating God Who is rich in mercy, O venerable father, thou didst fill the cruet of oil like Elijah, a great vessel, marveled at with faith by them that beheld it.[^226]

Being pure in soul, in an ecstasy thou didst behold the whole world shining as under a single beam, God honoring thee, O all-blessed Benedict.[^227]

Working wonders in Christ, O venerable one, thou didst pray to the Contest-Master for water to flow forth, which remains, proclaiming thy wonders, O Benedict.[^228]

*Theotokion:*
He Who is supreme in the heavens, willed to take up His abode in thy womb, O pure one, that He might make us temples of the Trinity, who proclaim thee as the Birthgiver of God, O pure one!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: from corruption, O God, lead me up![^229]

*Troparia:*
Enlightened by the radiant beams of the Spirit, thou didst drive away the gloom of evil demons, O wonder-worker Benedict, most radiant luminary of monastics.

How marvelous was thy life, O blessed one; how most radiant was thy honorable conversation, whereby thou didst draw flocks of monastics unto saving understanding!

Having become a dweller in the heavenly kingdom, O God-wise Benedict: pray that this may be attained by them that faithfully bless thee ever.

*Theotokion:*

[Fol. 45r]
From thy holy womb the unwaning Sun of Righteousness shone forth and enlightened the faithful, O all-hymned Virgin Birthgiver of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Thou didst enrich thyself with the grace of God, and by thy works thou didst confirm thy calling; and thou wast revealed, O Benedict, pleasing servant of Christ God, in prayers and fastings, filled with the gifts of God's Spirit; and thou becamest a healer to the infirm, a driver-away of enemies, and a swift protector of our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Radiant were revealed the youths most wise in philosophy of old; for from a God-receiving soul, speaking of God with their lips, they sang: O supremely divine God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.[^230]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst acquire life-bearing deadness through putting away passions; therefore thou wast accounted worthy to raise the dead, O blessed Benedict, crying out: O supremely divine God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.[^231]

*Troparion:*
Admired by the multitude of the faithful, like unto great Elijah thou didst accomplish by thy venerable labors, having set up an enclosure for monastics, which abides continually, sheltered by thy prayers, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Thinking in their madness to put thee to death by wicked sorceries, O father, the senseless ones were put to shame, being convicted by the foreknowledge that was in thee, O Benedict, who wast guarded by the divine palm of the Creator.[^232]

*Theotokion:*
Heal the passions of my soul, O Virgin who didst ineffably conceive the Well-spring of dispassion; and grant me a shower of compunction, obtaining comfort for me yonder, O holy Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, sing, O ye works, as the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^233]

*Troparion:*
Having reigned over corrupting passions in Christ, O God-wise father, thou wast accounted worthy to dwell in the heavenly kingdom with all who lived virtuously and loved the all-seeing God.

*Troparion:*
Accepting thy holy prayers, God gave through thee things necessary for life unto them that were in want, greatly glorifying thee on earth by wonderworkings, O thrice-blessed Benedict.[^234]

*Troparion:*
Having adorned thyself with the beauties of divine virtues, thou didst pass over unto the beautiful bridal chambers, O father, to abide with God, and to delight in His divine beauty unto unending ages.

[Fol. 45v]
*Theotokion:*
Of thee was born, even as Isaiah cries aloud, O pure one, a Child, the Son of the Most High; and He is seen as thy Son, O Virgin, making them that honor thee sons of the Heavenly Father.[^235]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Unto God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who by eating had sorely fallen into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, with one mind in songs do we the faithful magnify.[^236]

*Troparion:*
Thou wast revealed as a great sun, illuminating creation with awesome signs, O God-bearer, and with the radiance of virtues; on this account, celebrating thy truly radiant memory, we are enlightened in the senses of our heart, O father.

*Troparion:*
The flocks of monastics gathered by thee praise thee day and night, having in their midst thy body, which richly pours forth rivers of miracles, O wise father, and continually enlightens their steps.[^237]

*Troparion:*
More brightly than the rays of the sun didst thou shine forth, O father, having fulfilled the commandments of God; and thou didst pass over unto the unwaning Light, praying that remission of sins may be granted unto them that faithfully honor thee, O ever-memorable Benedict.

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin who gavest birth to the Light, drive away the passions of my soul, and grant that I may purely behold the good, saving beauty of Him Who shone forth ineffably from thy most pure womb as a light of the Gentiles, O all-hymned one.[^238]

*Month of the Same, on the 15th Day: The Holy Martyr Agapius and with him Six Martyrs*[^239]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8:*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O sevenfold company of Christ's martyrs, the assaults of persecutors and death by the sword ye counted as naught; but having stripped yourselves with good courage, wisely for the contests, and having been clothed with victorious glory, ye were numbered with all the venerable, with whom we ever bless you as we glorify you.

[Fol. 46r]
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder":*
O martyr-sufferer Agapius, having loved the Well-spring of good things, the utmost of desired ends, thou didst hasten to drink the cup of martyrdom, calling upon the divine name of the living God. O Thy courage! O Thy endurance, by which thou wast visibly accounted worthy to obtain glory and radiance!

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder":*
O most blessed martyrs, unto voluntary slaughter ye delivered up yourselves, and with your blood ye sanctified the earth, and by your transit ye enlightened the air; and now ye dwell in the heavens, before the unwaning Light, ever praying for us, O most God-like ones.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Thee do we beseech, O most pure one, our Protectress: troubled in afflictions, despise not thy servants that they perish utterly, but hasten to deliver us from present wrath and sorrow, O all-holy pure one, full of God's grace; for thou art our wall and invincible help.

*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder" (Крⷭ҇тобг҃оро́диченъ: подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting sufferings willingly, the Virgin Thy Mother, O Master, cried out: Woe is me, sweet Child! How dost Thou suffer wounds unjustly, O Physician Who didst heal human infirmity and deliver all from corruption by Thy tender mercy?[^240]

*Canon, whose Acrostic is: "I bring a hymn of praise unto the seven martyrs." Of Kyr Joseph. Tone 8.*[^241]

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified.[^242]

*Troparion:*
Dwelling in the heavenly habitations, O martyrs, account them that praise you on earth worthy of enlightenment.

*Troparion:*
Pillars of the Church were ye revealed, pulling down the walls of idol madness, O most valiant passion-bearers of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast wounded with love for the Master of all, O praiseworthy martyr Agapius, and didst zealously choose to die for His sake.

*Theotokion:*
With the most radiant dawn that shone forth from thee, O Virgin, enlighten the pupils of my soul, that I may glorify thee.

*Ode 3, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.[^243]

*Troparion:*
Kindled with the fire of the love of Christ, ye quenched the furnace [Fol. 46v]
of idol madness, O martyr Agapius, by the streams of thy blood; therefore do we praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Ye were revealed as weapons cutting down the darkness of the demons by grace, O blessed ones; and now rejoicing ye have joined myriads of the noetic powers.

*Troparion:*
Having accomplished the course of martyrdom courageously, by the putting off of the body ye reached the kingdom on high, wherein ye acquired divine life, O martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
We know the Creator, Who through tender mercy appeared in bodily flesh from thy light-bearing womb, O Mother, as we hymn thee, O Mother, Ever-Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Christ is risen" (Воскр҃се):*
The sevenfold choir of holy martyrs suffered valiantly for the divine Trinity; by grace they destroyed the multitudes of noetic foes, and in spirit were united with myriads of heavenly minds: by their prayers have mercy on us, O Lover of mankind.[^244]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
From every threat and human malice save us, O all-holy Virgin: for we who flee unto thee, O Mother of God, and unto our God Who was born of thee, have thee as protection and intercession: pray Thou Him to deliver us from tribulations and sorrows.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The spotless Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd upon the Cross, cried out: My Child, what is this strange and unexpected sight? How is the Life of all condemned to death like mortals? But arise on the third day from the dead, O Word, even as Thou didst say, that rejoicing I may glorify Thee![^245]

*Ode 4, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
From the overshadowing mountain, O Word, from the only Birthgiver of God, the prophet with divine vision perceived Thee Who wast about to become incarnate, and with fear he glorified Thy power.[^246]

*Troparion:*
Having loved the commandments of Christ, O Agapius, thou wast manfully conformed unto His sufferings, being slain willingly like a lamb, O praiseworthy great-martyr.

*Troparion:*
Surrendering yourselves unto the sword, O all-wise and prudent martyrs, with the sprinkling of blood ye dyed your garments, clothed in which ye dwell in the heavens.

*Troparion:*
Warm was your love toward God! For its sake ye denied yourselves utterly, and enduring death by the sword, ye were accounted worthy of immortality.

*Theotokion:*
In thy womb thou didst receive the Word, O undefiled and pure one, Who cleansed the whole world from every transgression of wrongdoing; therefore we faithfully bless thee.

[Fol. 47r]
bless thee.

*Ode 5, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Thou Who from the night of ignorance didst enlighten the ends of the earth by the knowledge of God, enlighten me by the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.[^247]

*Troparion:*
Loving in deed as also in name, O glorious one, thou didst suffer for love of Christ our God, the King of all.[^248]

*Troparion:*
The decree of the fierce tormentor ye trampled down, O holy martyrs, and enlisting yourselves under Christ, ye destroyed the ranks of demons.

*Troparion:*
By sacred contests casting down the audacity of the lawless, with the crown of immortality were ye encircled, O martyrs of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
With divine hymns let us the faithful bless the Birthgiver of God, crying: Rejoice, O Gate through which the Lord alone passed![^249]

*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.[^250]

*Troparion:*
With two Alexanders, let us hymn two Dionysiuses, the glorious Agapius, Timolaus and Romulus, who suffered valiantly and vanquished the enemy's hordes.[^251]

*Troparion:*
Pouring forth rivers of healings, O martyrs, ye divinely relieve the passions of mortals; on this account, rejoicing in faith, we praise your valiant deeds.

*Troparion:*
Having lawfully accomplished the sacred contest, lawfully were ye crowned by the all-working Palm, O sevenfold martyrs, heavenly citizens, equals of the angels.

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Bride of God, who by thy holy Offspring didst wither the groves of godlessness: uproot the enemy's malice that sprouts within me.

*Ode 7, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
The youths who went down from Judea to Babylon of old trampled underfoot the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.[^252]

*Troparion:*
Willingly did ye surrender yourselves unto slaughter like lambs, fearing not torments, O all-praised warriors, but crying with good courage: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with love for the Master of all, O martyr Agapius, thou didst bow thy neck unto the sword, and wast numbered among the army of martyrs, crying: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
As a seven-branched lamp are ye seen in the house of the Lord, O glorious martyrs, enlightening the world, crying aloud in the radiance of grace: O God of our fathers, [Fol. 47v]
blessed art Thou.

*Theotokion:*
Loose me who am bound with the cords of ineffable transgressions, O all-immaculate one, granting me a shower of tears as I sing unto Him Who shone forth from thee: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou.

*Ode 8, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Unto Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush revealed unto Moses by fire the mystery of the Ever-Virgin, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^253]

*Troparion:*
Revealed by the working of the Spirit as physicians of diverse diseases, heal all our infirmities, that we may praise and glorify your divine memory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst love God, and by Him, O martyr, wast thou loved; therefore, having accomplished the divine contest, He led thee with thy fellow-zealots into the heavenly bridal chambers, O Agapius.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by love for the Master, ye cast down the violence of the deceiver, and adorned with glorious victories, ye dwell in heavenly resting places, O martyrs of the Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Heal the passions of my heart, O thou who didst bring forth the Well-spring of dispassion, and guide me unto divine life, that I may glorify thee, the Mother of the Savior, unto all ages.

*Ode 9, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Truly do we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.[^254]

*Troparion:*
Having become beautiful with the divine comeliness of martyrdom, ye stand before the Lord, and with Him, O passion-bearers, ye ever rejoice.

*Troparion:*
The angelic host receives you, O martyrs, who have been loosed from the body, leading you unto the heavenly city, unto the unwaning Light.

*Troparion:*
Cease not to remember before God us who today celebrate your sacred memory, O glorious martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
With the light of repentance enlighten me who am darkened through negligence, O all-immaculate one, that I may hymn and glorify thy majesty.

[Fol. 48r]
*Month of the Same, on the 16th Day: The Holy Martyrs Sabinus and Papas*[^255]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8:*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we name thee, O glorious one? A valiant soldier of the King and God, who manfully cast down the impiety of the bodiless spirits; a lamp shining with noetic light; an all-powerful destroyer of idols; a most fervent ascetic and most lawful sufferer: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones":*
What shall we now proclaim thee, O Sabinus? A river of living water pouring forth unto us spiritual breadth, when we are hemmed in by misfortunes; an inexhaustible inundation of healings; a cup pouring forth a holy draught; a most true wonderworker, an equal in honor of the bodiless spirits: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones":*
What shall we declare thee, O holy one? A guide of the wandering, or an intercessor for sinners; a true physician for them that are storm-tossed in misfortunes; a precious sweet-smelling lily of paradise; a first-fruit and confirmation of martyrs; an ever-flowing fountain of miracles, a valiant ascetic: pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones":*
Unto what hast thou likened thyself, O wretched soul, looking not up at all unto repentance, and fearing not the fire that awaits the wicked? Arise, and call upon her who alone is swift to intercede, and cry aloud: O Virgin Mother, pray Thy Son and our God to deliver me from the snare of the deceiver.

*Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Крⷭ҇тобг҃оро́диченъ):*
When the Ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb stretched out willingly upon the tree of the Cross, maternal-like she cried aloud, in agony with weeping: My Son, what is this strange sight, Thou Who givest life unto all as Lord? How art Thou put to death, O Long-suffering One, granting resurrection unto mortals? I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God![^256]

*Canon, whose Acrostic is: "In suffering fashion I praise Sabinus with love." Of Kyr Theophanes. Tone 8.*[^257]

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the malice of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God.[^258]

[Fol. 48v]
*Troparion:*
Delighting now in uncorrupt and divine dwelling and incomprehensible good things, O Sabinus, with the martyrs, O God-wise one, save them that praise Thee.

*Troparion:*
Having found thyself a sacrifice of praise unto Christ, patiently unto sufferings, in deserts didst thou dwell afar off, O Sabinus, exercising thyself in ascetical labors.[^259]

*Troparion:*
Delighting in spiritual flowers, a paradise adorned with virtues wast thou revealed, surrounded by the sword of martyrdom and recognized as food for God.

*Theotokion:*
Mortal lamentation hath fled away; for Life, God in hypostasis, ineffably proceeded from thy womb, O Birthgiver of God, renewing our nature, O Virgin.

*Ode 3, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.[^260]

*Troparion:*
Having made thy pure soul a temple, thou didst receive God into it, and didst cast down demonic temples, O Sabinus.

*Troparion:*
Finding God to be merciful for mercy's sake, an imitator of His sufferings becamest thou, when a disciple became a betrayer.[^261]

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth to God, the good of all creation, as truly the only Birthgiver of God, thou wast revealed higher than all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Wounded with love for the Deliverer, diverse wounds and lacerations didst thou endure, O holy one, with manly mind; and cast into the river, a holy end didst thou receive therein; and costly riches wast thou given unto all, pouring forth healings and loosing diseases for them that faithfully flee unto thee, O blessed Sabinus: pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that honor thy holy memory with love.[^262]

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As a virgin and only one among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of men bless thee: for the fire of the Godhead took up His abode in thee, and as an infant thou didst feed with milk the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and the human race worthily glorify thy most holy nativity, and in unison we cry unto thee: Pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that worship thy most holy nativity with faith.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried aloud weeping, and bitterly [Fol. 49r]
and bitterly weeping, she cried out: The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee; but mine inward parts are ablaze beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Long-suffering Lord. O abyss of mercy, and inexhaustible Fountain, have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto them that sing of Thy divine passions in faith.

*Ode 4, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.[^263]

*Troparion:*
As a lamb led to the slaughter, O martyr, thou didst utter nothing at all, being conformed unto thy Master, for Whose sake thou didst also despise death.[^264]

*Troparion:*
Adorned with chastity and living therein, having endured scrapings and wounds with thy firm mind, O Sabinus, thou didst cast down the enemies.

*Troparion:*
Having glorified Christ in thy bodily members, O martyr, with the spirits of the venerable thou didst receive divine and imperishable gladness.

*Theotokion:*
Delighting in thy gifts, O most pure one, we sing a thanksgiving hymn unto thee, knowing thee to be the Mother of God.

*Ode 5, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O Lover of mankind.[^265]

*Troparion:*
Having cast off weight by the scraping of thy flesh, and illumined thy soul, O godly Sabinus, thou didst soar on wings unto divine and true delight.

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest find the torrent of sweetness and the beauty of incorruption, O most glorious one, thou didst visibly spit upon the passing things of the world.[^266]

*Theotokion:*
Put to death the surgings of my passions, O pure one, delivering me from corruption and granting me the salvation of dispassion.

*Ode 6, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
A prayer will I pour out unto the Lord, and unto Him will I proclaim my griefs; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God.[^267]

*Troparion:*
Taking no heed for temporary things, as though in another's flesh thou didst endure wounds, bonds, lacerations, burnings, and receiving a blessed end, thou didst obtain from Christ crowns of glory, O Sabinus.

*Troparion:*
Truly was the Church of Christ filled with life-giving waters, having enriched thee, a fountain of miracles, from the depth of river waters, [Fol. 49v]
having found in thee a pearl of great price, O Sabinus.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst set upright fallen nature by thy childbirth, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, having brought forth the Creator and Master, Who bore our infirmities and saved us from mortal corruption by His passion.[^268]

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "The Firm" (Тве́рдыѧ):*
O divine branch, unwithering flower, most fruitful bough, O God-bearer Sabinus: fill with thy gladness them that honor thy memory with faith, and pray unceasingly for us all.[^269]

*Ode 7, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths in the furnace trampled underfoot the flame with boldness, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^270]

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest receive imperishable glory, thou didst spit upon the glory of this life, delighting in torments for the sake of God's glory, crying out unceasingly, O Sabinus: O God, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
By the divine bidding of the Master, thou didst cast away the abomination of the godless, having visibly chosen divine wisdom, O Sabinus, singing unceasingly: O God, blessed art Thou.

*Theotokion:*
The divine Word incarnate of thy womb saved the world, O Birthgiver of God; honoring Whom, we sing unto thee, crying: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the Fruit of thy womb![^271]

*Ode 8, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
When the musical instruments sounded in harmony, and the countless peoples worshiped the image in Dura, the three youths, refusing to obey, hymned the Lord and glorified Him unto all ages.[^272]

*Troparion:*
Tormented by the cruelties of the persecutors, but set upright by the love of Christ, Thou didst find inviolable dispassion yonder, O Sabinus, singing a hymn unto the Lord: We supremely exalt Thee unto all ages. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Adorned with endurance, O martyr, thou wast made bright with the beauty of sufferings, and didst shine forth as a star unto them that cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Brightly didst thou shine forth with dawn; for thou becamest the cloud of the Noetic Sun, O Mother of God, Who quenched the furnace of idol madness: Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Ode 9, Tone 8. Heirmos:*
Truly do we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, who through thee have been saved, O pure Virgin pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.[^273]

*Troparion:* Having hastened with love for the God of all, thou didst trample the waves of the river, having reached the harbor of incorruption, O glorious martyr. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:* Having the Savior's faith as a foundation, Thou didst not fear the assault of the godless; but from the depths Thou didst hasten unto the tranquility of life.

*Theotokion:* Having thee as a rampart and protection, and as our sole intercessor, O pure one, and possessing salvation, we are ever delivered from tribulations and transgressions.

*March 17: Commemoration of our Venerable Father Alexis, the Man of God*[^274]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (В̾схва́льнїи мч҃енницы):*

*Sticheron 1:* As a man of God we have known thee, both by name and in deed; for thou didst shine forth with virtues, having acquired boundless poverty and narrowness upon earth, and having enriched the faithful with miracles; therefore pray that there may be granted to our souls peace and great mercy.

*Sticheron 2:* With the desire that dews the flesh, thou didst quench the flame of lust, O Alexis; piously didst thou exchange bridal chamber for bridal chamber, and bodily sweetness for the divine angelic likeness; with them pray that there may be granted to our souls peace and great mercy.

*Sticheron 3:* Unknown didst thou abide, O wise one, enduring affliction before the gates of thy holy parents, while thine own servants mocked thee for a long time; and having died, thou wast revealed through miracles which thou didst work, healing infirmities and driving away the spirits of malice.

*If it be Saturday or Sunday, we also sing the Idiomelon, Glory, Tone 2:*

Thy quiet, silent, blameless, and meek life having Christ God loved, O most blessed Alexis, He hath revealed thee as a luminary to all the world, shining more brightly than the sun. For thy spouse and fleeting wealth thou didst count as dust, unknown at thy father's house, desi- -ring only Christ beloved by thee; but remember thy servants at the throne of Christ the King of all, our God, O venerable father, ceasing not to pray for thy servants.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (В̾схва́льнїи):*

Heal my soul that is grievously suffering from wicked passions, O most holy one, who didst bear Christ the Healer and Savior of all, Who healed every wound of him who was wounded by the devil's malice, and delivered us from death.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding her Lamb upon the Cross having neither form nor beauty, the ewe-lamb and immaculate Sovereign Lady, alas for me, sobbing did cry aloud: Whither hath Thy beauty departed, O sweetest One? Whither Thy splendor? Whither the grace radiant from Thy countenance, O my most beloved Son?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Having ascended unto virtue, and having purified thy mind, thou didst attain unto the desired and ultimate summit; and having adorned thy life with dispassion, and having embraced surpassing fasting with a pure conscience, abiding in prayers as though bodiless, thou didst shine forth like the sun in the world, O most blessed Alexis.

*Canon of the Venerable Father, whose acrostic is:*
*Thee, the man of God, I praise, O blessed one. By Lord Joseph.*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

In the deep of old, the all-armed power overwhelmed the host of Pharaoh; but the Word incarnate hath abolished all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparion:* The narrowest path didst thou tread, traversing a blameless and venerable life from youth, O wise one; therefore widen the narrowness of my mind to praise thee, as thou dwellest in the spaciousness of paradise, O Alexis.

*Troparion:* Having committed thyself wholly unto God, O blessed one, thou wast outside flesh and world; fleeting wealth [thou didst spurn], and preferred heavenly[^275] and enduring wealth, and the promised city, everlasting Sion.

*Troparion:* Thou didst loose the barrenness of thy mother's womb, being born as Samuel of old, O exceedingly rich one; and having conceived pure fear in the womb of thy heart, thou didst bring forth the spirit of salvation through the faith of divine virtues.

*Theotokion:* The co-unoriginate Son of God had thee, O Virgin, as the cause of His likeness unto us, having found thee alone pure above all creation, O immaculate one; therefore do all generations hymn thee and call thee blessed.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The desert, the barren church of the nations, hath blossomed as a lily, O Lord, by Thy coming, wherein my heart hath been established.

*Troparion:* Pierced by the desire of purity, thou didst exchange an earthly bridal chamber for a heavenly one, and the love of a wife for the sweetest angelic likeness.

*Troparion:* Thou didst abandon the worldly turmoil and the burden of wealth, and having become a sojourner from thy fatherland, O blessed Alexis, didst conform thyself to Christ's poverty.

*Troparion:* With tears and afflictions and self-mastery, O all-wise one, thou didst seek the good, when the light of understanding should come, granting dispassion to thy heart.

*Theotokion:* He Who is invisible to the angels on high, O pure one, being born below from thee, is beheld as perfect man, renewing the perishing world.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty" (Красотѣ̀):*

The beauty of the world thou didst abandon, O venerable one, exchanging fleeting wealth for that which is truly unfailing and abiding, O Alexis; therefore with all the saints we glorify thee and celebrate brightly in thy memory, asking to find through thy prayers, O blessed one, great mercy.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

Inconceivable and incomprehensible is the dread mystery of God wrought concerning thee, O Sovereign Lady full of divine grace; for having conceived the Uncontainable One, thou didst bring Him forth, clothed in flesh from thy most pure blood; Whom do thou ever pray, O pure one, as thy Son, that our souls may be saved.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The unwedded pure one and Thy Mother, O Christ, beholding Thee dead, hanging upon the Cross, maternally weeping did say: What hath the lawless and ungrateful Jewish assembly rendered unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many and great gifts, O my Son? I hymn Thy divine condescension.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou hast come from the Virgin, not an envoy nor an angel, but Thyself the Lord incarnate, and hast saved all of me, a man; therefore do I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:* Thou didst endure poverty, having become a beggar, poor even as Lazarus; for divine desire comforted thy heart, O blessed one, which longed for heavenly riches.

*Troparion:* A carefree and quiet life didst thou desire, eating but once[^276] -ce in the week, O Alexis, through longing for the unfailing delight that awaited thee, O all-blessed one.
