*Troparion:* Seeking to make thy heart a temple of God, thou didst love to sit ever in the house of the God-loving Mother of God, O glorious one, and to contemplate heavenly beauties.

*Theotokion:* O Virgin who didst bear the milk-fed Creator, satiate now my suffering and distressed heart with all understanding and divine compunction, I pray thee.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou hast been a mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access from the night of ignorance unto Thy Father, the Origin of light.

*Troparion:* Ever watering thy soul with sacred streams of tears, thou didst make it a truly hundredfold ear of grain, well preserved for the immortal Husbandman.

*Troparion:* Thou wast seen upon earth imitating the life of the bodiless ones, by thine exceeding self-mastery, O venerable father worthy of honor, and by diligence in prayers, whereby thou wast illuminated as with light.

*Troparion:* With the fire of prayers thou didst consume the substance of sin, O wise one; and by vigil standings thou didst lull all voluptuousness to sleep; and having fallen asleep, thou hast passed unto the unwaning radiance.

*Theotokion:* He Who dwells in the light took up His abode within thy holy womb, O pure one, building up again the world that was lost in the darkness of folly; Whom do thou pray to enlighten all who sing of thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Rolling in the deep of sin, I call upon the unfathomable deep of Thy tender mercy: from corruption, O God, bring me up.

*Troparion:* As thou didst become a living temple of God, the unwedded bridal chamber of Christ glorifies thee in thy beauty, and glorifies thee who wast revealed as a vessel of grace.[^277]

*Troparion:* Thy prayers unto God were acceptable as incense, O blessed one; whence by thy life thou didst make fragrant the minds of the faithful with the grace of the Spirit.

*Troparion:* Adorned with a holy life, Christ again gives Thee unwillingly to Thy fatherland, O glorious one, who didst flee from fleeting glory.

*Theotokion:* The Creator, Who remakes the laws of nature, O pure Sovereign Lady, is ineffably born of thee, and deifies me through boundless mercy.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*

Having regarded the house of thy parents as an alien dwelling, thou didst abide therein in beggar's guise; and after thy repose having received a crown of glory,[^278] glory, marvelous wast thou revealed upon earth, O Alexis, man of God, the joy of angels and men.

*Ikos:* With life and words thou wast adorned, and the Church of Christ thou didst magnify by thy life, causing father and mother, emperor and people to celebrate; and having preserved that which is according to the image of God by deeds, O venerable Alexis, thou didst shine forth in the world by thy chastity; therefore the grace of thy virtues irradiates all who in faith celebrate thy memory, O father; for thou wast revealed as the joy of angels and men.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread spiritual dew over the pious youths: He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.

*Troparion:* Thou didst endure, O venerable one, sitting before the parental gates, wholly unrecognized, enduring insults from servants, bitterly mocked, and afflicted by great poverty.

*Troparion:* Having mortified the mind of thy flesh, beholding thy parents yet unrecognized by them, thou didst conceal the necessity of nature, O venerable one, and the contempt of thy foolish servants who afflicted thee.

*Troparion:* O wonder! How didst thou ever abide in poverty amid the wealth of boundless humility, O Alexis? How didst thou endure mocking and reproaches from thine own servants, O all-wise one, who knew not thy life?

*Theotokion:* Thou wast the minister of a great mystery, O all-immaculate one; for thou didst incarnate God Who delivered us from great evils, who magnify thee, O pure, blessed one, full of divine grace.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its operation by divine command, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Troparion:* Unknown before as a son to thy parents, at the time of thy departure thou didst reveal the secret unto them, being manifested to the glory of our God, O glorious one, Who hath greatly and worthily glorified thee.

*Troparion:* The Lord with a great voice reveals Thee to all Rome, a hidden treasure, O exceedingly rich one, lying in beggar's form, and enriching all who come to Thee in faith with the gifts of healings.

*Troparion:* The princes of the people and kings came together, and the priests, O blessed one,[^279] to bury thee, by the divine beckoning; and beholding a great vision, they marveled at the miracles, O venerable one, which thou didst work by the divine power of the Spirit.

*Theotokion:* Renewing us who of old were corrupted through transgression, the Lover of mankind was incorruptibly incarnate from thy most pure womb, O all-immaculate one, and delivered all from the corruptions of sin, O Bride of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather the scattered; therefore the all-hymned Birthgiver of God do we magnify.

*Troparion:* Thou didst pour forth healings while being carried to the grave, God glorifying thee greatly, O blessed one; for the sightless saw the light, and those who before were mute received speech, O all-glorious Alexis.

*Troparion:* Thou wast seen lying in state, and being carried to burial, like the sun emitting beams of healings most gloriously, O father, driving away dark passions, and burning demons, and enlightening the godly-wise.

*Troparion:* The foremost of the patriarchs came to bury thee, and the emperor most Christ-loving of all, princes, elders, and youths, and the choirs of monastics, by the divine beckoning, being sanctified by thy touch, O blessed one.

*Troparion:* Thou wast caught up, borne upon the chariot of virtues, and thou restest where the ranks of the venerable, the assembly of apostles and martyrs, patriarchs, and all the righteous abide; with them remember us who honor thee, O Alexis.

*Theotokion:* We offer thee Gabriel's greeting, O all-immaculate one, saying: Rejoice, untilled field; rejoice, loosed curse; rejoice, fountain of living water, boast of the venerable, Birthgiver of God, ever-virgin.

*March 18: Commemoration of our Father among the Saints, Cyril, Archbishop of Jerusalem*[^280]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As a noble one" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Like a star didst thou shine forth, and didst enlighten the faithful with the sacred splendors of thy dogmas, and didst darken heresies[^281] darkened heresies, and vanquished them to the end; and as a servant thou didst multiply the talent given unto thee, having well-pleased the Master, O godly-wise one, into Whose hands thou didst commit thy spirit, O divinely-blessed Cyril.

*Sticheron 2:* By the wisdom of thy words and the radiance of thy life, like a star of many lights, O worthy of honor, thou didst shine forth in the midst of the council of the fathers; the godless Macedonius thou didst strangle with the sinews of grace, who foolishly blasphemed the holy, life-creating, divine Spirit, and manifestly committed lawlessness.

*Sticheron 3:* The ruinous mind of the most foolish Manes thou didst put to shame, having excellently and most wisely refuted the vile doctrines of his frenzy, O chief of teachers, splendor of priests, divine champion of the Church of Christ; therefore, glorifying thy holy dormition, we celebrate it.[^282]

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:*

Choice repentance and saving medicine, O thou who didst bear God my Savior, and streams of tears, and meditation upon the dread and fearful hour and unbribable judgment, do thou grant me to escape through thy prayers, O Virgin, from trembling torment, and to obtain divine grace.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:*

Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, the ewe-lamb and Thy Mother marveled, and cried aloud: What vision is this, O longed-for Son? Hath the ungrateful and lawless assembly rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon, whose acrostic is:*
*Cyril do I praise, the president of Jerusalem. By Lord Theophanes.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The three captains of old, O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, in the deep of dispassion do Thou drown, I pray, the tripartite part of my soul, that unto Thee as on a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory.

*Troparion:* Having adorned thy soul with forms of virtues, thou didst make it a vessel of the grace of the Holy Spirit, whence thou didst draw up the abyss of wisdom, drying up the deeps of heresies, O Cyril.

*Troparion:* Thou didst render thy mind superior to material things, appearing as a companion of immaterial ministers; and with the fire of thy words thou didst consume all the matter of heresies, O Cyril, unquenchable luminary of the Church.

*Troparion:* With the sweet-breathing fragrance of thy words, O most wise one, thou didst drive far away the intolerable filth of all the heresies of him who was named after madness,[^283] as a most true shepherd, having become the sweet fragrance of Christ.

*Theotokion:* From the weight of passions, from the tempest of thoughts, from the arrows of the crafty one, from every assault of the adversary, do thou save, O pure Mother of God, the souls of those who hymn thy ineffable childbearing, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

As the barren church of the nations hath brought forth, and the assembly multiple in children hath grown weak, unto our marvelous God let us cry aloud: Holy art Thou, O Lord.

*Troparion:* Shining with the splendors of spiritual wisdom, O father, thou didst clearly explain to those on earth the tri-hypostatic Godhead of the Trinity, whereby we have been delivered from the darkness of delusion.

*Troparion:* Thou wast revealed as a harp of the All-Holy Spirit, O godly-wise one, sounding forth the hymn of Christ's manifestation, Whom thou didst proclaim in two natures, sweetening our souls.

*Troparion:* A house of holiness was thy soul revealed to be, wherein the Father, and the Son, and the Life-Originating Spirit supernaturally made Their abode; unto Whom we sing: Holy art Thou, O Lord.

*Theotokion:* Not even a heavenly mind can recount thy childbearing that surpasses understanding, O Maiden; for in thy womb, O pure one, thou didst conceive the Word of the primal Mind, Who framed all things by His Word.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꋋ́дрости):*

Having been enriched with the wisdom of the Word, thou didst pour forth living rivers of teachings, and didst water all the thought of the pious; and with the divine staff thou didst pasture the flock on the grass, and didst nourish it with divine understanding; therefore as a great shepherd and teacher, and champion of the faithful, we praise thee, O holy Cyril, crying: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

As a Virgin and alone among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead made its abode in thee, and as an infant thou didst suckle the Creator and Lord; therefore both the angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord we cry unto thee: Pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who worship in faith thy most holy childbearing.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the ewe-lamb exclaimed weeping, and bitterly sobbing cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain: have mercy, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing in faith of Thy divine passions.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thy virtue hath covered the heavens, and the earth is full of Thy glory, O Christ; therefore in faith do we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:* Thy heart having received the stream of transcendent wisdom, O venerable one, poured forth an abyss of teachings, drowning the minds of the impious.

*Troparion:* One power, one essence, one will of the tri-hypostatic Godhead didst thou proclaim, and the torrent of the delusion of polytheism, O Cyril, didst thou dry up.

*Troparion:* The filthy and most foolish mind of Manes,[^284] as one possessed of mind and divinely taught, O blessed one, having consumed with the splendors of thy words, thou didst manifestly refute.

*Theotokion:* As higher than all creations, O pure one, thou didst conceive the Creator of creation; and having borne Him beyond nature, Who renewed our nature.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as light, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparion:* With the staff of thy honorable wisdom, O hierarch, thou didst pasture thy flock upon the water of Orthodox worship, O blessed one.

*Troparion:* Thy word, illuminated by divine light, O Cyril, dispersed the mist of ignorance of the enlightened in Jerusalem.

*Theotokion:* The night of passions and the blackness of evils covers my soul, O all-pure one; do thou who didst bear the Giver of light illumine me, I pray.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out, praying in the sea-monster: From corruption deliver me, O Jesus, King of powers.

*Troparion:* As a river issuing from another Eden didst thou come forth, full of the life-flowing waters of the Spirit, and the church's furrows, O hierarch, thou dost water.

*Troparion:* With the beams of thy words, as from the tempest of the sea of impiety, thou dost save the people, O most sacred one, calling them unto the calm harbor of Orthodoxy.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst heavenify human nature that was grounded on earth, O all-immaculate one, and this that was corrupted thou didst remake; therefore, O Maiden, with unceasing voices we glorify thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 1:*

By thy tongue, O wise one, with divine inspirations, thou didst enlighten thy people to honor the Trinity, single and undivided in essence, yet divided in persons; therefore rejoicing, we celebrate thy all-holy memory, putting thee forward as an intercessor with God.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The three youths in Babylon, turning the tyrant's command into folly, cried in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:* Having the fire of the fear of God in thy mind, O father, thou didst reduce the substance of passions to ashes, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:* Having extinguished the flame of passions with tears, O blessed one, thou didst preserve the brightness of thy soul unharmed, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparion:* Having a living stream by the outpouring of the Spirit, unto thy soul, O godly-wise father, thou didst pour forth rivers of teachings, watering the Church that piously honors thee.

*Theotokion:* He hath sanctified thee, the Holy One Who borrowed flesh for Himself from thy flesh, O Birthgiver of God, the Lord God of our fathers Who lives in the saints.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

O almighty Deliverer of all, having descended, Thou didst bedew those who acted piously in the midst of the flame, and didst teach them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, sing ye the Lord.

*Troparion:* Having shaken off the slumber of negligence from the eyelids of thy soul, O blessed one, thou hast now fallen asleep with the sleep becoming to the righteous, and hast come unto the day of unwaning light.

*Troparion:* With the lightnings of thy words, thou didst consume the brushwood heresy, O ever-memorable one, and didst enlighten the faithful to honor the Trinity in unity of essence, depicted in three Persons.

*Troparion:* Having cleansed thyself by fasting, unto Him Who became dead for thy sake thou didst offer unbloody sacrifice, as a divine hierarch and as[^285] a minister of ineffable mysteries, O Cyril.

*Theotokion:* Having been delivered from the ancestral curse through thee, O pure Mother of God, blessed Sovereign Lady, we bless, O all-glorified Virgin, thy most holy childbearing, O all-pure one.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed through the disease of disobedience brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding of the fruit of thy womb, hast blossomed forth blessing unto the world; therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:* Desiring to behold the ineffable glory of Christ, O hierarch, thou didst strive to mortify the soul-corrupting passions, to make thy heart a dwelling-place of higher and better wisdom; therefore in faith we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:* As a star of many lights, Christ the Sun set thee upon the height of the Church, O Cyril, illuminating with the beams of sacred teachings the hearts of those who faithfully celebrate thy light-bearing memory, O venerable one.

*Troparion:* Thou wast a companion and fellow-minister of the angels; for having lived upon earth as one bodiless, O father, thou wast revealed as a partaker with the venerable, and an equal in station with the hierarchs; with them remember us this day.

*Theotokion:* Show my heart, which is barren of divine virtues, O most pure Birthgiver of God, to be fruitful, O Virgin who wast born of a barren woman by the will of Him Who transforms all things by His nod, that I may hymn thee, the all-praised one.

*March 19: Holy Martyrs Chrysanthus and Daria*[^286]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

*Sticheron 1:* Having received the illumination of divine knowledge, thou didst enlighten the eyes of thy heart, O most wise one, and didst sensibly drive away the darkness of delusion, and didst confess Christ, the Lord of all, Who assumed flesh; whence, being strengthened by the power of the Spirit, O Chrysanthus, thou wast shown to be above tortures, O all-praised one.

*Sticheron 2:* The weapons of delusion and the burning fires of sensual pleasure thou didst account as a spider's web; and having been placed in a dark prison, thou wast illuminated with divine radiance, and filled with spiritual fragrance while standing in the midst of the stench[^287] stench; and as a most excellent adorner of the bride, thou didst bring unto Christ an immaculate bride who had sought to defile thee.

*Sticheron 3:* Wounded with the sweetest love of the Creator, thou didst turn away completely from the delusion of godlessness, and didst enter within the divine bridal chamber, betrothed unto Christ through many bodily torments, O Daria of great renown, divine receptacle of the Spirit, adornment of martyrs, and beauty of virgins.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:* The weakness and palsy of my soul do thou transform into strength and power, O Virgin, all-immaculate Mother, that in fear and love I may perform and work the ordinances of Christ; so that I may escape the intolerable fire, and through thee receive heavenly inheritance and unending life, ever rejoicing.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:* Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Canon, whose acrostic is:*
*The golden-radiant flower of the martyrs do I honor. By Lord Joseph.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having traversed the Red Sea's deep with dry feet, ancient Israel of old, by the crosswise stretched hands of Moses, conquered Amalek's power in the wilderness.

*Troparion:* Adorned with a crown shining more brightly than gold, O martyr, and radiant with the beauties of martyrdom, thou dost stand before the Master, praying for us who honor thee, O worthy of honor.

*Troparion:* Thou wast wounded with the sweet desire of thy Creator, O martyr; and having despised the fair things of the world, thou didst give the whole strength of thy heart, O Chrysanthus, fervently unto Him Whom thou didst desire.

*Troparion:* Armed with faith, thou didst cast down the manifold-wily one who sought, O martyr, to entice thee by womanly pleasure; and thou becamest a pure vessel of purity, O Chrysanthus.

*Theotokion:* Inaccessible by divine nature, He appeared accessible unto me, having taken flesh from thee, O Virgin; Whom Daria desiring, patiently suffered, and was led unto Him as a bride.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and my refuge and my firm support.

*Troparion:* Like gold tested by the fire of torments wast thou, O Chrysanthus, bearing the image of the King's sufferings.

*Troparion:* Having abandoned the vain rhetoric of the rhetoricians, thou wast captured by the cords of the disciples of Him Who beforehand made thee wise, O Chrysanthus.

*Troparion:* Thou didst obey thy lover who betrothed thee unto Christ, O all-wise one, having abandoned a carnal lover for the sake of the honorable faith.

*Theotokion:* Jesus, Who was born in the flesh from thy womb, O pure Virgin, betroths unto Himself Daria as a pure bride and martyr.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*

Thou didst desire the most beautiful beauty, and didst surpass visible beauties; and by thy truly golden words, O blessed Chrysanthus, thou didst lead unto Christ the glorious Daria, who endured sufferings and put the tormentor to shame; with her remember all of us who celebrate your memory in faith.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* Incomprehensible and past finding out, O Sovereign Lady joyful in God, is the dread divine mystery wrought in thee; for having conceived the Uncircumscribed One, thou didst bear Him clothed in flesh from thy most pure blood; Whom do thou ever implore, O pure one, as thy Son, that our souls may be saved.

*Stavrotheotokion:* An ignominious death by crucifixion didst Thou willingly endure, O compassionate One; and she who bore Thee, O Christ, was wounded beholding Thee. Through her supplications, for the compassion of Thy mercy, O only all-good Lord and Lover of mankind, have compassion and save the world, O Thou Who takest away the sins of the world.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:* The flashing beams of the Spirit, entering thy pure heart, made thee wholly radiant, O martyr, as thou didst fervently cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:* With an incorruptible robe woven of divine goodness from on high did the Creator clothe thee, who didst preserve thy body blameless, O blessed one, and crowned thee as a victor.

*Troparion:* By nobility of heart thou didst shake off bodily pleasures, enjoying the incomparable divine beauty; and rejoicing[^288] thou didst accomplish the contest of thy martyrdom.

*Troparion:* Thou wast deified by participation in deifying union, O passion-bearer; and rejoicing, thou didst take up thine abode in the heavenly bridal chambers, as an immaculate bride of the Master of creation.

*Theotokion:* Having preserved thee as a Virgin after childbearing even as before childbearing, O all-pure one, the Incomprehensible One was incarnate of thee, bringing the martyr Daria unto Himself as a bride.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as light, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee in faith.

*Troparion:* Having thy mind cleansed from material things, O blessed one, thou didst destroy the malice of the enemy, O passion-bearer.

*Troparion:* Having become a most sacred lover of purity, thou didst transform the maiden's fiery love unto the Lord, O all-praised one.

*Troparion:* Beholding the tormentor cast down and trampled underfoot, with a joyful mind, O most blessed one, thou didst magnify the Master.

*Theotokion:* Who can tell thy mystery, O all-pure one? For beyond mind and word thou didst bear God the Word, two-fold in nature.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:* As thou didst hymn the Master of all, O ever-memorable one, light from heaven surrounded thee on all sides and illumined thee when shut up in the dark dungeon, O Chrysanthus.

*Troparion:* Rome, having acquired thee, who art glorified by honorable martyrdom, like a golden star, O martyr, is adorned with thy contests and thy divine wonders, O Chrysanthus.

*Troparion:* Having appeared fair, highly adorned, and most glorious unto the most beautiful Word, O virgin martyr Daria, thou wast heavenified through all kinds of bodily pains.

*Theotokion:* As a young child, the Virgin bore Thee, the pre-eternal Word, Whom maidens desiring, were led as brides after Thee through her, O King of all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* In the Persian furnace, the Abrahamic children, burned with love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Troparion:* A golden-appearing flower of the martyrs wast thou, and an assembly[^289] of sufferers thou didst lead unto Christ by thy divine words and wonders, O martyr acceptable to God; with them we faithfully call thee blessed now.

*Troparion:* When thou wast shut up naked in a foul brothel, O blessed one, Jesus illumined thee with divine fragrances and light-bearing glory; Whom with a pure mind, O all-wise one, thou didst love.

*Troparion:* Christ sent a wild beast to be a guardian of thy virginity,[^290] manifestly driving away the harm of the impious, as thou didst sing, O all-praised one: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord.

*Theotokion:* By divine essence the Son is uncircumscribed, but in thy womb, O Virgin, He became circumscribed in flesh for our sake; blessed art Thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girt with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* With a firm mind, O blessed one, opposing the vainly-wise, thou didst endure bodily tearings; and being burned with torches, thou didst remain unconsumed, singing with the youths, O Chrysanthus: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Adorned with Christ's wisdom, thou didst put to shame the insolence of the foolish, and didst confound the mind of the deceiver who seduced Eve; having piously taken captive his weapon, O wise one, thou didst bring unto God a bride chosen for the sake of faith.

*Troparion:* By unity of soul ye escaped carnal union; ye were shown to be pure vessels of the Almighty, and were brought into the heavenly temple, O sufferers, crying unto Christ: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Restraining carnal passions with divine dew, Chrysanthus and Daria reduced the fire of torments to ashes with the dew of the Spirit; and they attained crowns of life, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:* Like a rose in the midst of worldly thorns, O most pure one, God found thee unspotted, and made His abode in thy pure womb, and filled the world with mystical fragrance, as it cries: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who hath joined together the separated natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:* O sacred[^291] heralds of divine things, a divine army joined itself unto thee, having been delivered from vile delusion; and by the sword they were offered as blameless sacrifices, O martyrs, unto Him Who was sacrificed like a sheep for our sake.

*Troparion:* Ye appeared in the midst of wild beasts like lambs, O passion-bearers, preaching the divine incarnation of Him Who poured Himself out even unto flesh; and through sufferings ye divinely inherited a painless end.

*Troparion:* Today every city and country brightly celebrates, the city of Rome calling together, setting forth your divine sufferings and struggles as an immaterial feast, O holy ones.

*Troparion:* Ye were taken up unto immeasurable glory, O martyr Chrysanthus and Daria; and crowned together, ye stand before the almighty Word, praying for us who ever call you blessed.

*Theotokion:* Mind and heart tremble, contemplating thy childbearing that passes understanding, O Maiden; for thou didst bear in thy womb God the Word, Who delivers through thee from every distress those who honor thee.

*March 20: Venerable Fathers Slain at the Monastery of St. Sabbas*[^292]

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron 1:* Cleaving to the utmost of desires unto the end, O venerable ones, ye accounted all worldly beauties as refuse; and by vigil and prayers, by rain and scorching heat, ye acquired the things that abide alone; having dwelt together in unity, by grace ye were shown to be fellow-citizens with the angels.

*Sticheron 2:* Beaten with cudgels, pelted with stones, and cut to pieces with weapons, ye did not break the unity of mind, O martyrs, bound by love and brotherly desire; but being put to death together and cut limb from limb, O passion-bearers, ye were laid upon the divine table as blameless sacrifices.

*Sticheron 3:* Exhausted by fire and suffocated by strangling, ye surrendered your souls as blameless sacrifices, O glorious martyrs, together into His hands; and ye were joined to the ranks of the bodiless powers, and inherited everlasting glory[^293] which do ye unceasingly pray that we who praise you may partake of.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:* Choice repentance and saving medicine, O thou who didst bear God my Savior, and streams of tears, and meditation upon the dread and fearful hour and unbribable judgment, do thou grant me to escape through thy prayers, O Virgin, from trembling torment, and to obtain divine grace.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:* Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, the ewe-lamb and Thy Mother marveled, and cried aloud: What vision is this, O longed-for Son? Hath the disobedient and lawless assembly rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many wonders? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon, whose acrostic is:*
*In hymns I glorify the single-minded martyrs. The work of Lord Stephen of St. Sabbas.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

A hymn let us send up, O ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: We sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparion:* When I desire to praise in hymns the divinely-crowned multitude of Thy sacred martyrs, O Christ, grant me from heaven the light of understanding by their supplications, that I may sing a divinely-proclaimed song.

*Troparion:* Ye cast away the filth of flesh and spirit, O exceedingly rich ones, having escaped the deadly pleasures of the world; and having lived a life equal to the angels, ye passed over unto the highest habitation.

*Troparion:* Having manifestly guided your members and senses by the law of the Spirit, ye showed yourselves to be sacred temples of God, O God-bearing martyrs, for Christ took up His abode in you.

*Troparion:* Having left corruptible wealth upon the earth, O holy ones, and having despised the fair things of life as a dream, having well-pleased Christ, ye have now received an unshakable kingdom.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast shown to be the spiritual ladder which Jacob, the chosen of God, beheld, through which by an ineffable word the Bodiless One came forth, having become flesh without change, beyond word, from thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Lord, Creator of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, firm support of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:* Having consecrated your whole life, O martyrs, blamelessly in truth unto all-seeing God, ye offered yourselves as divine holocausts[^294] and received perfect crowns.

*Troparion:* As true disciples of God-bearing Sabbas, having desired departure without delay, walking irreversibly along the sorrowful path, ye attained unto unending life.

*Troparion:* Having cultivated the seed, the word sown within you, O venerable ones, and having watered it abundantly with streams of tears, ye brought forth fruit, offering unto God a hundredfold ear of grain.

*Theotokion:* Having dwelt bodily in the Virgin, O Lord, Thou didst appear unto men as it was fitting to behold Thee; and her Thou didst reveal as true Birthgiver of God and help of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́иннѡ):*[^295]

By self-control having mortified the fiery appearances and motions of passions, O hermits acceptable to God, following the footsteps of the only God Who created all things, as an acceptable sacrifice, living and consecrated, ye were offered by the slaughter of barbarian hands, being beaten, butchered, and violently cut down.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* O thou joyful in God, pure and blessed one, for the tender mercy of the compassion of Him Who was born of thee, with the heavenly hosts and archangels and with all the bodiless ones, pray unceasingly for us, that He may grant us before the end pardon and cleansing of sins, and amendment of life, so that we may find mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:* Ever protected, O Virgin, by the Cross of thy Son and God, we conquer demonic assaults and wiles, truly hymning thee as the true Birthgiver of God; and in love all generations call thee blessed, O all-pure one, even as thou didst foretell; therefore grant remission of our transgressions through thy prayers.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou didst mount upon horses, even Thine apostles, O Lord, and didst take their reins into Thy hands; and Thy riding became salvation unto those who sing in faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Beholding the invincible martyrs pining away in the waterless wilderness, yet adorned with various virtues, the enemy was fiercely inflamed, covered with envy, and prepared himself for battle.

*Troparion:* Having rooted deceit in his disciples, the crafty serpent stirred up the barbarian tribes unto mutual slaughter, by whom he also sought to drive the venerable ones out of the wilderness; fiercely[^296] fiercely seizing those roundabout, even as he desired; yet he was not able to drive away these invincible, righteous combatants; and being vanquished invisibly, he wretchedly writhed in scheming.

*Troparion:* Full of poison and frenzy, the originator of evil rushed forward with armed men, and like a wild beast attacked the blessed ones, and by intolerable wounds poured forth streams of blood.

*Troparion:* They who fixed their hopes upon earthly things demanded fleeting substance, the treasures of corruptible gold; but the noble ones, having placed their hope upon God, cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Like wild beasts they mercilessly struck the venerable ones with cudgels and swords and stones, commanding them to tell who their elders were; but they all remained confirmed in the law of love.

*Theotokion:* Mother of God, thou art the adornment of the apostles, the firm support of the holy martyrs, and the boast and salvation of the venerable, who faithfully and sweetly sing: Glory to Thy power!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray.

*Troparion:* Having learned the bond of perfection, love, O Savior, and being instructed by Thee, the venerable ones laid down their souls for their friends, imitating Thy voluntary passions that surpass the comprehension of men.

*Troparion:* Beyond nature, O Christ, Who wast willingly born in the flesh, by Thy command they were shown to be stronger than death; for having conformed themselves to Thy law, the godly-wise zealously strove to die praise-worthily for their friends.

*Troparion:* Having sprinkled their souls with the spiritual hyssop of baptism, ye were straightway sprinkled with your own blood, O venerable ones; and tested like gold in the surging of the fire, ye became an acceptable and sweet-smelling sacrifice unto God.

*Theotokion:* Having acquired maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, do not despise, we pray, the kindred care for us; for we Christians put thee forward alone unto the Master as a merciful propitiation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Unto the Lord Who is able to save me, out of the deep of sins I cried, and from corruption He brought up my life, for He is the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:* Of the threats[^297] of the barbarians the favorites of Christ feared not; for by unceasing contemplation they set their life before death.

*Troparion:* Thinking to grow rich, the impious impoverishers unjustly tormented them, who had cast away corruptible things, and acquired the incorruptible.

*Troparion:* Clothed with invincible power, the martyrs of Christ conquered the spiritual deceivers and the insolence of the barbarians.

*Theotokion:* As upon a light cloud the reigning Lord came unto thee, O all-immaculate one, to cast down the handmade idols of Egypt.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*

Despising the enjoyment of earthly and corruptible things, O most blessed ones, ye manifestly chose the wilderness life, and hated the fair things of the world and temporary food; whence ye were accounted worthy of the kingdom of heaven, rejoicing with the choirs of martyrs and fasters; therefore honoring your memory, we diligently cry aloud unto you: Deliver us from perils, O fathers!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Having quenched the all-destructive power of the Chaldean furnace by the figure of the angel who descended, the youths cried unto the Creator: Blessed art Thou and praised, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* The battles of the opposing hosts, visible and invisible, grew faint together, beholding the martyrs in wounds faithfully hymning: Blessed art Thou and praised, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Betrothed unto Thee, O Christ, and wounded with desire for Thee, they loved the only One Who is; having cast away family and fatherland and wealth, they fled unto Thee, O Deliverer, God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* The foul murder of the barbarians did not turn Thy lovers away, O Christ, from wilderness worship of God; for they feared not those who kill the body, keeping Thy commandments as an unforgotten renewal.

*Troparion:* Having turned their desire from the world unto Thee, O Christ, and armed their wrath only against sin, having embraced all-wise thought, Thy favorites well-pleased Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:* Having received from God the divinely-written law within the tablets of their hearts, O blessed ones, ye martyrs proclaim the one God in Trinity, crying unto Him: Blessed art Thou and praised, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* Ye who walk in the light of the Lord, come, let us hymn the divine gate of the King of kings, Mary[^298] the pure Mother, truly the Birthgiver of God, as the hope of our souls.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* While the instruments of impiety sounded another song, the God-proclaimed harps of piety sang: Bless the works of the Lord, Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:* Emulating the heavenly ranks, equal to the angels, in all-night standings during vigils they sang: Bless all the works of the Lord, Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:* Taking up the Cross, whole-souled the martyrs followed Thee, O Master, singing: Bless all the works of the Lord, Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:* Having zealously purified body and soul in the Spirit, ye offered yourselves as whole-burnt offerings unto God: scorched in the fire, O fathers, exalting Christ unto all the ages.

*Triadicon:* Let those who blaspheme Thee as being in two hypostases, the Indivisible One, be put to shame, as honoring a quaternity; for we worship Thee, the One of the divine Trinity.[^299]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* At this the heaven was appalled, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the ranks of angels and of men magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:* Variously the hermits stripped themselves, and having overcome the wiles of the ruler of this world, manfully crushed the barbarian malice like sufferers, having endured their assaults. Therefore also they received double crowns from the righteous Arbiter of the contest.

*Troparion:* Living an ascetic life in dens and caves, O blessed ones, ye were shut up in the dark abyss of a narrow cave, stained with blood, and by the burning of fire, choked bitterly by vapor mingled with smoke, ye were revealed as victorious ones.

*Troparion:* We sing of the superhuman struggles through which ye suffered against sin, standing against it youthfully as glorious soldiers of Christ; for ye conquered gloriously. And now standing with the ranks of the saints in the highest, be mindful of us.

*Troparion:* Together with your father and guide, as sincere children, O blessed ones, robed in heaven with the radiance of the Three-hypostatic light: pray for us, your fellow disciples and singers, that common peace be granted to the churches, and salvation to our souls.

*Theotokion:* As the cause of heavenly life, and for us who had wretchedly fallen into death, having become the Mother of the Deliverer, thou hast recalled us thou hast recalled us, O all-pure one; and hast accounted us worthy to run again toward our primal fatherland. Therefore, O Mother of God, we unceasingly magnify thee.

*March 21: Our Venerable Father and Confessor James the Bishop.*

*At Vespers: Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

Desiring the everlasting and never-passing good, thou didst despise passing and perishing glory; and taking up the cross, thou didst follow Him Who was crucified, and didst subdue the flesh by ascetic labors. For this cause, we celebrate thy memory, O James.

Crucified unto the world and the passions, O James, thou didst keep thy soul undefiled and unblemished, unmingled with earthly delineations of vanity, and winged unto heavenly things by mystical visions. Therein thou didst place beforehand thy life, O venerable one, as a sincere friend of Christ, as a most faithful favorite.

Guileless, chaste, merciful, and a lover of the brethren, humble-minded and meek, filled with love, adorned with almsgiving, patient in endurance, adorned with faith and hope, thou wast shown forth, O ever-memorable one, embellished by prayers, and sacredly illumined with divine splendors.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* Wash away the filth of my passionate heart, O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God, and all its wounds and festerings that come from sin, purify, O pure one, and still the instability of my mind: so that I, thy wretched and unprofitable servant, may magnify thy power and great protection.

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Christ, the Lover of mankind, crucified, and His side pierced with a spear, the all-pure one wept and cried aloud: What is this, O my Son? What have the ungrateful people rendered unto Thee for the good things Thou didst do for them? And dost Thou hasten to leave me childless, O most beloved One? I marvel, O compassionate One, at Thy voluntary crucifixion!

*Canon of Ignatius, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Come, O ye peoples Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He brought out of the bondage of Egypt, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:* Having received into thy soul the Trinitarian enlightenment, O James, thou didst forsake the dark delusion of the world, and didst obtain otherworldly glory.

*Troparion:* Taking the Cross upon thy shoulder, O venerable one, thou didst follow Him Who was crucified; and having lived the monastic life in all-wisdom, by self-mastery thou didst wither away the passions, O father.

*Troparion:* A vessel capable of the gifts of the Spirit wast thou from thine early youth,[^300] and a citizen and heir of Sion on high, O blessed James.

*Theotokion:* Alone thou didst bear, O Maiden, God Who is uncontainable by all, Who for His goodness became contained in thee: Whom do thou beseech to save those who hymn thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who hymn Thee.

*Troparion:* Fair was thy life, thy speech seasoned with the salt of love, O divinely-wise one, thy heart full of tender compunction: for this cause we bless thee in faith.

*Troparion:* Venerating the divine icon of Christ Who appeared in the flesh for our sake, thou didst endure persecutions, O father, and perils and afflictions, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:* As a pure sacrifice thou didst offer thyself unto the Lord by putting sin to death; and as a lawful hierarch thou didst offer unto Him the bloodless sacrifices.

*Theotokion:* As a truly golden censer, and the jar of manna, and the divine mountain, and the most fair palace of God, do we name thee, O Virgin.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily go before" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):* Having forsaken the world, thou didst follow Christ, and manifestly subdue the flesh by self-mastery, O most blessed James; whence thou didst receive the holy anointing of priesthood. And now thou art translated unto the heavenly choirs, praying for all of us who praise thee.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* Alone having given birth unto the Creator of all, alone having adorned humanity by thy childbirth, O Bride of God: deliver me from the snare of the deceitful enemy; establish me upon the rock of the desires of Christ, diligently praying unto Him Whom thou didst enflesh.

*Stavrotheotokion:* When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word O Word of God, weeping maternally she said: What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death? Willing to give life unto the dead, as Thou art compassionate!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, the report of Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:* By all-night supplications having put to death the pleasures of the flesh, thou wast revealed as a heavenly lamp of dispassion, O venerable one.

*Troparion:* Ever washed by streams of tears, O all-blessed one, thou wast shown forth as a pure dwelling of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:* Humble and meek, compassionate and guileless, and chaste hierarch wast thou, O venerable one.

*Theotokion:* O unwedded Sovereign Lady, who didst conceive God in thy womb, deliver all from perils and sorrows.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments; for we know no other God besides Thee.

*Troparion:* Truly, O venerable one, thou didst supernaturally abhor desire for glory that is exalted among men, O father; and thou becamest a lamp shining with the splendor of humility.

*Troparion:* By much self-mastery and diligent vigilance, O venerable one, in prayer and hardship thou didst seek God, Who hath translated Thee unto the mansions on high.

*Troparion:* Keeping thyself free from worldly cares, thou didst stand in all-night prayers, receiving the divine rays with the eye of the soul, O divinely-wise father, hierarch James.

*Theotokion:* O all-pure one, who didst ineffably give birth unto Him Who in the flesh was wounded upon the Cross, as thou art compassionate, heal my heart wounded by the assaults of the crafty one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Whirling about in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy lovingkindness: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:* The streams of thy tears, O venerable one, dried up the sea of cruel passions, and obtained consolation in the world beyond for thy precious soul.

*Troparion:* Guileless, meek, chaste, venerable, gentle, humble-minded and tranquil, and filled with enlightenment wast thou, O all-glorious father.

*Troparion:* Together with the divine shepherds thy memory is glorified, O father, as of a good shepherd, O wise pastor James; for as a venerable one thou didst tend the flock.

*Theotokion:* He Who made all things by His will He Who made all things by His will, having willed, dwelt in an unwedded womb: honoring with incorruption those who were diseased with corruption, as He is merciful.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree; cast into the midst of the fire, bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Many persecutions and unremitting trials didst thou endure, conquering the iconoclasts and shining with Orthodoxy, enlightening all who behold thee, and crying, O James: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Pondering the dread coming of Christ, in tender compunction thou didst finish thy whole life, until thou didst find, O blessed one, perfect purification of offenses, and eternal consolation, true enlightenment, and ineffable glory.

*Troparion:* Thou wast revealed as a lamb in the meekness of thy soul unto Christ the Shepherd; and guided by Him, thou wast known in deed as a pastor of rational sheep, O father, singing ever with them: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* O glorious, unwedded, pure, and blessed one, the correction of the falling, the deliverance of those who sin: save me, a prodigal, save me as I cry unto thy Son: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Unto Him Who descended into the fiery furnace to the Hebrew youths, and changed the flame into dew, sing unto God as the Lord, O all ye works, and exalt Him unto all the ages!

*Troparion:* Thy most pure mind, enlightened with divine purity, wisely turned away from demonic darkness, the gloom of the passions, O venerable one, crying: Sing, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him unto all the ages!

*Troparion:* The burning arrows of the enemy didst thou extinguish, O wise one, with downpours of unceasing tears; and inflamed with the love of Christ, O all-blessed one, thou didst burn up the frenzied thorns of material pleasures.

*Troparion:* By thy prayers preserve from assaults and perils this flock, O all-wise one, which thou didst gather by thy teachings, and which in faith celebrates thy sacred, precious, and light-filled divine memory.

*Theotokion:* Being a living fountain, who didst give birth unto the Water of life, give drink, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unto my soul give drink, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, unto my soul melted by the flame of sin: that I may glorify thee unto all the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption through eating, and for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, let us the faithful magnify with songs in one accord.

*Troparion:* After many labors and countless afflictions, after toils and sorrows truly, thou didst rest in the haven, bearing the heavenly wealth of divine virtues, O most sacred father, embellishment of venerable ascetics.

*Troparion:* A lamp of discernment, a throne of feeling, guileless, meek, merciful, and full of tender compunction wast thou, weeping and washing thy bed,[^301] as David said. Together with him, be mindful of all who honor thee in faith.

*Troparion:* As a daystar, as a temple of the Spirit, as a blossom of mystical fragrance, being filled with joy,[^302] as a high-branched tree, as a partaker of eternal glory, and as a wise hierarch do we honor thee, O James.

*Troparion:* The streams of tears, truly sent down abundantly into the soil of the heart, grew a ripe ear of grain, nourishing the souls of those who ever honor thee with faith, O divinely-wise hierarch, our father James.

*Theotokion:* Spare me, O Lord, spare me when Thou art about to judge me, and condemn me not to the fire, nor rebuke me in Thy wrath: the Virgin who bare Thee, Christ, the multitudes of angels, and the martyrs with the venerable ones beseech Thee.

*March 22: Holy Hieromartyr Basil, Presbyter of the Church of Ancyra.*

*At Vespers: Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*

Adorned with the vesture of the priesthood, thou didst serve God like an angel, becoming a priest unto Him Who appeared for our sake in material flesh, O renowned one. Whence, as a perfect sheep thou wast slaughtered, and becamest a pure sacrifice; and now thou hast offered thyself upon the altar on high. Therefore with joyful voices we ever bless thee we ever bless thee, crying aloud unto thee: Cease not to pray that our souls may be saved.

*Sticheron 2:* Having thy skin torn by unjust judgment, thou didst endure afflictions, O glorious one, looking toward the painless end and toward the honors prepared for those who have suffered; sharpened by fire like iron, O all-blessed one, thou wast an unyielding sword, cutting down all the regiments of the enemy. Therefore all the tongues of the pious honor thee today with rejoicing, O valiant-minded sufferer, Basil worthy of glory.

*Sticheron 3:* Adorned with divine wounds, bound thou didst run from city to city, binding the tormentor and directing the journey of thy steps; and entering straightway into the city of Caesarea, therein thou didst receive the blessed end; and as a victor crowned thou didst ascend unto the heavenly city, and now thou standest before God the King: Whom do thou beseech to save and enlighten our souls.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:* Wretchedly have I descended into the depths of many fallings, wretched that I am through my despondency and slothfulness; and now am I held fast by mistrust and despair. Be thou my help and cleansing and salvation, O all-pure one, most zealously granting me consolation. Unto thee do I pray, and make myself dear unto thee; and falling down I cry in faith unto thee: Let me not become utterly a rejoicing unto the deceiver.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:* Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding hanging upon the Tree Thy Son and God, Who uncontainably hung the earth upon the waters and created the whole creation; for I shall arise and be glorified, and I shall crush the realms of Hades by My might, and shall destroy his power, and as I am compassionate I shall deliver the prisoners from his malice, and as the Lover of mankind I shall bring them unto My Father.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: I praise thy pains, O great martyr. By Joseph.*
*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:* Loving strongly the glory of Thy kingdom, O King of all, the glorious Basil suffered upon earth; therefore by which do Thou account us worthy of the heavenly kingdom.

*Troparion:* Thy praiseworthy life and pure conversation, O glorious one, the path of thy struggles, showed thee to be an heir of God, and made thee a citizen of heaven.

*Troparion:* Being obedient unto the Lord by a good life, O all-wise one, thou didst make the enemy subject, and with thy feet thou didst tread brightly upon him, warring against him, O martyr Basil.

*Troparion:* By the streams of thy blood thou didst truly drown the noetic Pharaoh with all his army; and thou didst reach the land, O wise one, where the cloud of sufferers ever rejoices.

*Theotokion:* Being the Only-begotten of the Father, having willed, He dwelt in thy most pure womb, and became man, O pure one, willing in His goodness to save mankind.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion:* Thou wast lifted up like a lamb upon the tree, O all-blessed one, enduring scrapings with a steadfast mind, and casting down the loftiness of the enemy by the power of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:* Through the beauty of thy wounds, O martyr, thou didst appear most fair in soul and body, sending up voices of thanksgiving with a pure mind unto Thine Arbiter of the contest.

*Troparion:* Seeking the great calling with high-minded conduct, thou didst endure great struggles, O wise one; and him who ever greatly lifted himself up, thou didst abase, O martyr.

*Troparion:* Having been a guardian of the divine commandments, O divinely-wise one, when shut up in prison thou didst open unto the faithful the path of instruction, leading them into the breadth of understanding.

*Theotokion:* Discerning thy conception from afar by the Spirit, O Maiden, Isaiah cried out in awe: Behold, a virgin shall conceive in the womb![^303]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Боже́ственныя вѣ́ры):* Thou wast the beauty of the Church, the bulwark of piety, and the destroyer of impiety, O Basil. Having endured torments as though bodiless, thou didst join the incorporeal hosts, rejoicing, O glorious martyr. Beseech Christ God to grant our souls great mercy.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* He was not separated from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb; but God became incarnate and remained what He was, Who after thy childbirth preserved thee, His Virgin Mother, all-immaculate, even as before childbirth, the only Lord: Him do thou diligently beseech to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:* The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross Him Who had blossomed forth from her without travail, weeping maternally as was meet, cried aloud cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer voluntarily, willing to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner becoming God, crying aloud from a pure understanding, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparion:* Seeing thy divine countenance radiant with spiritual splendor, the wicked tormentor marveled, O martyr; and as a servant of darkness, he senselessly grew foolish.

*Troparion:* Adorned with the higher wisdom, by divine power thou didst confound the Hellenic sages; and having suffered patiently, thou didst receive the crown of victory.

*Troparion:* Stretched out and dislocated on every side, the Word Who was stretched out upon the Cross strengthened thee, O Basil, granting dispassion unto the faithful through His passion.

*Troparion:* I sacrifice not to demons, neither do I fear death nor the sight of torments! cried the sacred Basil: I confess the one God known in the Trinity!

*Theotokion:* I hymn thee, O Sovereign Lady much-hymned, I bless thee, O Virgin, through whom all we men have been blessed, and have been truly delivered from the ancient curse.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* With Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love the souls of those who rise early unto Thee, I pray, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them out from the darkness of sin.

*Troparion:* Bound with the weight of iron, having chosen to bear the lightest yoke of the Lord, thou didst humble the proud enemy who raged fiercely against his Creator, O martyr.

*Troparion:* Bound joyfully with iron fetters, O martyr, thou didst pass from place to place: being adorned with these like necklaces, O Basil, and beholding thine Arbiter of the contest.

*Troparion:* With streams of blood thou didst truly dye thyself, O martyr, like a vesture, O glorious one; and surrounded with the crown of victory, rejoicing in twofold manner unto Christ, O Basil.

*Troparion:* Being strengthened by the grace of the Cross, thou didst endure a long and grievous journey, wretchedly driven, singing: In the path of Thy testimonies I have walked, rejoicing and being glad, O Word.[^304]

*Theotokion:* Higher than the Cherubim, who becamest the heavens, better than creation, the rampart of the wise martyrs, let us honor the only Birthgiver of God with songs let us honor with songs.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, I run to Thy calm haven and cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion:* Thou wast exalted by thy sufferings, and didst cast down the enemy, O glorious great-martyr; and thou hast departed unto God, crowned with the victor's wreath at the end of thy struggles, O praiseworthy one.

*Troparion:* Emulating Him Who stretched out His hands upon the Cross for thy sake, O wise martyr, being lifted up upon the tree, thou didst patiently endure the scrapings, wounding the deceiver by thy wounds.

*Troparion:* Bound for Christ's sake, O Basil, thou didst bind the bodiless enemies; and loosed from the flesh, O glorious one, thou didst utterly destroy their malice by divine grace.

*Theotokion:* I hymn thee, O all-hymned one, whom the hosts of the heavenly ranks hymn; and I pray thee: Heal the passions of my soul, O pure one, and deliver me from eternal fire.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):* Having lawfully finished the course, and having kept the faith, O hieromartyr Basil, therefore thou wast accounted worthy of crowns of martyrdom, and wast shown forth as an unshakable pillar of the Church, confessing the Son to be co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, the indivisible Trinity: Whom do thou beseech to deliver from perils those who honor thee, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O divinely-wise Basil!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The angel made the furnace bedewing for the venerable youths, but the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Shining with the illumination of divine grace, thou didst pass through the darkness of torments, becoming a partaker of the never-setting day, O wise one, and crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Thy heart, O wise one, fixed upon the rock of divine understanding, was not shaken by the winds of temptations, O sufferer, as thou didst cry unto the Deliverer of all: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Raging in madness, the tormentor commanded that thou be cut into thongs,[^305] O glorious one; and seeing thee endure as if bodiless, he in no wise understood to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* As gold in the furnace of torments, thou didst shine brightly with wounds, and an expulsion of passions having become [an expeller of evil passions], thou didst cry, O wise Basil: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* Knowing virginity to have remained incorrupt after childbirth, O Virgin, unto Him Who was born of thee beyond understanding, thou didst cry to the Savior and God: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew upon the venerable ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water;[^306] for Thou dost work all things, O Christ, solely by willing: Thee do we exalt unto all the ages!

*Troparion:* Being wholly stretched out toward God, thou didst count bodily afflictions as a dream; whence unto the blessed and painless life, O martyr passion-bearer, rejoicing thou hast passed over.

*Troparion:* Being wounded with the love of the Almighty, thou didst stoutly endure the tearing of thy flesh; and the foe mighty in malice thou dost drown in the streams of thy blood, O most blessed Basil.

*Troparion:* Churchly adornment of the Church of Christ, representation of the Savior's passions, steadfast martyr, confirmation of the faithful: let Basil the Great[^307] be praised in hymns!

*Troparion:* Otherworldly glory and never-waning light, the heavenly Kingdom, an unfading crown, a sorrowless life, and ineffable joy, O martyr, after thy contest thou wast accounted worthy to find.

*Theotokion:* The human race was accounted worthy of salvation through thee, O all-pure one, full of divine grace; for thou alone didst bear for us the Savior, Whom we exalt unto all the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate appeared unto men: Him magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:* Having been loosed from the flesh, thou wast accounted worthy to see the fair splendors of the saints, O most rich one; and thou hast been united with the angelic hosts, singing unceasingly with them: Holy, Holy, Holy, all-working and all-powerful Trinity!

*Troparion:* Seen as a light, O most blessed one, as a great sun in the heaven of Christ's Church, by the light-beaming rays of thy suffering and the divine splendors of thy miracles, thou dost illumine the souls of all who praise thee.

*Troparion:* Thou thyself with the beauties of sufferings, O all-glorious, God-wise passion-bearer, hast adorned thyself, and thou dost stand before the fair Christ, now encompassed with a crown of righteousness encompassed; therefore we believers honor thee, rejoicing.

*Troparion:* The earth was sanctified by the divine burial of thy sacred and divine body, O Basil; the spirits of the righteous leaped for joy, having thy spirit in their midst, O blessed one: with them remember all of us who commemorate thee.

*Theotokion:* Dread is the manner of thy childbirth; for God was born, having become incarnate, O Virgin: Him do thou entreat to deliver us all from dread torments, who honor thee with unhesitating faith, O pure and unwedded Mother.

*March 23: The Holy Venerable-martyr Nicon and his two hundred disciples who suffered with him.*[^308]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я́кѡ до́бля):*

Bodily pleasures and the insubordination of the flesh thou didst overcome with the bridle, O blessed one, through the grace of abstinence; having conquered like a valiant warrior the boastfulness of the enemy by the power of the Spirit: whence thou becamest a guide of monastics, who ever follow thy all-wise and divine preaching, O Nicon.

Having shone forth like the sun from the East, O all-glorious one, thou didst enlighten the whole universe with the brightness of miracles, bringing the choir of thy disciples like bright stars: with them thou didst suffer stoutly, having been beheaded by swords; and having placed your souls into the hands of the Master Sovereign...

...having placed your souls: neither famine, nor peril, nor nakedness, nor wounds, nor indeed the most violent death, could separate you from the love of God, O right marvelous ones;[^309] but as lambs to the slaughter, following the Shepherd, from the West unto the never-waning rest of the Heavenly Kingdom ye have attained, O crown-bearing passion-bearers.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same Tone and Podoben:* Deliver my humble soul from condemnation and grievous transgressions, O most holy Bride of God, and rescue me from death by thy prayers; and grant that I may obtain justification on the day of trial which the assemblies of the saints have obtained; show me purified by repentance and outpourings of tears before the end.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same Podoben:* When the ewe-lamb and Thy Mother beheld Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Lord, she marveled and cried: What is this sight, O most desired Son? Hath the faithless and lawless assembly rendered this unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing of thee who art named for victory, O blessed one. Joseph."[^310] Tone 4.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:* Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unmoistened feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-forming hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:* Having conquered the craftsman of malice by ascetic wrestlings, thou wast crowned with the beauties of suffering: and now thou standest before God, praying for us who honor thee, O venerable Nicon.

*Troparion:* Strengthened by the power of Him Who confirmed our weaknesses, thou wast able and didst act valiantly of old in battles, O blessed one; and having become a victor, thou didst run unto Christ's baptism.

*Troparion:* The most blessed company of thy disciples is adorned with martyrdom, having first been enlightened, O wise one, by fastings; and by twofold wrestlings it hath woven twofold crowns.

*Theotokion:* The door of Thy descent unto us, O Lord, supernaturally brought forth the Incomprehensible One, twofold in natures, Who art mystically called the Orient and the Sun and the Light.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Thy Church makes glad in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!

*Troparion:* Girded with spiritual warfare, O martyr of Christ, thou didst arm a divine army for the contests of suffering.

*Troparion:* Having first inflamed the flesh ascetically, O all-glorious one, thou didst hasten by the wounds of suffering to conquer the demonic hosts.

*Troparion:* Having come like a star from the East, O Nicon, thou didst reach unto the West; therefore having died thou didst set, and hast shone forth unto Christ.

*Theotokion:* The company of martyrs represented Thy slaughter, O Master, having known Thee Who didst bud forth from the unwedded Maiden.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben:*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Having lived venerably, O Nicon, at first, thou didst gather a choir of those obedient unto God, who piously ever followed thee; having shone forth from the East unto the West, like a light-bearing sun with the rays of sufferings, having diminished delusion by the gore of your blood.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the transcendent God: unceasingly entreat Him with the bodiless hosts, that remission of transgressions and amendment of life be granted unto us before the end, who sing of thee with faith and love as is due, O only all-hymned one.

*Stavrotheotokion:* When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, weeping maternally she spoke: What new and strange wonder is this, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to make alive the dead, as Thou art compassionate?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Delighting in divine outpourings of light, unharmed thou didst pass through the torrent of torments, O right marvelous Nicon; and the crown of victory, O martyr, thou didst worthily receive.

*Troparion:* Beauteously fashioning with splendors of virtues, thou didst strive as an ascetic; having first put to death the bodily passions, and secondly having conquered by suffering the demonic hosts.

*Troparion:* Ye arrayed yourselves with the weapon of piety against the enemy, O God-wise ones, and by glorious death ye received immortality, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Theotokion:* Though thou wast created in nature, the Creator, having loved thee, O Virgin Mother, showed thee forth as Mother unto Himself; whom now the choirs of sufferers most splendidly entreat.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as a light, a holy light, turning from the gloom of ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith.

*Troparion:* Dutifully obeying the divine instructions of thy mother,[^311] thou hadst mighty strength in battles, O Nicon, learning what is best.

*Troparion:* Thy life, illumined by the light of virtues, possessed suffering as a most beauteous adornment, O God-wise and all-honorable one.

*Troparion:* Obeying the divine-originating laws, the martyrs acted valiantly, and against the lawless by divine help, manfully conquered.

*Theotokion:* The timeless Light, the Father's Radiance, thou didst bear unto us, O all-immaculate one, Who became subject to time through the wealth of goodness.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed of the demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:* From the East thou didst press forward like a radiant sun, O God-wise one, bringing the choir of thy disciples like stars; and ye enlightened the West with your rays.

*Troparion:* By the drops of thy blood, O martyr, thou didst drown the sea of polytheism; and by the depth of thy contests thou didst choke the fierce inventor of malice and the all-crafty one.

*Troparion:* As a lily ye blossomed forth in the verdant meadow of suffering, and as a sweet-breathing rose ye perfume our hearts with the beauties of martyrdom, O God-inspired passion-bearers.

*Theotokion:* Knowing thee to be the temple of God and an ensouled palace, O Sovereign Lady, the choir of passion-bearers entreats thee, who alone didst take away the reproach of women.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burned rather by the love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Being tested by wounds more than gold, O God-wise one, thou wast shown forth as a pillar of virtues, driving away demonic assaults, crying: Blessed art Thou, my God and Lord of our fathers!

*Troparion:* After thy death, thy cast-out body, O martyr, mystically emitting divine splendors of healings, drives away demonic harm, by divine power shown forth by Him Who made thee manifest.

*Troparion:* Being wholly delighted with divine gestures, O all-blessed one, thou didst patiently surrender thy body to wounds; and as a crown-bearer thou dost stand before the Almighty in the heavens, as thou didst desire, O venerable one.

*Troparion:* Being a shepherd of rational sheep, O wise one, thou didst preserve them unharmed from noetic wolves; and having settled with them in the heavenly sheepfold, thou standest before the Ultimacy of desires.

*Theotokion:* Leaving the seal of thy virginity wholly uninjured, O Maiden, the Father's Word made His abode in thee; and the glorious choir of martyrs embraced thee, proclaiming thee to be the pure Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Having stretched out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of lions in the den; and the children, zealous lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying: Bless all ye works of the Lord, the Lord!

*Troparion:* Sleeplessly bearing the radiance of thy heart, drenched with streams of tears, O venerable one, and with the blood of suffering, crying in the bridal chamber not made with hands, now thou livest: Bless all ye works of the Lord, the Lord!

*Troparion:* The beauty of ascetics and of sufferers wast thou shown to be, O venerable one; for in both didst thou show good pleasure, having acquired victories that amaze the mind against the one foe, O Nicon, crying: Bless all ye works of the Lord, the Lord!

*Troparion:* Having watered souls with blood, thou didst arouse them unto divine zeal, O venerable one; by the wounds of torments thou didst wound the demonic battle-array, showing forth the title that truly belonged to thee,[^312] crying: Bless all ye works of the Lord, the Lord!

*Theotokion:* Burst the heavy chains of my falls, O pure one, who by thy childbirth didst rectify Adam's fall, O all-hymned one; that with a pure mind and wakefulness of light I may cry: Bless all ye works of the Lord, the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the uncut mountain, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the parted natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:* The Crown-giver, seeing thee blossomed forth with the beauty of wounds and robed with a purple garment from thy martyric blood, settled thee with Himself, O Nicon, as thou dost divinely rejoice.

*Troparion:* How exceedingly good was Thy love which thou didst possess toward God, O Nicon! And warm was Thy zeal, kindled with divine fire, and marvelous Thy suffering, which united thee with the heavenly hosts.

*Troparion:* Having thee as a sun upon the Church's height in their midst, the choir of passion-bearers, illumined like stars with divine light, by light-giving splendors illumine the whole universe.

*Troparion:* Thou hast been taken up unto the incorruptible mansions, O martyr, presenting the twofold-hundred choir of sufferers[^313] unto the Crown-giver: with them remember those who celebrate your sacred memory today.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast shown forth as the dwelling-place of the Light Who appeared from thee for our sake: Him unceasingly entreat to enlighten the thoughts of us who piously sing of thee, O Virgin Mother all-immaculate.

*March 24: Forefeast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God.*

*In the evening: the usual Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day 2, and the Martyricon, and Prosomoia 3, and of the Forefeast on 4.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я́кѡ до́бля):*

The mystery hidden and unknown unto the angels, Archangel Gabriel confirms; and now he comes unto thee, the incorrupt and fair dove, the calling back of our race; and he cries unto thee, O all-pure one: Rejoice! Make ready by the word to receive God the Word in thy womb! *Twice.*

The light-bearing chamber, the pure womb of the Maiden of God, is prepared for Thee, O Master: come unto her and descend; have compassion upon Thy creation, which received warfare through envy, and was held in servitude to the deceiver, having lost its primal beauty, and awaiting Thy saving descent!

Gabriel the Archangel shall come manifestly unto thee, O all-immaculate one, and shall cry unto thee: Rejoice, loosening of the curse, restoration of the fallen! Rejoice, thou who alone wast chosen for God! Rejoice, chariot of the Sun of glory: receive the Bodiless One, Who desires to dwell within thy womb!

*Glory, both now, Tone 2, Idiomelon:* Today Gabriel announcs good tidings unto her who is full of grace: Rejoice, unwedded and virginal Mother! Marvel not at my strange appearance, neither be terrified, for I am an archangel: the serpent deceived Eve of old; but now I announce good tidings of joy unto thee, and thou shalt remain incorrupt, and shalt bear the Lord, O all-pure one!

*Entrance. Prokeimenon. And the readings of the day. And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts.*

*And where there are no Presanctified Gifts, we sing: of the Triodion, 3; and of the Forefeast, 3. Glory, both now: "Gabriel announcs good tidings."*
