*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion, and the Martyricon. Glory, both now, of the Forefeast, Tone 2:*

The mystery hidden from the ages is revealed today,[^314] and the Son of God becomes the Son of man, that receiving what is worse, He may impart unto me what is better. Adam was deceived of old, and desiring to become God, did not become so: God becomes man, that He may make Adam god. Let creation rejoice, let it dance for joy let nature dance for joy, for the archangel stands before the Virgin with fear, and brings the "Rejoice," the reverse of sorrow! O our God, Who didst become man through the tender compassion of mercy, glory to Thee!

*At Matins: Alleluia, and the Troparia of the Tone.*

*If it happen on a Saturday or a Sunday, at "God is the Lord," Troparion, Tone 4:*

Today the preludes of universal joy command us to sing the forefeast hymn: for behold, Gabriel comes, bearing the good tidings unto the Virgin, and unto her he cries: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*And the usual Kathismata. And the rest according to the custom.*

*Canon of the Forefeast, with the Heirmos on 6. And of the Triodion according to their order.*

*The composition of Theophanes. Tone 4.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:* Traversing the Red Sea's deep with unmoistened feet, ancient Israel of old, by the cross-forming hands of Moses, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:* The world rejoices in gladness, having perceived the Lord's descent upon thee; for through the tender compassion of mercy He comes down from the heavens to dwell within the Virgin's womb.

*Troparion:* Unto thee is the divine archangel ever sent, to herald beforehand the coming of the King of all and God, and to proclaim unto thee: Rejoice, restoration of the forefather!

*Troparion:* Light-radiant and all-golden jar, prepare thyself for the receiving of the living Manna;[^315] for He comes upon thee by the angel's voice, dwelling within thee beyond nature.

*Troparion:* The Lord Who ever lives in heaven desires to dwell within thy pure womb; for He comes to renew the nature of earthborn men, having wisely clothed Himself therein.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Not in wisdom and power and riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:* O earth, which in sorrow didst bring forth the thorns of passions, leap for joy, dance! For behold, the immortal Husbandman now comes, delivering thee from the curse.

*Troparion:* Mystical fleece, prepare thyself, O undefiled Virgin; for upon thee shall God descend like rain,[^316] to dry up the torrents of transgression.

*Troparion:* Divine scroll, be fair-adorned; for by the Father's finger the divine Word incarnate shall be inscribed in thee, loosing the irrationality of my transgression.

*Troparion:* Golden lampstand, receive the fire of the Godhead, shining forth through thee, and imparting light unto the world, whereby the darkness of our evils shall be destroyed.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Яви́лся єси́):*

By the descent of the All-Holy Spirit, Who is enthroned with the Father and of one essence, at the voice of the archangel thou didst conceive, O Birthgiver of God, the restoration of Adam.

*Glory, both now, another Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀):*

Today all creation rejoices, for the angel cries unto thee the "Rejoice," O blessed Mary and all-pure Mother of Christ God! Today the insolence of the serpent is darkened, for the bond of the forefather's curse is loosed; therefore we cry unto thee in every way: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* The mystery foreordained before the ages of old begins to receive manifestation: let earth and heavens rejoice in unison, and gladly shout aloud!

*Troparion:* Palace of the great King, open the divine entrances of thy hearing; for already Christ the Truth comes, to dwell in the midst of thee.

*Troparion:* To Him Who rectifies the fall of the foremother, and appears as Deliverer, dwelling within the unwedded womb, let us sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Habakkuk of old called thee an overshadowed mountain of virtues,[^317] out of which our God desires to appear, O all-immaculate Virgin, the only rectification of men.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as a light, a holy light, turning from the gloom of ignorance those who sing of Thee in faith.

*Troparion:* O undefiled ewe-lamb: for the Lamb of our God hastens to enter into thy womb, Who takes away our sins.

*Troparion:* The mystical rod of Aaron shall blossom, as it is written,[^318] showing forth unto us the divine Flower from the root of Jesse.

*Troparion:* Made fertile like a vineyard by the angel's voice, prepare thyself, O Virgin, for the budding forth of the ripe and incorruptible cluster of grapes.

*Troparion:* Let Isaiah, the greatest of the prophets, rejoice; for the Virgin of whom thou didst prophesy,[^319] conceives in her womb the Angel of Great Counsel!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed of the demonic blood by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side.

*Troparion:* Gabriel the Archangel says unto thee: Rejoice! For in thy womb thou shalt ineffably receive the joy which Eve lost by transgression, O Virgin.

*Troparion:* Let not the fire of the Godhead burn thy womb, be not terrified at all, O Maiden; for the burning bush of old depicted thee, burning yet in no wise consumed, O all-pure one.[^320]

*Troparion:* Rejoice, O Virgin, the Mountain which Daniel foresaw by the Spirit;[^321] for from thee the noetic Stone shall be cut without hands, and shall shatter the soulless temples of the demons.

*Troparion:* The King of peace comes unto thee, and through thee shall reconcile those who were conquered and deceived by the crafty counsel of the serpent, O Mother of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "To thee, the Champion Leader" (Взбра́нной):*

Unto all earthborn men thou art the beginning of our salvation, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: for the minister of God, the great captain Gabriel, was sent from heaven to stand before thee, and brought thee the joy of glad tidings; therefore we all cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Bride unwedded!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* He Who saved Thy children of Abraham in the fire and slew the Chaldeans, whom justice righteously overtook: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* The spiritual Ark, which the true Lawgiver having loved, desires to make His dwelling within thee, being filled with sweet fragrance; for through thee He shall renew those who have suffered corruption.

*Troparion:* The divine choir of the prophets, having perceived, O Maiden, the peaceful coming of the Deliverer upon thee, cries unto thee: Rejoice, redemption of all! Rejoice, only salvation of men!

*Troparion:* Be not terrified at the voice, marvel not at him that speaks: he is a servant of God, and hath come to reveal unto thee a mystery ineffable unto the angels, O unwedded, blessed Virgin!

*Troparion:* Thou seest all creation enslaved by the enemy, O Virgin; and He Who desires to have mercy upon it through the tender compassion of mercy hath shown good pleasure through thee: therefore do not disbelieve the words of the angel!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Having stretched out his hands, Daniel stopped the mouths of lions stopped the mouths of lions in the pit; and the Children, zealous lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Light cloud of light,[^322] unwedded maiden, prepare thyself: behold, the unwaning Sun shines forth exceedingly upon thee from on high, hidden within thee for a little while, but revealed unto the world, and destroying the gloom of malice.

*Troparion:* The foremost minister of the angels cried unto thee a joyful voice, announcing unto thee, O pure one, the Angel of Great Counsel[^323] incarnate from thee in His goodness, unto Whom we cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* He Who sits at the right hand of the Father, transcendent in essence, desires to make His dwelling in thee, O pure one, that He may set thee at His right hand as queen, His neighbor and fair one,[^324] and stretch forth His right hand unto all the fallen, and save us.

*Troparion:* As a pure rose of the valley, as a sweet-smelling lily, knowing thee, our Creator and Lord, hath now desired thy beauty, O pure one,[^325] and desires to become incarnate of thy blood, that in His goodness He may drive away the foul stench of delusion.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed, through the affliction of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, by the budding forth of thy bearing in the womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:* Eve indeed ate the fruit, which wrought all-destroying death; but in the midst of thee grows the Fruit that mediates immortality, Christ our sweetness, O Sovereign Lady: Whom hymning, we glorify thee.

*Troparion:* Having bowed Thy heavens, come down unto us,[^326] O Word: the virginal womb is already prepared as a throne for Thee, whereon seated as most mighty King, Thou shalt raise up from the fall the creation of Thy right hand.

*Troparion:* Pure, unsown field, receive by the word the heavenly Word, as fruitful wheat budding forth from thee, and nourishing the ends of the earth with the Bread of understanding: Whom worshipping, we glorify thee.

*Troparion:* Christ desired thy beauty, O all-immaculate one,[^327] and made His abode in thy womb, that He might deliver the human race from the deformity of the passions, and bestow upon it the ancient beauty: Whom worshipping, we glorify thee.

*If Saturday occur:*

*Exapostilarion. Podoben:*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "With the disciples let us come together" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ):*

Gabriel, having flown down from the heights, cried the "Rejoice," O Birthgiver of God: for having destroyed all the foremother's curse, He Who budded forth from thee blessed those who are of Adam.

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day twice, and the Martyricon.*

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*

A tongue which she knew not did the Birthgiver of God hear;[^328] for the archangel spoke unto her the words of good tidings; therefore, having faithfully received the greeting, she conceived Thee, the eternal God: therefore we also, rejoicing, cry unto Thee: O God Who without change wast incarnate of her, grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*And the prostrations are made for it, and the rest according to the custom.*

*Note.*

*It is fitting to know: If the Forefeast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God occur on a Saturday of the 3rd or 4th week of the Fasts:*

*On Friday evening and in the morning, a ringing of the two small bells, the censer according to the order.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day twice, and the Martyrica 4 of the Tone, and of the Forefeast on 4. Glory of the Forefeast, Tone 2: "Gabriel announces good tidings." Both now, the Dogmatic Theotokion of the current Tone.*

*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light." Prokeimenon. And the readings of the day. And "Let my prayer be set forth." And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts according to the custom.*

*Note: But if there are no Presanctified Gifts: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast on 6. Glory of the Forefeast, both now, Theotokion of the Tone. Then "O Gladsome Light," without entrance. Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day. After this, "Vouchsafe, O Lord." And the litany, "Let us complete our evening prayer."*

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion twice, written at "Lord, I have cried," and the Martyricon according to its Tone from the Octoechos. And we say the usual refrains: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes." Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. After "Now lettest Thou depart" and "Our Father," Troparion of the Forefeast. Then the litany, "Have mercy on us, O God," and three great prostrations. After this, "All-Holy Trinity." "Blessed be the name of the Lord," thrice.*

*Note: Then "It is truly meet," and dismissal. Great Compline without prostrations. After the 1st Trisagion, instead of "Lighten mine eyes," we say the Troparion of the Forefeast: "Today the preludes of universal joy." After the 2nd Trisagion, the usual troparia: "Have mercy on us, O Lord." Canon of the Octoechos* of the departed from the Octoechos. After "It is truly meet," the Kontakion of the Forefeast.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Forefeast, Tone 4: "Today the preludes of universal joy," thrice. And the usual Kathismata, and the Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos. We do not sing the Polyeleos for the departed. But we say Odes 7 and 8 beforehand on Friday. Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmos on 6, the Heirmos twice. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Forefeast. Glory, both now, another. But when the Four-Ode Canon arrives, we sing the Canon of the Forefeast as prescribed above, and of the Triodion on 8. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Forefeast. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos, namely of the Praises of the current Tone, the Martyrica; we omit the last verse, and say the usual refrains: "We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy," and "Let the brightness." Glory, both now, Tone 4, of the Forefeast: "A tongue which she knew not," and the rest. At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion of the Forefeast. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Forefeast, Odes 3 and 6. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Temple of Christ, if there be one; then the Troparion of the Forefeast. Glory, Kontakion of the Temple; both now, of the Forefeast.*

*Note.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on the Sunday of the Veneration of the Cross:*

*On Saturday at Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera 3, of the Cross 4, and of the Forefeast 3. Glory of the Cross; both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple. Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast, written at "Lord, I have cried."*

*At the Aposticha, the Stichera of the Octoechos. Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast, written at Matins at the Aposticha, Tone 4: "A tongue which she knew not." At the Blessing of Loaves, Troparion: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin, rejoice," twice; and of the Cross, 1. After this, "Blessed be the name of the Lord," thrice.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Resurrectional Troparion twice; Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast; and the rest as usual. Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4, and of the Cross on 6, and of the Forefeast on 4. Katavasia: "The most divine prefigured." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Forefeast, and Sessional Hymns of the Cross; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Cross. After Ode 9, Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast. The rest at the Praises and at the Liturgy is all as written for the 3rd Sunday of the Fast in order in the Menaion, except that at the Hours we say the Resurrectional Troparion* without changing; Glory of the Cross and of the Forefeast, saying them alternately. After "Our Father," the Kontakia at all the Hours of the Cross and of the Forefeast, saying them alternately.

*Note.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on Monday of the 4th, 5th, or 6th week of the Fast:*

*On Sunday evening, Stichera on 10: Penitential of the Octoechos 4, and of the Triodion 3, and of the Forefeast 3. Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. Entrance. Great Prokeimenon. Then "Vouchsafe, O Lord," and 3 prostrations. We say the litany, "Let us complete our evening prayer." Then we sing the Aposticha in the Triodion, with the usual refrains. Glory, both now, of the Forefeast, which is written at Matins at the Aposticha, Tone 4: "A tongue which she knew not." We sing "Now lettest Thou depart," and the Trisagion with prostrations. And the Troparia: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin, rejoice," with prostrations, and "Lord, have mercy," 40 times. And "O Heavenly King." After "More honorable," 3 prostrations, and dismissal. At Matins we sing Alleluia, and the Triadica of the Tone, and the usual Kathismata; and we sing the rest at Matins and at the Hours as indicated previously in order on the 24th day.*

*Note.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on Wednesday of the 4th week of the Fast, which is of the Veneration of the Cross:*

*In the evening and in the morning we sing everything with prostrations. On Tuesday evening, the censer according to the order. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Triodion 3, and of the Forefeast 3. Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast. Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Triodion; Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast.*

*At Matins on Wednesday, Canon of the Triodion with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Forefeast on 4. And where there is the Three-Ode Canon, we sing the Canon of the Cross with the Heirmos on 4, and of the Forefeast on 2, taking two troparia as one; and of the Three-Ode Canon on 8, followed by the Heirmos of the Three-Ode Canon. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Forefeast, and Sessional Hymn of the day; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Cross. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion, the Triadicon of the Tone. At the Praises, Stichera of the Cross, written at "Lord, I have cried," which were omitted. At the Aposticha of the Triodion, Glory of the Cross; both now, of the Forefeast. At the First Hour, the veneration of the Cross takes place according to the custom. Thus we sing the service of the Forefeast and of the Cross also on Friday if it occur in that same week.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on Thursday of the 4th week of the Fast:*

*On Wednesday evening at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion on 6, and of the Forefeast on 4. Glory of the Cross, Tone 8: "Today He Who is unapproachable in essence"; both now, of the Forefeast. Then Entrance, and the rest of the Presanctified Gifts. At Matins we sing everything as indicated in order on the 24th.*

*Note. It is fitting to know: If the Annunciation of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God occur on Wednesday of the 5th week of the Fast:*

*We sing the service of the Forefeast of the Annunciation, the Stichera and Canon, on Monday in order; and we sing the Menaion of the saint of the day at Compline.*

*Note. But if the Forefeast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God occur on Thursday of the 5th week of the Fast:*

*We sing the service of the Forefeast, the Stichera and Canon, beforehand on Wednesday in order, on account of the Great Canon; and we likewise sing the service of the saint of the day at Compline.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God occur on the 4th or 5th Sunday of the Fast:*

*At Great Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera 3, Anatolian 3, and of the Forefeast 4. Glory of the Forefeast; both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone; and the rest according to the custom. At Matins, the Resurrectional Troparion twice; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast; and the rest resurrectional. Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos on 4, and of the Birthgiver of God on 2, and of the Triodion on 4, and of the Forefeast on 4. Katavasia: "I will open my mouth." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Forefeast, and Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. After Ode 6, Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. After Ode 9, Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast; and the rest according to the custom resurrectional and of the Triodion. At the First Hour, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory of the Forefeast; both now, Theotokion of the Hours. But the services of the saints, John of the Ladder and Mary of Egypt, on these Sundays are omitted, and are sung when the Ecclesiarch decides.*

*If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on the 5th Saturday of the Akathist:*

*On Friday evening at "Lord, I have cried," we sing the Stichera: the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day twice, and the Martyricon; and of the Akathist on 4; and of the Forefeast* on 3. Glory of the Forefeast; both now, of the Akathist. Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day. "Let my prayer be set forth."

*Note: And the rest of the Presanctified Gifts. But we sing the Four-Ode Canon of the Triodion at Compline. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Akathist twice; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. And the usual Kathismata. The Kontakia and Ikoses of the Akathist according to their order. Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Akathist on 8. Katavasia: "I will open my mouth." After Odes 3 and 6, the Kontakia and Ikoses according to the custom. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Akathist, 2; Glory, both now, of the Forefeast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Akathist on 4, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ); Glory, both now, Tone 4: "A tongue which she knew not." Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*Note. If the Forefeast of the Annunciation occur on Lazarus Saturday:*

*The service of the Forefeast is sung beforehand on Thursday of that same week, as indicated on the 24th day of this month.*

*Note. It is fitting to know: If the Eve of the Annunciation occur on a Saturday or a Sunday:*

*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 4, Tone 4.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*

Having compassion upon creation, the Creator, inclined by His tender mercy, hastens to make His abode in the womb of the Virgin, the Maiden of God; unto whom the great archangel came, proclaiming: Rejoice, thou who hast been gladdened of God, now our God is with thee! Be not terrified at me, the King's chief captain: for thou hast found grace, which Eve thy foremother lost of old; and thou shalt conceive and give birth unto Him Who is of one essence with the Father. *Twice.*

Strange is thy word and thy appearance, strange are thy sayings and announcements unto me, says the Virgin unto the angel: deceive me not, for I am a virgin, knowing not wedlock; thou sayest that I shall conceive Him in an unwedded manner: and how shall my womb contain Him Whose majesty the heavens cannot contain?[^329] Let Abraham's tent teach thee now,[^330] which contained God, prefiguring from afar, O Virgin, thy God-receiving womb!

Having now come to the city of Nazareth, Gabriel greets thee, the animate city of Christ the King, Gabriel greets thee, crying unto thee: Rejoice, blessed one gladdened of God! For thou shalt receive God incarnate in thy womb, Who through thee calls back mankind unto the ancient blessedness in His tender mercy: blessed is the divine and immortal Fruit of thy womb, Who bestows purification and great mercy upon the world!

*Glory, both now, Tone 4:*

In the sixth month the chief captain was sent unto thee, the pure Virgin,[^331] to announce unto thee the word of salvation, and together to call unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee! Thou shalt give birth unto the Son Who is before the ages from the Father, and He shall save His people from their sins.[^332]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

The leader of the hosts on high, having flown down from the heavens to Nazareth, greets the Virgin: Rejoice, saying, pure chariot of the Godhead! God loved thee from the ages, and chose thee for His dwelling-place: as the Master's servant, coming unto thee, I cry: for thou shalt give birth unto the Lord, and remain incorrupt!

*Verse: Proclaim from day to day the salvation of our God [LXX Ps. 95:2].*

What is thy fiery appearance? the pure one said with amazement unto Gabriel: What is thy dignity, and the power of thy words? Thou proclaimest unto me that I shall conceive a Son; but I know not the experience of man: go far away! Deceive me not, O man,[^333] as the deceitful serpent deceived Eve my foremother of old!

*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song, sing unto the Lord, all the earth [LXX Ps. 95:1].*

The all-holy Spirit of God shall come upon thee, O all-pure one, Sovereign Lady who containest God, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee;[^334] and thou shalt give birth unto a Son Who keeps thy virginity uninjured: this Son is beyond genealogy; this One, having appeared, shall save His people, as He hath shown good pleasure.

*Glory, both now, Tone 4:*

In the sixth month the archangel was sent unto the pure Virgin, and having greeted her with "Rejoice," announced that the Deliverer should come forth from her: therefore, having received the greeting, she conceived Thee, the eternal God, Who ineffably wast well pleased to become man for the salvation of our souls.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Today is the crown of our salvation, and the manifestation of the mystery that is from the ages: the Son of God becomes the Son of the Virgin, and Gabriel announces the good tidings of grace: therefore let us also with him cry aloud unto the Birthgiver of God: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*March 25: The Annunciation of our Most Holy Sovereign Lady the Birthgiver of God and Ever-Virgin Mary.*

*It begins from Thursday of the 3rd week of the Holy Fasts, and extends unto Wednesday of Bright Week. And if it is a fast, we begin Vespers without a Kathisma and without prostrations.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day twice, and Prosomoia 3; and of the Feast 3, repeating two: Tone 6.*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*

Revealing unto thee the counsel before the ages, O Maiden, Gabriel stood before thee, greeting thee and proclaiming: Rejoice, unsown earth! Rejoice, unburnt bush![^335] Rejoice, depth hard to fathom! Rejoice, bridge leading unto the heavens, and lofty ladder which Jacob saw![^336] Rejoice, divine jar of manna![^337] Rejoice, loosing of the curse! Rejoice, calling back of Adam: the Lord is with thee! *Twice.*

Thou appearest unto me as a man, says the undefiled Maiden unto the chief captain: and how dost thou proclaim sayings beyond man? For thou hast said that God shall be with me, and make His abode in my womb: and how shall I be, tell me, a spacious receptacle and place of sanctification for Him Who surpasses the Cherubim? Deceive me not with falsehood: for I have not known pleasure, I have no part in marriage; how then shall I bear a child?

Where God so wills, the order of nature is overcome, says the bodiless one; and things surpassing man are accomplished: believe my true words, O all-holy, all-immaculate one! And she cried aloud: Be it done unto me now according to thy word,[^338] and I shall bear the Bodiless One, Who has borrowed flesh from me, that as the only Mighty One He may lead man up unto the primal dignity through the commingling!

*Glory, both now, Tone 6, Idiomelon of John the Monk:*

Gabriel the Archangel was sent from heaven to announce the conception unto the Virgin; and having come to Nazareth, he pondered within himself, marvelling at the wonder: O how is He Who is incomprehensible in the highest born of a virgin born of a virgin! He Who has heaven for His throne and the earth for His footstool[^339] is contained in a virgin's womb; He upon Whom the six-winged and many-eyed ones cannot gaze,[^340] was well pleased by a single word to become incarnate of her: the Word present is God's! Why then do I stand, and say not unto the Virgin: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee! Rejoice, pure Virgin! Rejoice, Bride unwedded! Rejoice, Mother of Life: blessed is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light."*

*Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and three of the Feast.*

*The Reading from Genesis (Chapter 28):*

Jacob went forth from the Well of the Oath,[^341] and went toward Haran. And he found a place, and slept there, for the sun was set; and he took of the stones of that place, and put them for his pillows, and slept in that place: and he dreamed. And behold, a ladder set up on the earth, whose top reached unto heaven; and the angels of God ascending and descending upon it. And the Lord leaned upon it, and said: I am the God of Abraham thy father, and the God of Isaac; fear not: the land whereon thou sleepest, unto thee will I give it, and unto thy seed. And thy seed shall be as the sand of the earth, and it shall spread abroad to the sea, and toward the south, and to the north, and toward the east; and in thee shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed, and in thy seed. And behold, I am with thee, keeping thee in all the way whithersoever thou shalt go; and I will bring thee back into this land; for I will not leave thee until I have done all that I have spoken unto thee. And Jacob awaked out of his sleep, and said: Truly the Lord is in this place, and I knew it not. And he was afraid, and said: How dreadful is this place! This is none other than the house of God, and these are the gates of heaven.

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Chapters 43 and 44):*

Thus says the Lord: It shall be from the eighth day and onward, that the priests shall offer your burnt-offerings upon the altar, and your peace-offerings; and I will accept you, says the Lord Adonai. And He turned me back by the way of the outer gate of the sanctuary, which looks toward the east; and it was shut. And the Lord said unto me: This gate shall be shut, and it shall not be opened, and no man shall pass through it; because the Lord God of Israel shall enter through it, and it shall be shut. For the prince, he shall sit in it to eat bread; he shall enter by the way of the porch of the gate, and shall go out by the way of the same. And He brought me by the way of the gate of the sanctuary that faces toward the north, in front of the temple; and I looked, and behold, the house of the Lord was full of glory.

*The Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 9):*

Wisdom hath built a house for herself, and set up seven pillars. She hath slaughtered her sacrifices, and mingled her wine in her bowl, and prepared her table. She hath sent forth her servants, calling with a lofty proclamation unto the bowl, saying: Whoso is foolish, let him turn aside unto me; and unto them that lack understanding she says: Come, eat of my bread, and drink the wine which I have mingled for you. Forsake folly, and ye shall live; and seek understanding, that ye may live, and set right understanding in knowledge. He that instructs the evil shall receive dishonor unto himself; and he that reproves an ungodly man gets himself a blot, for reproofs unto an ungodly man are wounds unto him. Reprove not the evil, lest they hate thee: reprove a wise man, and he will love thee. Give occasion unto a wise man, and he will be yet wiser; teach a just man, and he will increase in learning. The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, and the counsel of the holy ones is understanding: for to understand the law is the mark of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live a long time, and the years of thy life shall be increased unto thee.

*Then "Let my prayer be set forth."*

*And 3 great prostrations, and the rest of the Presanctified Gifts.*

*Note: But if there are no Presanctified Gifts: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera Prosomoia 3 in the Triodion, and of the Feast 5. Glory, both now: "Gabriel was sent." Entrance. Prokeimenon. And the readings of the day, and of the Feast 3. Then "Vouchsafe, O Lord." At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day twice, and the Martyricon. Glory, both now: "Today is joy." After "Now lettest Thou depart," Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and 3 great prostrations, and dismissal. At the trapeza we partake of wine and oil, on whatsoever day it occur, unto the glory of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God.*

*It is needful to know: If the Annunciation occur on any Saturday of the Fast, the readings of the day are read first, and after them 5 of the Feast: 1st, "Moses entered"; 2nd, "The Lord created me"; 3rd, "Jacob went forth"; 4th, "It shall be from the eighth day"; 5th, "Wisdom hath built a house for herself." In like manner 5 readings of the Feast are read if the Annunciation occur on a Sunday.*

*But if it is a fast, three are read in the evening, those written above; and at the Liturgy* and at the Liturgy these two.

*The Reading from Exodus (Chapter 3):*

Moses entered into the mountain of God, Horeb. And the angel of the Lord appeared unto him in a flame of fire out of the bush, and he saw that the bush burned with fire, and the bush was not consumed. And Moses said: I will go near and see this great sight, why the bush is not burnt. And when the Lord saw that he drew near to see, the Lord called unto him out of the bush, saying: Moses, Moses! And he said: What is it? And He said: Draw not nigh hither; loose thy shoe from thy feet, for the place whereon thou standest is holy ground. And He said unto him: I am the God of thy father, the God of Abraham, and the God of Isaac, and the God of Jacob. And Moses turned his face away, for he was afraid to look upon God. And the Lord said unto Moses: I have surely seen the affliction of My people who are in Egypt, and have heard their cry by reason of their taskmasters; for I know their sorrow, and am come down to deliver them out of the hand of the Egyptians, and to bring them up out of that land, and to bring them into a good and large land, into a land flowing with milk and honey.

*The Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 8):*

The Lord created Me the beginning of His ways, for His works. Before the ages He established Me in the beginning, before He made the earth, and before He created the abysses, before the watery springs came forth, before the mountains were settled; and before all hills He begets Me. The Lord made the lands and desert places, and the inhabited summits under heaven. When He prepared the heaven, I was present with Him; and when He marked out His own throne upon the winds; when He made firm the clouds above, and when He established securely the fountains beneath heaven; when He set for the sea its bound, and the waters shall not pass His commandment; and when He made firm the foundations of the earth: I was with Him, arranging all things; I was that wherein He rejoiced, and daily I delighted before His face at all times.

*And at the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck sufficiently, and then all of them. And when we are gathered in the temple, the priest having given the blessing, and censing:*

*We sing Great Compline, and "God is with us," with chanting, in Tone 8, by both choirs. And the customary troparia: "Having passed through the day:"*

*After the 1st Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast:*

*Today is the fountainhead of our salvation...*

*After the 2nd, Kontakion of the Feast:*

*To thee, the Champion Leader...*

*And after "Glory to God in the highest," we make the procession into the narthex. And the Litiya takes place according to custom. And we sing three Idiomelon Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1.*

*By Byzas:*

In the sixth month the Archistrategos was sent unto thee, the Virgin and pure one, to announce unto thee the word of salvation, and together to cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee; thou shalt bear the Son Who is co-eternal with the Father, and He shall save His people from their sins.

*By Anatolios:*

In the sixth month, the Archangel Gabriel was sent from heaven unto the Galilean city of Nazareth, to bring unto the Maiden the joy of the Annunciation. And coming unto her, he cried aloud, saying: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee! Rejoice, vessel containing Him Whom no essence can contain: for Whom the heavens could not contain, thy womb has contained, O blessed one! Rejoice, venerable recalling of Adam, and deliverance of Eve, the joy of the world, and gladness of our race!

The Archangel Gabriel was sent from heaven by God unto the undefiled Virgin, unto the Galilean city of Nazareth, to announce unto her the conception of a strange manner. The bodiless servant was sent unto the animate city and spiritual gate, to announce the descent of the Master's coming. The heavenly soldier was sent unto the palace of the throne of glory, to prepare for the Creator an inextinguishable dwelling-place. And coming unto her, he cried aloud: Rejoice, fiery-throned seat, more glorious than the four-faced creatures! Rejoice, seat of the heavenly King! Rejoice, unhewn mountain, most honorable receptacle: for in thee all the fullness of the Godhead was pleased to dwell bodily,[^342] by the good pleasure of the co-eternal Father and the co-operation of the All-Holy Spirit! Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*If it be Saturday or Sunday:*

*Glory, Tone 2:*

*By John the Monk:*

Let the heavens be glad, and let the earth rejoice! For He Who is co-eternal with the Father, co-beginningless and co-enthroned, having taken compassion and man-befriending mercy, set Himself into emptying,[^343] by the good pleasure and counsel of the Father, and dwelt in the Virgin's womb, pre-purified by the Spirit. O wonder! God is among men; He Who cannot be contained is in the womb; the Timeless enters into time! And what is more glorious: that the conception is without seed, and the emptying is ineffable, and how great is the mystery! For God is emptied, and becomes incarnate, and is fashioned, when the angel spoke of conception unto the pure one: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee, Who has great mercy!

*Both now, Tone 2:*

Gabriel announces good tidings unto her that is full of grace today: Rejoice, unwedded Mother, who hast not known wedlock! Marvel not at my strange aspect, nor be affrighted, for I am an Archangel. The serpent deceived Eve of old; but now I announce good tidings of joy unto thee, and thou shalt remain incorrupt, and shalt bear the Lord, O most pure one!

*But if it be a fast day:*

*Glory, both now:*

*Gabriel announces good tidings...*

*Then the customary prayers of the vigil.*

*And having entered into the temple, we sing:*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 4:*

In the sixth month, the Archangel was sent unto the pure Virgin, and having greeted her with "Rejoice," he announced that the Redeemer should come forth from her. Therefore, having received the salutation, she conceived Thee, the co-eternal God, Who ineffably wast well pleased to become man, for the salvation of our souls.

*Verse: Announce the good tidings from day to day of the salvation of our God.*[^345]

A tongue which she knew not did the Birthgiver of God hear; for the Archangel spoke unto her words of good tidings. Whence, having faithfully received the greeting, she conceived Thee, the pre-eternal God. Therefore we also rejoicing cry unto Thee: O God Who without change wast incarnate of her, grant peace unto the world, and unto our souls great mercy!

*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song; sing unto the Lord, all the earth.*[^346]

Behold, our recalling has now appeared unto us: beyond word God is united unto men! At the Archangel's voice delusion is driven away, for the Virgin receives joy: earthly things have become heaven; the world is loosed from the primal curse. Let creation rejoice and sing aloud: O our Creator and Redeemer, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Glory, both now, in the same Tone.*

*By Andrew of Jerusalem:*[^344]

Today is the joy of the Annunciation, the virginal solemnity: things below are united with things above. Adam is renewed, and Eve is set free from the primal grief; and the tabernacle of our nature, having received deification by the commingling, has become the temple of God. O mystery! The manner of the emptying is unknown, the wealth of goodness is ineffable. An angel serves at the wonder: the Virgin's womb receives the Son; the Holy Spirit is sent down; the Father on high is well pleased, and the change is accomplished by common counsel. In which and through which being saved, together with Gabriel let us cry aloud unto the Virgin: Rejoice, thou full of grace! From her is our salvation, Christ our God, Who, having assumed our nature, raised it unto Himself: Him do thou entreat, that our souls may be saved!

*Then "Now lettest Thou depart."*

*And after "Our Father": At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion, Tone 4:*

*Today is the fountainhead of our salvation, and the manifestation of the mystery which is from the ages: the Son of God becomes the Son of the Virgin, and Gabriel announces the good tidings of grace. Therefore let us also cry aloud with him unto the Birthgiver of God: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee! (Thrice)*

*And "Blessed be the name of the Lord," with chanting, thrice.*

*And the reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, both Sessional Hymns of the Triodion without litanies:*

*And the reading of the day. And after the 2nd Kathisma, litany, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*

The great Commander of the incorporeal angels, having stood forth in the city of Nazareth, announces unto thee, O all-pure one, the King of the ages and Lord: Rejoice, saying unto thee, O blessed Mary, the incomprehensible and ineffable wonder, the renewal of mankind!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*And the reading of the Feast.*

*After the 3rd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Къ красотѣ̀):*

Today all creation rejoices, for the Archangel says unto thee: Rejoice, O blessed, venerable, and all-pure Mother of Christ God! Today the serpent's pride is darkened, for the bond of the forefather's curse is loosed. Therefore we also unceasingly cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou full of grace!

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*And after the reading, candles are given unto the brethren. After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оудиви́сѧ):*

Gabriel from heaven calls "Rejoice" unto the venerable one, for she shall conceive in her womb the pre-eternal God, Who by His word composed the ends of the earth. Therefore Mary answered: I know not a man, and how shall I bear a son? Who has ever seen a birth without seed? And explaining, the angel spoke unto the Virgin Birthgiver of God: The Holy Spirit shall come upon thee, and the power of the Most High shall overshadow thee!

*Glory, both now, Tone 8, the same Podoben:*

Gabriel was sent unto the Virgin Mary, and announced unto her the ineffable joy, that without seed she should conceive, and not suffer corruption: For thou shalt bear the Son, the pre-eternal God, and He shall save His people from their sins. And He Who sent me bears witness, that I should cry unto thee, O blessed one: Rejoice! A Virgin thou shalt give birth, and after giving birth thou shalt still remain a virgin!

*And the reading of the Feast. Then the Songs of Ascents, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

*Announce the good tidings from day to day of the salvation of our God. Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song; sing unto the Lord, all the earth. "Let every breath." The Gospel according to Luke, section 4 [Luke 1:39-49, 56].*

*After Psalm 50, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 2:*

Gabriel announces good tidings unto her that is full of grace today: Rejoice, unwedded Mother, who hast not known wedlock! Marvel not at my strange aspect, nor be affrighted, for I am an Archangel. The serpent deceived Eve of old; but now I announce good tidings of joy unto thee, and thou shalt remain incorrupt, and shalt bear the Lord, O most pure one!

*Canon of the Feast, the heirmoi twice, and the troparia unto 12. Katavasia of the Feast, both choirs together. And where there is a triodion, there we sing the Canon of the Feast with the heirmos unto 6, and the triodion unto 8. Katavasia: the heirmos of the Triodion, both choirs together. The Canon, the composition of Lord Theophanes,[^347] the acrostic whereof is according to the alphabet, unto the 9th Ode.*

*Ode 1, Tone 4. Heirmos:*

I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.

Let David thy forefather sing unto thee, O Birthgiver of God, sounding the spiritual flute: Give ear, O daughter, unto the joyous voice of the angel, for he announces unto thee ineffable joy.

*The Angel:*

I cry unto thee rejoicing: incline thine ear and give heed unto me, as I announce the divine seedless conception; for thou hast found grace before God, which no other woman has ever found, O all-pure one.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Let me understand, O angel, the power of thy words: how shall that be which thou hast said? Speak more clearly: how shall I conceive, being a virgin maiden? And how shall I be mother of my Creator?

*The Angel:*

Thou thinkest that I speak deceitfully, as I suppose; and I rejoice beholding thy steadfastness.

Take courage, O Sovereign Lady, for when God wills, even most marvelous things are easily accomplished.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish thy singers spiritually, O Birthgiver of God, living and unstinting fountain, who have formed a choir for thee; and in thy divine glory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

A ruler has failed from Judah:[^348] the time is now come when Christ, the expectation of the nations, shall appear. But do thou tell me: how shall I, being a virgin, bear Him?

*The Angel:*

Thou seekest to learn of me, O Virgin, the manner of thy conception; but that is ineffable: the Holy Spirit, overshadowing thee with His creative power, shall accomplish it.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

My foremother, having accepted the serpent's counsel, was cast out from divine delight; therefore I also fear thy strange salutation, being wary of stumbling.

*The Angel:*

I am sent as God's minister, to tell thee the divine counsel: why art thou afraid of me, O all-blameless one, who am rather in awe of thee? Why art thou reverent before me, O Sovereign Lady, who honorably stand in reverence before thee?

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Shepherd Flutes" (Въ свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ):*

The Word of God is now come down upon earth; the angel stood before the Virgin, crying: Rejoice, blessed one, who alone hast preserved the seal, having received in thy womb the pre-eternal Word and Lord, that as God He may save the human race from delusion!

*Glory, both now, the same Tone, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное):*

The Archistrategos Gabriel was sent from heaven by God; swiftly he stood before the animate city, speaking clearly unto her: Thou shalt receive the Creator in thy womb, O Virgin, and shalt bear Him unchanged in the flesh. Therefore am I sent to announce unto thee the strange birth, O pure one, and I stand crying unto thee: Rejoice, Bride unwedded!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine is come, by an incorrupt hand, and has saved them that cry: Glory, O Christ, to Thy power!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Of old I heard a prophet who foretold Emmanuel,[^349] speaking of a certain holy virgin that should give birth; but I desire to understand how human nature shall endure the commingling of the Godhead.

*The Angel:*

The bush showed this forth, remaining unburnt when it received the flame,[^350] flame, O thou full of grace and all-hymned, showed forth the most glorious mystery in thee: for after giving birth thou shalt remain pure, ever-virgin.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Illumined by the radiance of God the Ruler of all, O preacher of truth, speak the truest words, O Gabriel: how, while my purity remains incorrupt, shall I bear in the flesh the bodiless Word?

*The Angel:*

With fear as a servant before a queen, with trembling, O Maiden, do I now stand, being in awe to gaze upon thee. For as the rain upon the fleece,[^351] the Father's Word shall descend upon thee, as He was well pleased.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, Who grants peace unto all that sing thy praise.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

I cannot understand the certainty of thy words; for many wonders have been wrought by divine power, signs and types of the law: but never has a virgin given birth without knowing a man.

*The Angel:*

Thou art amazed, O all-blameless one, for strange is the wonder concerning thee: for thou alone shalt receive the King of all incarnate in thy womb; and the prophetic sayings, enigmas, and legal types prefigure thee.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

He Whom nothing can contain and Whom none can see, how can He dwell in a virgin's womb which He Himself created? And how shall I conceive God the Word, co-beginningless with the Father and the Spirit?

*The Angel:*

Unto thy forefather David He promised to seat of the fruit of thy womb upon the throne of his kingdom;[^352] and the beauty of Jacob He chose as His own spiritual dwelling-place.[^353]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried out, praying in the sea monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Having received the voice of thy words of rejoicing, O Gabriel, I am filled with divine gladness; for thou tellest of joy, and announcest endless gladness.

*The Angel:*

Unto thee is joy given, O divine Mother of God; unto thee, "Rejoice," all creation cries aloud, O Bride of God: for thou alone hast been fore-named Mother of the Son of God, O pure one.

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Through me let Eve's condemnation now be abolished; through me let the debt be paid today; through me let the ancient borrowing be repaid in overflowing measure in overflowing measure!

*The Angel:*

God promised unto the forefather Abraham that the nations should be blessed in his seed, O pure one;[^354] and through thee the promise receives its fulfillment today!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*

To thee, the Champion Leader, we thy servants dedicate a feast of victory and of thanksgiving, as ones rescued out of sufferings, O Birthgiver of God: but as thou hast a power unvanquishable, from every manner of dangers deliver us, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, Bride unwedded!

*Ikos:*

An Archangel was sent from heaven to say unto the Birthgiver of God: Rejoice! And beholding Thee, O Lord, taking flesh together with the bodiless voice, he was amazed and stood, crying unto her such things as these:
Rejoice, through whom joy shall shine forth;
Rejoice, through whom the curse shall vanish!
Rejoice, recalling of fallen Adam;
Rejoice, deliverance from the tears of Eve!
Rejoice, height hard to climb for human thoughts;
Rejoice, depth hard to scan even for angels' eyes!
Rejoice, for thou art the King's throne;
Rejoice, for thou bearest Him Who bears all things!
Rejoice, star showing forth the Sun;
Rejoice, womb of the divine incarnation!
Rejoice, through whom creation is renewed;
Rejoice, through whom we worship the Creator!
Rejoice, Bride unwedded!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The God-wise ones did not worship creation rather than the Creator,[^355] but having manfully trampled down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, the highly hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

He Who commands the immaterial light, being united with a material body through His abundant tender-heartedness: with bright good tidings, with divine proclamations, dost thou now cry unto me: Blessed, O all-pure one, is the fruit of thy womb!

*The Angel:*

Rejoice, Sovereign Lady Virgin; rejoice, all-pure one; rejoice, receptacle of God; rejoice, candlestick of the Light; recalling of Adam, deliverance of Eve, holy mountain, most radiant sanctification, and bridal-chamber of immortality!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Purify my soul, sanctify my body, make me a temple capable of receiving God, a God-adorned tabernacle, an animate temple by the overshadowing of the All-Holy Spirit, and the pure Mother of Life!

*The Angel:*

As a radiant candlestick and a God-fashioned bridal-chamber do I now behold thee, as a golden ark: receive the Giver of the Law, O Bride of God, Who was well pleased to deliver corrupt human nature through thee!

...human nature!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Give ear, O Maiden, pure Virgin; let Gabriel declare the ancient and true counsel of the Most High: be thou prepared for the reception of God; for through thee He Whom nothing can contain has lived with men. Therefore also rejoicing I cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Every earthly mind is overcome, answered the Maiden, seeking to understand the most glorious things which thou speakest unto me! I have heard thy words, but I am afraid and trembling, lest through deceit, like Eve, thou send me far away from God. But nonetheless, behold, I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*The Angel:*

Behold, thy perplexity is resolved, says Gabriel unto these things: for well hast thou spoken, that the matter is hard to comprehend, while thou heedest the words of thy lips. Be not doubtful as of deceit, but believe as in reality; for rejoicing I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

This law is from God for men, says the blameless one straightway: from common love a birth proceeds; I know not a spouse's pleasure at all: how then sayest thou that I shall bear? I fear lest thou speak deceit. But nonetheless, behold, I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*The Angel:*

The words which thou speakest unto me, O pure one, the angel says again, are of the custom of mortal men's birth; but truly God do I promise unto thee, Who beyond word and understanding is incarnate as He knows from thee! Therefore also rejoicing I cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*The Birthgiver of God:*

Thou appearest unto me as a speaker of truth, said the Virgin: for thou art come as the announcer of universal joy. Truly I have purified soul with body, and according to thy word be it unto me,[^362] that God may dwell in me; unto Whom I cry with thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*At the 9th Ode, the brethren light candles again.*

*We do not sing "More honorable": but we sing the Refrain of the Feast:*

*O earth, announce good tidings of great joy; praise the glory of God, ye heavens!*

*Then the Heirmos:*

*As the animate ark of God...*

*In like manner the 2nd choir sings the Refrain and the Heirmos; and unto the remaining troparia of the Feast, the same Refrain.*

*And at the triodion, unto the troparia we sing as a refrain:*

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Unto the theotokia we sing the Refrain of the Feast.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

As the animate ark of God, let no hand of the profane touch it in any wise; but let the lips of the faithful unceasingly, chanting the angel's voice unto the Birthgiver of God, cry aloud with joy: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Troparia:*

Having conceived God beyond understanding, thou didst escape the custom of nature, O Maiden: for in giving birth thou didst avoid the corruption of mothers' nature, and didst remain what thou wast. Therefore worthily hearest thou: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

How thou pourest forth milk, O pure Virgin, no earthly tongue can express; for thou showest forth a strange thing of nature, surpassing the rule of lawful birth. Therefore worthily hearest thou: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

Mystically was it spoken concerning thee in the sacred Scriptures, O Mother of the Most High: for of old Jacob, seeing the ladder prefiguring thee,[^356] said: This is the step of God. Therefore worthily hearest thou: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

A marvelous sign did the bush and fire show unto Moses who was initiated into sacred things; and seeking its outcome in the passing of times, he said: In the pure Maiden I shall behold it! Unto whom, as unto the Birthgiver of God, let it be said: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

Daniel called thee the spiritual mountain;[^357] and Isaiah the parent of God;[^358] Gideon sees thee as the fleece;[^359] and David calls thee the sanctification;[^360] and another calls thee the gate;[^361] while Gabriel cries aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*Then the Heirmos of the Triodion; and the Refrain and Heirmos of the Feast, both choirs together.*

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Let us go up with the disciples" (Со оу҆чени́ки):*

The Archistrategos of the angelic hosts was sent from God the Ruler of all unto the pure Virgin, to announce the strange and ineffable wonder: for God, as man, is born an infant of her without seed, rebuilding the whole human race. Ye peoples, announce the good tidings of the renewal of the world! (Twice)

*Glory, both now, another Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*

The mystery of God which is from the ages is known today: God the Word, the Son of the Virgin Mary, for mercy's sake becomes man, and Gabriel announces the glad tidings of the Annunciation; with whom let us cry aloud unto her: Rejoice, O Mother of the Lord!

*At the Praises, "Let every breath praise the Lord," and we sing 4 Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*

Having flown down from the heavenly spheres to Nazareth, Gabriel came unto the Virgin Mary, crying aloud unto her: Rejoice! Thou shalt conceive the Son, Who is older than Adam, the Creator of the ages and Deliverer of those who cry unto thee: Rejoice, O pure one!

The co-eternal Word of the Father without beginning, not parting from the heights, has now come unto the lowly by reason of supreme tenderheartedness, having accepted mercy for our slipping; and taking Adam's poverty upon Himself, He was fashioned in what was alien.[^363]

Bringing good tidings from heaven unto the Virgin, Gabriel cried aloud: Rejoice! In thy womb thou shalt conceive Him Whom thou containest, yet Whom none can contain; and thou that givest birth shalt be shown forth of Him Who shone forth from the Father before the morning star.[^364]

The transcendent Word, having dwelt in thee in the flesh by the Father's counsel, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, has restored our human race which fell through the ancient curse; therefore we all faithfully cry unto thee with the angel: Rejoice, O Mother of Christ!

*Glory, both now, Tone 2. By Theophanes:[^365]*

The mystery which is from the ages is revealed today, and the Son of God becomes the Son of Man: that, taking the worse, He may impart unto me the better. Adam was deceived of old, and desiring to become God, he was not; God becomes man, that He may make Adam god. Let creation be glad, let nature dance for joy, for the Archangel stands before the Virgin with fear, and brings the "Rejoice," which is the opposite of sorrow: O our God, Who for the mercy of Thy compassion didst become man, glory to Thee!

*(If it be Saturday or Sunday, Great Doxology, and litanies, and dismissal.) But if it be a fasting day, we sing at the Aposticha of the Triodion the Idiomelon of the day, twice, and the Martyrikon. Glory, both now, of the Feast, Tone 8:*

Let the heavens be glad, and let the earth rejoice! For He Who is co-eternal with the Father, co-beginningless and co-enthroned, having taken compassion and man-befriending mercy, set Himself into emptying,[^366] by the good pleasure and counsel of the Father, and dwelt in the Virgin's womb, pre-purified by the Spirit. O wonder! God is among men; He Who cannot be contained is in the womb is contained in the womb; the Timeless enters into time! And what is more glorious: that the conception is without seed, and the emptying is ineffable, and how great is the mystery! For God is emptied, and becomes incarnate, and is fashioned, when the angel spoke of conception unto the pure one: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee, Who has great mercy!

*Then, "It is good to give praise unto the Lord," once. After "Our Father," the Troparion of the Feast, and litany; at the conclusion, three great prostrations, without dismissal of Matins. And holy oil is given unto the brethren, and an Idiomelon Sticheron is sung.*

*And the 1st Hour with Kathisma, Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast. And at the end, three great prostrations. And the prayer: "O Christ, the true Light." And dismissal.*

*And at the 3rd hour of the day, we go forth with the Cross in a Litiya outside the monastery; and having returned, we enter into the refectory. And we sing the 3rd and 6th Hours, with Kathismata, without prostrations. Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast.*

*At the Hours we do not strike the bell. At the 6th Hour we sing the Troparion of the Prophecy from the Triodion, and the rest according to their order, and the Paremia. And we read the homily whose beginning is: "Again the joy of the Annunciation."[^367]*

*We join also the 9th Hour with Kathisma and reading. After this, the Beatitudes quickly, without chanting, and without prostrations: "Remember us, O Lord." The lesser choir: "Glory." Choir: "The holy angel." "Remit, forgive." After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast. "Lord, have mercy" (40). "Glory, both now:" "More honorable than the Cherubim." And three great prostrations, and the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity." And dismissal.*

*And we sing Vespers without Kathisma and without prostrations.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we sing the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day, twice, without the Martyrikon; and 3 Podobna of the Triodion; and 3 of the Feast; and 3 of the Archangel.*

*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4. Idiomela:*

In the sixth month, the Archangel was sent unto the pure Virgin, and saying "Rejoice" unto her, he announced the good tidings that the Deliverer should come forth from her. Therefore, having received the greeting, she conceived Thee, the eternal God, Who ineffably wast well-pleased to become man for the salvation of our souls.

The Birthgiver of God heard a tongue which she knew not;[^368] for the Archangel spoke unto her words of good tidings. Whence, having faithfully received the greeting, she conceived Thee, the eternal God. Therefore we also rejoicing cry aloud unto Thee: O God Who wast incarnate of her without change, grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

Behold, our recalling has now appeared unto us: beyond word God is united unto men; by the Archangel's voice deception is driven away; for the Virgin receives joy, earthly things have become heaven, the world is loosed from the ancient curse. Let creation rejoice, and let it sing aloud with voices: O our Creator and Deliverer, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Stichera of the Archangel, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ въ мꙋ́ченицѣхъ):*

Gabriel the great, the most God-beholding mind, radiant and saving: he beholds the tri-sunned light, and with the ranks on high sings the divine and fearful song; he prays that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

The great mystery, previously unknown unto angels, and guarded before the ages: unto thee alone, O Gabriel, was it entrusted, and this to the pure one alone didst thou tell, having come to Nazareth; with her pray thou, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Refrain unto the verse:*

*Who makes His angels spirits, and His ministers a flame of fire.[^369]*

Ever being full of light, and doing the will, and fulfilling the commandments of the Ruler of all, O chief of the angels, all-excellent Gabriel, save them that honor thee with love, ever asking that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls.

*Glory, both now, Tone 6. Idiomelon:*

The Archangel Gabriel was sent from heaven to announce the conception unto the Virgin; and having come to Nazareth, he reasoned within himself, marveling at the wonder: O, how He Who in the highest is incomprehensible is born of a Virgin! He Who has heaven for His throne, and the earth for His footstool,[^370] is contained in the Virgin's womb! He upon Whom the six-winged and many-eyed cannot look, by a single word was well-pleased to be incarnate of her! The Word present is the Word of God; why then do I stand and say not unto the Virgin: Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee; rejoice, pure Virgin; rejoice, unwedded bride; rejoice, Mother of Life: blessed is the fruit of thy womb!

*And the priest makes the Proskomide during the Stichera. Entrance with the Gospel. "O Gentle Light." Prokeimenon, and the readings of the day, and two of the Feast: 1st, "Moses entered;"[^371] 2nd, "The Lord created Me."[^372] Then, the little litany, and the exclamation of the Trisagion, and the Trisagion.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

*Announce the good tidings from day to day of the salvation of our God.[^373]*

*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song, sing unto the Lord, all the earth.[^374]*

*The Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 306 [Heb. 2:11–18]: Brethren, both He that sanctifies and they who are sanctified are all of one...*

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*

*He shall come down like rain upon the fleece, and as drops that water the earth.[^375]*

*Verse: His name shall be blessed unto the ages; His name continues before the sun.[^376]*

*The Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 3 [Luke 1:24–38]: In those days, Elisabs conceived...*

*And the rest of the Divine Liturgy of Chrysostom.*

*Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos: "As the animate ark of God..."*

*Communion Hymn:*

*The Lord has chosen Sion, He has chosen it for a dwelling for Himself.[^377] [Alleluia.]*

*Note: We find in certain Typika that on this day of this honorable Feast there is sung: "Let my prayer arise;" and this, I know not by what custom it was appointed? For some out of self-will and lack of learning have added many things. But as I think, this should not be admitted into the full Liturgy of Chrysostom, both at the 6th Hour and when the Proskomide has taken place. For we say, on whatever day the feast of the divine Annunciation occurs, the Liturgy is of Chrysostom, and in the refectory there is dispensation for fish, on whatever day it falls. For this we have received from the divine Fathers, to eat fish on the day of the divine Annunciation. But "Let my prayer arise" began to be sung in the Liturgy of the Presanctified, and at the 4th hour; for there is no need for any sign of mourning to be sung on this day, for we honor it as the fountainhead of our salvation.*

*Note: If by reason of great necessity there is no Liturgy:*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Triodion, Podobna, 3; and of the Feast, 4; and of the Archangel, 3. Glory, both now, of the Feast. Entrance, Prokeimenon, and reading of the day, and of the Feast, 2. Then Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Gospel of the Feast; and after the reading, "Vouchsafe, O Lord." At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day, twice; and the Martyrikon. Glory, both now: "Today is the joy of the Annunciation." After "Now lettest Thou depart," Troparion of the Archangel. Glory, both now, of the Feast. Litany, and three great prostrations, and dismissal. In the refectory there is great consolation for the brethren: we eat* fish and drink wine, on whatever day it happens to fall, once in the day, except for Saturday and Sunday.

*We sing Small Compline in the narthex quickly, without prostrations and without canon: only at "Glory to God in the highest," three prostrations; and after the Trisagion, three prostrations. And after "More honorable," 3 great prostrations, with the prayer: "O Lord and Master of my life," and the other 12 [reverences]. And at the final Trisagion, 3 prostrations. And dismissal.*

*If the Annunciation falls on the 3rd or 4th Saturday of the Fasts:*

*On Friday evening, we say no Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," 10 Stichera: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day, 2; and of the Feast, 8. Glory, both now, of the Feast. And the readings: first of the day, and of the Feast, 5. And "Let my prayer arise;" and the rest of the Presanctified.*

*And at the 1st hour of the night, we sing Great Compline according to custom. After the 1st Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast. After the 2nd, Kontakion of the Feast. After "Glory to God in the highest," the procession, as was indicated for a fasting day. At Matins the entire service is of the Feast; neither the Four-ode Canon, nor anything else of Saturday is sung, but all of the Feast: and we sing also the Great Doxology. (The Four-ode Canon is sung on Thursday at Compline.) Likewise at the Liturgy, all is of the Feast. Before the Liturgy, at the 2nd hour of the day, we make the procession outside the monastery according to custom; and having returned, we sing the Liturgy. And the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Feast, Odes 3 and 6.*

*It is fitting to know: If the Annunciation falls on the 3rd Sunday of the Fasts [Sunday of the Cross-Veneration]:*

*On Saturday at Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Cross, 4; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, and 3 of the Feast, Idiomela (the Aposticha of Great Vespers, with their refrains); Glory, both now, of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, both now, of the Feast. Litany, and dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of the Cross, 3 Podobna; and of the Feast, 4 Podobna. Glory, of the Cross, Tone 3: "O Christ our God;" both now, of the Feast, Tone 6: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Then the Entrance, and 5 readings. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, 3 Idiomela, Tone 1; and of the Cross, 3 (written at the Praises). Glory, of the Feast; both now, of the Cross. At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional [Stichera] according to the alphabet. Glory, of the Cross; both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion* of the Feast, twice; and of the Cross, once; and reading from the Apostolic Epistles, or of the Feast.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion of the Tone, twice; Glory, of the Cross; both now, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia; and reading in the Commentary Gospel.*

*Polyeleos, and all the Sessional Hymns of the Feast, once each; Glory, both now, of the Feast. And reading of the Feast. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the Feast. "Let every breath praise the Lord," and Gospel of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ;" Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon: Resurrectional with Heirmos on 4; and of the Cross on 4; and of the Feast on 6. Katavasia of the Feast: "I shall open my mouth." After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Cross, and Ikos; then Sessional Hymns of the Cross; Glory, both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos; and the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." Exapostilarion: Resurrectional; Glory, of the Cross; both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Feast, 3, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ); and an Idiomelon, Tone 2: "The mystery which is from the ages;" with their refrains. Then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God;"[^378] and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the day. Glory, the same; both now: "Thou art most blessed." Great Doxology. And the order of the veneration of the Cross takes place according to custom; and after this, litanies, and dismissal. Glory, both now, the Evangelical Sticheron; and the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; and the 1st Hour in the narthex.*

*At the 1st Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Cross; both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Cross, and the rest; and the complete dismissal.*

*At the 2nd hour of the day we go forth outside the monastery, as was indicated before; and having returned, we sing the Hours, at which are said the Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Feast, and of the Cross, alternating, and likewise the Kontakia.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4; and of the Cross, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Cross, and of the Feast; Glory, Kontakion of the Cross; both now, of the Feast. Instead of the Trisagion we sing: "Thy Cross we adore, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn, first of the Feast, then of the Cross. Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos: "As the animate ark of God." The Liturgy is of St. Basil the Great.*

*Likewise also on all the Sundays of the Holy Fasts in which this Feast happens to fall, the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great is celebrated without variation.*

*If the Annunciation falls on Monday of the 4th, 5th, or 6th week of the Holy Fasts:*

*On Sunday at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Feast, on 4; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion of the day, twice, and the Martyrikon; Glory, both now, of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. Little litany, and dismissal. At Great Vespers, after the customary psalm, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, 3; and of the Feast, 7. Glory, both now: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; and 3 readings of the Feast. And after this the litany: "Let us all say;" then "Vouchsafe, O Lord;" "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, with the refrains of the Feast; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast. At Matins we sing the service of the Feast, as was indicated above for a fasting day in course.*

*And on Monday of the 4th week of the Fasts, at the 1st Hour, the veneration of the precious Cross takes place according to custom.*

*If the Annunciation falls on Wednesday of the Cross-Veneration [4th week]:*

*The Stichera, and Doxastica, and the Canon of the Cross are omitted; and they are sung on Monday or on Friday of the same week; but here we sing the entire service of the Annunciation with the mid-week Triodion as was indicated on the 1st day in course. And at Matins, at the 1st Hour, the veneration of the Cross takes place according to order.*

*If the Annunciation falls on the 4th or 5th Sunday of the Fasts:*

*On Saturday at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Feast, on 4; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, and the Aposticha of the Feast from Great Vespers, with their refrains; Glory, both now, of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast; little litany, and dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and one Anatolian [Eastern Sticheron]; and of the Feast, 6. Glory, both now, Tone 6: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; and 5 readings of the Feast. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast; Glory, Tone 8; both now, Tone 2. At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Stichera; Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading from the Apostolic Epistles, or of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory* both now, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia; and reading in the Commentary Gospel. Then the Polyeleos, and all the Sessional Hymns of the Feast, once each; and reading of the Feast. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the Feast. "Let every breath praise the Lord," and Gospel of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ;" Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon: Resurrectional with Heirmos on 4; and of the Triodion on 4; and of the Feast on 6. Katavasia: the Heirmos of the Feast. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Feast; Glory, both now, the same; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos; and we read the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." Exapostilarion: Resurrectional, and of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera on 8: Resurrectional, 4; and of the Feast, 4, with their refrains, and with the Doxasticon; then the refrain: "Arise, O Lord my God;"[^379] and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the day; Glory, the same again; both now: "Thou art most blessed." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Resurrectional Troparion; litanies, and dismissal. Then Glory, both now, the Evangelical Sticheron. And holy oil is given unto the brethren from the lamp of the All-Holy Birthgiver of God, while we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron of the Feast; and the procession into the narthex; and the 1st Hour. At the 1st Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Feast; both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast; and the rest, and the complete dismissal.*

*Note: At the 2nd hour of the day, we make the procession outside the monastery, as was indicated above in course; and having returned, we sing the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great. The Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4. After the Entrance, Resurrectional Troparion, and of the Feast; Glory, both now, Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn, first of Sunday, and then of the Feast.*

*If the Annunciation of the Most Pure Mother of God falls on Wednesday of the 5th week of the Fasts:*

*We sing the service of the Forefeast of the Annunciation, the Stichera and Canon, on Monday in course; and the Menaion of the Saint we sing at Compline. For the Great Canon is transferred from Thursday to Tuesday of the same week; and we sing the Great Canon with the Tuesday Tri-ode, for the sake of the feast of the Most Holy Mother of God: for on Wednesday, on the feast itself, no Kathisma is said, and no prostrations are made. And we sing the Apodosis of the Feast on Thursday. Therefore on Wednesday evening, Stichera with prostrations, and the Great Canon, are not sung on Thursday: for it was sung beforehand on Tuesday.*

*And on Wednesday of the 5th week of the Fasts, we sing the entire service, evening and morning, of the Feast with the Triodion, according to the Typikon, as was indicated above on a fasting day.
