And we sing the Apodosis of the Annunciation on Thursday at Matins with the Apostolic Tri-ode: as is shown on Monday of the 4th and 5th weeks of the Fast, for the Apodosis of the feast of the Annunciation.*

*If the Annunciation of the Most Pure Mother of God falls on Thursday of the 5th week of the Fasts, when the Great Canon is sung:*

*For the sake of the Feast, the Great Canon of Lord Andrew is transferred, and we sing it on Tuesday of the same week with the Tuesday Tri-ode; and the Apostolic Tri-ode we sing with the feast of the Annunciation; and afterwards the Katavasia of the Triodion.*

*If the Annunciation of the Most Pure Mother of God falls on Friday of the same week:*

*We sing the Stichera and Canon of the Forefeast of the Annunciation in advance on Wednesday in course, on account of the Great Canon; and the Saint from the Menaion we sing at Compline.*

*If the Annunciation of the Most Pure Mother of God falls on Saturday of the Akathist [5th Saturday of Great Lent]:*

*On Friday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day, 2; and of the Feast, on 8. Glory, both now, of the Feast: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance; and readings of the day, and of the Feast, 5. After "Let my prayer arise," 3 prostrations; and the rest of the Presanctified; and at "Blessed be the name of the Lord,"[^380] 3 prostrations; and at the dismissal, 3 prostrations.*

*Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck, and then all of them. And having gathered into the temple, the priest having given the blessing, we sing Great Compline. And the priest censes as is the custom. And after "Glory to God in the highest," we go forth into the narthex, and make the customary Litiya, and sing 3 Idiomela Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1; Glory, both now: "Gabriel announces good tidings;" and the customary prayer of the Vigil from the priest; and upon concluding, we enter again into the temple. And we sing at the Aposticha the Idiomela Stichera with their refrains. Glory, both now: "Today is the joy of the Annunciation." At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice. After the 1st Kathisma, the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader,"[^381] and 3 others, and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion; and reading of the Akathist. After the 2nd Kathisma, 3 Kontakia, and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader," as was shown before; and reading of the Akathist; and the Polyeleos; and all the Sessional Hymns of the Feast; and reading of the Feast.*

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone. Prokeimenon of the Feast. "Let every breath praise the Lord;" Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with Heirmos on 8; and of the Akathist on 6. Katavasia of the Feast, both choirs together. But the Four-ode Canon we do not sing here; rather we sing it beforehand, on Thursday at Compline. After Ode 3, 3 Kontakia, and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion. After this, Sessional Hymn, and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, 3 Kontakia, and 3 Ikoses; then the Kontakion: "O all-hymned Mother," thrice; and the 1st Ikos; and the Kontakion: "To thee, the Champion Leader;" and we read the Prologue, and the Synaxarion. At Ode 9 we do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim," but we sing the refrains of the Feast, as was indicated for the Feast itself. Unto the troparia of the Canon of the Akathist the refrain is: "Most Holy Mother of God, save us." Then at the Katavasia we sing the refrain and the Heirmos: "As the animate ark of God;" and a bow. Exapostilarion of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ), on 4; Glory, both now: "Let the heavens be glad;" Great Doxology; Troparion of the Feast; litanies, and dismissal. And holy oil is given unto the brethren according to custom; and the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; and the 1st Hour in the narthex.*

*At the 1st Hour, Troparion of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast, and the rest; and dismissal; and the procession outside the monastery. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Odes 3 and 6 on 8. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Feast.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Lazarus Saturday:*

*On Friday evening, we say no Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," the Idiomelon Sticheron of the day, twice: "Having accomplished the forty days to our soul's profit,"[^382] the short one; and of the Feast, 3: "The eternal counsel;" and of Saint Lazarus, 5. Glory: "Having accomplished the forty days to our soul's profit," the long one; both now, of the Feast: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance; Prokeimenon, and reading of the day; and of the Feast, 5 readings; and the rest of the Presanctified. In the refectory, dispensation for wine and oil.*

*Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck sufficiently, and then all of them. And having gathered into the temple, we sing Great Compline. After the 1st Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast. After the 2nd, Kontakion of the Feast. And after "Glory to God in the highest," the procession into the narthex. And we sing 3 Idiomela Stichera of the Feast, and 3 Idiomela of the Triodion (written at the Praises: "Wishing to show forth the glory of Thy Godhead, O Christ");[^383] Glory: "Let the heavens be glad;" both now: "Gabriel announces good tidings." At the Aposticha, the Idiomela Stichera of the Feast, with their refrains. Glory "Thou didst stand before the tomb;" both now: "Today is joy." After "Our Father," Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and blessing of the loaves, and reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory: "The general Resurrection;" both now, of the Feast. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Triodion, Tone 1: "Having had compassion on the tears of Martha and Mary," twice; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading concerning Lazarus. After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Triodion, Tone 5: "The fountain of wisdom," twice; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading concerning righteous Lazarus, or of the Feast. Then Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Gospel of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ;" and Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and of Saint Lazarus, both canons on 8; likewise also the four-ode canon. Katavasia of the 2nd canon of Lazarus, Heirmos: "Having traversed the water," both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of Saint Lazarus; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast and Ikos, and reading. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain of the Feast, as was prescribed on the Feast itself. And to the canon of the Triodion we say the refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee." Katavasia: "Truly the Mother of God;" then Refrain of the Feast, and Heirmos: "As the animate [Ark];" after this: "Holy is the Lord our God," in Tone 1. Exapostilarion of the Feast, twice; Glory and both now, of Lazarus. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of Lazarus, 3. Then the refrains: "Arise, O Lord my God;"[^384] and "I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart."[^385] Glory, of Saint Lazarus: "A great and marvelous [wonder];" both now, of the Feast: "The mystery hidden from the ages." Great Doxology. Troparion of the Feast; Glory and both now: "The general Resurrection." Litanies, and dismissal; and catechesis; and the 1st Hour, in the narthex: Troparion of the Feast; Glory: "The general Resurrection;" both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of Lazarus. Thus we say the Troparia on the other Hours as well; the Kontakia we alternate. And at the 2nd hour of the day, we make the procession outside the monastery. (Note: And having returned, we sing the Hours.) At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4; and of Lazarus, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and of Lazarus; Glory, Kontakion of Lazarus; both now, of the Feast. Instead of the Trisagion, we sing: "As many as have been baptized into Christ." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, first of the Feast, then of Lazarus.*

*...of Lazarus. And instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos of the Feast. Communion Hymn, first of the Feast, then of Saint Lazarus.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Palm Sunday:*

*On Saturday at Small Vespers, Stichera of the Palms, on 4; Glory and both now, of the Feast: "In the sixth month." At the Aposticha, the Idiomela Stichera of the Feast from Great Vespers, with their refrains; Glory and both now, of the Palms: "I sing of Thy fearful [dispensation]." Troparion of the Feast; Glory and both now: "The general Resurrection." Small Litany, and dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3, repeating them to 6; and of the Palms, on 4. Glory, of the Palms: "Today the grace [of the Holy Spirit];" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day, and readings of the Feast, 5; and of the Palms, 3. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Palms, 3. Glory, of the Palms: "Six days before [the Passover];" both now, of the Feast: "Gabriel announces good tidings." At the Aposticha, Idiomela Stichera of the Palms, Tone 8, with their refrains; Glory, of the Palms: "Today the grace [of the Holy Spirit];" both now, of the Feast: "The mystery hidden from the ages." At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice; and of the Palms, once; and reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory and both now: "The general Resurrection." After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns of the Palms; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading. After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Palms; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading. Then Polyeleos of the Feast, and Sessional Hymns, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon of the Palms; "Every breath;" Gospel of the Palms. Psalm 50. But we do not say "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ." And when the brethren kiss the Holy Gospel, the Superior distributes the palms with candles to the brethren, according to the custom. And instead of "Through the intercessions of the Mother of God," we sing: Glory: "Today Christ [enters the holy city];" and both now, the same. Then: "Have mercy on me, O God." After this, Sticheron, Tone 6: "Today the grace [of the Holy Spirit]." Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8; and of the Palms with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia of both canons, each choir its own Heirmos. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Palms, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain of the Feast, as was prescribed. And of the Palms we sing according to custom, with the refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee." And at the Katavasia, we sing first the Refrain of the Feast, and the Heirmos of the Feast: "As the animate [Ark];" and afterwards of the Palms. Then: "Holy is the Lord our God," in Tone 4.*

*Exapostilarion of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Palms, 3. Glory, of the Feast: "Let the heavens be glad;" both now, of the Palms: "Six days before [the Passover]." Great Doxology. Troparion: "The general Resurrection;" Glory and both now, of the Feast. Litanies, and dismissal, and catechesis; and the 1st Hour, in the narthex; and dismissal.*

*And at the 2nd hour of the day, we go forth with a Litiya outside the monastery, as was indicated in order; and having returned, we sing the Hours. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory: "The general Resurrection;" both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakia. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast (Note), Ode 3 on 4; and of the Palms, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, we say the verse: "Proclaim from day to day the salvation of our God."[^386] Troparion of the Feast, and of the Palms: "The general Resurrection;" Glory, Kontakion of the Palms: "Upon the throne in heaven;" both now, of the Feast: "To thee, the Champion Leader." Then Prokeimenon of the Palms, and of the Feast. Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn, of the Feast (Note), and of the Palms. At the Liturgy: Instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos of the Palms: "God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us." But if it is a temple of the Annunciation of the Mother of God, we sing instead of "It is truly meet" the Heirmos: "As the animate [Ark]."*

*If the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God falls on Great Monday:*

*On Palm Sunday, at Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, on 4; Glory and both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast from Great Vespers; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st Antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, on 6; and of the Palms, on 4. Glory, of the Palms, Tone 6: "He who hath heaven as His throne;" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; readings of the Feast, 3. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast; Glory and both now, of the Feast: "Gabriel announces good tidings." At the Aposticha, Stichera of the day, Idiomela, with their refrains; Glory, of the Palms: "From branches and palms;" both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast. After the 1st Kathisma and after the 2nd, Sessional Hymns of the day; and reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma, Small Litany. Sessional Hymns of the Feast, both; and reading of the Feast. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymns, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "Proclaim from day to day;" "Every breath;" Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast.*

*(Note: The Gospel of the day is omitted, if the Annunciation falls on any day of this week.)*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and the Triodion (three-ode canon) on 8. Katavasia of the Triodion: the Heirmos, both choirs together. But where there is no Triodion: we sing the canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 14. Katavasia of the Feast. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos; and Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. Prologue, and Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain of the Feast: "Proclaim good tidings, O earth;" then the Heirmos of the Feast: "As the animate [Ark]." To the troparia of the Triodion, the refrain: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee." Then we sing the Heirmos of the Triodion; afterwards, the Refrain of the Feast, and the Heirmos: "As the animate [Ark]." Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, on 4; Glory, of the Feast: "The mystery hidden from the ages;" both now, of the day: "The withered fig tree." Then the Superior or reader says: "Glory to Thee Who hast shown us the light." Litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer." At the Aposticha, Idiomela Stichera of the day, Tone 1: "The Lord as He comes;" refrain: "We were filled [in the morning with Thy mercy];"[^387] "Having attained, O ye faithful;" refrain: "We have rejoiced for all our days, for the days wherein Thou didst humble us;"[^388] "O Lord, as Thou comest to the Passion;" refrain: "And look upon Thy servants;"[^389] "O Lord, unto the mystery;" refrain: "And let the brightness [of the Lord our God be upon us];"[^390] "O Lord, the most perfect [wisdom]:" Glory, Tone 8: "A second Eve;" both now, of the Feast: "Let the heavens be glad." Then: "It is a good thing to give thanks unto the Lord,"[^391] twice. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast. Litany; and 3 great prostrations. After this, "O come, let us worship," thrice; and the 1st Hour. Troparion of the Feast; both now, Theotokion: "What shall we call thee?" After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast; and at the end, 3 great prostrations. Then: "O Christ, the true Light;" and dismissal.*

*(Note: And at the 3rd hour of the day, we make the Litiya outside the monastery. And having returned, we sing the 3rd Hour and the 6th, with Kathismata and with reading; and we read the Tetra-Gospel. And at them is said the Troparion of the Feast; we alternate the Kontakia of the day and of the Feast. And at the end of each Hour, 3 great prostrations. Likewise we sing also the 9th Hour; and the Beatitudes simply, without singing; and "Remember us, O Lord;" and the rest. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast. "Lord, have mercy," 40. Glory and both now: "More honorable;" and 3 great prostrations. Immediately we begin Vespers...) without prostrations, and without a Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day: "The withered fig tree;" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 2. Then Small Litany, and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Alleluia, of the Feast; Gospel of the Feast, and of the day. And the rest of the Liturgy of Chrysostom. Communion Hymn of the Feast.*

*(Note: And where, on account of great necessity, there is no Liturgy:)*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: and we sing of the day, 3, Tone 1, repeating the first two; and of the Feast, 5. Glory, of the day, Tone 8: "A second Eve;" both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 2. Then Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, of the Feast, and of the day. "Vouchsafe, O Lord." Litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Aposticha, of the day, Tone 5: "O Lord, the most perfect wisdom," twice, with the refrains of the day. The other verse, Tone 8: "The withered fig tree;" Glory, Tone 5: "O Lord, as Thou comest to the Passion;" both now, of the Feast. "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and dismissal. Thus we sing also on the other days of Passion Week. In the refectory, dispensation for oil and wine only. At Compline, there are no prostrations, except after "Glory to God in the highest," 3 prostrations. At the Trisagion, 3 prostrations; at the end, 3 great prostrations, and 12 small. At the final Trisagion, 3 prostrations. And we sing the Triodion (three-ode canon) of Lord Andrew.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Great Tuesday:*

*On Monday evening, we do not say a Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day: "A second Eve;" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 3. "Let my prayer be set forth;"[^392] and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day; and the rest of the Presanctified. In the refectory, we drink wine.*

*(Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck, and then all of them likewise. And having gathered into the temple, we sing Great Compline. "God is with us:" we sing with both choirs, with chanting. The priest at the beginning censes according to custom. And after "Glory to God in the highest," the procession into the narthex. And we sing at the Litiya the Idiomela Stichera of the Feast; Glory and both now, of the Feast: "Gabriel announces good tidings." At the Aposticha, Stichera...) Idiomela of the Feast, 3, with their refrains; Glory and both now: "Today is joy." At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice. After both Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Triodion; and reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma, Small Litany, and Sessional Hymns of the Feast; and reading. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon; "Every breath;" Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 14. Katavasia of the Feast: the Heirmos, both choirs together. But where there is a two-ode canon of the Triodion: we sing the canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; the two-ode canon on 8. The last Heirmos of the Triodion, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos; and we read the Prologue. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain and Heirmos of the Feast, as was indicated on Great Monday. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera, by [rule]: of the Feast, on 4; Glory, of the day: "With slothfulness of soul;" both now, of the Feast: "The mystery hidden from the ages." At the Aposticha, Idiomela of the day with their refrains, as was prescribed on Monday: "We were filled in the morning;" Glory, of the Feast: "Let the heavens be glad;" both now, of the day, Tone 6: "When Thou shalt come in glory." Troparion of the Feast; Litany; and 3 great prostrations. Then: "O come, let us worship," thrice; the 1st Hour. At the end thereof, 3 great prostrations. And the prayer: "O Christ, the true Light;" and dismissal.*

*(Note: And at the 3rd hour of the day, we make the Litiya outside the monastery. And having returned, we sing the Hours with Kathismata; and the Tetra-Gospel is read. We say at the Hours the Troparion of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. The Kontakia we say of the day and of the Feast, alternating them. And at the end of each Hour, 3 great prostrations. And the 9th Hour without a Kathisma. The Beatitudes quickly, without singing; and "Remember us, O Lord;" and the rest, as was prescribed. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast. "Lord, have mercy," 40. Glory and both now: "More honorable;" and 3 great prostrations. Immediately we begin Vespers; "O come, let us worship," thrice. Note: We sing Vespers without a Kathisma, and without prostrations.)*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Idiomela of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 2. Small Litany, and exclamation of the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, and Alleluia, of the Feast; Gospel of the Feast, and of the day. And the rest of the Liturgy of Chrysostom. Communion Hymn of the Feast. In the refectory, we dispense for oil and wine only. And we sing Small Compline, without prostrations; and we sing upon it also the Triodion (three-ode canon) of Lord Andrew of Crete.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Great Wednesday:*

*On Tuesday evening, we sing Vespers without a Kathisma, and without prostrations. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day, Tone 7: "Unto thee, the talent;" both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 3. "Let my prayer be set forth;" and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day; and the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified. In the refectory, cooked food without oil; but we drink wine.*

*(Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck, and all of them likewise. And we sing Great Compline. "God is with us," with both choirs. The priest censes at the beginning according to custom. And after "Glory to God in the highest," the procession into the narthex; and we sing at the Litiya the Stichera of the Feast; Glory and both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera; and Glory and both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice. And after both Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Triodion; and reading of the day. After the 3rd Kathisma, Litany; and Sessional Hymns of the Feast, both; and reading of the Feast. Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon; "Every breath;" Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 14. Katavasia of the Feast, the Heirmos, both choirs together. Note: But where there is a Triodion (three-ode canon): we sing the canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Triodion, on 8. Katavasia, the Heirmos of the Triodion. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the refrains of the Feast, as was indicated beforehand. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast on 4; Glory, of the day, Tone 6: "Today Christ comes;" both now, of the Feast, Tone 2: "The mystery hidden from the ages." At the Aposticha, of the day, with their refrains, as was prescribed on Great Monday; Glory, of the Feast...) Tone 8: "Let the heavens be glad;" both now, of the day, the same tone: "O Lord, the woman that had fallen into many sins;"[^393] and the rest according to what was written before.*

*(Note: And at the 3rd hour of the day, we make the procession outside the monastery; and having returned, we sing the Hours with Kathismata; and the Tetra-Gospel is read. And the rest of the Hours and of the Liturgy, as was prescribed on Monday and Tuesday of this week. And at the end of the Hours there is mutual forgiveness in the monastery according to custom. And we sing Compline according to what was prescribed, and the Triodion (three-ode canon) of Lord Andrew of Crete.)*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Great Thursday:*

*On Great Wednesday evening, we sing Vespers without a Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day; both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 3. "Let my prayer be set forth;" and 3 prostrations. Gospel of the day; and the rest of the Presanctified. In the refectory, we drink wine.*

*(Note: At the 1st hour of the night, the great bell is struck, and all of them likewise. And having gathered into the temple, we sing Great Compline. The priest censes according to custom. And after "Glory to God in the highest," the procession into the narthex; and we sing at the Litiya the Idiomela Stichera; Glory and both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Stichera; and Glory and both now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and reading of the Feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory and both now: "When the glorious disciples."[^394] Then Polyeleos; and Sessional Hymn, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon; "Every breath;" and Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, the Heirmos twice; and of the day with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia, the Heirmos of the day, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast; and Ikos, and Prologue, and Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the refrains of the Feast, as was indicated beforehand. Afterwards, Heirmos: "Come, O ye faithful, let us enjoy the Master's hospitality;" then Refrain of the Feast, and Heirmos. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast on 4; Glory, of the Feast; and both now, of the day. After this, "Glory to God in the highest," and Litany. Then at the Aposticha, Stichera of the day with their refrains; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. After "It is a good thing to give thanks;" and after "Our Father," Troparion of the day; Glory...) both now, of the Feast; and Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God." Then: "O come, let us worship," thrice; and the 1st Hour, at which is the Troparion, and the Paremia of the day, and the catechesis of the Feast; and dismissal.*

*(Note: And at the 5th hour of the day, the procession takes place outside the monastery; and having returned, we sing the Hours in the narthex: the 3rd, 6th, and 9th; at which we say the Troparion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," we alternate the Kontakia. "Remember us, O Lord;" and the rest, with mute choir. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast; "Lord, have mercy," 40; and the prayer: "O All-Holy Trinity;" and dismissal. "I will bless the Lord at all times,"[^395] we do not say.)*

*In the evening, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones, at the 8th hour of the day; and we sing Great [Vespers]. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light;" and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 2. Small Litany; then Trisagion. Prokeimenon of the day, and of the Feast; Epistle of the day, and of the Feast. And the rest of the Liturgy of Basil the Great. Instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos: "Come, O ye faithful, let us enjoy the Master's hospitality." (Note: But in a temple of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God, the Heirmos: "As the animate [Ark].") And instead of the Cherubic Hymn, we sing the Troparion, Tone 6: "Of Thy mystical supper," thrice; the same we sing also instead of the Communion Hymn, once. And when the brethren receive Communion, we sing instead of "Receive the Body of Christ," the same: "Of Thy mystical supper;" and instead of "Let our mouths be filled," once, with Alleluias. (Note) In the refectory, we do not dispense for fish, but only for oil and wine.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Great Friday:*

*On Thursday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the day, 5; and of the Feast, 5; Glory, of the day: "Offspring of vipers;" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 3. Small Litany, and exclamation, and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day; and the rest of the Divine Liturgy of Basil the Great, as was explained previously. In the refectory, we dispense for oil and wine. We sing Small Compline with the Triodion (three-ode canon). After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast.*

*(Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones, and we begin Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory and both now, of the day: "When the glorious disciples." After this, Small Litany. And immediately the deacon...) or priest says: "And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel." And the Superior reads the 1st Gospel; and afterwards the Antiphons, and the Gospels of the Holy Passions, according to their order. And after the reading of the 7th Gospel, the reading of the day. After this, Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon of the Feast; "Every breath;" Gospel of the Feast; and immediately the 8th Gospel of the Holy Passions. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Then the prayer: "O God, save Thy people;" and we: "Lord, have mercy," 12. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 14. Katavasia of the Feast, both choirs together. But where there is a Triodion (three-ode canon): we sing the canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6, the Heirmos twice; and the Triodion on 8; Katavasia, the Heirmos of the Triodion, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos; Glory and both now, Sessional Hymn of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos, and Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain of the Feast. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Then the 9th Gospel. At the Praises, Idiomela Stichera of the day; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Then the 10th Gospel. After this, "Glory to God in the highest;" Litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer;" and exclamation. After this, the 11th Gospel. Then at the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Then the 12th Gospel. After this: "It is a good thing to give thanks unto the Lord;"[^396] and after "Our Father," Troparion, Tone 4: "Thou hast redeemed us [from the curse of the law];"[^397] Glory and both now, of the Feast. Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God." Priest: "Wisdom;" and dismissal. And we sing the 1st Hour as was indicated on Great Friday: we say at it the Troparion of the Feast, and of the day, and the Kontakion of the Feast.*

*And at the 5th hour of the day, we make the procession outside the monastery; and having returned, we sing the 3rd Hour, the 6th, and the 9th, with Troparia, and Paremias, with the Epistles, and Gospels, and readings, and with their remaining service. And at them are said the Troparion of the Hours of Great Friday, which is of the day, and of the Feast; Kontakion of the day, and of the Feast; and the rest as was indicated on Great Friday. But Psalm 33 we omit here, and say it at the Liturgy. And at the 8th hour of the day, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones; we begin Vespers, and the priest performs the Proskomedia. At "Lord, I have cried," we sing Stichera of the day, 6; and of the Feast, 4; Glory, of the day: "The whole assembly of transgressors;" both now, of the Feast: "Was sent [the Archangel Gabriel]." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, and readings of the day; and of the Feast, 2. Then Small Litany, and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel, first of the Feast, and of the day. Communion Hymn of the Feast. And the Divine Liturgy of Chrysostom is celebrated. Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos of the Feast: "As the animate [Ark]." In the refectory, we eat dry food (xerophagy), and drink wine.*

*Concerning the same Feast: If it falls on Great Saturday:*

*On Friday evening, at the 10th hour of the day, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones; and we sing Vespers. At "Lord, I have cried," Idiomela Stichera of the day, on 6; and of the Feast, on 4; Glory, of the day; and both now, of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, and readings of the day, and of the Feast, 5. Then Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day. After this, the Litany: "Let us all say;" then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening;" "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Aposticha, Stichera of the day with their refrains; Glory, of the Feast; and both now, of the day. Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart;" after the Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast; Glory and both now: "The noble Joseph." Priest: "Wisdom;" and dismissal. We sing Compline in the cells; Kontakion of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast.*

*(Note: And at the 1st hour of the night, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones; and we sing Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the day, Tone 2: "The noble Joseph," once; "When Thou didst descend unto death," once; Glory: "Unto the Myrrh-bearing women;" and both now, of the Feast: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation." Then we sing the Blameless (Kathisma 17), in Tone 5. The priest censes at the 1st "Glory," likewise also at the 2nd and at the 3rd, according to custom. At the conclusion of the Blameless, we sing the Resurrectional Troparia: "The assembly of angels," and the rest. Then Small Litany, and Sessional Hymn of the day; then Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon of the Feast; "Every breath;" Gospel of the Feast. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos on 8; the Heirmos twice; and of the day, with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia, the Heirmos of the day, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the day and Ikos, and Sessional Hymn of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos, Prologue, and Synaxarion. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the refrains of the Feast, as was indicated. Afterwards, Heirmos of the day: "Weep not for Me, O Mother;" then Refrain of the Feast, and Heirmos. "Holy is the Lord our God,"[^398] in Tone 2. Exapostilarion...)* of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Stichera, Idiomela of the day, 3; and of the Feast, Prosomoia, 3; then Idiomelon of the Feast, Tone 8: "Let the heavens rejoice," with the refrain: "Proclaim from day to day the salvation of our God;"[^399] Glory, of the day; and both now: "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. And the entrance takes place as written in the Triodion. After this, Prokeimenon and Paremia of the day. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day. Litany, and dismissal. And the 1st Hour we sing in the narthex; and the remaining Hours are sung at their proper time.*

*At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast, and of the day; we say the Kontakia, alternating them. The Beatitudes quickly, without singing: "Remember us, O Lord," and the rest. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the day; Glory and both now, Kontakion of the Feast. "Lord, have mercy," 40; and prayer: "O All-Holy Trinity;" and dismissal.*

*On Holy and Great Saturday evening, at the 10th hour of the day, the bell is struck in the great bell and in all the heavy ones. For having assembled in the temple, and the priest having given the blessing, we sing Vespers according to custom without a Kathisma.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we sing Stichera: Resurrectional, 3, Tone 1: "Accept our evening prayers;" and of Great Saturday, Idiomela, 3, Tone 8; and of the Feast, on 4. Glory, of the day, Tone 6: "This present day mystically;" and both now, of the Feast: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." We do not say the Prokeimenon, but immediately the priest says: "Wisdom;" and the readings of the day, according to their order. And at the conclusion of the readings, Small Litany, and exclamation. And instead of the Trisagion, we sing: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia."[^400] Prokeimenon of the day, and of the Feast. Epistle of the day, and of the Feast. And instead of the Cherubic Hymn, there is sung: "Let all mortal flesh keep silence;"[^401] and in order the Divine Liturgy of Basil the Great. Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos: "Weep not for Me, O Mother." But in a temple of the Annunciation of the Mother of God, we sing the Heirmos: "As the animate [Ark]." Communion Hymn of the day, and of the Feast.*

*It is meet to know: That if the Feast of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God falls on the very Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha (which is Kyriopascha):*[^402]

*On Great Saturday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses; and we sing Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of Great Saturday, 3; and of the Feast, 4. Glory, Tone 6: "This present day mystically;" and both now, of the Feast, the same Tone: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance with the Gospel. "O gladsome Light." The Prokeimenon is not said, but immediately the priest says: "Wisdom;"* And the readings: 1st reading of the day: "In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth:"[^403] one Paremia. Then of the Feast of the Annunciation, 5 readings. Then also the remaining readings of the day, according to their order. And in order the Divine Liturgy of Basil the Great. And after the dismissal of the Liturgy, there is eating, and the reading of the Acts of the Holy Apostles according to their order, as was indicated on Great Saturday. And the reading having finished, the priest having given the blessing, we say the Trisagion; and after "Our Father," "Lord, have mercy," 12; Glory and both now: "O come, let us worship," thrice. Then Psalm 50. And we sing the Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos on 6; and of Great Saturday, with the Heirmos on 8. Katavasia, the Saturday Heirmos, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, and Sessional Hymn of the day; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the day, and Ikos; and reading of the day. After Ode 9, the Trisagion; and after "Our Father," Troparion: "When Thou didst descend unto death;" Glory and both now, of the Feast. Litany, and dismissal.*

*And if there is time early, the reading of the day is added; while it is being read, the Superior distributes candles to the brethren. The lamplighter, having taken the blessing from the Superior, goes and strikes in all [the bells]. And after the reading, we go out into the narthex, with lamps preceding the Superior; and the doors having been shut, he censes the entire brotherhood according to rank. And standing before the great church doors, he censes in the form of a cross, thrice, and says: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial;" and we having answered: "Amen." And the Superior begins the Troparion, Tone 5: "Christ is risen from the dead," thrice. Then the brethren sing it thrice. Superior, verse 1: "Let God arise;"[^404] and the brethren: "Christ is risen," once. Superior, verse 2: "As smoke vanishes;"[^405] and the brethren: "Christ is risen," once. Superior, verse 3: "So let the sinners perish;"[^406] and the brethren: "Christ is risen," once. Superior, verse 4: "This is the day which the Lord hath made;"[^407] and the brethren: "Christ is risen," once. And immediately the Superior with a loud voice: "Christ is risen;" and he opens the doors, and the Superior enters with the precious cross, with lamps preceding him, and the brethren singing: "And upon those in the tombs bestowing life." And when he enters into the sanctuary, we say "Glory," and sing the Troparion of the Feast, Tone 4: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation;" and both now, the same. Then the Litany Great Litany: "In peace let us pray to the Lord." And after the exclamation, the Superior begins the Heirmos: "The Day of Resurrection;" and we sing the Canon of Pascha with the Heirmos on 8, the heirmoi twice; and the Canon of the Annunciation with the Heirmos on 8, the heirmoi twice. And afterwards the heirmoi of both choirs, each choir its own; then Katavasia: "The Day of Resurrection," both choirs together; and the Troparion: "Christ is risen," thrice. And after each ode, Small Litany, and exclamations according to their order. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha, and the Hypakoe; and the reading in the Theologian, whereof the beginning is: "I will stand upon my watch."[^408] After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast of the Annunciation, and Ikos; and the reading also in the Theologian, whereof the beginning is: "It is the Day of Resurrection."[^409] Then Prokeimenon of the Feast, Tone 4: "Proclaim from day to day;" verse: "Sing unto the Lord a new song." "Every breath;" Gospel from Luke, pericope 4: "In those days, Mary arose."[^410] Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice; and the Sticheron: "Jesus having risen," thrice, in Tone 6. After this, Ode 7 and the remaining two. At Ode 9, we sing the refrains of Pascha, and of the Feast. Exapostilarion of Pascha, once; and of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera on 4; and of the Annunciation on 4, with their verses; then both choirs together, 1st verse: "Let God arise;" Stichera of Pascha, Tone 5, with their remaining verses; Glory, of the Annunciation, Tone 8: "Let the heavens rejoice;" and both now, of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection." And the kissing (Paschal greeting) according to custom. And the homily of Chrysostom. Litanies, and dismissal. The procession outside the monastery does not take place. The Hours are sung according to the Typikon of Pascha. At the Liturgy, the Antiphons of Pascha. And after the Entrance we say: "In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel."[^411] "Christ is risen," thrice; Glory, Troparion: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation;" and both now, Kontakion: "Though Thou didst descend into the grave." Instead of the Trisagion, we sing: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel: first of Pascha, then of the Feast.*

*(Note: And when the time arrives at the Liturgy to read the Holy Gospel, the priest reads the Gospel of Pascha with the deacon, as was indicated at the Liturgy on Sunday. Then a single deacon reads the Gospel of the Annunciation according to custom, as always. And for that Gospel they do not strike in the bell. And in order the Divine Liturgy of Chrysostom. Instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos of Pascha. Communion Hymn of Pascha, and of the Feast.)*

*Concerning the same Feast: If the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God falls on Monday, on Tuesday, or on Wednesday of Bright Week:*

*On the Holy and Great Sunday of Pascha: At Small Vespers, after the priest says: "Christ is risen," thrice, and the customary verses; Glory and both now: "Christ is risen." At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, on 4; Glory and both now, of the Feast, Tone 2: "The mystery from all eternity;" then "O gladsome Light" without entrance. Great Prokeimenon: "Who is a great God,"[^412] and its verse; then "Vouchsafe, O Lord." At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Sticheron, 1; then Idiomela Stichera of the Feast, 3, Tone 4, from Great Vespers Aposticha, with their refrains; Glory: "Gabriel announces the glad tidings;" and both now: "The Day of Resurrection." "Christ is risen," thrice; Glory and both now, Troparion: "Today is the fountainhead of our salvation." Small Litany, and dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, while the priest censes, and when he says: "Glory to the holy, and consubstantial;" he sings: "Christ is risen," thrice, and its verses; Glory and both now: "Christ is risen," thrice; then Litany. At "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and Anatolian (Eastern), 1; and of the Feast, 6. Glory and both now, of the Feast: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Entrance. "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon, Tone 7: "Who is a great God like our God," with its verses. And 5 readings of the Feast. (If on Sunday evening, after this the priest says: "And that we may be accounted worthy;" Gospel according to John, pericope 65: "The same day at evening;" ending: "I will not believe.")[^413] Then Litany. At the Litiya, Idiomela Stichera of the Feast; Glory and both now: "Gabriel announces the glad tidings." At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Sticheron, 1; then the refrain: "Let God arise;" and we sing the Stichera of Pascha, with their refrains; Glory, of the Feast: "Today is the joy;" and both now, of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection;" and at the end: "Christ is risen," thrice. Troparion of the Feast, thrice; and blessing of the loaves, and distribution, and reading of the Feast.*

*At Matins: after the priest gives the blessing, and says: "Christ is risen," thrice, and its verses; and after the conclusion, Glory, Troparion of the Feast; and both now, the same; then Great Litany; and Polyeleos, and Sessional Hymns, and reading of the Feast. Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4; Prokeimenon and Gospel of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," thrice. Psalm 50 we do not say; but immediately: Glory: "Through the intercessions of the Mother of God;" and both now, the same. Then the refrain in Tone 6: "Proclaim from day to day;" and we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron Idiomelon Sticheron of the Feast, Tone 6: "The Archangel Gabriel was sent." Then the priest says the prayer: "O God, save Thy people;" "Lord, have mercy," 12. And after the exclamation, we sing the Canon of Pascha with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8; Heirmoi of Pascha and of the Feast, twice each. Katavasia of the Annunciation, the Heirmos, both choirs together. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; and readings of the day. Then: "Jesus having risen," thrice. At Ode 9, refrains of Pascha, and of the Feast. Exapostilarion of Pascha, 1; and of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Stichera of the Tone, 3; and of the Feast, Prosomoia, 3; then: "Let God arise;" and we sing the Stichera of Pascha with their refrains; Glory, of the Feast: "Let the heavens rejoice;" and both now, of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection." "Christ is risen," thrice; Glory and both now, Troparion of the Feast. Litanies, and dismissal. And the holy oil is given to the brethren. And the Litiya takes place outside the monastery. The Hours we sing of Pascha according to custom.*

*At the Liturgy, the Antiphons of Pascha. At the Entrance, we say the verse: "In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel."[^414] "Christ is risen," thrice. Troparion of the Feast; Glory, Kontakion of Pascha; and both now, of the Feast. Instead of the Trisagion: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel: first of the Annunciation, then of the day. And in order the Divine Liturgy of Chrysostom. Communion Hymn of the day, and of the Feast.*

*(Note: And let it be known that not by our own discretion [did we determine] how the service of the Annunciation ought to be sung during the days of the Holy Forty Days, and in the week of the Great Sunday, on Holy Pascha, and what hath been commanded concerning this. For having closely followed the Typikon of the Great Church, and having examined the Synaxaria of the venerable Studite monastery and of others; and not only so, but having gone through the Jerusalem Typika, we have established these things. Let no one therefore marvel at the changes that take place—both in Stichera and Canons, and in readings, Prokeimena and Epistles, Alleluias and Gospels, and Communion Hymns—which have been commanded so to be changed; but let him examine the day on which the Paramon (Eve) of the Annunciation falls, and likewise the day of the Feast itself; and thou shalt find that when the Paramon falls on a fasting day, for the sake of the honor of the Holy Forty Days we sing the Stichera of the day, and afterwards of the Annunciation, and the Liturgy of the Presanctified. And from that point the Feast takes its beginning, and all things precede...) all things precede those of the Annunciation.*

*(Note: It is meet to know that if this Feast falls on whichever day of the Great Week, or on Lazarus Saturday, or on Palm Sunday, we have established beforehand the service of the Annunciation, since this Feast is prior to the Passion of Christ, in order that there be no fasting on it. Furthermore, we have judged concerning the great days of Holy Week, if the Annunciation falls upon them: at Vespers the Stichera of the day precede, and afterwards those of the Annunciation, for the sake of the fast, as we said beforehand, and for the surpassing glory of the day. And that the Proskomedia take place on Great Friday if the Annunciation falls thereon, we have established; for even if the Canons command otherwise, yet we found in the Great Cathedral Church, in the shrines of the Mother of God, that a complete Divine Liturgy with prosphoras takes place: therefore, these things being so, we have established on Great Friday the Liturgy of Chrysostom.)*

*(Note: Chapter concerning the Apodosis of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God:)*

*It is meet to know: that if the Annunciation falls on Friday of the 3rd or 4th week of the Fasts, we sing the entire service of the Feast with the Triodion according to the Typikon, as was indicated beforehand in order. At Matins on Saturday the 10th [hour], we sing the Apodosis of the Feast. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory, of the Archangel; and both now, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Feast, and reading of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8; and of the Archangel on 6. Katavasia of the Feast. But the Four-Ode Canon we do not sing here, but sing it beforehand at Compline. After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Archangel, and Sessional Hymn, twice; Glory and both now, of the Feast; and reading. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos. At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the Refrain of the Feast; then the Heirmos; and at each Troparion of the Feast we sing the same Refrain; and afterwards the Heirmos. Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the Archangel; and both now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera on 6: of the Feast, 3; and of the Archangel, 3; Glory and both now, of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Archangel; Glory and both now, of the Feast. Litanies, and dismissal, and the 1st Hour. At the 1st Hour, Troparion of the Feast, and of the Archangel, and Kontakion of the Feast, and the rest; and complete dismissal. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 and 6. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and of the Archangel; Glory, Kontakion of the Archangel; and both now, of the Feast. Prokeimenon of the Feast; Epistle* Alleluia, and Gospel of the day, and of the Feast. Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing the Heirmos of the Feast. Communion Hymn of the Feast.*

*(Note: But if the Apodosis of the Annunciation falls on Sunday, the 3rd of the Fasts [Sunday of the Holy Cross]: On Saturday at Small Vespers, customarily from the Triodion. *(Note: We sing the service of the Archangel Gabriel on Monday.)*

Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the day, and of the Feast. Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing: "All creation rejoices in thee."*

*If the Apodosis of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God falls on Monday of the 4th, 5th, or 6th week of the Fasts:*

*On Sunday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Triodion, 3; and of the Feast, 4; and of the Archangel, 3. Glory and both now, of the Feast. Entrance. Great Prokeimenon.[^419] Litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer." At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Triodion of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day, 2; and the Martyrion. Glory, both now, of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Troparia according to the Lenten order, and 3 great prostrations, and the Dismissal. Small Compline without prostrations; prostrations begin from "Glory to God in the highest." At it we say the daily Canon of the Menaion. On Monday at Matins we sing the entire service, as is written in the order on the 26th day.*

*If the Apodosis of the Annunciation falls on Wednesday of the 4th Week of the Fasts, which is the Cross-Veneration week:*
*On Tuesday evening, at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day, 2; and the Martyrion; and the Prosomoia of the day, 3; and of the Feast, 4. Glory, of the Cross; and both now, of the Feast. Entrance. Prokeimenon, and the reading of the day, and of the Feast. And in order, the Divine Liturgy of Chrysostom. But the service of the Archangel we sing on another day, or at Compline. Compline and Matins we sing as was prescribed before.*
*At Matins, the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Cross on 4. Where there is the Triodion (Three-Ode Canon): we sing the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Cross on 2; and the Triodion we sing according to their order on 8; after which, the Heirmos of the Triodion. After Ode 3, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; and the Sessional Hymn of the Triodion; Glory, both now, of the Feast. After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Cross. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the Triodion. At the Praises, Stichera of the Cross on 4: written on Tuesday evening at "Lord, I have cried." Glory, both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, of the Triodion. Glory, the Idiomelon of the Triodion; and both now, the same again. At the First Hour, the Veneration of the Cross; the Hours with prostrations according to the Lenten order.*

*Note.*[^420]
*If the Apodosis of the Annunciation falls on Thursday of the 5th Week of the Fasts:*
*On Wednesday evening, and on Thursday at Matins, we sing the entire service of the Feast, with the Triodion, as was indicated for a fasting day.*
*And the Apodosis of the Annunciation we sing on Thursday at Matins with the Apostolos Three-Ode Canon: for the Great Canon is transferred, and is sung in anticipation on Tuesday of this same week.*

*If the Apodosis of the Feast of the Annunciation falls on Saturday of the Akathist:*
*On Friday evening we sing Stichera on 10: of the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day twice; and of the Feast of the Mother of God on 8. Glory, of the Triodion: "The mystery hidden from the ages;" and both now, of the Feast: "Gabriel was sent." Entrance; and the reading of the day; and of the Feast, two. Little Litany, and the Exclamation the Trisagion exclamation: "For holy art Thou, our God." Then we sing: "Holy God." Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse, of the Feast; and the rest of the Liturgy of Chrysostom. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Akathist three times. After the 1st Kathisma, 3 Kontakia and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion; and the reading of the Akathist. After the 2nd Kathisma, 3 Kontakia and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion; and reading. Psalm 50. The Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Akathist on 8. Katavasia: "I will open my mouth;" both choirs together. But the Four-Ode Canon we do not sing here, but sing it beforehand at Compline. After Ode 3, 3 Kontakia and 3 Ikoses; and again the 1st Kontakion; and then the Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and the reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, 3 Kontakia and 3 Ikoses; then the Kontakion: "O all-praised Mother," 3 times; and again the first Ikos and Kontakion, and reading. At Ode 9 we do not sing "More honorable," but sing the refrain of the Feast. Then the Heirmos: "As unto a living ark;" the Exapostilarion of the Feast twice. At the Praises, Stichera of the Triodion on 4; Glory, both now, of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast, litanies, and Dismissal, and the First Hour. At the First Hour, Troparion of the Feast. After the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast, and the rest; and the complete Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Akathist, Ode 3 and Ode 6. Prokeimenon: "My soul doth magnify the Lord." Epistle of the day, then of the Mother of God. Alleluia of the Mother of God. Gospel of the day, then of the Mother of God. In place of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos: "As unto a living ark." Communion Verse: "I will take the cup of salvation."*

*If the Apodosis of the Annunciation of the Most Holy Mother of God falls on Lazarus Saturday, or on Palm Sunday, or on one of the days of Passion Week, or of Bright Week: then there is no Apodosis of the Annunciation, and the Stichera and Canon of the Afterfeast and of the Archangel are sung at Compline on the preceding days of the 6th week.*

*(Ornamental woodcut tailpiece cartouche) (Decorative woodcut headpiece band)*
*In the same month, on the 26th day: Synaxis of the Archangel Gabriel.*

*At Matins, the usual Stichologies, and Sessional Hymns of the Triodion. Then Psalm 50. And the Canon of the Mother of God on 4; and of the Archangel on 6. Canon of the Mother of God, whose acrostic is according to the alphabet, in Tone 6:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having traversed the deep on foot as if upon dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparion:* Gabriel, radiant torch of the ranks on high, was sent today unto the Virgin, crying: Rejoice, O undefiled Bride; with thee is the Creator and Lord of all!

*The Mother of God:* I desire to know thy strange salutation, said the Virgin; for these words are unusual for mankind. Tell me rather, teaching me, what joy is this that thou hast spoken?

*The Angel:* Most plainly do I say unto thee, standing before thee with all reverence, O all-pure one; incline thou thine ear: The power of the Most High shall come upon thee, and thou shalt be overshadowed by the all-effecting Spirit.

*Another Canon of the Archangel. A composition of Joseph, in Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I will appear radiant in festival, and rejoicing will I sing her wonders.

*Troparion:* As thou art of light, drawing nigh through immaterial participations unto the transcendent, divine, and immaterial Light, I pray thee, O Archangel of the Lord: Enlighten my mind by thy prayers, that I may hymn thee.

*Troparion:* Let us form a divine choir today, rejoicing and honoring the foremost of the bodiless minds, who announced the ineffable joy that hath come into the world through goodness.

*Troparion:* Having thee as our greatest protector, and intercessor before God, and wall and fortress, O Gabriel, we who love thee are delivered from perils and from the harm of the serpent, as we praise thee.

*Theotokion:* When Gabriel beheld thee pure and all-undefiled, he cried aloud with radiance: Rejoice, O Lady, who knewest not wedlock,[^421] salvation of men, praise and glory of the Angels!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful *of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*The Mother of God:* I see that thou speakest deceitfully unto me now, O angel; for how can a material, corruptible, and earthly nature contain the Immaterial One, Who is clothed with the light of incorruption?

*The Angel:* Wisely hast thou spoken, O pure one, yet have good will thus; and let the bush convince thee, which burned yet was in no wise consumed by fire, prefiguring thine ineffable childbearing.

*The Mother of God:* Thou criest words of life unto me; yet the mountain that smoked of old when it received God convinces me that a material nature cannot at all receive the Immaterial One.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish thy singers, O Mother of God, living and bountiful fountain, who have gathered as a spiritual choir, and vouchsafe unto them crowns of glory in thy divine glory!

*Troparion:* By participation in the most immaterial Light, being truly immaterial, O Gabriel, and being enlightened immaterially, thou wast seen as a second light, ever enlightening material men who hymn thee.

*Troparion:* Thou wast accounted worthy of the greatest renown, revealing unto us the great mystery, O greatest of the Angels, whereby we who greatly honor thee have been lifted up from the earth unto the greatest height.

*Troparion:* Appear from heaven unto all who ever seek thee with love, and calm the storm of perils and afflictions that is stirred up against us, O Gabriel the Archistrategos!

*Theotokion:* Revealing unto thee of old the hidden mystery, O Maiden, Gabriel cried: Rejoice, O palace of God, in which God hath dwelt, deifying men, as He is compassionate!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):* Holding the primacy of the bodiless ministers, the dread mystery predetermined before the ages was entrusted unto thee alone, O glorious Gabriel: the ineffable childbearing of the Holy Virgin, crying aloud unto her: Rejoice, O full of grace! Therefore, we the faithful ever bless thee with gladness as is meet.

*Glory, both now, the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in godly manner, crying out from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*The Angel:* Thy womb, O all-pure one, is become the carriage of the Sun, and thou shalt bear *the unwaning Light unto men! Gabriel with great joy proclaimed unto the Virgin.*

*The Mother of God:* Depart from my doors, speak not words whose fulfillment thou canst in no wise show, O Archistrategos! Why dost thou trouble my mind and soul, speaking these things unto me?

*The Angel:* The prophetic trumpets were accounted worthy from afar to perceive the depth of this mystery, O all-undefiled one; and I, who am His servant, stand before thy doors.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:* Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Thou wast a pillar through the initial divine participations in ancient times, O most marvelous Gabriel, enlightening the Prophet Daniel, and explaining through the Spirit the revelation of things incomprehensible.

*Troparion:* With clay lips we praise thee joyfully, though thou art fiery by nature: Deliver us from the fire that burns continually, O Gabriel, by thy divine intercessions!

*Troparion:* Clothed more brightly than the sun in a divine vesture gleaming with unapproachable glory, O Archistrategos of ministers, thou standest ever before the Heavenly King!

*Theotokion:* Gabriel, having understood through the Spirit that thou art pre-purified, O all-undefiled one, cries aloud unto thee manifestly: Rejoice, deliverance from the curse, and restoration of the forebears!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine in Thy love the souls of those who watch early unto Thee, I pray, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them out of the darkness of sin.

*The Mother of God:* I see thee shining, O angel, with angelic splendors, said the all-undefiled one; yet I hesitate to believe quickly without first learning every certainty.

*The Angel:* Beholding human nature corrupt, the Creator hath been pleased to dwell in thee, O pure one, revealing now in dread manner the mystery hidden from the ages!

*The Mother of God:* I have been betrothed unto Joseph, yet even until now I have not known union, O Archangel; having then no knowledge of man, how shall I give birth? Thou speakest unto me words contrary to nature!

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:* All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst give birth unto the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn *thee.*

*Troparion:* Enlightened by participation in the Primal Mind, thou wast seen as a second light, crying with countless ranks of angels: Holy is the Father and Creator, the Son co-unoriginate, and the Spirit co-eternal!

*Troparion:* Thy form[^422] is fiery, thy beauty exceedingly marvelous, astounding every mind, O Gabriel; great is thy glory, O supreme leader of the divine bodiless hosts, adornment of all who faithfully hymn thee!

*Troparion:* When the godly Zechariah of old beheld thee standing at the time of censing, he became dumb; for he believed not thy dread announcement which thou didst utter, O Gabriel the Archistrategos.

*Theotokion:* Being the sanctuary of sanctification, O all-undefiled one, at the voice of Gabriel the Archangel thou didst bear the Holy God Who rests in the Saints, Who sanctifies all and delivers from calamities.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One!

*The Angel:* Jesus, the Creator of nature, is not subject unto the laws of nature, O Maiden; and let Aaron's rod convince thee, which budded without moisture, prefiguring that thou shouldst give birth without knowing wedlock.

*The Mother of God:* These dread and venerable words of thine amaze me as I comprehend them; and desiring to believe, I ponder the majesty of the height, and I am awed by it, O Archistrategos!

*The Angel:* Wilt thou not submit unto my words? Daniel foresaw thee as the mountain, O pure one, from which was cut without human hands the Stone which mightily tore down the idol temples and altars!

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:* Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye divinely wise, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:* Earthly tongues are unable to honor thee, a heavenly[^423] and radiant mind, enlightened most brilliantly beyond intellect in ineffable manner by divine splendors.

*Troparion:* O far-shining ray of the sun, Archistrategos of the fiery ministers: By thy light-bearing supplications unto the Mother of God, snatch away from the darkness of passions those who hymn thee!

*Troparion:* Destroy the counsels of the heathen, confirm the Orthodox faith, put an end to Church schisms, O Archangel, by thy holy prayers unto God!

*...by thy prayers.*[^424]

*Theotokion:* O pure one, thou wast obedient unto the divine words of Gabriel, and didst bear in the flesh the co-unoriginate Word, Who hath delivered the world from unreason.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:* Thou art the glorious minister and intercessor of the most radiant, venerable, all-creating, ineffable, and dread Trinity, O Archistrategos! Now pray unceasingly that we may be delivered from all perils and torments, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, protection of thy servants!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The Angel made the furnace sprinkle dew upon the venerable youths, but the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*The Mother of God:* In marvelous manner thou hast said, O Gabriel, that the Bodiless One shall come with a body into the world. Speak then clearly: How shall my womb contain Him, Whose majesty the heavens cannot contain?

*The Angel:* I declare unto thee, and understand, O all-undefiled one, how the tent of Abraham received Him of old and contained God, O all-pure one; therefore, doubt not now, but lovingly receive the greeting, the joy unto men!

*The Mother of God:* The greatness of the matter troubles me; yet since the All-Good hath willed to dwell in me beyond comprehension, behold, both in soul and body I am preserved a pure temple for Him!

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:* The divinely wise served not the creation rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* The sanctified birth, O glorious one, thou didst announce unto Zechariah of old inside the divine temple, as he stood and sang unto the Deliverer and God: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* The marvelous beauty of thy temple, O Gabriel, radiantly illumines the souls of the faithful, and bids them cry with a loud voice: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Enlightened[^425] mystically by participation in the Primal Light, O Archistrategos, thou wast truly seen as a second light, ever enlightening those who sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:* Gabriel the Archistrategos was sent, announcing joy unto thee, O pure Mother of God, for whose sake sorrow ceased, and the curse was truly rendered *barren, and blessing blossomed forth unto the faithful unto the ages.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Out of the flame Thou didst shed dew upon the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one with water; for Thou, O Christ, doest all things solely by Thine own willing. Thee do we exalt unto all ages!

*Troparion:* At the voice of the angel thou didst conceive the Light; therefore we all cry unto thee: Rejoice! O door of the Light, vision hardly to be gazed upon, throne of the Most High, O Virgin Mother of God!

*Troparion:* Rejoice, fulfillment of the sublime mystery; rejoice, manifestation of things incomprehensible; rejoice, reconciliation of mankind unto God; rejoice, O Virgin, restoration of the fallen!

*Troparion:* Rejoice, parent of the Master, the Giver of light; rejoice, haven of those tossed about in the tempest, O all-undefiled, true, and fruitful vine; rejoice, thou who hast budded the cluster of immortality!

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:* The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious youths in the furnace; then prefigured, but now fulfilled, it stirs the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:* In sacred hymns, a holy and sacred people sings rejoicing: For thou, O Archangel, didst announce unto the Virgin most sacred among us, beyond intellect and word, the all-causing Word taking flesh for our sake. Therefore we honor thee unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Immaterially united unto the Great and Primal Mind, O Archangel, with thy fiery lips thou singest the dread hymn which all the angelic choirs sing: Hymn the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Manifestly adorned with divine blessings, thou passest through the heavens and likewise the earth, fulfilling the holy desires of Christ, the God of all, O Gabriel, leader of the Angels, beauty of those who faithfully praise thee!

*Troparion:* The Word, desiring in hypostasis to unite with men in the flesh, had thee running before Him and preparing the holy palace, O holy Gabriel, as thou didst cry: Hymn the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* As the fair throne of the King, as surpassing all creation, as having alone inherited[^426] the Supersubstantial One Who deified men by a more excellent union of ineffable and marvelous childbearing, let us hymn the Blessed Virgin!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was made manifest unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we bless thee!

*Troparion:* Rejoice, all-blessed[^427] cause of joy! Rejoice, honorable, good-fruited, and fair-blooming olive tree bearing the Nourisher of all! Rejoice, fountain of living water, O all-pure one, urn and paradise of God; rejoice, O all-undefiled!

*Troparion:* Purification of souls and bodies: Rejoice, thou through whom healings flow unto men, cause of the deification of all! Rejoice, light cloud bearing the Sun Who illumines the world with light-beaming rays!

*Troparion:* We cry a joyous hymn unto thee: Rejoice, O pure one, praise of the Martyrs and Apostles, marvelous theme of the Prophets; rejoice, adornment of Hierarchs and Venerables, for whose sake the heavenly rejoice with the earthly!

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:* Thou standest with dread serving before the throne of grace, and shining with effusions of light which are beyond intellect, being sacredly deified; and seen as a light, thou dost enlighten those who honor thee with faith, O holy Gabriel the Archistrategos!

*Troparion:* Thou art seen as heaven adorned with stars, with divine splendors; and like a general thou holdest in thy hands a most radiant scepter, traversing all the earth, ever doing the good will and delivering the faithful from calamities!

*Troparion:* Calm the fierce barbarian storm that is ever stirred up against thy servants; put an end to church schisms; grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing to thee; and grant victories unto the king,[^428] O Gabriel, by thy fervent intercession!

*Troparion:* O most excellent and all-glorious pair, Michael and Gabriel, standing before the throne of divine glory: Ask remission of sins and change of evils for all, as intercessors and as those who alone in all things imitate goodness!

*Theotokion:* The light of brilliance shone forth through thy childbearing and enlightened the whole universe, and destroyed the prince of darkness, O Mother of God *all-pure one, praise of the Angels, and salvation of all men who praise thee with unceasing voices.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

The Pre-eternal Mind, having established thee, Gabriel, as a second light by divine participations, enlightening the whole universe, and revealing unto us the truly divine and great mystery hidden from the ages: that He Who is bodiless is taking flesh in the virginal womb, and becoming man in order to save man! *(Twice)*

Standing before the throne of the Thrice-Sun Divinity, and richly illuminated by the divine splendors that unceasingly emanate from thence: Deliver from the gloom of passions those on earth who joyfully keep festival and praise thee, and illumine them with enlightenment, O Gabriel the Archistrategos, intercessor for our souls!

Cast down the insolence of the Hagarenes that frequently falls upon thy flock; put an end to the schisms of the Church; calm the tempest of immeasurable temptations; deliver from perils and tribulations those who honor thee with love and flee under thy protection, O Gabriel the Archistrategos, intercessor for our souls!

*Glory, both now, in Tone 2:*

Gabriel announces the good tidings unto her that is full of grace today: Rejoice, unwedded and unespoused Mother! Marvel not at my strange appearance, neither be affrighted, for I am an Archangel. The serpent deceived Eve of old; but now I announce joy unto thee, and thou shalt remain incorrupt, and shalt bear the Lord, O all-pure one!

*At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion twice; and the Martyrion. Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

*(Ornamental woodcut tailpiece band)*

*In the same month, on the 27th day: Our Holy Mother Matrona of Thessalonica.*

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Thou didst resist Jewish fierceness and insolent cruelty with courage of soul, O manly-minded one; foreseeing future enjoyment, O divinely wise one, remaining ever unmoved unto the ages *unmoved; which thou hast obtained, having departed from the earth unto the heavenly bridal chambers and unto the indestructible choir, O glorious one.*

Thou wast accounted worthy to behold the majestic beauty and comeliness of the Kingdom[^429] of thy Bridegroom, adorned with the wounds of thy steadfast suffering, and hast worthily drawn near unto the Fountain of good things; whence thou hast richly received the fruit of divine gladness, O blessed one, and immortal good renown.
