Neither the yoke of slavery, nor female weakness, nor hunger, nor wounds hindered thee from being likened unto the steadfastness of the martyrs, O all-glorious one; for with fervor of soul thou didst endure torments. Whence thou hast obtained the heavenly bridal chamber, and hast been adorned with the crown of grace, standing before thy Creator.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, in the same tone and podoben:*

Deliver my humble soul from condemnation and grievous transgressions, O all-pure Bride of God, and rescue it from death by thy prayers, and grant that I may obtain justification on the day of trial, which the assemblies of the saints have obtained; show me purified before the end by repentance and outpourings of tears.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same podoben:*

When Thy Lamb and Mother beheld Thee nailed to the Cross, O Lord, she marveled and cried: What is this sight, O most desired Son? Hath the faithless and lawless assembly rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I sing the divine glory of Matrona." A composition of Theophanes.[^430] Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Traversing the Red Sea's surge with dryshod steps, ancient Israel of old, by Moses' hands stretched out crosswise, conquered the might of Amalek in the wilderness.

*Troparion:* Now dancing in choir with the divine bodiless ranks before God, the Cause of all, and clearly enjoying the illumination of the Godhead: Visit those who sing to thee!

*Troparion:* Christ God, Who deigned to take the form of a servant, desiring to deliver him from corruption and mortal bonds, betrothed thee, O martyr bride, from the yoke of slavery.

*Troparion:* Though being of female nature, yet living by His grace, thou wast able through the weakness of the body, by divine *power to cast down the haughty pride that formerly boasted beyond measure.*

*Theotokion:* The door of Thy coming unto us, O Lord, the Orient, Sun, and Light—as Thou art mystically called—shone forth supernaturally, [bearing Thee] two in natures, the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying: Thou art my strength, O Lord, both refuge and foundation!

*Troparion:* With steadfastness of mind thou didst endure most lawless, cruel, and grievous torment, O glorious and all-blessed Matrona.

*Troparion:* Feeling painful wounds, thou didst endure valiantly; and through condemnation to darkness, thou hast received divine radiance.

*Troparion:* The streams of thy blood quenched the furnace of impiety, and watered the faithful with the waters of piety.

*Theotokion:* As table and ark, having received into thy womb, O all-pure one, the unoriginate Word of God, with divinely inspired voice we praise thee.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезна́чалное сло́во):*

Having piously enslaved thyself unto the Almighty, thou wast not enslaved by the impiety of thy mistress, O pure one; but thou didst steadfastly endure dark imprisonment and the burning of wounds, and hast departed unto God, O divinely wise martyr Matrona. Pray for us who celebrate thy memory!

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

Fervent and invincible advocate, sure and unashamed hope, rampart, protection, and haven of those who flee unto thee, O pure Ever-Virgin: With the Angels entreat thy Son and God to grant peace unto the world, and salvation, and great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee hanging voluntarily upon the Cross between thieves, O Christ, Thy Mother, torn with maternal anguish, cried: O sinless Son, how art Thou unjustly nailed to the Cross like an evildoer, desiring to make alive the human race, as Thou art compassionate?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Beholding Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Strengthened by divine power, O Matrona, thou didst escape the bitter servitude of thy mistress; for thou didst acquire a humble disposition of soul, serving the one Master.

*Troparion:* Roaring and raging in drunkenness, and torn with fury *the most impious one, in her Jewish cruelty, lacerated thy body with wounds, O martyr passion-bearer.*

*Troparion:* Thou didst display youthful endurance, O martyr, when shut up in dark places and tormented with hunger; but thou hast found the reward: the table of immortality.

*Theotokion:* The gold-beaten lampstand prefigured thee, the true Mother of God, who didst bear God incarnate, the Radiance of the Godhead, Who hath enlightened all.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from the darkness of ignorance those who sing to Thee in faith.

*Troparion:* She hath acquired a radiant crown of praise from Thee,[^431] O Giver of life, and hath passed from darkness unto divine illumination.

*Troparion:* Christ hath established thy feet upon the rock of faith, O blessed one, unto Whom thou didst wisely direct thy steps.

*Troparion:* Truly having a divine, wise, and godly mind, thou shinest in the choir of martyrs, O all-blessed Matrona.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst bear the form of the Godhead and the form of humanity, O Mother of God, an unconfused union according to an ineffable union.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy ribs.

*Troparion:* What thou didst desire, O passion-bearer Matrona, thou hast now wisely attained, being deified by unceasing divine inclination; and thou hast been worthily accounted worthy of ever-existing glory. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:* In Christ neither slave nor free is judged, but the comeliness of virtue adorned with habits of piety, whereof thou hast shown thyself a doer, O invincible passion-bearer.

*Theotokion:* We know thee as God's temple and bridal chamber, the urn, lampstand, and table containing the writing within: the Word Who was incarnate for mercy's sake, O all-undefiled one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burned rather by love of piety than by flame, cried: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:* With love and boldness thou hast truly been united[^432] unto thy Lover, O all-praised one, thankfully crying: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory *O Lord! (Twice)*

*Troparion:* With gladness and joy thou hast passed, full of diverse virtues, O Matrona, from earth unto the heavenly choirs, O glorious one, having obtained an immortal inheritance.

*Theotokion:* Enlightened and adorned with all virtues, beyond word thou didst bear the incarnate Word of the Father, O Virgin Mother. Blessed art thou among women, O all-undefiled Sovereign Lady!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Daniel, stretching forth his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Having put off the flesh, thou wast accounted worthy to behold the rays of the rich Light, and to dwell in the heavenly tabernacle instead of the dark and gloomy tabernacle wherein thou wast shut up, O glorious one, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! *(Twice)*

*Troparion:* Fair is the crown wherewith thou art now crowned by the life-originating right hand of the Almighty, O glorious one; for beholding thee, His bride, dyed red with the streams of thy blood, He took thee to Himself with joy; unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:* Truly having given birth unto God, thou hast truly been shown to be the Mother of God, O all-pure one, confirming for the faithful the divine appellation according to each birth. Therefore with divine wisdom we glorify thee as the Birthgiver of God, O all-undefiled Sovereign Lady.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The cornerstone, Christ, was cut out without hands from thee, O Virgin, the unhewn mountain, uniting the separated natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:* Thy light-bearing day shines with noetic light-rays, with most radiant splendors of the Thrice-Sun illumination, O martyr; wherein now pray that those who sing to thee may be illumined.

*Troparion:* Thou hast found rewards for painful wounds, O passion-bearer: the heavenly inheritance in the Church of the firstborn, O glorious one; wherein keeping festival, preserve us by thy prayers!

*Troparion:* Truly thou art now united unto Christ, thine immortal Bridegroom, having blossomed radiantly with His divine wounds, O all-wise one, and shining with the precious blood of martyrdom.

*Theotokion:* Break the cords of my transgressions now, O Virgin Mother of God, who didst bear the Fountain of grace, O only divinely blessed one; and fill me with soul-sweetness, that I may worthily magnify thee.

*In the same month, on the 28th day: Our Venerable Father Hilarion the New; and the Holy Wonderworker Stephen.:*

*Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

Having acquired an undefiled life, patience and meekness, unfeigned love, boundless temperance, all-night standing, divine contrition, faith, and true hope with compassion, O father: Thou didst live upon earth in the flesh like an Angel, O blessed Hilarion, intercessor for our souls.

Thou wast an earthly Angel and a heavenly man, O venerable one: a fountain of contrition, a stream of compassion, an abyss of miracles, a surety for sinners, truly a fruitful olive tree of God, anointing with the oil of thy labors the faces of those who faithfully praise thee, O marvelous Hilarion.

Thy mind, enlightened by divine understanding, rose superior to bodily passions, unmingled with earthly things, bearing the impress and imaging within thyself the divine beauty; and being wholly perceived as most godlike through the cooperation of the Spirit, O Hilarion our father, adornment of monastics.

*Other Stichera of Saint Stephen, the same tone, Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Having been a most true monk, a most radiant star, and a fountain of contrition, thou wast shown full of love, meek and quiet, compassionate and humble, gentle and blameless, O father Stephen. Therefore Christ appointed thee shepherd over the flocks of the venerable, honored with wonderworking.

All-night vigil, ineffable temperance, unceasing prayer, a divine life, sincere faith and hope, Orthodox[^433] wisdom, marvelous conduct, and greatest compassion thou hadst, O father; and having died before the end, thou didst shine forth in glorious signs: Therefore we honor thee, venerable Stephen.

As a star of many lights, as an unsetting sun, as an ensouled heaven adorned with divine miracles like stars, as a fragrant garden and paradise of *sweetness: as a fountain of sanctification, as a most great physician of the infirm, as a shepherd, and as our good protector, we praise thee, Stephen.*

*Glory, both now, Theotokion, the same tone and podoben:*

With showers of the All-Holy Spirit, O all-pure one, dew my mind, who didst bear Christ, the Drop that by compassions washed away beyond number the boundless transgressions of men; dry up the fountain of my passions, and by thy prayers account me worthy of the stream of living delight evermore.

*Stavrotheotokion, the same podoben:*

When Thy Lamb and Mother beheld Thee nailed to the Cross, O Lord, she marveled and cried: What is this sight, O most desired Son? Hath the faithless and lawless assembly rendered these things unto Thee, which enjoyed Thy many miracles? But glory to Thine ineffable condescension, O Master!

*Canon of the Venerable Hilarion. Whose acrostic is: "I will praise thy quiet ways, O father." A composition of Joseph.[^434] Tone 2.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* In the deep of old, the overwhelmingly armed power laid low the whole army of Pharaoh; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the most glorious Lord, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparion:* Having been compassionate and meek, O father, chaste and quiet truly in spirit: By thine intercession,[^435] O Hilarion, account me worthy of divine compassion, that I may now sing of thee in the quiet of my heart.

*Troparion:* Purified from childhood, O father, taking up thy cross, thou didst follow after the Lord; the passions of the flesh thou didst wither by temperance and frequent prayers,[^436] and wast adorned with dispassion.

*Troparion:* Having received the Lord's light yoke, and following His ordinances, O venerable Hilarion, thou didst cast off the heavy burden of sin, O all-wise one, having acquired the wilderness,[^437] O venerable one.

*Theotokion:* Rend the cords of my transgressions, O Sovereign Lady, praying unto thy Son and God; and cast down the sin that ever torments me, that being saved I may ever praise thee, O all-undefiled one.

*Another Canon, of Saint Stephen. Whose acrostic is: "I honor thy divine labors, O all-blessed one." A composition of Lord Joseph.[^438] Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:* Piously celebrating thy divine memory, O divinely wise father, we ever glorify the Lord of all Who hath glorified thee, Stephen, with many miracles.

*Troparion:* Thy mind, all-wisely enriched by divine inclinations, completely turned away from the passions of the flesh, and showed thee an Angel upon earth, Stephen.

*Troparion:* Thou didst endure the pains of asceticism, O father, and didst mortify the unrestrained passions of the flesh; and thou didst clothe thyself with dispassion as with a radiant garment.

*Troparion:* Having crucified thy members through temperance, thou didst live unto Christ, O father, Who by the Cross destroyed death, and enriched thee with the abundant grace of healings.

*Theotokion:* By an ineffable word, O all-pure one, thou didst conceive Him Who holds all the ends of the earth, and didst bear Him: Whom entreat fervently to have mercy on us.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* The barren Church of the Gentiles blossomed like a lily, O Lord, by Thy coming, wherein my heart hath been established.

*Troparion:* Thy life shone forth like the sun, O venerable one; by radiant deeds it enlightened the choirs of monastics and drove away demonic darkness.

*Troparion:* Thou wast most quiet in heart, O venerable one, and humble in spirit, and full of divine love, ever having a welcoming disposition.

*Troparion:* With the drops of thy tears thou didst quench the coals of sin, and both before the end and after the end, O wise one, thou pourest forth an abyss of healings unto those who hasten to thee.

*Theotokion:* Heal the wounds of my soul, O Sovereign Lady, and direct the movements of my mind unto the desires of the Word incarnate from thee, I pray.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:* Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and founded the earth upon the waters, O Almighty One.

*Troparion:* By venerable deeds and virtuous accomplishments, O father, thou didst shine forth like an all-radiant star in the assembly of monastics, and didst illumine all under the sun with miraculous splendors.

*Troparion:* By the power of the grace of the Spirit, thou didst cast down the bodiless foes, and didst ascend gloriously through humble-mindedness; and thou wast a personal worker of miracles, O wise father.[^439]

*Troparion:* Thou didst stop the mouths of the heretics with the word of truth, O father *and having endured persecutions, thou hast received the divine crown, being without wounds a most excellent martyr,[^440] O wise one.*

*Theotokion:* That God might become man, God was made man from thee alone, O pure one, the Unoriginate receiving a beginning. Therefore, O Virgin, we the faithful call thee blessed.

*Sessional Hymn [of St. Hilarion], Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Thou didst lose thy life [for Christ's sake],[^441] O all-blessed one, having been a most excellent sacred minister, truly most consecrated to Christ. Therefore after many sweats and labors, rejoicing, thou hast departed unto the heavenly[^442] tabernacles; and now thou pourest forth streams of healings upon us.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn [of St. Stephen], Tone 4, Podoben: "The soldiers keeping watch" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*

Shining like the sun, O father, thy memory enlightens the hearts of the faithful, driving away passions and taking away darkness. Cover those who celebrate it with pure faith; preserve us from worldly temptations, O father Stephen.

*Both now, Theotokion:*

O unwedded and pure Virgin Mother of God, sole protectress and cover of the faithful: Deliver from tribulations, sorrows, and grievous circumstances all who have hope in thee, O Maiden; and save our souls by thy divine prayers.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding Thee dead, stretched out upon the Cross, O Christ, the All-Undefiled Mother cried: O my Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, what is this Thine ineffable dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the creation of Thy most pure hands, O Compassionate One?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an envoy nor an angel, but Thyself incarnate, O Lord, and hast saved me, the whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Having purified thy heart, thou didst wing thy mind with pure ascents, to gaze upon the incomprehensible beauty of the only God, and to be illumined with splendors from thence.

*Troparion:* In the exposition of divine words, thou didst draw divine understanding from thence, O father; and didst piously transmit it unto all who hasten unto thee, Hilarion.

*Troparion:* By the sweetness of thy words, calm the hearts of those who hasten unto thee; for in compassion thou hadst love for those whom thou didst teach with acceptable words, O venerable one.

*Theotokion:* Rejoice! For He made thee Lady and Sovereign over all, O all-pure one; for from thy womb *for from thy womb He was pleased to be incarnate, delivering His creation from the power of the enemy.*

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:* Foreseeing in spirit the incarnation of the Word, O Prophet Habakkuk, thou didst proclaim, crying: When the years draw near, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown. Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:* Watered by the drops of thy holy tears, thou wast shown to be a fruitful tree, O wise Stephen, bearing the fragrant fruits of virtues, and pouring forth countless miracles; therefore we piously honor thee.

*Troparion:* Thy temple is seen as a divine paradise, O wise one, having the shrine of thy relics, producing diverse flowers of miracles, wherewith the hearts of those who ever praise thee in faith are delighted, O Stephen.

*Troparion:* Thou loosest passions and calmest the grievous diseases of men unto the glory of Christ, from Whom, enriched with grace for thy labors, thou wast magnified, enlightened, and glorified, O our father, glory of monastics!

*Theotokion:* O holy Mother of God, sanctify us, who didst bear in the flesh the All-Holy One, Who willed to become like unto men; and by thy prayers show all to be partakers of heavenly life, O all-pure one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have access unto Thine Author of light, the Father, from the night of ignorance.

*Troparion:* Thou didst weave for thyself a robe of salvation, O venerable one, from thine own afflictions; clad in this, thou didst clothe the prince of darkness with everlasting shame.

*Troparion:* By temperance thou didst wither the pleasures of the flesh, O venerable one; and thou hast received heavenly grace, O father Hilarion, to heal all manner of human diseases.

*Troparion:* Thy hands unceasingly lifted up unto God delivered the pious from the hands of the deceitful one, O father, and granted health unto everyone that was sick.

*Theotokion:* Thou hast been the strength of the faithful and the fortress of the venerable; for through thee, O pure one, better things flowed unto all, and those dwelling upon earth ascend unto heaven.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:* Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God, for we know no other God besides Thee; we call upon Thy name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.

*Troparion:* Having become myrrh-breathing through divine virtues, after thy falling asleep thou didst pour forth, O wise one *fragrant myrrh, being marveled at by faithful men who honor thee with love.*

*Troparion:* With greatest displays of awesome miracles, O all-wise Stephen, thou didst shine forth, both before thy death, O praiseworthy one, and after thy departure, and at thy sacred burial.

*Troparion:* Having finished thy life in Christ, Stephen, our divinely wise father, thou didst settle with the saints;[^443] and now thou sanctifiest those who honor thee with faith.

*Theotokion:* Having given birth, O all-undefiled one, unto God Emmanuel, Who visibly became man out of compassion: Beseech Him, as He is the Lover of mankind, O pure one, to have compassion upon the people that have sinned.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparion:* Mortified unto the whole world, O Hilarion, having departed thou now livest in the heavenly temples, and ever pourest forth the life-bearing operation of healings.

*Troparion:* Gracious, meek, gentle, blameless, venerable, well-contrite, and quiet in spirit thou wast, O most rich father.

*Troparion:* Adorned with boundless compassion, and honored for the beauty of thy love of the poor, O Hilarion, thou wast shown to be a most excellent close servant of God.

*Troparion:* How venerable and blameless is thy life! How most excellent and fair thy character! How awesome the miracles which thou ever performest, O all-marvelous one!

*Theotokion:* The undefiled bridal chamber of God, the fire-formed throne of the Almighty, the Mother of God, all generations of generations call thee blessed.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:* Imitating the Prophet Jonah, I cry: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee!

*Troparion:* Thou makest straight the lame, O father, thou givest sight to the blind,[^444] thou causest the paralyzed to walk in Christ, and thou drivest away evil spirits, O venerable Stephen.

*Troparion:* A stream richly pours forth evermore from thy precious shrine as from a pure fountain of healings, O blessed one, drying up every torrent of passions.

*Troparion:* A worker of miracles and a true shepherd thou wast shown to be, O father Stephen: Preserve evermore thy flock, which thou didst gather by thy many sweats.

*Theotokion:* Descending upon thee like dew upon the fleece, O Mother of God, the Savior visibly dried up the streams of godlessness, and watered the souls of those who ever honor thee.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The god-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread a spiritual dew upon the pious children, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified.[^445]

*Troparion:* With the weapons of thy ascetical struggles thou didst wound the bodiless serpent, and didst utterly destroy him. Therefore thou hast received the honor of miracles, to heal the most grievous passions of both souls and bodies, O venerable one.

*Troparion:* Thy word proceeds like dew, driving away the burning heat of despondency, O venerable one, from those who draw near to thee with faith, O our father, and ever enlightening their characters with virtues in the Spirit.

*Troparion:* Having made thy mind ruler over the passions, thou didst manifestly reign as an excellent victor; and thou didst vanquish the alien nations, and didst drive away, O wise one, the all-destructive passions of demons.

*Troparion:* The winds of temptations shook not the immovable pillar of thy heart, O venerable one; for it was established upon the rock of the love of the Lord, strengthened by which, thou wast a divine confirmation unto many, O father.

*Trinity:* I sing Thee as three in number of Persons, O supersubstantial, undivided, unconfused Trinity, a Unity in essence, incomprehensible, one God Almighty, Father, Word, and Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:* From afar the grace of the Spirit prefigured thee with prophetic proclamations, naming thee a holy fertile mountain, a saving door, a most new scroll, O pure one, and the Ark.

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:* The fire touched not nor troubled Thy children in the furnace, O Savior. Then the three as with one mouth sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:* Thou didst minister as a priest, as with a coal of heavenly ascetical life; thou wast shown forth, O father, wholly the fragrance of Christ, and thou ever fillest the countenances of the faithful with sweet fragrance, working miracles beyond understanding.

*Troparion:* Shining with the splendors of Orthodoxy, thou didst drive away the darkness of heresies, O wise one; and having endured persecutions for very many years, thou wast a martyr without blood. Therefore we faithfully praise thee.

*Troparion:* Having acquired great patience, self-control, faith, and true love, thou wast shown to be a father of orphans, a protector of widows, compassionate and humble, O Stephen, the glory and boast of monks *and praise.*

*Troparion:* The choir of rational beings[^446] marveled at thy all-night standing, and prayerful waiting, at thy tears and sighing, and at thy true love toward the Creator, by which thou didst overthrow their wiles, O Stephen.

*Theotokion:* How dost thou nourish with milk the fatherless Son Who nourishes all breath, O pure Mother? How dost thou carry in thine arms Him Who holds all things? Thy wonder is beyond understanding. Therefore we ever sing thy greatness.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The fiery furnace in Babylon of old divided its operation by the divine command, consuming the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Having forsaken the transitory glory of life, O father, and fleeting pleasure, thou hast received in the heavens the imperishable glory and food that passes not away, O all-wise one, with all who fear the Lord.

*Troparion:* Thou didst pass through thy whole life in mourning; therefore thou wast accounted worthy of blessedness and consolation, which the saints inherit. Rejoicing, thou hast now settled in the land of the meek, O venerable one, as one who is prudent and understanding.

*Troparion:* Honorable before God was thy death shown to be, O father, most sacred one; for thou didst honor His icon, and didst endure persecutions, being afflicted by the tormentors, O venerable one, being shown as a martyr[^447] in this.

*Troparion:* Thou didst manifestly calm the indignation of the fishermen, O father, and didst multiply their catches, which they had not obtained before; for God gave ear unto thy prayers, O venerable one, blessed sacred minister.

*Theotokion:* Wash away from me the filth of passions and the mire of sin, O thou who art full of divine grace, and open the door of repentance unto me who have stumbled, and who am held fast in transgressions, O pure one, meeting with them and having provoked God to anger.

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him of Whom the angels and all the heavenly hosts are afraid as Creator and Lord, praise, ye priests, glorify, ye children, bless, ye peoples, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Having accomplished thy venerable course in a venerable manner, O blessed one, thou hast been united with the venerable and with all the chosen. With them we honor thee, and bowing before the shrine of thy relics, O Stephen, divinely bearing father *having acquired purity of mind, O wise one, thou wast a fair mirror of the Spirit, receiving His manifestations and enlightening the people by thy teachings.*

*Troparion:* A most glorious thing is seen, O all-blessed one, performed most wisely by thee; for from a single sight of thee, a woman, being loosed from the infirmity that beset her, walked upright.

*Trinity:* Let us sing of the unoriginate Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the undivided Beginning, the co-eternal Dominion, the tripersonal Nature, one Glory, and one Kingdom.

*Theotokion:* The Bodiless One was divinely incarnate of thee; beseech Him, O all-pure one, to mortify the passions of my flesh, and to make alive my soul which is slain by sins.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* The Son of the unoriginate Parent, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather the dispersed; therefore the all-hymned Mother of God do we magnify.

*Troparion:* Thy tomb, pouring forth healings by the grace of the Spirit, dries up the showers of passions, drives away the spirits of delusion, and enlightens the hearts of all who faithfully call thee blessed, O venerable Hilarion.

*Troparion:* As a rose thou didst blossom in the garden of fasting, as a sweet-smelling lily, as a fragrant apple, and thou hast filled with sweet fragrance[^448] the souls and hearts of all the pious, O glorious Hilarion, intercessor for those who praise thee well.

*Troparion:* Thou hadst compassion, love, humility, and almsgiving, a simple disposition, true faith, hope, an honorable life, and an upright character, O venerable Hilarion, adornment of monastics.

*Troparion:* Thy memory hath shone forth today like the sun, truly enlightening the hearts of the pious with the brightness of thy labors and the radiances of miracles, O father; wherein remember all who faithfully honor thee.

*Theotokion:* We bring unto thee the voice of Gabriel, rejoicing: Rejoice, we cry, O place of sanctification! Rejoice, O glory of the venerable and salvation of all the faithful, through whom we have been deified, O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin!

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:* The light-bearing cloud, upon which the Master of all descended from heaven like dew upon the fleece, and was incarnate for our sake, becoming man, the Unoriginate One: We all magnify her as the pure Mother of our God.

*Troparion:* Following in the steps of those who lived piously and venerably upon earth, O God-bearer *who lived upon earth, thou wast shown to be meek, guileless, compassionate, humble, and gentle, O Stephen, and full of divine love; for this cause we faithfully praise thee.*

*Troparion:* As sweet-smelling myrrh, as a most pure lily, as a fair-flowering rose, O father, thou pourest forth sweet fragrance from thy precious shrine, driving away foul-smelling diseases by countless healings from those who draw near to thee with fervent faith.

*Troparion:* As a divine shepherd and imitator of the great Chief Shepherd Christ, we pray thee, O divinely wise one: Save us, thy sheep,[^449] from wicked wolves even now by thy holy intercessions evermore.

*Troparion:* Thy bright memory, shining forth like a great sun, brightly illumines creation; celebrating it piously, we cry to thee, O Stephen: Having illumined the minds of all by thy prayers, shelter and preserve us!

*Theotokion:* Being a lover of sin, I live in negligence, and I tremble before the unbribable tribunal, O pure one. At which do thou preserve me uncondemned by thy holy entreaties, O Bride of God, that as my protectress I may ever call thee blessed.

*In the same month, on the 29th day: The memory of our venerable father Mark, Bishop of Arethusa, and Cyril the Deacon, and others who suffered under the tormentor Julian.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (О преславнаго чудесе):*

*Sticheron 1:* O martyr[^450] and hierarch, venerable one, sacredly radiant with the light of the priesthood, thou didst shine forth with the unwaning brightness of martyrdom; and now thou hast passed over unto the never-setting radiance, in which the choirs of martyrs rejoice, and where the angelic ranks keep festival. With them ever remember us, O blessed one, acceptable unto God!

*Sticheron 2:* O martyr and passion-bearer Cyril, having been shown to be a minister of divine things, thou didst follow Christ; and being sacrificed like a pure slaughter, thou wast offered upon the divine table, O divinely blessed one, and hast obtained an honorable inheritance *ever abiding in the splendors of the saints, having become holy both in calling and in deed.*

*Sticheron 3:* O all-praised martyrs of Christ, by the dew of divine grace ye passed through the burning furnace of torments unharmed; and ye were accounted worthy to dwell beside the waters of rest, having received the victory over your labors. Therefore rejoicing with faith, we celebrate your sacred memory today, O holy ones, glorifying Christ.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* We pray thee, O all-pure one, our protectress: Despise not thy servants who are troubled in sorrow, that we perish not utterly; but hasten to deliver us from the present wrath and grief, O thou who art full of divine grace, most holy and pure; for thou art our rampart and invincible help!

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Thee nailed to the Cross, O Jesus, and willingly accepting sufferings, Thy Virgin Mother, O Master, cried out: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, O Physician Who hast healed human infirmity, and delivered all from corruption by Thy tender compassion?[^451]

*Canon, whose acrostic is: "I honor Mark and Cyril the divinely blessed." A composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* That which was cut severed the uncut sea, and the sun beheld the land which it had never seen; the water drowned the cruel enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable path. And a hymn was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:* Standing before God the King of all with the angels, O all-blessed martyrs, ask deliverance from transgressions for us who bless you with love, and who sing in praise: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:* The Holy Spirit appointed thee a high priest, Mark, to shepherd the people in all abundance with the spiritual staff of dogmas; with whom, O blessed one, thou didst sing: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparion:* Having extinguished the flame of polytheism with streams of your blood, O wise ones, ye suffered with joy, having utterly put to shame the Hellenic sages; and now with the martyrs ye stand before Christ, crying: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Theotokion:* From the incorrupt Maiden God is incarnate, Whom the choirs of the bodiless ranks glorify with trembling as Creator of all and the only Unchangeable One, and men upon earth ever hymn, singing: Let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Plant Thy fear, O Lord, in the hearts of Thy servants, and be Thou our confirmation, who call upon Thee in truth.

*Troparion:* Thou didst not offer honor unto dumb idols, O all-blessed one, but didst most stoutly endure manifold torments, O Mark of valiant wisdom.

*Troparion:* Through valour of mind, O glorious Cyril, thou didst endure mockery and torments of most lawless men, O martyr and passion-bearer.

*Troparion:* Stripped naked, O much-suffering martyrs, and crushed with wounds, ye remained steadfast, preferring nothing before the love of Christ.

*Theotokion:* From thee hath shone forth, O all-undefiled Virgin, the Sun of righteousness, Who illumines all under heaven with most glorious splendors.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси):* Enlightened by true grace, ye brightly guide the ends of the earth in godliness, O glorious hieromartyrs; therefore with faith we call you blessed.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:* Having stretched out thy most pure hands, O Virgin Mother, shelter those who hope in thee and who cry unto thy Son: Grant Thy mercies unto all, O Christ!

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Thy Son lifted up upon the Tree, O all-pure one, torn in thy maternal heart, thou didst piteously cry aloud: Woe is me! How hast Thou set, O my timeless Light?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* From the overshadowing mountain, O Word, the Prophet prophetically foresaw Thee about to be incarnate of the only Mother of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.

*Troparion:* Thy blood which poured out upon the earth, O divinely wise one, truly went up worthily unto the Savior, and it watered the souls of the pious, stirring them unto divine zeal.

*Troparion:* Manifestly enlightened by the spiritual dawn, ye most gloriously drove away the evil night of godless blasphemy, O martyrs Mark and Cyril, all-blessed ones.

*Troparion:* Heal our infirmities, O Benefactor, through the prayers of Mark and of the divine Cyril; and send down Thy mercies, O Tender-hearted One, upon all who hymn Thee.

*Troparion:* By divine power and the grace of the Word, O martyrs of Christ, casting down the temples of idols, ye were shown forth by faith to be the temple of the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:* Thou wast shown to be the spacious dwelling-place of God, a lofty throne *and lofty throne, and door, O Mother of God, through which He passed by an ineffable birth, calling us back from corruption.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* From the night of ignorance, O Lord Who hast enlightened the ends of the earth with divine knowledge, illumine me in the morning of Thy love for mankind!

*Troparion:* Manifest luminaries, having become divine hieromartyrs, ye enlighten all by the divine splendors of the Spirit.

*Troparion:* Beholding the divine rewards, O all-blessed one, thou didst most stoutly endure unbearable wounds and illnesses, O Mark.

*Troparion:* Being a sacred minister and offering bloodless sacrifices unto Christ, O blessed one, thou didst offer thyself as a fragrant sacrifice unto the Lord.

*Theotokion:* By a strange childbirth thou didst give birth unto the King of all, O all-pure Mother of God, for the benefaction[^452] of creation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Jonah in the whale, O Lord, Thou didst settle alone; save me also, bound by the nets of the enemy, as Thou didst save him from corruption!

*Troparion:* Having endured manifold torments and an intercessory death, O divinely wise Cyril, thou keepest festival in the ever-existing glory in the heavens, praying for us.

*Troparion:* Having the living word of God in thy heart, O glorious Mark, thou didst wisely draw peoples and nations unto the divine faith, and didst gloriously finish thy course.

*Troparion:* Ye were sacred ministers, and ye offered yourselves as whole-burnt offerings through torments, perfect sacrifices unto the Lord; and brightly ye stand before the King of all in the highest.

*Theotokion:* Ask for us forgiveness of debts and amendment of life, O Virgin, who sing the praise of thy majesty and glorify thee with pious mind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The children coming from Judea in Babylon of old trod down the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* Enlightened by the radiance of the Holy Trinity, O divinely blessed ones, ye drove away the darkness of delusion, and obtained glory as ye sang: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* Having left the things below and inherited the things on high, O God-bearing Mark and divine Cyril, let them be honored as they sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* Having shaken off the slumber of negligence at the time of struggles, ye finished your course; and ye were joined unto the assembly of the passion-bearers as ye sang: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:* From thee *salvation beyond understanding and the Word shone forth, O Virgin, unto those sitting in the darkness of death; and He made those below to be heavenly, who cry: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Having become victors over the tyrant and the flame by Thy grace, giving diligent heed unto Thy commandments, the children cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Shown to be victors over the tyrant, judging him steadfastly, ye received incorruptible crowns, O martyrs, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Armed with the shield of love and faith, O sufferers, with the sword of divine valor ye cut down the enemy's battle-arrays, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:* Having suffered upon earth, O holy ones in the highest, ye received the crown of righteousness from the hand of Christ, crying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Trinity:* As a Unity in essence, and as a Trinity honoring Thee in hypostatic characters, O Father Almighty, and Son, and Holy Spirit, I cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:* As the jar of the living manna we proclaim thee, O blessed one: table, and rod, and censer, golden lampstand, mountain, and door, unburnt bush, throne of the Most High, and salvation of the world.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Every ear was struck with fear at the ineffable condescension of God: How the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, becoming man from a virgin's womb! Therefore, O all-pure Mother of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Troparion:* Following in the steps of Christ Who suffered and mortified the passions, O martyrs, ye received the trial of many wounds; and having exposed the godless worship of the enemies, ye were magnified, and were manifestly likened unto the angels.

*Troparion:* Ye were shown forth as fiery coals, having communion with God, O all-blessed ones, and ye burned up the matter of polytheism by grace; and rejoicing ye passed over unto the noetic Light, O divinely blessed ones, illumining our minds.

*Troparion:* Having established the pillars of your souls upon the noetic rock, O all-praised ones, ye were not shaken by the assaults of many torments. Therefore ever confirm our thoughts, which are shaken by the wiles of the enemy, O gloriously victorious martyrs *ye were taken up unto the most radiant citadel of martyrdom, O passion-bearers, unto a great height; and ye were accounted worthy to receive the unshakable Kingdom of God, O most sacred Mark and glorious Cyril. Pray ye that we all may obtain it!*

*Theotokion:* By thine intellectual radiance, O Maiden full of divine grace, enlighten our minds, and hearts, and thoughts, that walking uprightly along the paths of life, we may obtain mercy, ever proclaiming thy praises.

*In the same month, on the 30th day: The memory of our venerable father John, Author of the Ladder.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (О преславнаго чудесе):*

*Sticheron 1:* O father John, venerable one, truly thou didst ever carry the praises of God in thy throat,[^453] meditating on the most practical, divinely inspired words, O all-wise one; and thou wast enriched by the grace flowing thence, having become blessed, overthrowing the counsels of all the ungodly.

*Sticheron 2:* O father John, most glorious one, having cleansed thy soul with fountains of tears, and having propitiated God by all-night standings, thou didst soar aloft unto His love and beauty, O blessed one; which thou now worthily enjoyest, unceasingly rejoicing with thy fellow-sufferers, O divinely wise, venerable one.

*Sticheron 3:* O father John, venerable one, having winged thy mind unto God by faith, thou didst abhor the unstable confusion of the world; and having taken up thy cross, thou didst follow the All-seeing One; having subjected the untamed body unto the spirit by the ascetical struggles of fasting, through the power of the Holy Spirit.

*If it happen on Saturday or Sunday, the idiomelon is sung:*

*Glory, Tone 6:* Venerable father, into all the earth hath gone forth the proclamation of thy righteous deeds; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors, thou didst destroy the hosts of demons, and hast attained unto the angelic ranks, whose life thou didst blamelessly emulate. Having boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for our souls!

*If it be a fasting day, after the stichera is sung:*

*...Glory, both now, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion: written on the 31st day.*

*At the Aposticha, Glory, Tone 8. Idiomelon:* We honor thee, O our father John, as the instructor of a multitude of monks; for by thy path we have truly learned to walk uprightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ, and didst expose the power of the enemy, O converser with angels, companion of the venerable and of the righteous! With them pray unto the Lord, that our souls may find mercy.

*Troparion, Tone 8:* With the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren wilderness, and with sighs from the depths thou didst bear fruit unto a hundredfold of labors; and thou wast a luminary unto the universe, shining with miracles, O John our father: Pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved!

*Canon. A composition of Ignatius. Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Traversing the water as dry land, and escaping the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:* Having ascended unto the immaterial and noetic Light from the mourning of material things, O venerable John, when by thy prayers wilt thou enlighten me completely?[^454]

*Troparion:* Having tasted the sweetness of self-control,[^455] thou didst cast away the bitterness of the passions; therefore, more than honey and the honeycomb, O father, thou sweetenest our senses.

*Troparion:* Having mounted unto the height of the virtues, and having spat upon downward-dragging pleasures, thou wast shown forth to be the sweetness of salvation, O venerable father, unto thy flock.

*Theotokion:* Having ineffably given birth unto the Wisdom and Word of the Father, heal the grievous wound of my soul, and calm the illness of my heart.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparion:* With the coal of fasting, O venerable one, thou didst burn up the thorns of passions, and thou warmest the assemblies of monastics.

*Troparion:* Thou wast wholly compounded of ascetical spices as a sweet-smelling myrrh of purification,[^456] O venerable one, unto an odor of divine fragrance.

*Troparion:* Having trained thyself in the laws of asceticism, thou didst drown the passions like another host of Egyptians[^457] of old, by the streams of thy tears.

*Theotokion:* Calm my unstable turmoil of thoughts, O pure Mother of God, directing their motion unto thy Son.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben:*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а́ггльскїй):* Offering the ever-blooming fruits of teaching from thy books, O all-wise one, thou sweetenest the hearts of those who give heed unto them with sobriety, O blessed one; for it is a ladder leading souls upward from earth unto heaven and unto the abiding glory, for them that honor thee with faith.

*Then the Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):* Having supernaturally shone forth through contemplation and action, thou shinest with teachings; thou didst set down divinely wise laws for us, O blessed one, having set forth the steps of the beauty of virtues, and having exposed the deceits and snares of malice. Therefore thou leadest upward those who follow thy divine teachings, from passions unto impassibility, O John, equal to the angels: Pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of transgressions unto them that lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* As Virgin and unique among women, who without seed didst give birth in the flesh unto God, all the generations of mankind call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead settled within thee, and as an infant thou didst suckle the Creator and Lord. Therefore, we of angelic and human race worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud unto thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto us who venerate thy most holy childbearing in faith!

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly wailing, cried aloud: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy Crucifixion, which Thou endurest for the tender compassions of mercy, O long-suffering Lord, abyss of mercy and unemptying fount! Have compassion, and grant forgiveness of sins unto them that sing of Thy divine Passions in faith!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:* As a most sweet-smelling flower-garden and an ensouled paradise of virtues, thou didst blossom forth with self-control, whereby thou nurturest all who honor thee.

*Troparion:* As a lawgiver of spiritual discipline and the gentlest rule of monastics, having truly acquired thee as another Moses and David,[^458] O father, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:* Delighting in the waters of self-control, thou didst appear, O blessed one *O blessed one, a most fair-flowering branch, O father, bringing forth clusters of piety.*

*Theotokion:* Him Who came under time, having timelessly shone forth from the Father, hast thou borne for us, O Mother of God; pray Him to save those who sing of thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O Light unwaning, and alien darkness hath covered me, wretch that I am? But turn me back, and direct my paths unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:* Having extinguished all the passions with the dew of thy ascetical struggles, O blessed father, abundantly by the divine fire of love and faith, thou wast a luminary of self-control, and an enlightenment of impassibility, and a son of the day.

*Troparion:* Having nurtured the cluster of faith by thy divine husbandry, O father, thou didst cast it into winepresses and didst press out the labors of spiritual discipline; and filling the spiritual cup of self-control, thou makest glad the hearts of thy flock.

*Troparion:* Having courageously endured the assaults and wounds of invisible enemies, thou wast shown forth as a pillar of endurance, confirming thy flock with divine mantle upon the pastures of self-control, and upon the water of nourishment,[^459] O blessed one.

*Theotokion:* Following the words of thy lips, O all-immaculate one, we call thee blessed; for truly the Lord Who hath done great things with thee hath magnified thee,[^460] and He Who was born of thy womb hath shown thee to be the true Mother of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:* Thou didst gather in thy soul the divine wealth of the Spirit: blameless prayer, purity, integrity, perpetual vigilance, and the labors of self-control, whereby thou wast known as the house of God.

*Troparion:* Having transcended the baseness of earthly matter, O all-wise one, and having winged thy mind with immaterial prayer, thou wast shown forth as an heir of the resting-place on high, through perfection of life.

*Troparion:* By ascetical sweats, thou didst truly quench the burning coals of the enemy's arrows; and kindling the fire of faith, thou didst burn up the rebellions of the heresies of unbelief.

*Theotokion:* From Zion the beauty of the Most High shone forth, through the assumption of flesh according to the ineffable union, invested from thee, O ineffable one, and enlightened the world.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):* Upon the height of self-control the Lord hath set thee as a true guide, like an unerring star that guids the ends of the earth with light, O John, our father and instructor.

*Ikos:* Truly a house of God didst thou make thyself, O father, by thy divine virtues, manifestly adorning it like bright gold, setting forth faith, hope, and true love, expounding the divine laws; having trained thyself by self-control like an incorporeal being, having acquired understanding, courage, chastity, and humility, whereby thou wast exalted. Therefore, being enlightened by unceasing prayers, thou didst soar on the wings of secret visions, and didst receive the heavenly habitations, O John, our father and instructor.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The children who came from Judea in Babylon of old trod down the flame of the furnace by faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* On the verdant pasture of the Kingdom on high, O father, thou didst nurture the flock, and with the rod of dogmas driving away the beasts of heresies, thou didst sing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* Thou didst enter into the bridal chamber on high of the Kingdom of Christ, clad in a garment worthy of Him Who called thee,[^461] wherein thou also didst recline at table, crying aloud: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* The river, flooded with sins, didst thou show thyself to be by self-control, O father, drowning the wicked thoughts and cleansing the defilement of those who cry in faith: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:* From thy womb came forth the incarnate Lord of all, O Virgin; therefore, confessing thee to be the Mother of God in orthodox fashion, we cry unto thy Son: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts hymn: Praise and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Having truly acquired thee as an ensouled pillar and an image of self-control, O father, we all honor thy memory, O John.

*Troparion:* The multitude of monastics rejoices, and the choir of the venerable and righteous keeps festival; for worthily hast thou received the crown with them.

*Troparion:* Adorned with virtues, thou didst ascend into the bridal chamber of ineffable glory, singing a hymn unto Christ God unto the ages.

*Theotokion:* Despise not those who require the help that comes from thee, O Virgin, who hymn and exalt thee above all unto the ages.

*...unto the ages.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Let us people honor in song the pure Mother of God, who received the fire of the Godhead within her womb without being consumed, and magnify her in hymns.

*Troparion:* As a physician of those suffering in transgressions from God, O blessed one, thou wast shown forth as a destroyer and expeller of cunning spirits; therefore we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:* Having left the earth as a shadowy dwelling, O father, thou didst settle in the land of the meek,[^462] and with them thou rejoicest, delighting in divine sweetness.

*Troparion:* Today is a festive day, for it calls together the assemblies of monastics into a spiritual choir, unto a banquet and nourishment of incorruptible life.

*Theotokion:* He Who was born of thee settled within thee, O all-immaculate one, Who cast down the murderer who of old deceptively deceived the first ancestors in hideous guise, and He saved us all.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):* Thou didst turn away from attachment unto the world as from defilement; and having withered the flesh by fasting, thou didst renew the strength of the soul, O venerable one, and wast enriched with heavenly glory, O ever-memorable one. Therefore cease not to pray for us, O John!

*Theotokion:* Truly we confess thee to be the Mother of God, O Lady, who have been saved through thee; for thou didst ineffably give birth unto God, Who by the Cross destroyed death and drew unto Himself the assemblies of the venerable; with whom we praise thee, O Virgin.

*In the same month, on the 31st day: The memory of our venerable and wonderworking father Hypatius, Bishop of Gangra.*[^463]

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder!" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 1:* O divinely wise father Hypatius, having been well nurtured by self-control, thou didst ascend unto the most glorious height of virtues, clearly reaching forth unto the more secret contemplation, beholding the beauty of Christ; and having enlightened Thy mind and thought with His splendors, thou didst shine forth the beams of wonders unto all.

*Sticheron 2:* O father of fathers Hypatius, having shone forth with the light of Orthodoxy, thou didst enlighten the minds of those who run unto thee in faith, O venerable one; and having darkened the pupils of Arius,[^464] thou didst drive him away from the Church of Christ. Therefore, having all acquired thee as a luminary, we honor and call blessed thy sacred memory!

*Sticheron 3:* O sacred father Hypatius, having in thy heart the King Christ, Whom thou didst clearly preach as consubstantial with the Father,[^465] thou didst shine forth with rays of wonders, guiding all the world under the sun with light: thou didst slay the dragon,[^466] and by thy prayers thou didst cause warm streams of water to flow forth for the healing of passions.

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O vessel of virginity, and pure and fair dwelling of God, I pray thee, O all-immaculate one: Comfort my soul, harmed by the serpent's poison and mortified by transgressions; that I may glorify thee, who hast magnified all the faithful, O thou who art full of divine joy!

*Stavrotheotokion:* What is this sight beheld by mine eyes, O Master! He Who upholds all creation is lifted up upon the Tree, and Thou diest Who givest life unto all! The Mother of God cried aloud, weeping, when she beheld Him Who ineffably shone forth from her as God and Man, lifted up upon the Cross.

*Canon, whose acrostic is: With songs I crown the glorious Hypatius. A composition of lord Joseph.[^467] Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* The three mighty captains: He Who was born of the Virgin, drown the tripartite soul in the deep of impassibility, I pray Thee, that as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a hymn of victory unto Thee!

*Troparion:* Let us all gladly praise in tuneful hymns, as a universal luminary, the divine passion-bearer and wise hierarch, fount of wonders, good shepherd, and confirmation of the Church.

*Troparion:* The divine river that flooded thy pure soul, O most blessed one, hath poured forth for us, O all-wise one, the most pure waters of many wonders, which gush forth streams that drown the turbid passions, O Hypatius.

*Troparion:* Having mortified carnal pleasures through self-control, O Hypatius, thou didst have the true Life, Christ Who died for our sake, living within thy soul and directing all thy paths toward Him.

*Troparion:* A spotless temple of the Trinity didst thou make thy heart and soul, and sacred temples unto the Lord in praises, O venerable hieromartyr, thou didst build *thou didst build, having been a saving guide unto thy flock.*

*Theotokion:* The Savior and God didst thou incarnate from thy pure blood, O pure one, Who hath shown forth unto us His wise hierarch, O all-pure one, a saving and fervent protector, truly marvelous Hypatius.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:* With all thy heart thou didst cleave unto the Fountain of good things; and shining brightly thence with richest divine splendors, O sacred father.

*Troparion:* Hedged about with divine laws, by divine power thou didst destroy the assembly of lawless heresies, O father, saving thy flock, O father Hypatius.

*Troparion:* Enlightened by the light of the Spirit from the lamp on high, O wise one, lamps from the water were shown shining unto those who came by night.[^468]

*Troparion:* Fulfilling the blessed choir of divine fathers which Christ gathered together at the First Council, O blessed one, thou wast shown forth crushing the most foolish Arius.

*Theotokion:* Deliver me from every assault of the adversary serpent, O pure Virgin Mother, and enlighten my heart who glorify thee, the all-immaculate one, in faith.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "While the stone was sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́тану):* Thou didst shine forth in the world, O hierarch Hypatius, enlightening with wonders those who run unto thee in faith; and having suffered by blood, O wise one, thou didst become yet more united unto Christ, and thou dancest with the angels in heaven, praying for us. Glory to God Who hath strengthened thee! Glory to Him Who hath crowned thee! Glory to Him Who through thee works healings for all!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* Stretching forth thy divine hands wherewith thou didst bear the Creator Who took flesh in His goodness, O most holy Virgin, implore Him: To deliver us from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who dwelt in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who hath delivered us through thy childbearing!

*Stavrotheotokion:* Beholding Thee crucified upon the Cross by the lawless, and Thy side pierced by a spear from the common soldiers, O Savior, the all-pure one bitterly wailed, and marveling at Thy great and fearful endurance, cried aloud: Glory to Thy love toward mankind! Glory to Thy goodness: by Thy death Thou hast immortalized mortals *Glory to Thy goodness: by Thy death Thou hast immortalized mortals.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Seated in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the transcendent in divinity came with incorruptible arm, and saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:* Keeping the guileless word and teaching sincerely, thou didst proclaim the Word co-eternal with the Father; having vanquished the Arian delusion, O hieromartyr, wonderworker worthy of all wonder!

*Troparion:* The most bitter waters flowing from the mountains didst thou show to be healing by a mere sign,[^469] having acquired living water flowing into life eternal in thy heart, O father.

*Troparion:* Having ministered as a priest in venerable fashion, and having suffered according to the law, thou didst show thy priesthood to be most radiant through the shed blood, for the love of Him Who poured out His precious blood for the redemption of mankind.

*Troparion:* The grace working wonders upon thee did not delay to give over unto bitter demons those who bitterly and godlessly put thee to death,[^470] O most blessed and sacred martyr of Christ!

*Theotokion:* Streams of thy gifts, fountains of healings, and distributions of divine riches are dispensed from the life-originating arm of the Almighty Who was born of thy womb, O Bride of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst give birth unto the timeless Son, Who grants peace unto all that hymn thee!

*Troparion:* The comprehension of thy grace surpasses the mind; tongue cannot tell the boundless abysses of thy wonders, O martyr, hierarch, and shepherd, adornment of the people of Gangra, and luminary of all creation!

*Troparion:* Thou didst slay the dragon, committing it to the fire, and didst show forth the divine grace most richly dwelling in thee, both before the emperor and before all Orthodox people,[^471] O most sacred one!

*Troparion:* As a seal and guardian of the treasury, the emperor depicted thine icon, O father,[^472] honoring thee thereby and manifestly proclaiming that the honor paid to an image passes over to the prototype,[^473] O Hypatius!

*Troparion:* Having struggled greatly, thou wast greatly glorified, performing wonders in sacred fashion: driving away diseases, expelling demons, frustrating their assaults, and vanquishing the destructive evil counsel of heresies.

*Theotokion:* Thou didst give birth unto the hypostatic Wisdom of God, O all-pure Virgin Mother of God *Mother of God, Who hast shown the saints to be all-wise; therefore the haughtiness and cruel craftiness of the author of malice hath fallen!*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which flowed from Thy side for mercy's sake!

*Troparion:* Thou didst ascend unto the height of virtues, O Hypatius, and didst draw near, O blessed one, unto the most pure Fountain of divine gifts, and didst pour forth an abyss of wonders.

*Troparion:* The casting of stones became a crown for thee,[^474] and unto the heavenly height both steps and a ladder, whereby having ascended, thou didst draw near unto God in intimacy.

*Troparion:* Thy shrine ever pours forth healings, and the blessing received from thy precious dust, O father, truly consums malice like dust.

*Troparion:* Having suffered according to the law, and having been honored as a martyr in deepest old age, thou didst pass over rejoicing unto thy fathers; and worthily didst thou receive the crown of thy labors from Christ.

*Theotokion:* O wonder of all wonders most new! How a Virgin, without knowing man, having conceived within her womb Him Who upholds all things, contained Him without being straitened!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Thou Who didst save Thy children of Abraham in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice righteously snared, O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* The crawling serpent opened not its mouth against thee at all, but like a slave went into the fire, obeying thy most holy command, O ever-memorable one, as thou criedst: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:* Having mingled the shedding of blood with tears, O wise one, thou didst compound one drink from both, a delicious torrent for thee to be remembered unto ages, O wonder-worthy praise of martyrs!

*Troparion:* Entering as into a flower-garden of thy venerable sufferings, O blessed one, we sweeten our soul with fragrance, knowing thee to be the sweet aroma of Christ, and we cry aloud, O Hypatius: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:* O pure one who knewest not wedlock, who without seed didst conceive Him Who is consubstantial with the Father, and in the flesh didst bear Him from thy holy blood, O Bride of God: Cease not to pray now, that He may save all!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious children in the furnace; then indeed in figure *in figure, but now in reality, it awakens the whole world to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!*

*Troparion:* Thy voice, having mingled with the light and being illumined by divine splendors, destroyed the gloom of heresies, O all-wise one, and guided unto the day of Orthodoxy the people crying aloud: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Having appeared most fair as a reed-pen,[^475] O all-wise one, thy tongue inscribed the law by grace, blotting out all the ancient figures upon the tablets, O Hypatius; therefore, as a wise scribe, we hymn thee in song unto all ages.

*Troparion:* As a divine pillar of fire, reaching unto the heavenly height, an unshakable bulwark, an indestructible wall, the confirmation of the Church, the honor of hierarchs, and the fair beauty of martyrs, let wonderworking Hypatius be honored!

*Troparion:* Thou wast accounted worthy to behold Him Whom thou didst desire, being released by the manifestation of truth; for while thy warm blood was yet dripping, O martyr, thou didst ascend unto the ultimate desire, dancing with the divine ranks of martyrs and the venerable unto all ages!

*Theotokion:* Held fast by many tribulations, and imperiled by the turmoil of sin, do thou, O pure Lady and Mother of God, guide me now unto the sorrowless longing, the tranquility of the Spirit; for thou art the haven of Christians. Therefore we sing thy praise unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Eve indeed, through the sickness of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the blossoming of thy childbearing, didst bud forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:* Like a sweet-scented rose and a sacred lily, O hierarch, like a cypress, like divine and fragrant myrrh, thy shrine pours forth the fragrance of wonders, expelling foul-smelling diseases, O all-honored hieromartyr!

*Troparion:* Thou wast shown forth as a converser with the prophets, and of the sacred martyrs who finished their course well in blood; equal in standing with the holy and venerable, and a partaker with all the righteous who have been from the beginning of time,[^476] O wonder-worthy one!

*Troparion:* Thy memory hath shone forth unto us today brighter than the sun, enlightening our hearts with immaterial light, who in faith call thee blessed therein, O Hypatius, and prayerfully praise thee *with immaterial light; with thy light enlighten our thoughts, O Hypatius, who celebrate thy divine and light-bearing memory; and deliver from fire, and torment, and every temptation by thy supplications, those who piously call thee blessed!*

*Theotokion:* Exalt thy grace, O pure one, magnify thy supplication, and bring forward the angelic ranks, O Mother of God: the choirs of the apostles and prophets, and all the righteous with the martyrs, that we who honor thee may be saved![^477]

*(Cul-de-lampe: An ornate Baroque woodcut engraving of a woven wicker basket overflowing with blossoms of roses, poppies, botanical foliage, and ears of grain, marking the conclusion of the Month of March.)*[^478]

---

# FOOTNOTES AND SCHOLARLY COMMENTARY



[^1]: Augustus III (1696–1763), King of Poland and Grand Duke of Lithuania (1733–1763), Elector of Saxony (as Frederick Augustus II).

[^2]: Sylvester Lubieniecki-Rudnytsky (d. 1777), Basilian Bishop of Lutsk and Ostroh (1756–1777), appointed Exarch of the Metropolitan See of Kyiv.

[^3]: Hypatius (Ipatiy) Bilynsky (1704–1771), Protoarchimandrite of the Basilian Order of Saint Josaphat (1751–1771).

[^4]: The year 7269 Anno Mundi in the Byzantine era corresponds to 1761 Anno Domini.

[^5]: During Great Lent, if the commemoration of a saint without festal rank falls on a weekday, the penitential service with "Alleluia" and prostrations replaces "God is the Lord" and the dismissive troparion. Outside of Great Lent, the troparion is sung at Vespers and Matins.

[^6]: Slavonic witness reads "я҆́ги бл҃гоизво́ли", an epigraphic dialectal/orthographic variant for "я҆́кѡ бл҃гоизво́ли".

[^7]: In the 1761 Pochaiv imprint, Folio 3 (leaves p5–p6) is bound before Folio 2 (leaves p7–p8). Leaf p5 opens mid-sentence with the conclusion of the Ikos of St. Eudokia from the bottom of Folio 2v (leaf p8), followed by Ode 7 and Ode 8 of the Canon. In accordance with Master Translation Standard MTS-1 (Rule 15), the physical facsimile sequence of the codex is preserved.

[^8]: Lenten rubrical distinction: if March 2 falls on a weekday in Great Lent, the penitential office with "Alleluia" is sung; outside of Lent, the dismissive troparion of the Hieromartyr is chanted.

[^9]: The 1761 Pochaiv imprint prints the Podoben heading as "Прелѣ́тнѡ" (representing Greek "Ταχὺ"), the Ruthenian equivalent of the canonical Byzantine automelon "Ско́ро предварѝ" ("Speedily anticipate").

[^10]: Epigraphic witness reads "Гроте́нческїѧ церко́вь", referencing the local shrine/sanctuary where the relics of St. Eudokia of Heliopolis were translated and venerated.

[^11]: The Slavonic acrostic reads "Бж҃їе да́нїе бы́лъ є҆сѝ лѣ́потнѡ блаже́нне" ("Thou hast fittingly been a divine gift, O blessed one"), highlighting the Greek etymology of the name Theodotus (*Θεόδοτος*, "given by God").

[^12]: Slavonic "О́гньнымъ строга́нїа" reflects Greek ὀνυχισμοὺς (iron claws or flesh-scrapers) applied to the martyr's body.

[^13]: Slavonic "Мо́ря ѕловѣ́рїе" represents the maritime setting of Cyrenia in Cyprus, or an epigraphic calque on Greek μῶρον (folly, senseless impiety) collated with Byzantine menaia.

[^14]: Slavonic "на ѻ́дръ раже́нъ" refers to the iron gridiron or fiery bed of spikes (Greek ἐπὶ τηγάνου / ἐπὶ κλίνης πυρωθείσης) upon which St. Theodotus was tortured.

[^15]: The Slavonic acrostic reads "Трїе́хъ велича́йшихъ страда́нїа че́стїю мꙋ́кѡвъ" ("I sing the glory of the three greatest prize-bearing martyrs through the honor of their sufferings"), translating the Greek acrostic of the canon by Joseph the Hymnographer: Τῶν τριῶν μεγίστων ἀθλοφόρων τὴν δόξαν ᾄδω.

[^16]: Slavonic "натреби́щныхъ... кро́ви" refers to pagan sacrificial blood poured out upon idol altars (тре́бище), which the martyrs abolished through their confession.

[^17]: Allusion to the renowned hagiographical tradition of St. Gerasimus of the Jordan taming and healing a wounded lion along the banks of the Jordan, which served the saint in his monastery.

[^18]: The Slavonic acrostic reads "Гера́сі́ма хвалю̀, ꙗ҆́кѡ вѣне́цъ плету̀" ("I praise Gerasimus, as weaving a crown"), corresponding to the Byzantine Greek acrostic of the Matins canon: Γεράσιμον μέλπω σε, πλέκων ὡς στέφος.

[^19]: Slavonic "положе́нїемъ бг҃а бы́лъ є҆сѝ" reflects the patristic doctrine of deification (theosis) by adoption/grace (Greek θέσει θεός), contrasting created humanity elevated by divine energy with Christ Who alone is God by essence (φύσει θεός).

[^20]: Allusion to the renowned miracle of St. Gerasimus of the Jordan and the lion, which served the saint in his monastery and, according to the Vita, grieved and died upon his tomb.

[^21]: An allusion to LXX Psalm 91:13: "The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall be increased like a cedar in Lebanon."

[^22]: The Slavonic acrostic reads "Пою̀ ко́нѡна мꙋ́ченика вѣнцено́снаго" ("I sing Conon the crown-bearing martyr"), which represents the Byzantine Greek acrostic of the Matins canon: Τὸν στεφηφόρον Κόνωνα μέλπω μάρτυρα.

[^23]: According to the ancient Vita of St. Conon of Isauria, at seven years of age the Archangel Michael appeared to him in the form of a radiant man, taught him the Christian faith, and instructed him in divine mysteries.

[^24]: St. Conon converted his pagan parents, Nestor and Nada, to the Christian faith; his father Nestor subsequently received the crown of martyrdom for refusing to sacrifice to idols.

[^25]: The Vita records that St. Conon had authority over unclean spirits, commanding them to dig caves, till gardens, and guard the city, forcing the demons to acknowledge Christ's power.

[^26]: The 42 Holy Martyrs of Amorium (Phrygia) were Byzantine military commanders and notables captured by the Abbasid Caliph al-Mu'tasim during the sack of Amorium in 838 AD; after enduring seven years of imprisonment and refusing apostasy to Islam, they were beheaded on the banks of the Tigris River on March 6, 845 AD.

[^27]: Constantine Baboutzikos, Basil, Callistus Melissenos, Aetius, and Theophilus were leading generals and patricians among the 42 Amorion martyrs.

[^28]: Ascribed to Patriarch Methodius I of Constantinople (843–847 AD), a contemporary of the martyrs who composed hymns in their honor shortly after their execution.

[^29]: Composed by a hymnographer from the monastery of Souka (Saint Chariton) in Palestine, known as "the Soukiote" (Greek: τοῦ Σουκιώτου).

[^30]: "Agarenes" (Slavonic: а҆га́рѧне), descendants of Hagar, the standard Byzantine liturgical designation for the Saracens/Muslim Arabs of the Abbasid Caliphate who sacked Amorium.

[^31]: The Matins canon is the work of Ignatius the Deacon (c. 775–848 AD), Metropolitan of Nicaea and renowned Byzantine hymnographer.

[^32]: St. Theodore Krateros ("the Strong"), one of the prominent Byzantine military commanders martyred at Amorium, noted for his noble bearing and steadfast endurance.

[^33]: Lit. "a pair of sufferers with a four-numbered decad" (Slavonic: дво́ица... съ четверочисленною десѧти́цею), referring poetically to the Forty-two Martyrs of Amorium (2 + 40).

[^34]: An allusion to the steadfast refusal of Callistus and his companions to accept apostasy to Islam, which the hymnographer characterizes as a "second defilement" that could never taint their baptismal purity.

[^35]: The reference to deliverance from the "oppression of blasphemous heretics" reflects the 9th-century context of the Iconoclast controversy, under which many of the Amorion martyrs and their contemporary hymnographers lived prior to the Triumph of Orthodoxy in 843 AD.

[^36]: "Ishmael" (representing the Saracen caliph and Arab forces, regarded biblically as Ishmaelites) refers to Caliph al-Mu'tasim and his representatives who repeatedly interrogated the captive martyrs.

[^37]: An allusion to the translation of the holy relics of St. Theodore Krateros and his victorious transit to the heavenly realm.

[^38]: The bodies of the 42 Amorion martyrs were thrown into the Euphrates or Tigris River by order of Caliph al-Mu'tasim after their decapitation on March 6, 845 AD.

[^39]: According to the Greek and Slavonic Vitae, a miracle occurred whereby the severed heads of the martyrs miraculously rejoined their bodies in the waters, and their relics were recovered by local Christians.

[^40]: An allusion to the famous opening words of St. Basil the Great's Homily on the Forty Martyrs of Sebaste (Homilia XIX in sanctos quadraginta martyres, PG 31:508): "What satiety can there ever be for a lover of the martyrs in remembering them?"

[^41]: The Holy Hieromartyrs of Cherson (Taurica, modern Crimea) were missionary bishops sent from Jerusalem in the early 4th century (c. 300–315 AD) to evangelize the Scythians and Greeks of Tauric Chersonesus. The seven celebrated bishops are: Basil, Ephraim, Eugene, Elpidius, Agathodorus, Aetherius, and Capito.

[^42]: The Vita records that St. Basil of Cherson was expelled by the pagan inhabitants, but when the only son of a local dignitary died, the grieving parents called upon Basil, who through prayer to Christ raised the youth from the dead, resulting in the conversion and baptism of many citizens.

[^43]: St. Capito, appointed bishop of Cherson by Emperor Constantine the Great, was challenged by the pagan population to prove the power of Christ by entering a burning lime-kiln or furnace; he entered the flames clad in his episcopal vestments, remained unharmed, and gathered glowing coals into his phelonion, bringing about the miraculous conversion of the entire city.

[^44]: The Church Slavonic acrostic reads "Седмочи́сленный па́стырей ли́къ почита́ю" ("I honor the sevenfold choir of pastors"), translating the Byzantine Greek acrostic of Joseph the Hymnographer: Ἑπταρίθμων ποιμένων τιμῶ χορείαν.

[^45]: St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886 AD), one of the most prolific Byzantine liturgical poets, celebrated for his canons in the Menaion and Parakletike.

[^46]: Another reference to the miracle of St. Basil of Cherson raising the son of the city's ruler from the dead through prayer to Christ.

[^47]: Although the bishops hailed from different regions (Jerusalem, Athens, etc.), they were united in their missionary episcopate to the city and region of Cherson in Taurica.

[^48]: The three holy youths Ananias, Azarias, and Misael in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3), a standard typological parallel for St. Capito's miraculous preservation in the furnace of Cherson.

[^49]: St. Ephraim of Cherson preached the Gospel to the Scythians along the Danube, where he suffered martyrdom by decapitation during the Diocletian persecution.
